# Features — gisik-nokma

Legend: ✅ user-observable · ⚙️ internal subsystem · ? inferred/unverified

## 1. Reference base

- ⚙️ **Base design documents** — the three upstream documents that designed the base: the tracker (2026-08-18), the calendar view (2026-08-19), and the calendar's implementation plan. `reference/design-docs/`

## 2. Product design

- ⚙️ **Product design spec** — the brainstorm's approved output: audience (non-technical operators), the honesty contract, event-sourced architecture with a shared rule engine, workflow engine and builder, offline sync with contested claims, permissions and visibility, views and search, testing discipline, and the two dogfooding targets. Approved and merged (PR #1). `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-gisik-nokma-product-design.md`
- ⚙️ **Implementation plan series** — eleven task-by-task plans covering the whole build, ledger through deployment and dogfooding, written in parallel from one pinned contract and then reconciled. Reviewed and merged via PR #2; execution in progress, plan by plan. `docs/superpowers/plans/`
- ⚙️ **Series coordination document** — the single authority for cross-plan interfaces, naming, and ownership, and the record of every decision taken at review: where a plan disagrees with it, it wins. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-20-00-series-coordination.md`

*(Product feature sections begin once implementation starts. Sections 3–13, and the matching `test-cases/` buckets, are already reserved one per plan by the coordination document. The brainstorm that produced the spec started from `docs/brainstorm-kickoff.md`.)*

## 3. Foundation & event ledger

- ⚙️ **Monorepo workspace** — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo, TypeScript strict, Node 22, ESM, Vitest; packages `@gisik-nokma/domain`, `@gisik-nokma/rule-engine`, `@gisik-nokma/server`, `@gisik-nokma/ui`, `@gisik-nokma/app-core`, `@gisik-nokma/sync-client`, `@gisik-nokma/web`. The gate is one `build`/`test`/`typecheck` triple per workspace package — a count re-derived by running it, never written down here, because a number in prose has a shelf life that ends at the next package. `build.outputs` names `.next/**` beside `dist/**`, without which turbo would report a cache hit for a Next build whose output it never stored; `globalEnv` names `SERVER_ORIGIN` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SERVER_URL`, because the latter is BAKED INTO the browser bundle and a cached build compiled against a different one would otherwise be served as if it were current. Root configs `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `turbo.json`, `tsconfig.base.json`; dev Postgres via `docker-compose.yml`.
- ⚙️ **CI pipeline (skeleton)** — GitHub Actions workflow on every pull request and push to `main`: a single job installs via pnpm, brings up a Postgres 16 service container matching `docker-compose.yml`'s user/password/port, and runs the turbo gate (`build`, `test`, `typecheck`); IST convergence rides the same per-test-database `ALTER DATABASE` the test harness already runs, not a container-level override. The independent-CI precondition for the coordination doc's auto-merge protocol. Scoped to what Plans 1–2 landed — lint, `check:env` (Plan 11 T6), the client half of the fixtures suite and the dual-client job (Plan 10), the e2e job (Plans 4/6), and the interim-plumbing gate (Plan 10) land with their owning plans; Plan 11's own catalogue task owns the full pipeline under §13. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- ⚙️ **Event envelope & vocabulary** — the exact `EventEnvelope` contract with zod validation, ULID helpers, and the versioned event-type registry; pure `parseEvent` refuses unknown types, unknown versions, invalid payloads, malformed envelopes, and — for a registration that declares an `entityKind` — an envelope whose `entity.kind` differs (`entity-kind-mismatch`; layered with the rule engine's own kind checks by design, since fixtures and offline replicas bypass `parseEvent`). Provisional seed vocabulary: `workspace.created` v1, `workspace.renamed` v1; the registry now also holds `workflow.defined` v1 (Plan 2). `packages/domain/`
  - ⚙️ **An assistant's write is a distinct provenance, not a flavour of the web's** — `ORIGINS` carries `'mcp'` alongside `'web' | 'mobile' | 'desktop' | 'server'` (MCP plan Task 0, owner ruling 1). In a product whose stated purpose is a board that cannot lie about its own history, a write claiming `'web'` when a machine made it is the history lying about who acted. `Origin`, `z.enum(ORIGINS)` and the permission gate's `CLIENT_ORIGINS` all DERIVE from the array, so the member was the whole edit — the gate gained an `mcp` case it did not have to be told about, and no migration was needed (`origin text NOT NULL`, no `CHECK`, no Postgres enum, verified across every migration). The rule engine's fixture harness now imports `ORIGINS` instead of re-spelling it. Case 3.163. `packages/domain/src/envelope.ts`, `packages/rule-engine/src/fixtures/harness.ts`
- ⚙️ **Contest event vocabulary** — `claim.contested` v1 and `claim.contested_resolved` v1, the two types that make a sync-time refusal into ledger history instead of a silent drop (spec honesty invariant 5, coordination amendment 16). The contest carries the refused claim whole (`originalEvent`, a full `SubmittedEvent`) and is refused if the claim arrives without its `payload` — `z.unknown()` makes that key optional in zod, so the envelope door's own payload-presence check (Plan 4's amendment) is what stops the ledger from recording that something was claimed without recording what. `null` and `{}` still pass: an empty claim is a claim, an absent one is not. Resolution is `redone` | `converted-to-comment` | `discarded`, and the first two must link the event they produced — a redo without its replacement id, or a conversion without its comment id, is refused. Both types bind `entityKind: 'ticket'` in the registry, so a contest filed against a workflow or a workspace is refused at the door with `entity-kind-mismatch` rather than appended to an append-only table where it could never be corrected. Alongside them: `renderContestedClaimText(payload)`, the one attributed, IST-dated, rule-naming sentence a banner shows and a "convert to comment" writes into the ledger forever, and `plainTextToDoc(text)`, the inverse of `docToPlainText` — the minimal ProseMirror doc that conversion needs because bodies are PM JSON. `packages/domain/src/events/contest-events.ts`, registrations in `packages/domain/src/vocabulary.ts`
- ⚙️ **Backlog rank keys (`rankBetween`) and `ticket.ranked`** — backlog/closed spec §8.1, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 1). `rankBetween(before, after)` returns an order-preserving fractional key strictly between its neighbours over the alphabet `0-9a-z` (36 symbols, so plain string comparison IS the order), extending the shorter side — so inserting between the same two tickets forever only lengthens ONE key and renumbers nobody, which is what makes a reorder exactly one claim instead of a column rewrite. `compareByRank` orders ranked tickets first by key, then the unranked by `ticketId` ascending (a ULID is creation order), falling back to `ticketId` on equal keys so the order is total and stable rather than decided by the sort's own tie-breaking. `ticket.ranked` v1 carries `{rank}`, is registered against `entityKind: 'ticket'`, and joins `TICKET_EVENT_TYPES`; the reducer folds it onto `TicketState.rank` and moves nothing else — `stateId` is untouched for the same reason it is on the closed pair, because a reorder is not a status change and letting it look like one would put a move nobody made into the transition log four-eyes reads. **The key space excludes keys ending in `0`, and the payload door enforces it.** Nothing in the alphabet sorts below `'0'`, so `rankBetween('a', 'a0')` has no answer to give — and does not refuse one either: measured with a step cap, it appends `'0'` forever (`NONTERMINATING at i=20001`). Every producer is safe by construction (the midpoint digit appended is always >= 1), so the door is the single place a hand-written key could reach the ledger; `ticketRankedPayload` therefore refines with `isRankKey` rather than the looser `RANK_KEY_REGEX`, which admits `'a0'`. Backlog-only is the ENGINE's door (`checkTicketRanked`, Task 2), not the schema's. **A ticket nobody has ranked carries `rank: null` — the key PRESENT and null, not absent** (Task 3, 2026-09-18, superseding this bullet's Task-1 text, which said the opposite and said why it was deferring). Task 1's creation branch deliberately omitted the field because `rowToTicketState` builds a `TicketState` from COLUMNS and there was no `rank` column yet, so the reducer and the server's read could not agree in SHAPE. Task 3 adds the column, and then the decision is measured rather than weighed: the dual-client rig compares whole `TicketState`s with `toEqual`, so a reducer that omits forces the server read to omit, and an omitting server read puts an absent key on the single-ticket wire for every unranked ticket — which `mapRank` would have to tolerate, the exact shape case 13.603's second `it` forbids. **Made `rowToTicketState` omit the key when the column is NULL and 13 rig cases across all four dual-client files reddened**, every one a one-key shape mismatch. So: creation writes the null, `rowToTicketState` reads the column unconditionally, the replica reads `state.rank ?? null` at the boundary, and every reader still spells `?? null` — for the legacy test literals' sake, not the producers'. Spec §8.1 states the same answer in the domain's words (`null` = never ranked). Cases 13.694–13.696 (13.696 tightened from `rank ?? null` to `toBeNull()` by Task 3, since presence is now the contract). `packages/domain/src/rank.ts`, `packages/domain/src/events/ticket-events.ts`, `packages/domain/src/ticket-state.ts`, `packages/domain/src/entity-state.ts`, `packages/domain/src/vocabulary.ts`
- ⚙️ **Backlog rank on the wire — `proj_tickets.rank`, `TicketCard.rank`, `ListRow.rank`** — backlog/closed spec §8.1, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 3). The column is one field with one meaning, `text NULL`, so unlike the `closed` pair beside it there is no fold and nothing that can disagree with anything: `upsertTicket` writes it from `TicketState.rank` on both the INSERT and the ON CONFLICT SET list, and `rowToTicketState` reads it unconditionally, which is what keeps the server read model the same SHAPE as the reducer's output (the dual-client rig compares the two with `toEqual`). `TicketCard.rank` and `ListRow.rank` are REQUIRED `string | null`, and the key rides every card-building route through `assembleTicketCards` rather than a second per-route enumeration. **The client half is real, not assumed:** `mapRank` maps the key on BOTH bodies it is served on — `mapListRow` (the list) and `mapTicketState` (the single ticket) — refusing a key outside the alphabet with `isRankKey`, the domain's own predicate rather than the looser `RANK_KEY_REGEX`, and refusing an ABSENT key instead of defaulting it (the 13.603 lesson). Ordering is unchanged and deliberately so: the list route keeps its `ticket_id` keyset cursor (plan ruling R1) and carries `rank` on every row for the Backlog tab to sort client-side. There is no index on the column — the server never sorts by it, and an index no query reads is indistinguishable from one that works. Cases 13.702–13.704. `apps/server/migrations/1789200000000_proj-tickets-rank.sql`, `apps/server/src/projections/tickets.ts`, `apps/server/src/read/ticket-cards.ts`, `packages/domain/src/read-models.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/view-types.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/replica/replica-data-source.ts`
- ⚙️ **Client envelope builder** — `newSubmittedEvent(...)`, the one place a client mints an event: it fills `id` (ULID), `version` and `clientTime` and copies the rest of the caller's claim. `serverTime` and `seq` are absent by construction, because they are the server's to assign at ingest and a client that invented them would be asserting facts it does not own. `packages/domain/src/submit.ts`
- ⚙️ **IST day boundaries** — `istDayOf(instant)` and `istDayBounds(day)` against the fixed +05:30 offset (India has no DST), plus `IST_TIME_ZONE`: the single origin for where a day starts. "Due today", "overdue", calendar-cell assignment and day counts across the whole product resolve here, never from the server's locale or the viewer's device. `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`
- ⚙️ **IST instant rendering & clock skew** — `formatIstInstant(instant)` is the ONE way the product prints an absolute instant to a person (`'21 Aug 2026, 00:00 IST'`). Its day comes from `istDayOf`, so a printed timestamp can never disagree with the calendar cell the same instant lands in; it does constant-offset arithmetic with no `Intl` and no date library, because Hermes on mobile does not reliably ship a timezone database and a silent fallback to the device's zone would be the offline copy disagreeing about when things happened. Anything that is not a well-formed, real ISO instant travels back verbatim rather than as an invented time: unparseable, year-extended, calendar-impossible (`2026-02-30`), a day claim like `2026-08-20` that `Date.parse` would call midnight UTC, or a string with no stated offset that would otherwise be read in the device timezone. `IST_CLOCK_NOTE` is the line a surface puts beside a time so a collaborator in another timezone knows which clock they are reading. Beside them, `clockSkewMs` / `GROSS_CLOCK_SKEW_MS` (10 min) / `hasGrossClockSkew` compute skew from the two clocks the envelope already keeps: DERIVED, never stored and never projected, so the flag is identical wherever it is computed and an unparseable client claim counts as gross rather than as zero. `packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts`
- ⚙️ **Plain-text document rendering** — `docToPlainText(doc: unknown): string`, a dependency-free ProseMirror/Tiptap JSON walker (coordination amendment 12): total on junk input, mention nodes `{type:'mention', attrs:{userId, label?}}` render as `@label` falling back to `@userId`, image/attachment nodes render as nothing. Pulled forward as pre-work (Plan 5 Task 1) because `proj_search` (Plan 5), Plan 7's editor, and Plan 9's notifications all consume it; landed here in §3, and Plan 5's own catalogue task (Task 16) has since run — 2026-09-02 — and deliberately LEFT it here rather than relocating it to §7, because the bullet belongs beside the ledger material it renders. Its TEST CASES are 7.1, 7.2 and 7.159 in `test-cases/07-api-realtime-search.md`, where §7's reservation held: `doc-text.test.ts` had been cited by no `test-cases/` file at all until that task ran. `packages/domain/src/doc-text.ts`
- ⚙️ **Design tokens — the one colour origin** — `primitives` (hex palette) resolved into 35 semantic `TOKEN_ROLES` for light AND dark, emitted as `--gn-*` CSS custom properties (`themeStylesheet()`: light default, dark by explicit `data-theme="dark"` or by OS preference when unchosen). The origin declares its own `CONTRAST_PAIRS` and the suite verifies **all 33 pairs** in both themes — a palette edit that breaks readability fails the build. *(That count read "all 7 pairs" until 2026-09-08 and had been wrong since the pre-launch sweep added eighteen and `/mcp/tokens` a nineteenth — a stale number in the sentence whose whole job is to say how much is guaranteed. Recounted from the array, not incremented.)* **The palette is Apple HIG increased-contrast as of 2026-09-08** (`docs/hig-adoption-brief.md` §8) — the *accessible* variants, not the stock ones, because the stock palette failed **15 of these 27 pairs**. Four values deliberately are not Apple's, each with its reason at the line: `green700` `#1e7a34` is kept because Apple's `#248A3D` is the single pair that still fails (`successText` at **4.1233** vs 4.5 on the shipped `onColorWarmLight` `#fbf7f3` ground, against the incumbent's **5.0662**); the border primitives are untouched until the border sweep can remove their declarations in the same change; the dark status inks are untouched because §8 is silent on them and all clear; and the muted-text greys are solid rather than the alpha the brief flags as its own reconstruction. `categoryOpen` also stopped sharing a primitive with `textMuted`: Apple's accessible grey is right for a chip dot at 3:1 and **fails the text job at 4.16 on `surfaceSunken`**. Cases 13.281–13.282. **Borders then came off entirely** (R1(c), 2026-09-08): 54 declarations removed across `ui.css` and `globals.css`, the `border` role remapped to a grey that clears 3:1 so the state-bearing dashed markers it still paints are honest, and `border`/`borderStrong`/`focusRing` on `surface` declared as contrast pairs — **all one change, because declaring them alone is a red gate and remapping alone puts a heavy rule on every card while no gate objects.** Non-text contrast measured on the rendered signed-in surface with one instrument before and after: **13 of 20 classes below 3:1 → 0 of 6.** Non-primary controls are now identified by fill plus an accent semibold label (fill 1.09–1.15 and carrying nothing; label 5.31–6.81 and carrying it all). Three text fields keep their borders deliberately under `hig`/`minimal` — an empty field has no label to identify it. **Under `neo` (the shipped default as of Task 14) the field is deliberately BORDERLESS instead** — see the Segmented control bullet below for that exception and its owner-accepted waiver. Cases 13.283–13.284. Coordination amendment 11; no colour literal is legal anywhere else in any client package. **The scanner enforcing it judges POSITION, not shape** — comments are stripped over the whole file, and a hex counts only where a colour is applied: a CSS declaration value, or a string in script. It has to: `#270` — a PR number in prose — is three hex digits and therefore a legal CSS shorthand colour, so no tightening of the pattern could separate the two, and on 2026-09-07 it turned `main` red. The value-position rule also stops a CSS **ID selector** (`#abc { }`) being read as a colour, which was latent from the start. **What it deliberately no longer catches: a colour written inside a comment** — amendment 11 governs what the client ships, and a hex in prose ships nothing. **`StyleName` is a third runtime axis (`hig` | `minimal` | `neo`), added 2026-09-09 beside `ThemeName` for the neomorphism direction** — `radiusOf(style, role)` resolves two radius roles (`card`, `control`) and `shadowValueOf(style, theme, role)` resolves five shadow roles (`raised`, `sunken`, `overlay`, `sunken-compact`, `selected`) to a full CSS `box-shadow` value or `'none'`; `themeStylesheet()` emits both alongside the colour variables under `[data-style="minimal"|"neo"]` and their dark/OS-dark arms, with `hig` reached through the unmarked base exactly as `light` is. **The owner's exported neo-round prototype palette is ADOPTED for surface/raised/sunken/overlay/fill3/fill4 in EVERY style, including `hig`** (the shipped cool palette's `paper`/`sand`/`coal*` primitives are retired) — `accent` stays shipped pending the owner's word on the prototype's own accent picker, and the accessibility floor is kept by recorded minimal nudges (each a docblock line: export value → shipped value → why) rather than by lowering a `CONTRAST_PAIRS` minimum. Shadow colours are alpha primitives and carry the same no-`CONTRAST_PAIRS`-row rule the fill ladder already has. Cases 13.337–13.338, 13.341, 13.347. **A fourth shadow role, `sunken-compact`, joined 2026-09-16 (owner round 3: "check the prototype for the shadows of the sunken badges. looks like too much shadow for a small badge")** — `[data-style="neo"] .gn-chip` and `[data-style="neo"] .ticket-type-list__badge` had been carved with `sunken`'s own 4px/4px/9px inset (the same treatment `.gn-field__input`/`.ticket-type-list__row` legitimately use), which the prototype's own `--chipShadow` (`desktop.dc.html:59` light, `:71` dark) never specifies for a badge-scale element — it defines its own smaller 2px/2px/4px step. Three of the new role's four layer colours are byte-identical to primitives the file already had (`shadowNeoRaisedDarkLight`, `shadowNeoHighlightLight`, `shadowNeoSunkenHighlightDark`, verified by rgba→hex conversion, not eyeballed); only the dark theme's first layer needed a new one, `shadowNeoSunkenCompactDarkDark` (`rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` → `#0000008c`, distinct from `sunken`'s own dark-layer `rgba(0,0,0,0.6)`). `hig`/`minimal` stay `[]` for it, matching the prototype's `--chipShadow:none`. Every remaining `--gn-shadow-sunken` call site in `ui.css` was individually reviewed against the prototype's three geometries and confirmed field/tray-scale, correctly on `sunken`, EXCEPT `.gn-segmented__item--on` (`ui.css:2474`), which the prototype's own `--segOnShadow` gives a THIRD, distinct 3px/3px/6px geometry the product does not yet have a role for — flagged, deliberately left alone at the time, a separate scope decision pending the owner's word (**resolved owner round 5, 2026-09-16 — see below**). Case 13.411; the emission-list/declared-list cross-check gap the fix also closed is documented at that case. `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/ui/test/tokens.test.ts`. **The owner's word arrived, Round 2d (2026-09-09): `accent` is retired to the prototype's own accent hex** (`blue600`/`blue300`, shared by `focusRing`/`categoryActive`, re-verified at their 3:1 floor with margin). **Its light arm moved again 2026-09-18 (Step 2c-1, T1): `blue600` now carries the REFRESHED export's value, a darker blue, and that narrows the reason the split below exists without retiring it.** The light hex now clears 4.5 as TEXT on the plain `surface` (**5.07**) and the raised one (**5.81**) but still fails `surfaceSunken` (**4.42**) — so a role that clears two backgrounds and fails the third is still not one to rely on for text, and a NEW role, `textAccent`, carries that guarantee instead: it is NOT `accentText` (which means "colour on an accent-FILLED background", e.g. a primary button's label, and is unchanged) but its opposite, "accent-hued text ON a neutral surface" (links, ghost-button labels, a search-result title). Case 13.360 pins both halves of the measurement rather than the old one-sided claim. **The same refresh (Step 2c-1, 2026-09-18) moved the neo LIGHT surface `neoPaper` to the export's new hex — a slightly lighter warm paper — and gave that arm five semantic colours it previously inherited from the base: `danger`, `warning`, `success`, `contested` and `restricted`, with `customMarker` following `restricted` because it is documented as sharing that primitive.** The mapping is by meaning rather than by the export's variable name — the export calls two of them `--pink` and `--purple` where this product names roles — and every one clears its floor (5.83–7.91 as a ground; `dangerText`/`warningText`/`successText` on them 6.52–7.02). The export's new two-axis SELECTOR is deliberately NOT ported: it keys on `data-theme="light"`, and this product treats light as the ABSENCE of that attribute, so adopting it verbatim would have dropped the whole light-neo set for every user who has never chosen a theme. Its values are ported; its selector is not. Cases 13.675–13.681. `textAccentLight`/`textAccentDark` are derived, not the export's own values, to clear 4.5 against every surface/raised background in every style AND the `.gn-button--ghost` fillTertiary-composited background AND `surfaceSunken` — the last two beyond what the two declared rows strictly require, at zero extra cost. A new pairless `brand` role (`brandOrange` `#ee7733`, one hex, both themes — the prototype's own only fixed hue) now fills the sign-in icon tile; it carries no `CONTRAST_PAIRS` row because its actual composition (`textInverted` glyph on `brand` fill) measures 2.69 in light — below even 3:1 — for a purely decorative, `aria-hidden="true"` glyph, the same trade-off the export itself ships. Cases 13.358, 13.360, 13.365. **`neo`/dark's `surfaceRaised`/`surfaceSunken` REVERTED to `neoNavy` itself, 2026-09-10 (owner: "revert to prototype's exact dark hex and fix the accent colour ratio instead" — the dark+neo input box read visibly lighter than the prototype's own screenshot).** They had been stepped lighter (`#29314c`/`#3b425b`) specifically so the THEN-bare `accent` role could still clear 4.5 as text against them — a real constraint when it was written, but one that stopped binding the SAME DAY once `accent` split into `accent` (fill-only) and `textAccent` (above) — `CONTRAST_PAIRS` has never named bare `accent` on `surfaceRaised`/`surface` since. Verified empirically before reverting: every declared row for `neo`/dark still passes with MORE margin on the darker hex, since every foreground role in play is light-toned. `neo` is now exempt from the dark-only fill-separation rule (13.341) in BOTH themes, matching how `neoPaper` already made light exempt — this style's whole visual language is shadow-only depth. Case 13.360's own `accent`-on-`neoNavySunken` assertion flipped from failing (4.11) to passing (≈6.39) as a direct consequence, updated to match rather than left asserting the old fact. **A 34th role, `textSubtle`, 2026-09-15 (owner: *"do it. all of them. even the label3. adopt it."*)** — a THIRD dim text step below `textMuted`, for the furniture the owner's Help prototype puts on its `--label3`: eyebrows, group counts, the disclosure caret, the pager's `Next`. **PARTIALLY REVERSED the same day (owner: *"lets move the styling towards the guide"*) — three of its five sites are back on `textMuted`:** the sidebar's `TOPICS` eyebrow, its group counts and its caret, because the design guide that direction points at knows only two text weights. The two sites in the document column (`.gn-help__section-eyebrow`, `.gn-help__pager-label`) keep it, since the guide never covers them. The role is therefore still live and all three of its `CONTRAST_PAIRS` rows still stand; what is lost is the sidebar's separation between furniture and links, which is exactly what the third step was introduced to buy — recorded at the rule rather than left to be rediscovered. The same move took the sidebar's hover ground from the alpha `fillQuaternary` to the solid `surfaceSunken`, which is a net GAIN for verifiability: every ink composited over it is now a declared row the suite checks, where the alpha ground could only ever be hand-measured. The reference's own `--label3` is a 52% alpha and **could not be adopted literally** — composited over this product's four light surfaces it measures **3.00 / 3.10 / 2.87 / 2.96**, not merely under the 4.5 read text owes but under the 3.0 a decorative mark owes on two of them, so there was no honest `minimum` to declare for it and a `minimum: 3` row would have been red the moment it was written. What is ported is the STEP, not the hex, and the reason it can be: this palette sits higher than the reference's throughout (`textMuted` measures **8.94** on `surface` where the reference's `--label2` composite measures 6.13), so a third level derived at this baseline keeps the reference's proportional hierarchy while staying readable. `textSubtleLight` `#5e5e62` / `textSubtleDark` `#a0a0a5` are the dimmest greys on Apple's own neutral hue that clear 4.5 on EVERY surface in their theme — binding constraint `surfaceSunken`, **4.61 light / 4.54 dark**, with one step dimmer failing outright in each (`#606064` → 4.47, `#9e9ea3` → 4.43). Three `CONTRAST_PAIRS` rows at 4.5 (`surface`, `surfaceRaised`, `surfaceSunken`) rather than the one `/help` currently needs, on this array's own "GAP THIS BLOCK CLOSES" doctrine — the undeclared co-occurring pair is the one a future edit breaks in silence, and the derivation clears all three by construction. **`grayAccessible` was NOT reusable and the reason predates this change:** it is the status-dot grey, split out of `inkMuted` precisely because it fails text at 4.16 on `surfaceSunken` (re-measured: 4.27 / 4.90 / **3.73** / 4.16). No `STYLE_COLOR_OVERRIDES` entry — `neo` varies only surfaces and the fill ladder, never a text role, so all three styles resolve this through `themes`, which is why `neoPaper`/`neoNavy` are in the measured set rather than assumed. Cases 15.26–15.27. **The TYPEFACES joined the origin 2026-09-15 (owner: *"use the fonts from the attached prototype, everywhere."*)** — `fontStacks`/`fontStackOf`/`fontVariableName` resolve two `FontRole`s to `--gn-font-sans` (`-apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "SF Pro Text", "Helvetica Neue", system-ui, sans-serif`) and `--gn-font-mono` (`ui-monospace, Menlo, monospace`), both transcribed from the desktop prototype's own `body` rule and its inline code declarations. They are the only design values in the file that vary with NEITHER axis, so `themeStylesheet()` emits them once under their own bare `:root` ahead of the colour blocks, outside `declarationsFor` — inside it they would be six identical copies free to diverge, and emitting first means a future per-style face added where it belongs would win the tie at equal specificity. **What this actually fixed is an absence, not a preference:** the product named a font in exactly one place, `apps/web/app/globals.css`, and `ui.css` carried zero `font-family` declarations, so `apps/desktop` — which loads `ui.css` and nothing else — painted every glyph in the browser's default SERIF. That is the identical defect class to the undefined-tokens one `apps/desktop/src/theme.ts` records, one property along, and it has the same cure. **What was deliberately NOT ported: the prototype's `font-size:17px; line-height:22px` on that same `body` rule.** `rem` resolves against `html`, not `body`, so adopting it would rescale only the text that inherits a size and leave every `rem`-sized rule anchored to 16 — a type scale split down the middle across screens the request never named. `/help` already carries the reference's 17/22 per element, where it was measured. Same reasoning for the prototype's `input,textarea,select,button{font:inherit}`: `button` already had the shorthand by the 2026-09-08 ruling, and the other three take `font-family: inherit` only — the family is what "use the fonts" asks for and it moves no box, while the shorthand would carry SIZE and shift text under two ratcheted baselines. **Two `<code>` elements got the mono face and one did not, and the third is named rather than glossed:** `.gn-help__code` (`ui.css`) and `.gn-invite-mint__url` (`globals.css`) had no `font-family` at all — an absence a grep for the property cannot find, since neither DECLARED a wrong font, both leaned on the UA for the right one — and both now read `var(--gn-font-mono)`. The ticket editor's `code` mark and `codeBlock` node, which StarterKit leaves on, still do not: `packages/editor/src/styles/editor.css` declares no font, its header states that package must not depend on `packages/ui`, and it is imported only by the React Native WebView entry, which installs no token stylesheet — so a `var()` there resolves to nothing today and, without a fallback, would be worse than the status quo (an unresolvable custom property makes `font-family` invalid at computed-value time and the code would inherit the SANS). That one waits on the webview getting tokens at all, the same question `installTheme` answered for desktop. **A sibling absence, same class, found the same day by looking at the element instead of grepping the property (15.34):** `.gn-help__search-input` never declared `border`, so the Help search field wore Chrome's UA default — measured live as `2px inset rgb(118, 118, 118)`, an `inset` style that paints its right and bottom edges light and its top and left dark, in a grey no token chose. It takes `.gn-field__input`'s treatment now (bordered under `hig`/`minimal`, the pure neumorphic well under `neo`), which is what the owner asked for in those words: *"i want it to be like the rest of the input boxes."* Cases 15.28–15.34; the mobile half is 12.397, numbered in its own section's series. **A 35th role, `tintWarning`, 2026-09-15 (the workspaces-prototype-port plan's Task 1)** — the invitation banner's warm/warning wash, an ALPHA pair (`tintWarmWarning`/`tintNavyWarning`, one value per theme) rather than a solid, shared by all three styles since `neo` overrides neither. Alpha roles cannot carry a `CONTRAST_PAIRS` row (`tokens.ts:228` — `relativeLuminance` ignores alpha, so a declared pair would measure the tint's own colour rather than what it composites into); the obligation is checked against the COMPOSITE instead, a bespoke test block following the pre-existing `fillTertiary` precedent. All twelve measured ratios (3 styles × 2 themes × 2 foregrounds) clear 4.5 with margin, tightest at neo/light's `textMuted`, 7.434 — 65% above the floor; the alpha-nudge contingency (the `fillNavyHig3` precedent) was evaluated and not needed. A second, related gap the same plan's Task 3 closed the same day: the chip's own `text`-on-`fillTertiary` composite, over the chip's ACTUAL ground (`surfaceRaised`, since it sits on `.gn-card`) rather than the two pre-existing `fillTertiary` checks' `textAccent`-on-bare-`surface`. Cases 13.403–13.404. `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `packages/ui/test/tokens.test.ts` **Owner round 4, product-wide (2026-09-16): the prototype's text was never pure black/white, so neither is ours.** The owner: *“seems the text color in the prototype is not really white. follow that,”* ruled PRODUCT-WIDE rather than scoped to `/workspaces` — `apps/desktop/src/main.tsx` imports this same stylesheet, so both shells move together. Four new primitives (`labelWarmLight`/`labelWarmDark`/`onColorWarmLight`/`onColorWarmDark`, from `desktop.dc.html`'s `--label`/`--onColor`) replace `ink`/`white`/`fog`/`navy900` on six roles: `text` (both themes), `accentText` (both themes), `textInverted` (both themes — reusing the `onColor` pair per `ui.css:2596`'s own comment tying its semantic to the export's `--onColor`), and `dangerText`/`warningText`/`successText` in LIGHT theme only (their dark values were already non-pure inks and are untouched). `ink`/`white`/`fog`/`navy900` are now referenced by no role in `themes` — kept at the time, pending an owner decision on whether they still earn their place. **Owner round 5 (2026-09-16): `ink` and `fog` deleted from `primitives` outright** — re-checked repo-wide immediately before deletion (`packages/ui/src`, `packages/ui/test`, `apps/`, and beyond) and neither had a referent anywhere, code or test. `white` and `navy900` are kept per that same ruling, though the same repo-wide check found no live referent for them either as of this change — the two lines sometimes read as pinning them (`tokens.test.ts:757`–`758`) are inside a comment describing a PRIOR assertion, not a current one. Two stale prose mentions this same check surfaced and fixed in the same change set: the `brand` role's pairless-exemption docblock still named `white`/`ink` and cited the pre-round-4 numbers (2.87/7.32) instead of `onColorWarmLight`/`onColorWarmDark`'s current 2.69/6.02, and the `textAccent`-vs-`accentText` docblock still named `white`/`navy900` for `accentText` instead of the round-4 primitives, both corrected in `tokens.ts`. The pair count stays **33**: no `CONTRAST_PAIRS` row was added, removed, or loosened; every affected row was re-measured before and after, across all three styles, and stays clear of its minimum — tightest is `warningText` on `warning` (light), 5.197 → **4.876** against a 4.5 floor. Two previously-pinned measurements that named the old pure values moved with them and were re-measured rather than adjusted-to-pass: the Apple-green `successText` counterfactual (13.281, 4.40 → 4.12, still short of 4.5 as designed) and the pairless `textInverted`-on-`brand` exemption (13.365, light 2.87 → 2.69, still below the 3:1 it is exempt from). `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `packages/ui/test/tokens.test.ts` **A fifth `ShadowRole`, `selected`, joined 2026-09-16 (owner round 5) — the gap the `sunken-compact` fix (above) flagged and deliberately left alone. `TOKEN_ROLES`' own count of 35 colour roles is unaffected — shadow roles are a separate enumeration.** The prototype's `--segOnShadow` (`design-system/interactive/desktop.dc.html:29` hig light, `:43` hig dark, `:51` minimal, `:65` neo light, `:77` neo dark) is not another step on the sunken scale — it is RAISED (drop shadow) under `hig`, `none` under `minimal`, and INSET under `neo`, so no existing role name (`sunken-*`) could honestly describe it; it is named for the STATE it expresses (the chosen item in a group) rather than the component or a scale position, matching the `sunken-compact` precedent. All six layer colours across the five style×theme combinations turned out to be byte-identical to primitives the file already had — `shadowHigLight`/`shadowHigDark` (hig's own layers), `shadowNeoRaisedDarkLight`/`shadowNeoHighlightLight` (neo light), `shadowNeoSunkenCompactDarkDark`/`shadowNeoSunkenHighlightDark` (neo dark), and, for hig light's second layer, `fillWarmHig4` — a FILL primitive reused for a shadow layer because its hex is byte-identical (`#273a630f`, verified by rgba→hex arithmetic, not eyeballed); no new primitive was needed. `.gn-segmented__item--on`'s base rule now reads `var(--gn-shadow-selected)` (replacing `var(--gn-shadow-raised)`) and resolves correctly for `hig`/`minimal` directly; the `[data-style="neo"] .gn-segmented__item--on` override drops its now-redundant `box-shadow: var(--gn-shadow-sunken)` line and keeps only its `background: var(--gn-surface-sunken)` (unaffected — a shadow role carries no background). Case 13.412; case 13.411's own hardcoded `EXPECTED_SHADOW_ROLES` emission-coverage literal needed a manual addition for `'selected'` to keep covering it — confirmed by mutation-dropping `'selected'` from `SHADOW_ROLES` before and after that addition. `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/ui/test/tokens.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e-viewport/responsive.spec.ts`
- ⚙️ **Accessible-name ratchet** — the counts of `aria-label` / `role` / `aria-selected` / `aria-describedby` per route are baselined one-way, on both the signed-out and the signed-in surface, so a restyle that drops one goes red naming the route and the attribute. Built as Task 0 of the HIG per-screen plan and BEFORE any screen was touched, because that work is delegated to smaller models and **a rule enforced by a test survives a delegation while a rule enforced by attention does not**. It measures the rendered DOM, not the source, so it sees what actually reaches assistive tech. It counts rather than reads: swapping one name for another passes, losing one does not. Cases 13.285–13.287; re-used by Task 14's `StyleToggle` addition to `AppNav` (case 13.342) — the signed-in ratchet rises by exactly one `tablist` + three `tab`s per route, the signed-out ratchet is untouched (`AppNav` never renders signed-out). **Both halves of that parenthetical are HISTORY, not the current tree (2026-09-09): `AppNav` now mounts on `/help` and `/account`, which are signed-out-reachable, so the signed-out ratchet is no longer untouched by it — checked against `e2e-viewport/accessible-names-baseline.json`'s own recorded `/help` numbers (`role`/`ariaSelected` both baselined at `0`, a floor that cannot fall; `ariaLabel` only rises, from `ThemeToggle`'s own label), so this side needed no file change. `StyleToggle` also moved OFF `AppNav` onto `/account` as a `<select>` (owner request), which DOES fall the signed-in `e2e-stack` ratchet — its `apps/web/e2e-stack/viewport.spec.ts` carries the full dated account of what left, why all seven of that file's baseline keys were deleted (not hand-lowered) rather than guessed, and that only one of the seven (`/w/:workspaceId`, via `MUST_NAME`) still asserts anything until a stack window re-records the rest under `UPDATE_ACCESSIBLE_BASELINE=1`.** **That sentence is HISTORY since 2026-09-18: the seven keys WERE re-recorded** (`e2e-stack/accessible-names-baseline.json` holds all seven, written by the stack run that day), so the ratchet asserts on every route again. **2026-09-20 — `/p/:projectId` was measuring the WRONG BOARD, and the repair is what makes its number mean anything.** That route resolved its project as the FIRST project link on `/w/<id>`, a position rather than a project: the live dev database holds a second project (`gggg`, created through the real web UI 2026-09-10) and it sorts ahead of the seeded `Shop operations`, so the ratchet read `gggg`'s board. *(How far back: `gggg`'s own event log records `project.archived` 2026-09-16 04:27:47 and `project.restored` 2026-09-19 18:40:20, and the workspace home renders live projects only — so it was out of the DOM across 2026-09-18 and the recorded baseline was taken on `Shop operations`, legitimately. The wrong-board window starts at that restore; an earlier version of this sentence said "since 2026-09-10" and was too wide.)* Measured with the instrument itself: `gggg` reads `{ariaLabel: 9, role: 9}`, `Shop operations` reads `{ariaLabel: 65, role: 14}` against the recorded `{66, 14}` — `role` matching exactly corroborates that the recorded page was `Shop operations`, and the archive/restore log above independently confirms it. It reported `LOST ariaLabel: baseline 66, now 9`, a catastrophic-sounding accessibility regression that was nothing of the kind. The route now names the seeded project (`e2e-stack/helpers/board.ts`) and refuses to fall back to a neighbour; **it still reds, correctly, at `66 → 65` — one genuinely-lost accessible name on the real board, unattributed, owned by whoever next touches that screen.** `apps/web/e2e-viewport/accessible-names.ts`
- ⚙️ **Token-discipline scanner** — walks every client source tree (present and future — trees that do not exist yet are skipped, so a new client package is covered with no edit) for hex, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, `hsl()`/`hsla()`, `oklch()`/`oklab()` and `color-mix()` literals, exempting only `tokens.ts`. Proven against planted defects before the clean-tree assertion is trusted; it does NOT detect bare CSS colour keywords (`red`, `tomato`). The "future tree" clause stopped being a promise when `apps/web/app` and `apps/web/src` arrived: a `#b3261e` planted in `apps/web/app/globals.css` fails THIS package's suite, naming the other package's file — `apps/web/app/globals.css:13 #b3261e` — which is what the repo-relative hit paths were added for. `packages/ui/test/helpers/colour-scan.ts`
- ⚙️ **Base UI components** — Button, Chip, Card, Avatar, Dialog, Sheet, Table: BEM class names, colour only via `var(--gn-*)`, `type="button"` by default so forms are never submitted by accident, Escape-and-scrim close on the modal pair, and horizontal scroll contained inside the table wrapper rather than the page. **Radius and elevation are now style-axis variables, not literals (Task 14, 2026-09-09):** `.gn-button`/`.gn-card`/`.gn-dialog` read `var(--gn-radius-control|card)` instead of a hardcoded pixel value (`.gn-sheet` has no radius of its own — it docks flush to the screen edge), and `.gn-card`/`.gn-dialog`/`.gn-sheet`'s `box-shadow` reads `var(--gn-shadow-raised|overlay)` instead of the old generic `--gn-shadow` scrim — so switching `data-style` restyles every one of these without a per-component override. The ghost/secondary fill-plus-label affordance (13.284/13.289) is untouched by any style: no `[data-style]` block anywhere in `ui.css` touches `.gn-button`. Case 13.340. **The neutral `Avatar`'s fill is `var(--gn-fill-tertiary)`, not the `var(--gn-surface-sunken)` it shipped with (owner report, 2026-09-18):** under `neo` the three surface roles are one hex (`neoPaper #F1EAE3`), so an untinted avatar on a card was the same colour as its parent with a boundary ratio of exactly 1.0000 — no disc at all. It was shadow-only under `neo` and fill-difference under `hig`/`minimal`, and a shadow was rejected as a second mechanism for one boundary. Only the UNTINTED variant was affected: `tint` is opt-in and the four `--gn-avatar--tint-*` rules set their own fill, which is why it read as intermittent. The initials still clear 4.5 as read text on the composites in all three styles and both themes (measured 8.75–10.83). Case 13.732. **THE SHEET AND ITS SCRIM NOW MOVE (owner report, 2026-09-19, case 13.961).** The owner: *"gn-sheet__scrim. the surface that shows when you open a ticket. check with the prototype and match it."* **The COLOUR was already right** in all four style × theme combinations — measured against the prototype's `--overlay` from both sides and again rendered — so the mismatch was MOTION: `.gn-sheet__scrim` computed to `animation: none 0s`, `.gn-sheet` had none at all, and `gnSlide` existed nowhere, so the sheet snapped open. Both now carry the prototype's own values (`desktop.dc.html@6d7b67c0a52c`): `gnFade .2s ease` on the scrim — which all four of its scrims carry — and `gnSlide .26s cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1)` over a `translateX(28px)` on the panel, a travel checked against OUR width rather than copied blind (its sheet is 660px, ours is 640px, both docked right). **ALL THREE MODAL PANELS ARE NOW ANIMATED, and the sentence that said otherwise was the change's own worst error.** An earlier version of this bullet claimed **"the prototype draws no centred dialog anywhere"**, left `.gn-dialog` unanimated on that basis, and called `gnRise` a keyframe "used by nothing". **Every part of that was false.** A grep for the scrims' `background:var(--overlay)` returns four hits; three are side panels, and the fourth — `desktop.dc.html:1570` — is the scrim of a **CENTRED** dialog whose container (`:1569`) is `align-items:center;justify-content:center` and whose panel (`:1571`) carries exactly `animation:gnRise .24s cubic-bezier(.32,.72,0,1)`; `mobile.dc.html:1206-1208` draws the same pair. *The grep was run; the step that would have falsified the reading — checking each hit's CONTAINER rather than the hit — was not.* So `.gn-dialog` now rises on the prototype's own `gnRise .24s` over `translateY(14px)`, the fade is on the SHARED `.gn-dialog__scrim, .gn-sheet__scrim` rule **which the prototype does support**, and the guard count is stated as it is: the `animation: none` shape was used **once** before this change (`.gn-help`'s), not three times — the others cover `transition`. **`ui.css`'s own `gnFade` comment was corrected in the same change**, because it said the keyframes lived at the foot of the Help section *"rather than in a shared block"* on the grounds that `gnFade` was *the only animation in this stylesheet* — which the overlay work makes false; all three keyframes now sit in a shared block at the head of the file. **No unit test can see any of this** (jsdom has no layout engine and no animation timeline), so the case is a declared-value scan whose docblock states its own blind spots, and **the motion itself was watched in a real browser** — Chromium via Playwright over the same stylesheet bytes and the component's own markup, with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` emulated at the engine level. **That case's own uniqueness check was refuted and repaired in the same round:** it asserted "exactly one rule per selector" while covering only `.gn-sheet__scrim` and filtering out `none`, so an appended `.gn-sheet { animation: none; }` left the suite green with the motion dead — measured in a browser. It now counts every declaration on all three selectors, and that mutation reddens it. **The prototype's "Full screen" ticket mode is a named GAP, not built**: it turns the scrim into `var(--surface)` and makes the panel full-bleed (`sheetFullLabel`, used at `:1119`) — a feature, not a scrim value. `packages/ui/src/components/`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/ui/test/sheet-motion.test.ts`
- ✅ **Segmented control, filled field, tinted refusal panels, page header** — the four shared HIG pieces (`SegmentedControl` in `packages/ui/src/components/segmented-control.tsx`; `.gn-field*`, `.gn-page-header*`, retinted `.gn-refusal*` in `ui.css`). The ghost button gets the rule it never had. Cases 13.288–13.291. **Radius is style-axis-driven as of Task 14 (2026-09-09)** — `.gn-segmented`/`.gn-segmented__item`/`.gn-field__input`/`.gn-refusal` read `var(--gn-radius-control)`, `.gn-page-header__icon` reads `var(--gn-radius-card)`, and the segmented on-state and `.gn-shell__refusal`/`.gn-shell__expired` (`apps/web/app/globals.css`) follow the same pattern. **Under `data-style="neo"` only, the field input takes the pure neomorphic look — sunken, no border** (owner ruling 2026-09-09): `[data-style="neo"] .gn-field__input` drops 13.291's border for `background: var(--gn-surface-sunken)` and `box-shadow: var(--gn-shadow-sunken)`; `hig`/`minimal` keep the bordered look unchanged. The shadow-edge contrast (1.24 light / 1.16 dark, both below 3:1) is an OWNER-ACCEPTED WAIVER, recorded rather than hidden. Case 13.339. **The SELECTED SEGMENT joins it under `neo`, 2026-09-15 (owner: *"in the prototype it was sunken style. i want it to follow that"*)** — `[data-style="neo"] .gn-segmented__item--on` (at the time) overrode the base `var(--gn-shadow-raised)` with `var(--gn-shadow-sunken)`, so the chosen segment read as PRESSED rather than lifted. The reference does not give this control one treatment but five, splitting on the style axis (`--segOnShadow` in `design-system/interactive/desktop.dc.html`): a drop shadow under `hig` in both themes, `none` under `minimal`, and `inset` pairs under `neo` in both — and the `neo` pair's own geometry (3px/3px/6px) matches neither existing shadow role exactly, which the two-role approximation above papered over. The base rule was already correct for two of the three styles at the time — `--gn-shadow-raised` resolves to `none` under `minimal` — and only `neo` was inverted, which is precisely why it read as backwards: `neo` is the shipped default. **Superseded owner round 5 (2026-09-16): a dedicated fifth `ShadowRole`, `selected`, replaces the two-role approximation** — see the Design-tokens bullet above for the primitive reuse. The base rule now reads `var(--gn-shadow-selected)` directly and resolves correctly for all five style×theme combinations without any `[data-style="neo"]` override for `box-shadow`; the override survives only for `background: var(--gn-surface-sunken)`, which is NOT `var(--gn-surface-raised)` as an earlier draft of this entry said — `neo`'s `surfaceRaised` and `surfaceSunken` are in fact byte-identical (`neoPaper` light, `neoNavy` dark, both collapsed to one hex by design), so the correction changes no rendered pixel, only the accuracy of this sentence and of `ui.css`'s own inline comment. Being the shared control, it moves everywhere `SegmentedControl` is used — sign-in's method picker, `/account`'s appearance rows, `/help`'s Manual/FAQ switch, the ticket-type editor — which is the intent, since the reference defines the component once. **The TRACK FILL came off in the same breath** (owner: *"the background also needs to be removed from the tablist"*) — `.gn-segmented` no longer paints `var(--gn-fill-tertiary)`, because once the selected segment is a well pressed into the page, a tint behind the whole control gives that well a second ground to compose against; geometry keeps the segments apart instead. **Both were caught being wrong by a test before they went further, which is the part worth recording:** `13.362` witnesses the selected tab's rendered `box-shadow` in a real Chromium page, and the first commit of the sunken change claimed nothing in the suite read a computed style here — it reddened on all three viewports. That assertion now pins the `inset` geometry AND that the old raised geometry is gone, and its declared-value control moved from `--gn-fill-tertiary` (which this component no longer uses, so it would have stayed green while naming a property that had ceased to exist) to `--gn-surface-sunken`. Cases 13.400–13.401, 13.362. **`.gn-page-header__icon` fills with `var(--gn-brand)` and labels with `var(--gn-text-inverted)`, not `accent`/`accentText`, Round 2d (2026-09-09)** — the owner's neo prototype fills this exact tile with its one fixed brand hue, not the theme-varying accent. `border-radius` stays `var(--gn-radius-card)`, unchanged: the export hardcodes a 14px literal here under every style it ships (including its own `hig` default), which maps cleanly to neither `card` (20px under neo) nor `control` (14px under neo, coincidentally) — flagged as a minor, out-of-scope geometry delta rather than guessed at. `.gn-button--ghost` now reads `var(--gn-text-accent)`, not `var(--gn-accent)` — see the design-tokens bullet above for why. Case 13.358.
- ⚙️ **Shared `Switch` and `InfoTip`** (`packages/ui/src/components/switch.tsx`, `info-tip.tsx`, 2026-09-18) — the two primitives the Settings → Workspace restyle added to the shared package. **`Switch`** is the `/account` notifications control HOISTED, not reimplemented: its CSS moved out of `apps/web/app/globals.css` (where it was five rule bodies plus a reduced-motion arm, all scoped to `.gn-account__notification-row input[type='checkbox']`, reachable only from that one page) into `ui.css` as `.gn-switch`, and both call sites now render this one component — the task's ruling was *"extract it into a shared component and use it in both places rather than copying its CSS; two toggles that drift is the divergence this repo keeps paying for."* Declarations are the originals: 51×31 track, 27px knob, the 20px of travel, and the `borderStrong` ring on the knob that pays WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 in both states and both themes (the ring exists because the prototype's hardcoded `#fff` knob is unrepresentable — no always-white role exists, the palette being theme-aware by design). It renders a real `<input type="checkbox">` restyled rather than a `role="switch"` div, so label association, checked state, disabled state and keyboard behaviour are the element's own; **the stated consequence is that to assistive technology it reads as a checkbox, not a switch.** What this component does NOT carry is `justify-content: space-between` — that is ACCOUNT row layout and stayed on `.gn-account__notification-row`, because a switch in a form field wants its control beside its label, not at the far edge of a card. **`InfoTip`** is the ⓘ disclosure beside a field, opening on hover, focus and click and closing on Escape, blur, pointer-leave or an outside click. Its mechanism is `last-project-nav.tsx`'s disclosure reduced to a single non-menu panel, and it is deliberately NOT a `title=` attribute (`ui.css:322` records the product's ruling against those). Three divergences from the nav item, each with a reason: no `suppressNextFocusOpen` ref (Escape does not move focus here, so the loop it breaks cannot form), no `role="menu"`/arrow-key navigation (one paragraph of prose is not a set of choices), and it does not take focus on open (it opens ON focus, so pulling focus inward would swallow anyone tabbing through the form). **Its `onClick` OPENS rather than toggling**, and that is a fix rather than a preference: a click focuses a button first, so React runs `onFocus` (open) and then `onClick`, and a toggling handler would read the state it had just been given and close the panel again — a click that appears to do nothing. **The panel is in the DOM only while open, so this is a DISCLOSURE and not a description**: the field it explains carries no `aria-describedby` pointing at it, and activating the button inserts a `role="note"` node that is never ANNOUNCED — no live region, no focus move — so a screen-reader user must go looking for it while a sighted user watches it appear. Both halves of that limit are recorded rather than implied away. **Two corrections the review forced, each measured rather than re-worded:** the panel carries a `border`, and that is load-bearing — it is `surfaceRaised` on a card that is also `surfaceRaised`, and `SHADOWS.minimal.raised` is `[]`, so under `minimal` the shadow resolves to `none` and the border is the only thing drawing an edge; and the trigger is **24×24**, the product's own floor for a dense in-list control (`ui.css:1246-1249`), where it shipped at 20×20. **A recorded gap with a named owner:** the 44px tap ratchet never sees this control because no roster covers the settings routes at all (`e2e-viewport/viewports.ts:171-176`, `e2e-stack/viewport.spec.ts:419-427`) — the ratchet stays green over a page it does not walk. Cases 13.899–13.901. `packages/ui/src/components/`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **Shared `Select` component** (`packages/ui/src/select/`) — a neumorphic dropdown replacing bare native `<select>`s: a non-searchable single-select (button trigger, `useSelect`), a searchable combobox variant (typeable trigger, `useComboBox`), and a multi-select mode with removable chips and a `maxSelected` cap that shows an aria-live message rather than disabling the remaining options. Built on `@react-aria`/`@react-stately` — the first runtime dependency `packages/ui` has ever taken on. The panel is portal-rendered (`useOverlayPosition`) so it escapes ancestor `overflow` clipping, needed by several Phase-2 dialog sites even though neither Phase-1 site below does yet. **Phase 1** migrated exactly two call sites — the ticket-type editor's Workflow select (searchable, `ticket-type-editor-screen.tsx`) and its Field-type select (non-searchable, `fields-editor.tsx`). Test cases 10.81–10.83. **Phase 2 Wave A** migrated 3 more sites in `apps/web`: the members-invite page's Channel and Role selects (`apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx` — only the Role picker carries a dedicated case, 3.132; the Channel select has none of its own) and the Style select (`apps/web/src/style-toggle.tsx`, cases 13.344–13.346). **A fourth `apps/web` site joined on 2026-09-18: the members roster's per-row role control** (same file, cases 13.824–13.828) — the first `Select` whose callers pass a per-instance `aria-label` to tell N structurally identical controls apart, and the first whose callers had to be written against the component's actual accessible-name behaviour (react-aria's `aria-labelledby` makes the trigger's name `<value> <aria-label>`, so an exact-string query matches nothing and an absence assertion anchored to that name can never fire; measured, and recorded in `test-cases/13`). **Phase 2 Wave B** migrated 5 more sites in `packages/app-core`'s workflow-builder UI: the canvas node's Category select (`state-node.tsx`), two rule-editor selects — the Field picker and the Earlier-move picker (`require-field-editor.tsx`, `require-different-user-editor.tsx`), both carrying the same `string` → `string | null` unset treatment so an unrecognized rule value shows the Select's own placeholder rather than falsely displaying its first option — and two repeated-instance target-state selects, one per dropped/outgoing state (`state-mapping-dialog.tsx`, `workflow-rebind-dialog.tsx`); the latter is a genuinely separate component from the former despite the near-identical UI, because it authors a one-shot map between two UNRELATED workflows that rides its own migration request, never a `stateMapping` keyed inside one workflow's own version chain (design decision 10). Test cases 10.84–10.89. **Phase 2 Wave C** migrated the final 5 sites (4 files) in `packages/app-core`'s ticket-action views: the transition dialog's Move-to select (`transition-dialog.tsx`, Task 1), the reopen dialog's Reopen-to select (`reopen-dialog.tsx`, Task 2), the new-ticket dialog's Type and Parent selects (`new-ticket-dialog.tsx`, two selects in one file, Task 3 — Parent's `''` value is a real, always-selectable "None (project root)" option, added as a synthetic first `SelectOption` rather than treated with this rollout's usual value-or-null unset pattern), and the ticket list's Group-by select (`ticket-list.tsx`, Task 4 — no `string | null` migration needed, `grouping` is always one of 5 valid values). Test cases 3.51, 3.53, 3.65, 3.72, 3.79, 3.157, 3.164–3.166, 12.384, 12.386, 12.388. **THE ROLLOUT IS NOW COMPLETE: all 15 of the original audit's native `<select>` elements are migrated** — Phase 1's 2, Wave A's 3, Wave B's 5, Wave C's 5 — confirmed by a repo-wide sweep of `packages/app-core/src`, `packages/ui/src`, `apps/web/app` and `apps/web/src` finding zero remaining `<select>` JSX in scope (only comments/docblock prose, several of it deliberately narrating the pre-migration history of sites already converted). There is no Wave D and no further phase planned.
- ⚙️ **Client `DataSource` contract & view types** — coordination amendment 10 pinned as code: `DataSource` (`ingest`, a typed `query` surface mirroring the server reads, `subscribe(workspaceId, afterSeq, cb)`), the `StreamItem` frames (amendments 13 + 19 — **five** members since Plan 6 Task 8: `event`, `purge`, `scope-revoked`, `caught-up`, `error`, the event member carrying a `LedgerEvent` so it holds the `workspaceSeq` the resume protocol is defined in terms of), and the view types every client renders from (`BoardView`, `ListRow`, `TicketRead`, `AttentionItem`, `UserPreferences`, …). Views consume only this interface and never import HTTP code. **THIRTEEN methods since Plan 6 Task 14's fix round**: `getTicketTypes(workspaceId)` was added because `GET /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/ticket-types` had shipped in Plan 5 and no seam reached it, so the web shell hardcoded a four-entry ticket-type ladder and presented it as the workspace's own — see the New-ticket control below. It returns the domain's `TicketTypeDef` rather than a client view model, exactly as `getWorkflowDef` returns `WorkflowDef`, because the wire body already carries that type and there is nothing to rename. Scaffold only — the online HTTP + WebSocket source and the offline replica implement these same signatures later. `packages/app-core/src/`
- ⚙️ **Online stream subscriber** — `createStreamSubscriber({baseUrl, webSocketFactory?, random?})`, amendment 13's client half and the first implementation of `DataSource.subscribe`. It speaks the pinned frames verbatim and reconnects with exponential backoff from 500 ms capped at 30 s with ±20% jitter drawn from an injected `random`. The socket is an injected `WebSocketLike` seam — Plan 10's Tauri and React Native shells reuse it, and nothing in it assumes the DOM socket's deferred `close`. Two decisions carry their reasons at the site: the resume cursor advances from `caught-up`'s `seq` **and** from an event's `envelope.workspaceSeq` (never the per-entity `seq`), because `stream/hub.ts` sends `caught-up` exactly once per subscription and "only `caught-up` advances it" would guarantee unbounded replay on a long-lived connection — and it advances only after the consumer callback returns, since a returning synchronous callback is the only durability signal this seam has and over-advancing loses an event permanently while under-advancing costs a replay the consumer already dedupes. And `scope-revoked` is TERMINAL: delivered once, then the subscription shuts down, because reconnecting into a workspace you were just revoked from earns `not-a-member` forever. **It answers a TEXT `ping` with nothing, and that is a deletion rather than an omission (case 12.328):** the arms that declared `PingFrame`, parsed it and replied `{type:'pong'}` spoke a protocol no server implements — the heartbeat at `stream/routes.ts:319` is `socket.ping()`, a WebSocket PROTOCOL control frame the platform socket answers below JavaScript, and the server's message door answers `bad-message` to any text frame that is not a `subscribe`. Case 12.360 keeps them deleted, and 12.361 records the one residual a docblock carries and no test can: never inject a raw `ws` socket into this seam. `parseFrame` is hand-rolled rather than calling the domain's `serverStreamFrameSchema` — that schema is stricter than a client should be about id shape and a value import would resolve through `./dist` — and the schema is used as the test's ORACLE instead, pinning that everything it accepts this parser accepts. `packages/app-core/src/online/stream.ts`
- ⚙️ **Client submit factory** — `makeSubmittedFactory({actorId, origin, now?})`, the one door every claim the web client makes leaves through. It mints nothing itself: the ULID and the `clientTime` stamp come from the domain's `newSubmittedEvent`, so the web client, the desktop client and the offline replica cannot grow three ideas of what a claim is; the factory adds only the per-session half (who is signed in, which origin, which clock), and `serverTime`/`seq` stay absent by construction. Proven equal, field for field, to the domain builder's own output, and proven to mint claims the envelope door admits. `packages/app-core/src/submit.ts`
- ⚙️ **Provenance rendering** — `actorLabel`, `formatDate`, `formatDateTime`, `formatStateProvenance`: honesty contract 1 as code. A missing display name falls back to the raw actor id — never blank, never a fabricated "system". Every instant is rendered by the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, never by the device: the suite renders the same claim from `Pacific/Kiritimati` and `Pacific/Niue` and demands both the same words AND the IST words, so a formatter that is merely consistently wrong fails too. A day claim (`2026-08-30`) is shown as the day it already is rather than shifted through a zone it never had, an unparseable claim renders verbatim, and a calendar-impossible day claim renders verbatim rather than throwing at a rendering surface — the plan's unguarded `istDayBounds` call would have thrown on `2026-02-30`. The unfold text reads the SERVER clock, so no claimed clock can move a ticket onto a day it was never marked on. `packages/app-core/src/provenance.ts`
- ⚙️ **Pre-check state cache** — `ClientStateCache` / `createClientStateCache()`, a snapshot of what this client has SEEN, existing only to fuel the local rule-engine pre-check. It is never rendered as truth: `withCacheFeed(source, cache)` decorates a `DataSource` so ordinary `getTicket`/`getWorkflowDef` reads FEED it and stream frames invalidate it, and nothing is ever SERVED from it — a cache hit that short-circuited a read would put a snapshot on screen as a fact. Any stream event touching an entity DROPS the snapshot rather than patching it optimistically. Its one non-obvious member is `markChildrenComplete(parentId, children)` / `hasCompleteChildren`: `childrenOf` can only answer "the children I have seen", the parent-terminal invariant needs "all of them", and a partial child set reads to the rule engine as a parent with nothing open under it. Any ticket event voids EVERY vouch — targeted invalidation would leave a vouched-but-emptied child set behind, which is the pre-check's one route to a false pass. Nothing feeds a vouch yet; until a producer does (Plan 6 Task 7 or the board/list reads), moves into terminal states answer not-evaluated rather than passed. `packages/app-core/src/cache.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/cache-feed.ts`
- ⚙️ **Local pre-check** — `precheckEvent(submitted, cache, deps?)` runs the REAL `@gisik-nokma/rule-engine` in the browser build (spec honesty contract 2) for instant, named-reason refusal previews, plus `PARENT_TERMINAL_RULE` (`hierarchy.parent-terminal`) for the owner's reopen moment on `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented`, read through the same registered payload schemas the server's door uses rather than a cast. It is advisory: the server stays the authority. The line it holds is that a `passed` is never manufactured out of ABSENCE — four gates downgrade to `not-evaluated` instead, each closing a way the cache's silence would otherwise read as a fact: an uncached ticket or definition; an unwalkable workflow-version chain at EITHER end (a missing intermediate both invents `stranded-state` refusals and silently disarms four-eyes, which skips entries it cannot resolve; a cached definition OLDER than the version the ticket's own snapshot names makes the forward fold a no-op, so the state resolves cleanly against the wrong vocabulary and the client evaluates the pre-publish rules the server has already replaced); an accept into a terminal state with no vouched-complete child set; and — since Plan 3 landed `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented` as engine cases — a hierarchy claim of either type, entirely, because every engine accept for them is decided behind a ticket-type definition and this cache holds none. What survives for those two is the `hierarchy.parent-terminal` refusal, which reads only cached, server-served state. A roles-bearing `restrict-to` refusal downgrades too — memberships live server-side — while an assignee-only one stands, because the snapshot holds the assignee. `completeForPrecheck` stamps `seq: 0`, a position the ledger cannot hold, so the stand-in envelope is refused by the very door it would have to pass to become a fact. The standing canary that asserted the shared engine had no case for `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented` FIRED on Plan 3 Chain A's landing (2026-08-31), which is what it was for; it is superseded in place by one aimed at the next drift — that the engine reaches a ticket-type door this cache cannot answer, pinned in both directions so it goes red whether the dispatcher cases are removed or the adapter starts answering `null`. `packages/app-core/src/precheck.ts`
- ⚙️ **Live query hook** — `useLiveQuery(source, workspaceId, queryKey, load, debounceMs = 200)`, the refetch loop every view hook in the web client rides on: load once, then re-run `load` (debounced) on every `event`/`purge` frame the workspace stream delivers. `queryKey` names WHAT is being asked for (the project id, for a board): a changed key clears the previous answer and re-asks, and an answer that arrives for a key already left is dropped rather than rendered. The clear is a passive effect, so the window in which the previous project's data sits under the new project's heading is bounded to the single render between commit and effect rather than lasting the whole load — shortened, not removed. The key drives the reload and the WORKSPACE drives the subscription — two effects, so a key change never re-opens the stream (`subscribe` resumes from cursor `0`, and each re-open would replay the workspace from the start). Deliberately refetch-based rather than patch-based — the stream is an invalidation signal and the server projections stay the one source of rendered truth; folding raw events into rendered state is Plan 10's replica, built on reducers. Speaks only to `DataSource`, never to a URL or a socket. `packages/app-core/src/views/use-live-query.ts`
- ⚙️ **Derived progress text** — `formatRollup(rollup)` renders `"5 of 8 done · 2 restricted"`. Counts are over ALL direct children with no visibility filtering: the denominator never shrinks to what the viewer can see, and children the viewer cannot see are NAMED in the restricted count rather than pretended absent (spec §6, invariant 5). `packages/app-core/src/views/rollup-text.ts`
- ⚙️ **Board view** — `Board` + `useBoard(source, workspaceId, projectId)`. Columns are exactly the attached workflow's states, empty ones included and blocked ones marked; a project whose ticket types sit on different workflows gets one group per workflow rather than a merged column set that would invent columns no definition declares. Grouping is a rearrangement and never a filter — the same tickets grouped two ways render the same tickets, and each column's count badge agrees with the cards under it. Cards fold their state behind "who · when" and unfold to `"marked Doing by Anselma Marak on 19 Aug 2026"`, falling back to the raw actor id when a claim carries no display name; a reopen renders its `cause` as "reopened — live work added" instead of an anonymous flip; a claim still in flight to the server carries a "claim in transit" chip, which is a CLIENT-SIDE `BoardCard` flag and not a fourth `IngestResult` status. Every instant goes through the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, guarded from two absurd zones by tests that hand the worker back exactly the `TZ` they borrowed. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. Pointing the hook at another project re-asks rather than re-rendering the last one. A test guard holds that no view module imports anything but react, the shared packages and its own relatives, and calls no network API — reading `from`, bare, dynamic and `require` specifiers, matching on whole package names rather than prefixes, and walking `src/views/` recursively. **AN EPIC IS NOT A CARD HERE (owner, 2026-09-20): *"epics should never show up in the board, timeline and calendar views. they can only be in the list views."*** `boardNarrowing` drops a rank-1 card BEFORE `total` increments, so the column badge and the `N of M` strip both agree with the grid — counting it in `total` while never drawing it would make `shown !== total` true on every project holding an epic and tell a person with no filter in force that a filter is hiding work. The epic is read through the workspace's own rank-1 predicate (`isEpicType`, falling back to `isBuiltinEpicType` while the roster loads), never a name or an id match. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **`moveOutcome` — the transition gate, as a pure function (board-prototype-port plan Task 12, 2026-09-16; ticketFields/Ruling 25 and the whitespace trim/Ruling 26, same day)** — the one place Tasks 13 (move arrows) and 14 (drag-and-drop) both route a proposed ticket move through before anything is submitted. Built entirely over `transitionOptions` (immediately above) rather than reading `def.states`/`def.transitions` directly — a second derivation of "what moves are legal" would drift from the engine that actually enforces it. Three outcomes: `refused` (the target is not a declared edge from this state; carries every OTHER move that state DOES declare, by name, so the caller need not guess), `direct` (a declared edge whose rules are all already met — submit it), `needs-dialog` (a declared edge with at least one rule still unmet — open `TransitionDialog`). A move back onto the current state refuses like any other undeclared target, since `transitionOptions` already excludes `to === fromStateId`. Ships no UI. Takes `ticketFields` (Ruling 25) and checks a `require-field` rule against it through `fieldAlreadyClaimed` (`packages/app-core/src/views/field-claims.ts`) — the SAME predicate `TransitionDialog` reads, extracted rather than re-derived a second time, so an edge whose only rule is a field the ticket already carries answers `direct`, matching the dialog's own already-skips-that-input behaviour exactly rather than opening a dialog with nothing left to fill in. **`fieldAlreadyClaimed` TRIMS, whitespace-only counts as unset (Ruling 26)** — mirroring, not importing, `evaluateRequireField`'s own tested semantics (`packages/rule-engine/test/engine.test.ts`, "require-field and require-evidence refuse a blank value, not only an absent one"; Task 9's controller ruling); before Ruling 26 a ticket with `dueDate: '   '` answered `direct` here and the server refused it for real, with no display path on Tasks 13/14 — the same silent-failure shape `transitionOptions`'s own docblock warns against. The agreement is pinned by a real round trip against the exported engine dispatcher, not a self-comparison: `packages/app-core/test/views/field-claims.test.ts` (13.489). **This trim also changes `TransitionDialog`'s own rendering** — a blank-but-present field now correctly prompts instead of being silently skipped — a FIX, not a regression (the engine required this all along; the dialog was out of step), confirmed by the full 21/21 `transition-dialog.test.tsx` suite still green. `require-comment`/`require-evidence` have no "already satisfied" reading before a submission exists, so either always answers `needs-dialog`. `restrict-to`/`require-different-user`-only edges answer `needs-dialog` too, though the dialog they open prompts for nothing — a DELIBERATE choice (Ruling 25), not an oversight: both are server-decided, this client cannot certify either satisfied, and routing to `direct` would submit blind with no display path Tasks 13/14 were scoped to carry for the server's refusal. `packages/app-core/src/views/board-moves.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/field-claims.ts`; `packages/app-core/src/views/transition-dialog.tsx` refactored onto the same shared predicate, with no behaviour change of its own from the EXTRACTION (21/21 unchanged) — its blank-field rendering DOES change from the trim, and that change is intended.
- ✅ **Board move arrows — the first write path in this port (board-prototype-port Task 13, 2026-09-16)** — per-card `‹ ›` buttons that move a ticket between the columns its workflow actually DECLARES, never by column order. `MoveArrows` (`board.tsx`) resolves each arrow's targets via `arrowTargets` (`board-arrows.ts`; `arrowTarget`, the singular, is its head): every state in that direction for which `transitionOptions` declares a real edge, nearest first, walking past every column that does not — one declared target moves, several open a picker (the 2026-09-17 bullet below) — a workflow can declare `todo -> done` while `doing` sits visually between them, and `›` from `todo` lands on `done`, never `doing`. Both arrows always render, one per direction; the direction with nothing declared is a genuinely DISABLED button (native `disabled`, `opacity:0.4; cursor:not-allowed`), not a missing one — the move is absent, not the control. Every click routes through `moveOutcome` (immediately above) and never re-derives its verdict: `direct` submits the SHIPPED `{to}` `ticket.transitioned` payload with no dialog; `needs-dialog` opens the EXISTING `TransitionDialog`, aimed at the identical `(workflow, fromStateId)`; `refused` renders `MoveRefusalNotice`, naming the real alternatives BY NAME (`Can't move there — try Doing.`), never a bare "you can't do that" — `refusalCopy` is exported and pinned by a test that feeds it `moveOutcome`'s OWN `available` output, not a hand-typed string. **`ticketFields: {}` at every call site, by design, not a gap** — `BoardCard` carries no field claims (unlike `ListRow`, which does; the port's own design spec says so of this exact gap, in these words: "the dialog needs `ticketFields`... and `BoardCard` does not carry them"), so `{}` is the conservative reading: every `require-field` rule reads as unmet, meaning the failure mode is a needless dialog open on an already-satisfied field, never a `direct` submit the server then refuses with nothing on screen. **A live, ENABLED arrow click cannot itself land on `moveOutcome`'s `refused` branch, and this is proven rather than assumed** (`board-arrows.test.ts`'s own mutation witness: forcing the scan to the literal adjacent column reddens exactly the two skip-column cases and nothing else) — the arrow's own target and `moveOutcome`'s own check read `transitionOptions` off the identical `(workflow, fromStateId)`, so a target this component ever actually submits is, by construction, always declared.
