The branch stays real and is exercised at the level that IS reachable — the pure `moveOutcome`/`refusalCopy`/`MoveRefusalNotice` pipeline, fed real gate output — and stays load-bearing for Task 14's drag-and-drop (the next bullet below), whose drop target is NOT filtered this way; the SAME refusal copy now DOES fire for real there, proven end to end by a real `dragstart`/`drop` sequence rather than only at this pure-gate seam. **44×44px tap targets, not the metrics table's own 32×36** — a control at the prototype's number fails this repo's already-measured 44px WCAG 2.5.5 floor (`ui.css:2424-2430`; the tab rail's own identical, already-decided divergence for the identical reason, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-16-board-prototype-port.md:56`, applied here rather than re-litigated) — the visual treatment (`ctlBg`, accent text, 15px/600) is kept exactly as specified; only the tap target is raised. `ctlShadow` omitted, the SAME repo-wide open decision `.gn-button` and every other ported board control already states rather than re-deciding piecemeal. Wired into the real page (`apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`), gated on `session.kind === 'signed-in'` — the SAME precedent `CreateTicketFlow` already sets on this page and for the identical reason: an ungated click in the session-loading window, or from a signed-out viewer, would post an event with an empty actor and read back as a permission refusal rather than "not signed in yet". **`needs-dialog` does NOT pre-aim `TransitionDialog` at the arrow's own computed target** — only `(workflow, fromStateId)` are threaded through, and the person picks a target from the dialog's own dropdown exactly as they would opening `TicketSheet`'s "Move…" button; said plainly here because an earlier draft of the test suite and this task's own report claimed otherwise before review caught it. **A REJECTED `ingest` (transport failure, not a server answer) does not brick the card** — `submitDirect`'s `submitting` latch, which has no precedent in `TicketSheet.submitTransition`, is reset in a `try/catch/finally` rather than only after a successful `await`; found in review, mutation-witnessed both directions (`13.504`). **Both submit paths guard every local `setState` against firing after this card has unmounted** — a `mountedRef` (same idiom as `use-live-query.ts`'s `isCurrent()` and `TransitionDialog`'s own precheck `cancelled` flag), gating `setServerRefusal`/`setSubmitting`/`setDialogOpen` in `submitDirect` and `submitFromDialog`, but never `moves.onMoved()` itself, which reaches the PARENT (`board.reload()`) and stays safe to call regardless — found in review (a successful move on ANY card, this one or a sibling, can restructure the board and unmount a card whose own `ingest` is still in flight; React 18 drops the console warning for this, so no test in this file could ever have surfaced it, and none was added — the same structural, untested-by-design shape as its two cited precedents). **`submitFromDialog` GAINED a `try/catch` of its own (case `13.505`)** — found in the same review round: `TransitionDialog`'s `onSubmit` prop is `void`-typed and `MoveArrows`' own call site is fire-and-forget, so a transport failure inside the dialog's own submit used to become an unhandled promise rejection, leaving the dialog open with nothing on screen saying it failed. At the time this landed the fix reused `TransitionDialog`'s existing `serverRefusal` display — corrected below by Ruling 27, which found that reuse was itself the defect. Unlike the `mountedRef` guard immediately above, this fix IS independently mutation-witnessed, since a missing catch is a real behavioural gap rather than an unmount race React 18 hides. **RULING 27 (coordinator, 2026-09-16) — the client was minting refusals, and the rule engine forbids it in as many words.** `board.tsx` built `{ rule: 'no-response'|'transport', reason }` objects by hand (never through `refuse()`/`ACCEPT`, deliberately unexported by `rule-engine/src/index.ts:32-41` for exactly this reason) and fed them into `serverRefusal` — the same channel `TransitionDialog`'s own docblock calls "the authority's answer, shown VERBATIM". Neither value exists in `REFUSAL_RULES` (`rule-engine/src/refusal-rules.ts:9-25`); both rendered through the identical `Refused by the server — …` sentence a real refusal uses, so a dropped connection or an empty `ingest` response read as though the server had adjudicated and declined the move. **Fixed with a wholly separate, plain-sentence channel — never a `{rule, reason}` pair, never `refuse()`/`ACCEPT`.** `MoveArrows` gained `transportError: string | null`, kept apart from `serverRefusal` rather than tagged onto it; `TransitionDialog` gained the identical `transportError` prop (now REQUIRED, matching `serverRefusal`'s own required shape), rendered in its own `<p role="alert" className="gn-refusal gn-refusal--transport">`, never beside the words "Refused by the server". `ui.css` gained `.gn-refusal--transport`, reusing the `warning` role `.gn-refusal--preview` already uses (amber, not `--server`'s danger-red) rather than inventing a new token role. `ticket-sheet.tsx`'s own `TransitionDialog` mount passes `transportError={null}` with a comment explaining why: `TicketSheet.submitTransition` stays unguarded, untouched, and never fabricated a refusal either — it just never caught the rejection. **`13.504`/`13.505` amended, not silently rewritten** — quoted verbatim, before and after, in `test-cases/13-deployment-dogfooding.md`'s Ruling 27 section: the OLD assertion (`toHaveTextContent('network unreachable')`, a substring check) is proven, by an actual suite run against the fix, to have STILL PASSED under the fix (`PASS (29) FAIL (0)`) because the new sentence also contains that substring — the coordinator's own expectation that the fix would redden this case was measured false, reported rather than assumed. The NEW assertions check the corrected sentence, the `gn-refusal--transport` class, and the ABSENCE of both the refusal-shaped sentence and its class. **Case `13.506`** covers the sibling "silence" path (`results[0] === undefined`) that had zero prior coverage. **Mutation witness**: `board.tsx`/`transition-dialog.tsx`/`ticket-sheet.tsx` reverted in place to the exact pre-ruling commit (`git show f5041052:<path>`), the two affected test files re-run — `PASS (27) FAIL (3)`, exactly the three touched cases, each failing with the ruling's own defect text verbatim (`"Refused by the server — transport: network unreachable"`, `"Refused by the server — no-response: The server did not answer this move."`) — then restored byte-identical (`diff` confirmed clean) and the full suite re-confirmed green. **FLAGGED, not fixed: `MoveRefusalNotice` (the CLIENT-side refusal — `moveOutcome`'s `refused` verdict, never touched by a server) still wears `.gn-refusal--server`**, the same danger-red class this ruling just reserved for a real server verdict — its own docblock says so explicitly ("Shares `.gn-refusal.gn-refusal--server`'s existing danger-tinted style"). Milder than the fabrication this ruling fixed (the SENTENCE, `Can't move there — try Doing.`, never claims server authority — only the CLASS does), and pre-dating this ruling (Task 13's own first commit, `8e0f747a`, with its own considered reasoning), so left as a named, escalated question rather than changed unilaterally: `--preview` (`.gn-refusal--preview`, amber) is the closer analogue used by `TransitionDialog`'s own client-computed precheck refusal — but that class's own docblock calls its refusal "advisory, not authoritative", where `moveOutcome`'s refusal is a certain structural fact (an undeclared edge), not an uncertain guess, so the swap is not obviously correct either. No test pins the class today (`grep` confirmed), so a future change is mechanical whichever way the coordinator rules. `packages/app-core/src/views/board-arrows.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/transition-dialog.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`
- ✅ **Board drag-and-drop (board-prototype-port Task 14, 2026-09-16, the plan's final task)** — dragging a card from one board column and dropping it on another. **Ships no second decision point.** A drop's target (`toStateId`) comes from whichever column it lands on; rather than re-deriving `moveOutcome`/refusal/dialog/transport-error handling a second time, the dragged card's OWN `MoveArrows` instance (Task 13, immediately above) registers its real `attemptMove` closure into a shared registry (`MoveRegistry`, `board.tsx`) on every render, keyed by ticket id, and the target column's drop handler looks it up and calls it — the IDENTICAL function an arrow click on that same card would call, with the identical state (`refusal`/`serverRefusal`/`transportError`/`dialogOpen`) rendering in the identical place, under that same card. A drop and an arrow cannot disagree: they are one code path, reached from two input events, never two products. The registry is held by `Board`, not by each per-workflow group, so a drag starting in one workflow group and landing on a DIFFERENT group's column (a mixed-workflow board) still finds the dragged card's real registration and correctly names alternatives from the ticket's OWN workflow rather than doing nothing. **All three outcomes proven end to end, by a real `dragstart`/`dragover`/`drop` sequence, matching the design spec's own enumeration of the three drop cases**: a drop on a declared, rules-free column actually transitions the ticket — the SHIPPED `{to}` payload, `onMoved` called, no alert (`13.507`, the positive control the brief demands, mirroring `13.498`'s role for the arrows); a drop on an undeclared column refuses, naming the real alternative BY NAME rather than merely showing some alert (`13.508` — the case an arrow can never organically reach, since an arrow's own target is always declared by construction, so this is the first END-TO-END exercise of the refusal pipeline this port has); a drop on a declared, rules-bearing column opens `TransitionDialog`, unchanged from how an arrow would open it (`13.509`, reusing `13.501`'s gated-edge fixture verbatim — not re-proving the dialog's own submit mechanics, which `13.501`/`13.504`/`13.505` already do over the identical function this drop reaches). **`preventDefault()` on `dragover` is separately pinned (`13.510`)** because it is invisible to every outcome test above — `fireEvent.drop` in a test dispatches straight to the React handler regardless of what `dragover` did, so a suite built only from the three outcome cases could pass in full against an implementation where a real browser's default (disallow dropping) would silently defeat every drop; asserted directly via `fireEvent.dragOver(...)` returning `false` (the `dispatchEvent` return value when a cancelable event's default was prevented), not inferred from a drop succeeding. **A drop with no real `dragstart` behind it is a silent, inert no-op** (`13.511`) — an empty `DataTransfer` answers `''` from `getData`, which the registry has never seen, so `Map.get` answers `undefined` and nothing further happens; no ingest, no alert, distinguishing "the gate ran and refused" from "the handler found nothing to act on". **Every outcome-dispatch case (`13.507`–`13.509`) and the `dragover` mechanism case (`13.510`) is MUTATION-WITNESSED, not merely passing once**: disabling the registry's `attempt(toStateId)` call reddened exactly `13.507`/`13.508`/`13.509` and nothing else (`PASS (3) FAIL (3)`); removing `preventDefault()` from the `dragover` handler reddened exactly `13.510` and nothing else (`PASS (5) FAIL (1)`) — both mutations reverted and the full 6-test file re-confirmed green afterward. **`13.514` closes the one behavioural gap every other case leaves open: it drags and drops the SAME card TWICE in sequence**, proving the registry's `useEffect` re-registration publishes the FRESH `attemptMove` closure on every render — the one that sees `submitDirect`'s own `finally`-reset `submitting` flip back to `false` — rather than one frozen at first render, which would either brick a card after its first move or silently bypass the double-submit latch; neither bug touches any line the other six cases exercise. **`cursor: grab` is added in this same commit, beside the behaviour that makes it true** (Ruling 15's own instruction, `ui.css` Task 6 comment) — deliberately NOT declared on `.gn-board-card` itself, for two independent reasons stated at length in `ui.css`'s own comment: (1) `.gn-board-card` still renders on the READ-ONLY path (`moves` absent — session loading, signed out, or no capability wired), where the card genuinely cannot be dragged, and a grab cursor there would be exactly the "control that lies" shape Ruling 15 already named; (2) case `13.464`'s own `rule()` helper matches the FIRST literal substring `.gn-board-card {` it finds via a plain regex with no selector parser, so any new rule whose selector text happened to END in that substring — even a compound one — would silently hijack that case's match. The rule lives on `.gn-board__card-slot[draggable="true"]` instead (`13.513`, a CSS-binding gate proving the rule exists with the right body, the same shape `inbox-css-binding.test.tsx` already uses for `gn-timeline__bar`) — a selector containing no `.gn-board-card` substring at all (`__card-slot`, not `-card`), so it cannot collide, and keyed off the REAL `draggable` attribute rather than a second, parallel boolean class. `draggable` itself is set on the card's `<li class="gn-board__card-slot">` wrapper, gated on the SAME `arrows !== null` check that already gates the move arrows — never a second, independently-maintained boolean — so a card with no `MoveArrows` instance behind it (no `moves` capability wired, or this group's workflow still loading) is provably not draggable at all: `13.512` asserts `draggable="false"` on that path, mirroring `13.500`'s identical "no arrows at all" proof for the arrows themselves, and confirms a drop aimed at it anyway is still inert since `onDropCard` itself is `null` on that path (the column never even receives an `onDrop` handler). **Keyboard is unaffected, and this is proven rather than assumed**: `board-moves-ui.test.tsx` — the arrows' own 8-case suite, `13.498`–`13.506` — runs UNCHANGED, byte-identical, and stays green (this task edited no assertion, no aria-label, no disabled logic in it); `draggable` is a plain HTML attribute that adds no tabstop, no ARIA role and no accessible-name change, so the `‹ ›` buttons remain the complete keyboard path they were before this task, with drag-and-drop as a pure addition, never a replacement. **Two decisions stated rather than left for a reviewer to find**: a drop onto the SAME column a card is already in is refused like any other undeclared target (`transitionOptions` already excludes a self-loop, so `moveOutcome` treats it identically to any other wrong target) — no special case, consistent with the plan's own "same four outcomes" instruction; and a refusal renders where `MoveArrows` already renders it — under the SOURCE card, in its origin column — never a second rendering site near the drop point, which would be exactly the fork ("two products") this task was written to avoid. **RULING 28 (coordinator, 2026-09-16) resolved the tension this created rather than leaving it as a named risk**: the origin column CAN scroll out of view (the rail is `overflow-x: auto`), which is functionally the "silent snap-back" the design spec forbids for sighted users — `role="alert"` already covers assistive technology regardless of scroll position. Fix, keeping the single rendering site: `attemptMove` gained an optional `source: 'arrow' | 'drop'` parameter (default `'arrow'`, every arrow `onClick` unchanged) that a new `lastAttemptSourceRef` records; the registry wrapper `registerAttempt` registers now passes `'drop'` explicitly. The three mutually-exclusive notices are wrapped in one `<div className="gn-board-card__notice" ref={noticeRef}>` (`display: contents` in `ui.css`, invisible to `.gn-board-card__moves`'s own flex layout, so no visual change), and a `useEffect` calls `noticeRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest', inline: 'nearest' })` exactly when a notice is about to render AND the triggering attempt came from a drop (`13.515`, mutation-witnessed; `13.516` is the paired negative proving an arrow-reached notice never scrolls, also mutation-witnessed — `13.515` alone would still pass an implementation that scrolled unconditionally). **RULING 29 (same day) corrected `13.512`'s own assertion**: it pinned the literal `draggable="false"`, which is STRICTER than the CSS contract it guards (`[draggable="true"]` is equally unmatched by an ABSENT attribute) — a future refactor that omits the attribute instead of setting it `false` would have reddened it with no real regression behind it. Amended to `card.matches('[draggable="true"]')`, the literal selector string, verified both directions: a real regression (`draggable={true}` unconditionally) still reddens it, and the OLD assertion text, re-run against the phantom mutation for real rather than reasoned about, DOES fail where the amended one correctly stays green. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/board-arrows.ts` (docblock correction — no second module ships), `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ✅ **The whole board card opens the ticket (owner request, 2026-09-17)** — a click anywhere on `.gn-board-card`, not only its title, opens the ticket sheet. The listener lives on the `<li class="gn-board__card-slot">` wrapper, extracted into `BoardCardSlot` (`board.tsx`) so it can hold a ref — never on `Card` (`packages/ui`, documented "never itself interactive") and never as a `role="button"` (the card already contains buttons; the title stays a real `<button>` and remains the keyboard path and the accessible name). Three bail-outs: a click whose target is not a DOM descendant of the slot at all (`currentTarget.contains` — `Select` portals its dropdown to `document.body`, and React bubbles the synthetic click up the REACT tree regardless; `13.535`–`13.537`, found in review); a click whose target sits inside a control or inside the `TransitionDialog` subtree (`CARD_CLICK_BAIL_SELECTOR` — the dialog renders INSIDE the card and its scrim closes it without stopping propagation, so without this a close-by-clicking-outside would open the sheet in the same gesture) is that thing's click; and a click that follows `dragstart` is the tail of a drag (`draggedRef`, set on `dragstart`, cleared on the NEXT `mousedown` — not on `dragend`, not on a timer). `.gn-board__card-slot { cursor: pointer }` for the read-only path; the `[draggable="true"]` rule keeps `grab`. Cases `13.518`–`13.523` and `13.535`–`13.537` (`packages/app-core/test/views/board-card-click.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx`), every guard proven by deletion.
- ✅ **Move arrows: one declared target moves, several ask (owner request, 2026-09-17)** — `‹`/`›` now read the whole directional list (`arrowTargets`) instead of silently taking the nearest: exactly one entry → `attemptMove` as before (`direct` submits; `needs-dialog` opens `TransitionDialog` ALREADY AIMED at it, its comment/evidence prompts on screen from the first paint, no re-pick); more than one → the dialog opens as a picker, the arrow's own `aria-label` naming the choice ("Move to next state — Waiting on counterparty or Done"). **The dialog only ever offers what the gesture meant**: `›` the forward list, `‹` the backward list, a drop the dropped column — two new optional `TransitionDialog` props, `offerStateIds` (narrows `transitionOptions`, never widens it) and `initialToStateId` (read once on mount); `TicketSheet`'s "Move…" passes neither and is unchanged. `MoveArrows` mounts the dialog only while its `dialog` state is non-null, so every open is a fresh mount — which also closes a pre-existing wart where a cancelled pick survived into the next open. Cases `13.524`–`13.533`. Same change set, `13.534`: the drop registry moved from `useEffect` to `useLayoutEffect` — a card painted `draggable="true"` is droppable in the same commit, closing a load-exposed window in which a drop was a silent no-op (three drag-drop cases had flaked exactly that way; the new case fires the drop from a MutationObserver callback and failed deterministically under the passive form).
- ✅ **Close from a terminal board column (backlog/closed spec §5.2, 2026-09-17)** — `MoveArrows` renders a **Close** button beside the two arrows on any card whose column is `terminal`, `aria-label` `` `Close ${card.title}` ``. The category is derived LOCALLY, `stateCategoryOf(workflow, fromStateId)` (`@gisik-nokma/domain`), rather than threaded down as a second `column.category` prop — Ruling 35 (coordinator, 2026-09-17) corrected this task's own original brief on exactly that point: `MoveArrows` already holds both arguments `stateCategoryOf` takes, so a second prop carrying the identical fact under a different name would be a driftable second source for something this component can derive for itself. Clicking Close ingests one `ticket.closed` with the empty payload spec §1.2 gives the envelope-only event, then calls the SAME `moves.onMoved()` `submitDirect`'s own `committed` branch calls; the server's own `getBoard` excludes closed tickets (spec §3.2), so the reload — not a local optimistic delete — is what removes the card from the board. `closeCard` mirrors `submitDirect` (immediately above in this same file) and `ticket-sheet.tsx`'s own `closeOrReopen` (cases `13.588a`–`13.588c`) almost exactly: the identical `submitting` latch shared with both arrows, reset in a `finally` rather than only after a successful `await`; the identical Ruling-27 split of a real server refusal (`serverRefusal`, the rule and reason carried verbatim through the board's existing `role="alert"`/`.gn-refusal--server` notice) from a transport failure or a silent non-answer (`transportError`, its own "this close" sentence — "The server did not answer this close."/"We could not reach the server for this close — …" — never dressed as the server's own verdict); and the identical Ruling-34 pairing that clears `serverRefusal` alongside `transportError` on the silent branch, so a refused click followed by a silent one cannot leave a stale server notice standing beside the new one. A card in a non-terminal column renders no Close button at all — not merely a disabled one, mirroring the arrows' own "unavailable, not absent" doctrine in the opposite direction, since this column genuinely offers no such control. A click on Close never opens the ticket sheet, proven paired with a positive control (a click on the card's title button does) so the negative discriminates a real click-bail from a dead button. `.gn-board-card__close` (`ui.css`) reuses `.gn-board-card__move`'s own metrics verbatim — the 44px tap target, radius, colour tokens, focus ring, `:disabled` treatment — differing only in horizontal padding for a word rather than one glyph. Mutation-witnessed: forcing the terminal gate permanently true kills exactly the non-terminal negative case and no other (`PASS (4) FAIL (1)`). Cases `13.589`–`13.590`. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **List view** — `TicketList` + `useTicketList(source, workspaceId, projectId, currentUserId, filter?)`, with the pure model beside it (`countRows`, `groupRows`, `nestRows`, `visibleNodes`, `recentlyAssignedRows`, `columnLabel`). Per-user columns chosen and ordered, nested expand/collapse, *Recently assigned* pinned on top, and five groupings proven to rearrange the same rows with the same counts. **A default arrangement never wears the user's name, per HALF:** `prefs` carries `{columns, grouping}` as two `Chosen<T>` halves, there is deliberately no separate `prefsOrigin` prop that could be passed disagreeing with them, and the surface says *default arrangement — you have not arranged this project* when neither half was chosen and names the individual half (*default grouping — you have not chosen a grouping for this project*) when only one was. Touching a control transfers authority over THAT half only, so choosing a grouping on an unarranged project does not sign this client's stock columns with the user's name. **Grouping is a rearrangement, never a filter** — every row renders exactly once across the sections under all five groupings; **counts never shrink for visibility** — the summary reads the FLAT set, so neither a regrouping nor a collapsed subtree moves it, and a restricted child stays in the progress denominator with the restriction named; **nesting never hides a row** — a child whose parent is restricted renders at the root WITH the restriction named rather than dropped for want of somewhere to indent it. **The CLIENT owns grouping, and only because `TicketListView` is the complete row set**: the shipped `/v1/list` route groups per PAGE, so the online source asks for `grouping: 'flat'` and a TYPE tripwire on `keyof TicketListView` fails `typecheck` the day that view type grows a cursor. Every instant goes through `provenance.ts`'s one door onto the domain's `formatIstInstant`, guarded from two absurd zones with the probe that the ambient zone can move at all. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. **A NARROWED LIST NEVER WEARS THE PROJECT'S COUNT.** The hook takes an optional `FilterSpec` — hardcoded `{}` until the pagination cliff needed a way out — and the query key carries it, deeply order-stable, so a filter change re-asks rather than leaving old rows under a new control. When rows are narrowed the summary reads *N matching* and names the narrowing beside it, and the `· N done` half is DROPPED rather than recomputed: it is a fact about the project, and under the shipped narrowing it is structurally always zero. Renders through the shipped `Table` rather than a second copy of its markup. **The controls row and the column tray are restyled to the 2026-09-18 desktop prototype:** the picker is now a `Columns` toggle opening a chip BAND of five real checkboxes — each chip a `<label>` whose box and tick are drawn in CSS off `:checked`, so no second copy of "is this column shown" exists in JS — and because the real input is moved out of flow and made `opacity: 0`, its keyboard focus ring is re-drawn on the box beside it (`:focus-visible` only, so a mouse click draws nothing). A group's header band names its group and counts its SECTION's own rows, which is a count of membership rather than of the screen: collapsing a subtree leaves the band's number where it was. **Known gap (2026-09-18, the List-tab restyle to the desktop prototype): the prototype's tray offers EIGHT columns and its Group by SIX keys; this product ships five and five, and the difference is DATA rather than styling — so the tray renders five chips and no inert one.** **Workflow:** `ListCard.workflowId` reaches the server but `ListRow` drops it, and nothing resolves a workflow's NAME for a row, so there is neither a column nor a `Grouping` for it. **Priority:** no such field exists anywhere in `domain`, `app-core` or `server` — a zero-match sweep, not an assumption. **Ticket:** the id is a ULID and the tree carries no human-readable key (`ticketKey`/`ticketNumber`/`shortId`/`displayId` all return zero matches against a live positive control), so a column of ULIDs would not be the prototype's short id. The missing `workflow` grouping axis is the same Workflow gap seen from Group by. Ruled by the controller 2026-09-18 as **FILED, not built**: the Workflow column and axis are a `ListRow` widening plus a workflow-name lookup (a candidate next step), while Priority and a human ticket key are PRODUCT decisions rather than executor work. Prior art: the 2026-09-09 audit that first recorded the Workflow axis as missing from both Group-by and the Columns picker. `packages/app-core/src/views/list-model.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`. **THIS IS THE ONE VIEW THAT DRAWS AN EPIC AS A ROW (owner, 2026-09-20): an epic is a row here and nowhere else.** The Board, the Backlog, the Calendar and the Timeline all exclude it; the exclusion is per view and `list.rows` is deliberately left unnarrowed, so the parent picker still offers every ticket and `labels.epicTitle` still resolves a surviving child's epic.
- ⚙️ **`BulkCloseDialog` — one dialog, N `ticket.closed` in ONE batch** — backlog/closed spec §8.3, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 9, executor 2). The shared confirm both bulk surfaces open (`BulkCloseDialog({ open, tickets, dataSource, workspaceId, currentUserId, onClose, onClosed })`): title and confirm both read `Close N tickets` (singular `Close 1 ticket`), the body lists every title, and confirming submits **ONE `ingest` batch of N plain `ticket.closed`** — `payload: {}`, one `make` per ticket, in the listed order — rather than N calls. **Marked ⚙️ rather than ✅ deliberately: the MECHANISM ships here, and only one of its two surfaces exists.** **CORRECTED IN TASK 10 (2026-09-18): this bullet said "no surface calls it yet — Task 10's board column action and Task 11's list selection are the callers, and until one of them wires it nothing a user can see has changed."** That sentence is now half false and was rewritten in the same change set that made it false, per the no-silent-drift rule: Task 10 wires the BOARD surface, so the dialog IS user-visible there and the ✅ belongs to that feature (the bullet on the next line). This one stays ⚙️ because it describes the shared component, and the two marks answer different questions — **CORRECTED IN TASK 11 (2026-09-18): the sentence that stood here read "Task 11's list selection remains unwired, so the component still has exactly one caller", and Task 11 wires that surface in the same change set, so BOTH callers now exist.** The ⚙️ mark stays, because this bullet still describes the mechanism rather than either surface's capability. Spec §8.3's third bullet binds both halves at once ("one `ticket.closed` event per ticket; there is no 'bulk' event type and no server-side bulk endpoint"): one event per ticket keeps the ledger honest, and one CALL is the door's own shape, since `DataSource.ingest` commits an array in order and answers per position — a loop of N single-element calls satisfies the first half while turning one atomic door into N independent ones, which is exactly what a partial failure exposes. **Results are read BY INDEX** (the core's Ruling 37 shape): the answer at position i describes the envelope at position i and is never matched back by content, so a results array that is SHORT — or holed — cannot be read that way at all and is treated as silence, not as partial success: nothing is reported closed and the client's own sentence says so. Committed ids go to `onClosed`; refused ones stay listed under `Could not close` as `<title> — <reason>` (an `invalid` answer contributes its `message`, a `refused` one its `reason`), which is what makes the dialog stay open on a partial outcome rather than closing over the evidence. **The two sentences are the CLIENT's**: `We could not reach the server for this close — <message>` for a rejected promise and `The server did not answer this close.` for the short array, through `.gn-refusal--transport`, the same split `board.tsx`'s `closeCard` and `ticket-sheet.tsx`'s `closeOrReopen` already made — a person reading "Refused by the server" is reading the server's words and neither of these is one. **The dialog never closes itself:** `onClose` fires from Cancel, Escape and the scrim only, matching `NewTicketDialog` and `TransitionDialog`, and the parent owns the reload through `onClosed`; `onClosed` is called only when at least one ticket committed (calling it with `[]` would fire the parent's reload for nothing on every all-refused click — witnessed by 13.788, which exists because that decision survived deletion until the review round measured it). **Opening clears the previous attempt's outcome AND bumps the token every post-`await` write is gated on, so an answer that arrives after a dismissal never lands on the next opening** — case 13.787, the one defect in this component no test could see and the review round's finding: the plan's own consumer mounts the dialog once and toggles `open` (Task 10: "renders `BulkCloseDialog` once"), so the instance survives a dismissal and the stale closure writes a phantom `Could not close` carrying the PREVIOUS batch's refusal, plus an `onClosed` for tickets nobody just asked to close. **A fresh mount does not cover it** — the house mechanism case `13.532` pins for a remembered pick fixes stale STATE across opens and cannot fix a late PROP CALLBACK, since the old closure still holds the old `onClosed`. **What Tasks 10 and 11 must do, stated rather than inferred** (the dialog never dismisses itself): Task 10 must clear its `bulkClose` state inside `onClosed`, because `moves.onMoved()` alone reloads the board and leaves the dialog mounted and empty — **QUALIFIED BY TASK 10 (2026-09-18, three lines below): that instruction is correct only for a FULL success.** Taken unconditionally it deletes half of spec §8.3's Board bullet, because `onClosed` fires on a partial too; the shipped clear is `closedIds.length === tickets.length`, and the board surface's own ✅ bullet below carries the reasoning and the mutation that proves it. The sentence is left standing rather than rewritten because it is this component's own contract and the qualifier is the board's business; Task 11 must snapshot its tickets at click time and keep its own open flag, because its plan text wires `onClosed` → `list.reload()` with the selection cleared on reload — which would set `open` false and unmount the dialog the moment a PARTIAL close reports, destroying the `Could not close` rows spec §8.3 requires it to keep on screen. **DONE IN TASK 11 (2026-09-18), exactly as required and with the same conditional-clear/unconditional-reload shape the Board uses: the List's `pendingClose` is the click-time snapshot and its own open flag, released only on a complete close. The List's own ✅ bullet below carries the reasoning and the mutation (13.794) that proves it; the requirement above is left standing as the contract Task 11 was held to.** **R4 — the shared `Dialog`'s dismiss control is now named `Close dialog`** (`packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx`), not `Close`: its visible text is `×`, so its accessible name is whatever the attribute says and nothing in a screenshot or a `getByText` can tell two spellings apart, while "Close" is the least distinctive name available and collides with any dialog whose body puts a plain "Close" in it; `Sheet`'s counterpart has said `Close sheet` since Ruling 31 (2026-09-17), so this makes the pair consistent. **The commonly given reason is NOT the reason, and the difference is recorded rather than smoothed over:** Ruling 31's collision was `Sheet`'s dismiss versus the ticket sheet's Close button — `TicketSheet` renders `Sheet`, not `Dialog`, so `Dialog` was never involved; and a `Close N tickets` confirm could never have collided with a dismiss named "Close" anyway, because testing-library's `name:` is an EXACT string match. No shipped dialog body has a plain "Close" today, so this rename is preventive and consistency-driven. The blast radius was measured, not assumed: the full `ui` (325), `app-core` (1502) and `web` (459) suites were run against the change, and **no suite outside `packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx` queried the Dialog dismiss by name at all** — the one file that gained its witness, case 13.643, is the whole list. Cases 13.640–13.643, 13.787–13.788. `packages/app-core/src/views/bulk-close-dialog.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx`, `packages/app-core/test/views/bulk-close-dialog.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx`
- ✅ **`Close all (N)` on a terminal board column** — backlog/closed spec §8.3, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 10, executor 2). A column whose `category === 'terminal'` and which holds at least one card gains one action in its header: visible text exactly the spec's `Close all (N)`, accessible name `Close all N in <column name>` — the column is in the NAME because a board draws several columns at once and a page-level duplicate would make the control unaddressable by role and name. It opens the shared `BulkCloseDialog` on that column's cards, snapshotted at click time so a later reload cannot rewrite a dialog already on screen. **Three separate terms gate the button, and each excludes a different way of rendering a control that cannot work:** `bulkCloseFor !== null` (no `moves`, so no `dataSource`/`workspaceId`/`currentUserId` to open a dialog with — the whole control is ABSENT on the read-only path, not disabled); `category === 'terminal'` (anywhere else `ticket.closed` is refused by `close-requires-terminal`, so offering it would be offering a write the engine rejects — and the negative case holds a card, so it discriminates on the category rather than on the column being empty); and `cards.length > 0` (spec: "N = cards in the column", so `Close all (0)` is not a thing this action has to say). **★ THE CORRECTION THIS TASK CARRIES — the plan's text and the task's own dispatch were both insufficient, and the gap was a SPEC requirement.** The plan wires `onClosed` → `moves.onMoved()` and says nothing about dismissal; the dispatch then said to clear the board's `bulkClose` state in `onClosed`. Taken literally and unconditionally, that satisfies both documents and silently deletes half of spec §8.3's Board bullet, whose single sentence carries **both** halves: *"Per-ticket refusals … **stay listed**; the rest close and the board reloads."* `onClosed` fires on a PARTIAL close too (the dialog calls it whenever at least one ticket committed), so an unconditional clear unmounts the dialog and destroys the `Could not close` rows the same sentence requires it to keep on screen. **The clear is therefore CONDITIONAL — `closedIds.length === tickets.length`, i.e. everything this opening listed came back committed — and the reload is UNCONDITIONAL**, because "the rest close and the board reloads" holds on both outcomes: the committed tickets are gone from the server's board whether or not their siblings refused. Full success → the dialog LEAVES, which is the defect the dispatch was written to fix (`moves.onMoved()` alone reloads the board and leaves the dialog mounted and empty — every ticket closed, nothing but a Cancel left to press). Partial → the board still reloads, and the dialog STAYS with the refusal listed. **Witnessed, not reasoned, and the witness is mutation-proven:** case 13.792 drives a two-ticket column where the second is refused and asserts the reload AND the surviving `Could not close` row; the mutation that reinstates the dispatch's literal reading (clear unconditionally) reddens **13.792** (and 13.793, whose partial-close setup cannot reach its own subject without it). The plan's own mutation (render the button regardless of category) reddens 13.789's non-terminal negative; dropping the `cards.length > 0` term reddens 13.790. **★ AN INDEPENDENT REVIEW FOUND A REACHABLE PATH THIS TASK'S FIRST DRAFT HAD, AND THE FIX IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE.** `BulkCloseDialog`'s docblock states that a parent swapping `tickets` while the dialog stays OPEN is not covered by its reset and declares that this cannot arise for its two consumers — and that was **false for this one**: `open` is `bulkClose !== null`, so writing a second column's tickets into a non-null `bulkClose` swapped the list with no `open` transition, leaving the previous opening's `closedIds`/`failures` describing a list they were not about. The concrete failure is a FULL success that does not clear (stale ids subtracted from the new list's `pending`, so `committed.length < tickets.length`) with a `Could not close` row naming the OTHER column's ticket. **`openBulkClose` now refuses the swap, and the two alternatives were measured rather than not chosen: a `key`/remount DOES reset that stale state and is WORSE**, because the attempt token lives on the INSTANCE, nothing bumps a destroyed instance's token, and an in-flight answer then passes its own check and calls the old closure's `onClosed` — exactly the defect 13.787 exists to prevent. **The path is a keyboard one** (`Dialog` ships no focus trap and no portal, so focus can escape the modal; recorded below as a named follow-on on the SHARED component), and jsdom does not hit-test the scrim, which is why the invariant is testable here at all. Case 13.793; the guard-removal mutation reddens it and nothing else. `.gn-board__column-action` is added to the SAME rule body as `.gn-board-card__close`/`.gn-row-action` — a third class in one body rather than a copy, so the shared metrics cannot drift from themselves; like `.gn-row-action` it is a distinct class because the DOM is distinct (this button sits in `.gn-board__column-header`, not in a card) and only the metrics are shared. The dialog is rendered ONCE for the whole board (`open={bulkClose !== null}`), which is the mounting shape the component's own attempt-token guard assumes. **TWO OBSERVATIONS FROM THE SAME REVIEW ARE RECORDED WITH NAMED OWNERS RATHER THAN FIXED HERE, because each belongs to something larger than this task:**

- **WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name): the visible `Close all (2)` is not a substring of the accessible name `Close all 2 in Done`** — the parentheses break containment. The plan mandates that exact attribute string verbatim and the spec's copy verbatim, so this is a plan-text defect shipped as written rather than an implementer's slip; and it is NOT a new house pattern — the shipped, reviewed `apps/web/src/backlog-rank-controls.tsx:291` carries `aria-label="Move ${row.title} up"` over visible `Move up`, which breaks containment identically. `Close all (${n}) in ${column.name}` would satisfy it at no cost. **Owner: whoever next touches accessible names across the bulk-close and rank controls — fix BOTH controls together, since fixing one alone makes the pair inconsistent.** (Containment IS asserted where the house has chosen to hold it — the `/privacy` Back control, WCAG 2.5.3 — so this is a case-by-case standard rather than a gap nobody knows about.)
- **A straggler column can offer `Close all (N)` for a write the engine refuses.** The header gate reads `column.category` (the plan's explicit instruction), which for an ORDINARY column is the definition's own `state.category` — the same field the per-card Close derives locally via `stateCategoryOf` under Ruling 35 — but for a state no published definition names, the server falls back to the recorded `proj_tickets.state_category`. Such a column could render the action while every card in it renders no per-card Close and every `ticket.closed` is refused by `close-requires-terminal`, giving an all-refusal dialog. **Mitigating and why it is recorded rather than repaired:** the header `Chip` already displays that same `column.category`, so the control asserts nothing the surface does not; and the honest fix (deriving the gate from the workflow def) would move the action out of `BoardGroupSection`, which is deliberately hook-free and has no def on the read-only path. **Owner: whoever next touches `BoardGroupSection`'s capability props.** How often a straggler column arises at all remains unmeasured.

Cases 13.789–13.793. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/app-core/test/views/board-close.test.tsx`
- ✅ **`Close selected (N)` from the List** — backlog/closed spec §8.3, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 11, executor 2). The List tab's rows each gain a leading checkbox (`aria-label` `Select <title>`) and the controls row gains one action whose visible text is the spec's `Close selected (N)`, **enabled only while every selected row is terminal and not closed** — spec §8.3's own sentence, the cross-column case, and both of its terms are asserted against their own negative (a `Doing` row is terminal's; a closed `Done` row is the second term's, and the plan's own mutation — dropping `closed === null` — reddens that case and nothing else). The label counts the selection AS SHOWN — `listSelectedRows` is the visible rows filtered by `selectedIds`, and it is both the number printed and the list the snapshot is built from, so N always equals the ticked checkboxes on screen and the batch can never contain an id with no row. An id whose row has left the visible set (a narrowing, or a peer's close under `filter.closed === false`) contributes nothing to the count and re-ticks if the row returns; the box is hidden with the row rather than the label naming a ticket nobody can see. **An earlier draft of this bullet said the label "keeps the FULL selection count", which is stronger than the code holds; an independent review measured it and this is the correction.** It opens the shared `BulkCloseDialog` on the selected rows, snapshotted at click time, and confirming submits **the same one `ingest` batch of N plain `ticket.closed`** the Board's `Close all (N)` submits. **Gated on the session** (`session.kind === 'signed-in'`) exactly as the board's `moves`, the Backlog tab's `rowAction` and `rowDrag` are: `currentUserId` is `''` before the session resolves and while signed out, and a `ticket.closed` built from an empty actor is refused by `checkRolePermission` for a reason that names the person's permissions rather than the truth (case 3.144). A signed-out viewer gets no checkbox column at all, not an inert one. **★ THE CORRECTION THIS TASK CARRIES — and it is the SAME gap Task 10 found, on the second surface, caught before it could recur.** The plan's text wires `onClosed` → `list.reload()` with the selection cleared on reload, and §8.3's Board bullet is ONE sentence carrying both halves — *"Per-ticket refusals … **stay listed**; the rest close and the board reloads"* — which the List bullet inherits through "same dialog, same batch". `onClosed` fires on a PARTIAL close too. **The plan names neither a snapshot nor an `open` expression, so it does not produce the unmount — it fails to forbid it**: under the reading that makes `open` follow the selection, the clear unmounts the dialog in the same tick its `Could not close` rows are written and the person never reads the reason their ticket was refused; under the plan's other literal reading (live `tickets`, its own open flag) the refusal list still renders, because `failures` is the dialog's own state, but the report describes a selection that has since been emptied — `Close 0 tickets` over an opening that was two. **Both readings fall short of §8.3, and an independent review corrected an earlier draft of this bullet that said the plan "produces" the unmount.** **The fix is the Board's, stated for this consumer: the tickets are SNAPSHOTTED at click time (the reload cannot change what an open report is a report about) and the dialog keeps its OWN open flag (clearing the selection cannot unmount it)** — `pendingClose` is `BulkCloseDialog['tickets'] | null`, `open` is `pendingClose !== null`, and `onClosed` reloads UNCONDITIONALLY while releasing the snapshot only when `closedIds.length` equals the snapshot's length. **Witnessed, not reasoned, and mutation-proven:** 13.794 drives a two-ticket selection where the second is refused and asserts the refusal listed AND the dialog still mounted AND the list reloaded AND the selection cleared — the last being what proves the mount is not keyed on the selection; reinstating the plan's literal reading reddens **13.794 and nothing else**, and removing the snapshot reddens 13.794 and 13.795. The selection is cleared by `reloadListClearingSelection`, whose ONE caller is the bulk close's own `onClosed` — NOT the page's other reload sites, which are three BACKLOG writes (`BacklogStartButton`'s `onStarted`, the rank controls' `onRanked`, and `submitRank`'s partial-application reload) that say nothing about a List selection and must not clear one — and NOT on `list.rows`' identity, for the reason `rankOverrides` gives in the other direction: a background stream refetch also changes what the rows hold, and clearing there would wipe a selection out from under someone who never asked for a read. Dismissing (Cancel/Escape/scrim) does not reload and does not clear. **★ The same reachable path Task 10's review found is closed here BEFORE a review had to find it twice.** `BulkCloseDialog`'s docblock states that a parent swapping `tickets` while the dialog stays OPEN is not covered by its reset: `open` is `pendingClose !== null`, so a second selection written into a non-null snapshot is a swap with no `open` transition, leaving the previous opening's `closedIds`/`failures` describing a list they are not about (the concrete failure being a FULL success that does not clear, with a `Could not close` row naming the other selection's ticket). The opener refuses the swap — `setPendingClose((current) => current ?? snapshot)` — and **13.795's control half is what makes the refusal discriminating**: after dismissing, the same click on the same control opens a fresh dialog carrying the CURRENT selection, so the titles below are unchanged only because the GUARD refused. **It proves the click is live, not that the refused click arrived** — it is a third click, and a handler that swallowed clicks only while `pendingClose !== null` is observationally identical to the shipped guard, because a correctly refused click is a no-op. The mutation that removes the guard is what proves the guard. `TicketList` gains two OPTIONAL props, both absent-by-default so every other tab renders byte-identical markup: `selection` (`{ selected, onToggle }`, a CONTROLLED pair — the caller owns the set, because what makes a selection closeable, when it stops being true, and what the dialog is handed are all the caller's to decide and none is visible from inside the component) adding a LEADING checkbox column the way `rowAction`'s trailing one is added, and `controls`, a slot for the caller's own control rendered AFTER the summary sentence (13.647 asserts the ordering; a mutation that hoists it above the summary reddens that case and nothing else, while moving it to the END of the row leaves every case green — the position among the arrangement controls is deliberately unpinned because spec §8.3 says only "in the list's control row"). `.gn-list__bulk-close` joins the SAME rule body as `.gn-board-card__close`/`.gn-row-action`/`.gn-board__column-action` — a FOURTH member rather than a copy, so the shared metrics cannot drift from themselves, and like its siblings a distinct class because the DOM is distinct (this control sits in `.gn-list__controls`, not in a row or a card header). `.gn-list__select` is a new, self-contained cell rule: the platform's own checkbox kept rather than hand-drawn (with `accent-color` bound to `--gn-accent`, a token per style and theme), and the 24px WCAG 2.5.8 floor carried on the INPUT rather than the cell, since a `<td>`'s padding is not part of a control's hit area. Cases 13.647–13.649, 13.794–13.795. `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/app-core/test/views/ticket-list.test.tsx`, `apps/web/test/list-bulk-close.test.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`TicketList` row drag (`rowDrag`) and the tail drop zone** — backlog/closed spec §8.1, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 4). Marked ⚙️ rather than ✅ deliberately: the MECHANISM ships here. **CORRECTED IN TASK 5 (2026-09-18): this bullet said "no surface passes it yet — the Backlog tab is the first caller (Task 5), and until it wires `rowDrag` nothing a user can see has changed."** That sentence is now false and was rewritten in the same change set that made it false, per the no-silent-drift rule: Task 5 wires `rowDrag` on the Backlog tab, so the mechanism IS user-visible there. The ✅ belongs to that feature and is carried on the bullet below; this one stays ⚙️ because it describes the internal hook, and the two marks answer different questions. `TicketList` gains an OPTIONAL `rowDrag?: { onReorder(movedTicketId, beforeTicketId) }`; **absence is the entire pre-Task-4 behaviour** — no row is `draggable`, no handler is attached, no tail zone renders, and 13.705's negative arm plus a dispatch-level control pin both halves of that. When present: every `<tr>` is `draggable`, `dragstart` writes the row's `ticketId` into `dataTransfer` under **`DRAG_TICKET_FORMAT`, IMPORTED from `board.tsx:181` rather than re-spelled** (two spellings of one format drift apart silently, and a drifted format's failure mode is a drop that reads `''` — a reorder that does nothing and says nothing); `dragover` cancels the event and marks the row `gn-list__row--drop-before`; a `drop` on a row calls `onReorder(dragged, row.ticketId)` — "put `dragged` immediately before this row"; and a single tail zone after the tables takes a drop as `onReorder(dragged, null)` — "place last". **Three payloads are refused at the component rather than handed on:** an empty one; a drop of a row on itself (a move to where it already is); and **a payload that is not one of this list's own ticket ids**. That third one is the load-bearing refusal and it was MISSING until an independent review measured `onReorder('https://example.com/some-link', 'rd_a')` — `DRAG_TICKET_FORMAT` is plain `'text/plain'`, the UNIVERSAL format, so a link, a selection or a file dragged in from another application arrives **non-empty** and the "the payload is empty" test does not catch it. The Board reads the same format and is safe only because it then looks the value up in a registry that answers `undefined` for a stranger; this component has no registry, so the check is membership in the set of rows it is showing, applied at both drop targets. **Handlers are React props on the row, never a registry** — 13.534 recorded the flake a passive `useEffect`-registered attempt map produced on the Board (a callback registered by one render answering a drop belonging to another); the Board's registry exists because a drop there must reach the *dragged card's* `attemptMove` closure, while here the dragged id travels in the `dataTransfer` payload itself, so there is nothing to look up. The tail zone renders **once for the whole list**, not once per section: a grouped list mounts one table per section, and one drop target per section would put N elements on screen each claiming a part is the whole when a drop on any of them means the same order. `Table` (`packages/ui`) grows the one hook this needs — `rowProps?: (row) => HTMLAttributes<HTMLTableRowElement>`, spread onto each `<tr>` after the built-ins, with **`className` APPENDED** to `gn-table__row`(`--clickable`)/`rowClassName` in Ruling 21's `[own, extra].filter(Boolean).join(' ')` shape rather than replacing it (a hook that replaced it would strip the class the shared stylesheet keys on — an unstyled row, not an override); optional and additive, so `Table`'s other behaviour is byte-identical for every caller passing nothing. Three CSS classes (`.gn-list__row--dragging` — `opacity` only, so a dragged row keeps its own ground —, `.gn-list__row--drop-before` — an `inset` shadow, so marking a row never reflows the list mid-gesture —, and `.gn-list__tail-drop`) in ONE contiguous block in `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, token colours only. Cases 13.705 (seven `it`s, `packages/app-core/test/views/ticket-list.test.tsx`), 13.706 (`packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx`); the maximal fixture in 13.671's CSS-binding equality check gained a DRIVEN drag render, since two of the three classes exist only mid-gesture and a render that merely passed the prop would leave two styled classes unrendered. `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/components/table.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ✅ **Backlog ranking: drag to reorder, and Move up / Move down** — backlog/closed spec §8.1, second wave, 2026-09-18 (Task 5). The Backlog tab now draws its rows in RANK ORDER — ranked first by key, unranked last by ticket id — instead of the server's arrival order, and a row can be moved by dragging it or by a pair of per-row buttons. **The three pieces of rank arithmetic are pure and headless** (`packages/app-core/src/views/backlog-rank.ts`, 19 cases at 13.707): `sortByRank` (which delegates to the domain's `compareByRank` rather than restating "where do unranked rows go" — a second copy of that rule is exactly the drift this series keeps finding), `neighboursFor(sorted, movedId, beforeId)` → the `{before, after}` pair `rankBetween` is handed, and `moveTarget(sorted, movedId, 'up'|'down')` → the `beforeId` a button should use. They are separate from the component because `rankBetween` only throws on an INVERTED pair, so a neighbour pair that is merely off by one ROW yields a perfectly valid key in the wrong place and the tab renders it happily. **Two rules in there are the ones nobody guesses, and both are stated in the docblocks:** (1) the moved row is excluded from its own neighbour search — otherwise "move to the end" reads the last ranked row as the row being moved and builds the new key from its own old one; (2) when the row is placed last and the last row is UNRANKED, `before` is the last **ranked** row's key, because an unranked row has no key to sit after — reading `.rank` off the immediately preceding row answers `null`, and `rankBetween(null, null)` is the midpoint, so the row lands wherever the midpoint sorts rather than where the pointer was. `moveTarget`'s `down` points TWO rows below, not one, and that is not an off-by-one: the target is a `beforeId`, so "move down past the next row" means "place before the row after it" — pointing at the row directly below is a NO-OP that spends a round trip to move nothing. **`submitRank` is ONE path** (`apps/web/src/backlog-rank-controls.tsx`): both the buttons and the page's `rowDrag.onReorder` call the same exported function, which is the plan's own requirement and is measured rather than asserted — mutation M2 (ingest `before ?? 'i'` instead of the computed key) reddens both 13.709 drag cases *together with* 13.708's between-assertion, which a second independent neighbour calculation could not do. It computes the key, applies the optimistic override, ingests one `ticket.ranked`, and splits failures the way Ruling 27 requires (refusal / silence / transport failure, three different sentences, the same shape `BacklogStartButton` established). **The optimistic order is real:** the page holds `rankOverrides: Map<ticketId, rank>` which `sortByRank` reads over the server's rows, so the row moves under the pointer before the answer lands; it is cleared when the server's ANSWER identity changes rather than when the reload is CALLED — keying it on the call would drop the override the moment the request goes out and the row would snap back then jump forward again, two moves for one gesture. A non-committed answer clears the override too (the snap-back), via a `finally` that deliberately does not fire on `committed`. The two buttons carry `aria-label` `Move <title> up` / `Move <title> down` and are disabled at the edges — but only the genuinely last row's `Move down`, since the second-to-last row's means "place last" and must stay enabled. **The drag is gated on the same `session.kind === 'signed-in'` check as Start**, because a drag ends in an event carrying an `actorId`; the buttons appear and disappear with it. **THE UNRANKED TAIL — A DEFECT FOUND BY REVIEW, HELD RATHER THAN SHIPPED, AND FIXED UNDER AN OWNER RULING (2026-09-18, option B).** In a backlog where **nothing is ranked yet — the default state of every project, since `rank` starts `NULL` (13.702) — "place last" moved the row to the TOP.** Measured against the built module, not inferred: `[t1,t2,t3,t4]` all unranked, drag `t4` to the tail → `{before:null, after:null}` → key `'i'` → drawn order `t4 t1 t2 t3`. The cause is structural — `compareByRank` sorts every ranked row above every unranked one, so no rank key can place a row *after* an unranked row — and the same fact made an enabled `Move down` a no-op whenever it would step over one (`[A('g'),B('n'),C(null)]`, `Move B down` → key `'q'`, order unchanged `A B C`). It was the plan's §8 rule implemented faithfully, so it was recorded with a named owner rather than repaired by guess, and **the PR was held** — the controller's ruling that a short hold is cheaper than shipping it and correcting it in a second PR. **The fix, now shipped: `planRankMove` ranks the rows that have no key FIRST, in their drawn order, then applies the move, in ONE ingest** (N+1 `ticket.ranked` events, existing vocabulary, no schema change); `submitRank` publishes those keys optimistically too, without which the moved row would be the only ranked row on screen and would draw above the rows it was dragged past. **The accepted cost is handled explicitly rather than left implicit:** the results are read BY INDEX, and a batch that only partly commits reports its own `partial` outcome — on the transport channel, because part of it committed and it is not a verdict — drops every optimistic key and **reloads so the tab shows the server's state, not the requested one**. Cases 13.707–13.712. **KNOWN LIMITATION, MEASURED, NOT FIXED:** `DEFAULT_GROUPING` is `'epic'`, so with backlog rows under more than one epic the tab draws them grouped while the handlers compute from rank order — rank order `A('e1') B('e2') C('e1')` draws `A C B` under `epic` and `A B C` under `flat`. A move therefore lands where rank order says rather than where the screen says. Same class as the defect above and **not covered by its remedy**; owner: the ruling that priced both. `packages/app-core/src/views/backlog-rank.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/src/backlog-rank-controls.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/app-core/test/views/backlog-rank.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/backlog-tab.test.tsx`

The deferred ruling was approved and the remaining five metrics landed the same day — see the following bullet, Ruling 21.** Landed, all on `.gn-list-*` selectors this task owns: a new `.gn-list__table-wrap` around `<Table>` (`ticket-list.tsx`'s `ListTable`) gives the metrics table's "Container" row (`cardBg + cardShadow; radius cardR; overflow:auto` → `--gn-surface-raised`/`--gn-shadow-raised`/`--gn-radius-card`, the same three roles `.gn-card` itself already pairs) WITHOUT touching anything `Table` renders itself; the Group-by/Columns picker's `<summary>` becomes a pill (`min-height:36px; the title button (`.gn-list__open`) goes to 17px/22px; and the depth-based title indentation changes from an ungrounded `depth * 1.1rem` to the metric's own formula, `paddingLeft: ${16 + depth * 22}px`, computed in JS since depth is a render-time number. **DECLARED DIVERGENCE, not a match:** the metrics table's "Group header row" (`padding 11px 16px; trayBg + trayShadow`) is applied to `.gn-list__section-title` — the existing per-group `<h3>` outside the table — because this product's architecture renders one grouping's rows as a SEPARATE `<table>` per section (Group-by wiring this task's own brief holds fixed: "Keep the Group-by / Columns wiring untouched... this is a restyle, not a rewiring"), where the prototype draws a `<tr>` spanning the table's own columns. **FIVE metrics NOT implemented, and reported rather than built past a rule this task's own brief names by number:** "Table: min-width:820px", "Header cell", "Zebra" (odd rows `fill4`), "Data cell" (padding/15px/nowrap), and the title cell's own `min-width:260px` are each reachable only by putting a class on an element the shared `Table` component (`@gisik-nokma/ui`) renders itself — `<table>`, `<th>`, `<td>`, `<tr>` — and `table.tsx` has no `className` or per-column class prop today, unlike `Card` (`card.tsx:7`, `['gn-card', className].filter(Boolean).join(' ')`) which is exactly the shape that would fix this. The port's own global constraints name this precise trap for Task 9: *"if a list metric seems reachable only by editing `.gn-table`, stop and report rather than editing."* **A ruling request, not a landed change:** extend `Table`'s props the same way `Card` already is — an optional `className` on `<table>`, `headerClassName`, `rowClassName`, and a `TableColumn.cellClassName` — so every new rule these five metrics need could still be written on a `.gn-list-*` selector alone (`Table` has exactly one consumer repo-wide today, this file, confirmed by grep, so the change is additive and risk-free to every other would-be caller). Awaiting the repo owner's decision rather than landed on this task's own authority. **The visible result is a mixed one, not a finished restyle:** a rounded, shadowed card frame now sits around a header row, zebra, and data cells that still render at `Table`'s original, smaller metrics — named here so a reviewer does not mistake the frame for the whole job. `apps/web/test/list-prefs-wiring.test.tsx` (Group-by/Columns wiring) re-run unchanged. - ✅ **Ruling 21 (coordinator, 2026-09-16): `Table` gains optional class hooks, and the List restyle is complete.** The coordinator independently verified the ruling request's premise before approving it — `Table` has exactly one PRODUCTION consumer repo-wide (`ticket-list.tsx`) plus its own test (`packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx`) — then approved extending `Table` (`packages/ui/src/components/table.tsx`) with four optional props, all composed the way `card.tsx:7` already composes `Card`'s own `className` (`[own, extra].filter(Boolean).join(' ')`, appending to `gn-table*` rather than replacing it, so a caller passing nothing — every consumer before this change — gets byte-identical output): `className` on `<table>`, `headerClassName`/`cellClassName` on `TableColumn` (applied to that column's `<th>`/every row's `<td>`), and `rowClassName` on `<Table>` (applied to every `<tr>`). `ticket-list.tsx`'s `ListTable` now passes all four, giving `.gn-list__table` (min-width:820px), `.gn-list__table-header` (13px/18px/600/label2/trayBg/padding/nowrap, uniform across every column), `.gn-list__table-row:nth-child(odd)` (fill4 zebra), `.gn-list__table-cell--data` (12px 16px/15px/nowrap), and `.gn-list__table-cell--title` (min-width:260px plus, for the first time, PIXEL-EXACT static padding — the `<td>`'s own left padding is now zeroed by this new class rather than left uneditable, so the depth-dependent inline `paddingLeft` on the inner span composes against a zero base instead of an uneditable one). Every new rule is a plain `.gn-list-*` selector; the seven shared do-not-edit selectors (`.gn-card`, `.gn-chip`, `.gn-avatar`, `.gn-table`, `.gn-select`, `.gn-button`, `.gn-segmented`) confirmed unedited by a full-diff grep for any added/removed line beginning with one of them — zero matches. - ⚙️ **Preferences document hook** — `usePreferences(source, workspaceId)` → `{document, error, save}` (the exported `PreferencesResult`). **There is deliberately no `reload`:** an earlier shape returned one, nothing called it, and a reload bumps the generation counter without clearing `document` — so between the bump and the resolve `useTicketList` still reports `canSetPrefs: true` and a `setPrefs` landing there merges into a document the counter has abandoned. That the write wins anyway is an argument, not a measurement, and an export whose safety has not been measured is a liability. Deliberately NOT `useLiveQuery`: preferences are server-owned per-user rows (amendment 14) and no stream frame can carry one, so subscribing them to the ledger stream both wastes a refetch per ticket event and makes `use-live-query.ts`'s recorded F6 reachable — a debounced GET in flight when a `putPreferences` lands resolves afterwards under the same key with the PRE-WRITE document, and the arrangement the user just chose snaps back on screen while the server holds the new one. One generation counter orders loads against saves. `document` is `null` until it loads, never an empty document, because a whole-document PUT composed from a document this client has not read would erase every saved filter and every other project's arrangement — `useTicketList` refuses the write in that window and `TicketList` disables the controls so the refusal is visible. **Ruling P24 at the surface, BOTH of its asymmetries.** A project absent from the document reports `prefsOrigin: 'default'` and the view SAYS so, rather than presenting this client's stock arrangement as the user's own choice. The ruling's other asymmetry — a project present in the server's `listColumns` and absent from its `grouping`, or the reverse — used to be *erased* by a `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` fill inside `mapPreferences` before any view was handed the document, which is what P24 forbids in as many words (*"reaching for `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` there invents a preference the user never set … a project missing from `grouping` surfaces as **not set**, never as a default wearing a user's authority"*). **It is now represented rather than erased:** `ProjectViewPrefs`' two halves are each a `Chosen<T>` = `{origin: 'stored'; value: T} | {origin: 'default'}`, and the `default` arm carries NO value, so reading a value without first narrowing on `origin` does not compile — the ruling is enforced by the type checker rather than by convention. The stock values live in their own constants (`DEFAULT_LIST_COLUMNS`, `DEFAULT_GROUPING`); `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` is now the statement that *neither half is set*, and fusing those two facts in one constant is what let a default be read as a choice. `mapPreferences` reports an absent half `{origin: 'default'}`; `toServerPreferences` contributes no key for such a half, so a grouping chosen alone is stored alone and a stock value is never PUT under the user's name; the pair round-trips a half-set project as half-set. One vocabulary for both scopes: `PrefsOrigin` is declared in `view-types.ts` and re-exported by `ticket-list.tsx`, and the project-level origin is derived from the halves by the single exported `prefsOriginOf` rather than stored beside them. `packages/app-core/src/views/use-preferences.ts`

Both halves are pinned rather than asserted in prose: 13.881 fails if the project page renders the strip **or** makes the read, 13.882 fails if the removal took My Work's with it. The removal also dropped `attention.error` from that page's expired-session list, which is now THREE reads and not four. **The client's My Work and the server's `GET /v1/me/work` group on DIFFERENT AXES — a different model, not a rename:** the client is `{assigned, inTransit, contested}`, which is CLAIM STATUS, and the route answers `{open, active, terminal}`, which is STATE CATEGORY. All three server groups are this viewer's assigned work, so the whole route collapses into one of the three sections; `mapMyWork` records that the axis difference is adaptable and is *not* why `getMyWork` refuses. **`inTransit` is empty because there is no outbox, not because a read came back empty** — it IS the replica's outbox (Plan 10) and the web client is online-only (spec §5, whose same section places the outbox in the mobile and desktop replicas and nowhere else; Plan 6 carries it at line 23), so the emptiness is structural truth about this client's construction; the falsifiable half of that is asserted against the refusal register itself, which lists `ContestedClaim.title` and `ContestedClaim.claim.serverTime` and deliberately lists nothing about `inTransit`. A claim genuinely in flight is the `pending` view flag on one card, not a queue, and the in-transit card is badged distinctly so a claim the server has never seen cannot read as one it accepted. Contested cards name the RULE and repeat the engine's `reason` verbatim beside the claim's provenance. **The strip renders what exists and nothing else:** the union is discriminated over four kinds, the route emits three, and the flat list means an absent kind draws no heading, no count and no empty section — the word "Contested" appears nowhere when no contested item was given, and an empty strip renders no container at all rather than a reassuring all-clear on a surface that is structurally incapable of holding its fourth kind. **The strip wears the PROTOTYPE'S TREATMENT WHOLE as of 2026-09-18** (owner ruling, `desktop.dc.html:561-575`): a `--gn-tint-warning` card, a VISIBLE heading reading *"Needs your attention"* beside a decorative 20px warning triangle, and **the region's accessible name is that string character for character** — the shipped name was `"Needs attention"` beside that heading, which is WCAG 2.5.3's exact failure, and the fix keeps the `aria-label` attribute rather than naming the region from the heading because removing it would drop a counted `aria-label` on `/my-work`, where the accessible-name baseline is a floor of 8. A heading that never outlives its rows is not the "do not draw the heading" refusal: that rule is about PER-KIND sub-headings, and `items.length === 0` returns `null` before any heading is reached. Rows run title → chip → reason → claim (the claim is a product addition and stays last), `7px 0` over a 24px floor, and **the whole row is the click target** — the title button keeps its accessible name and its floor, and its `::after` stretches over the row. **The card's 4px `--gn-pending` left marker is GONE, and that is a design choice, not a reversal of a measurement** — `hig-per-screen/7a`/`8a` measured it at 4.36 over a 3:1 floor and concluded it was never contrast debt; that conclusion stands. `--gn-tint-warning` now has two consumers, both at page level on `surface`, which is the ground its composite is measured on. Cases 13.890–13.893. Reasons are the SERVER'S, rendered verbatim (owner decision: the spec's "user's language" means named reasons, not localisation), so the strip shows the route's own `Needed by 2026-08-30 — due within 7 days.` rather than a browser re-derivation computed without the workspace's thresholds. `KIND_TONE`/`KIND_LABEL` are total `Record<AttentionKind, …>` maps, so Plan 10's fourth arm fails `typecheck` here rather than reaching a user as a blank chip. Both reads REFUSE today (`ServingGapError`) and both hooks let the refusal reach `LiveQueryResult.error` rather than substituting `[]` or three empty sections. Every date goes through the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, guarded from three zones — an instant in the strip, a DAY CLAIM in the due line — each zone checked against an expectation computed WITHOUT the code under test, behind a control that the `TZ` knob moves at all and a control that the fixture straddles the zones. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. **A FOURTH section, "Shared with me", now stands beside the three (case 13.176)** — the errands whose audience names this viewer, read off `MyWorkResponse.shared` and mapped through the SAME `mapWorkItem` as the groups, so a withheld project name degrades and a missing state name refuses by one rule rather than two. It renders whether or not it has rows, because a section that vanished when empty is indistinguishable from one that failed to load — and for the guest it exists for it is the only section that can ever be non-empty. **Every row is openable, and an earlier gate that withheld the button when the project NAME was withheld is GONE because its reasoning was false** — `routes/board.ts` gates on WORKSPACE membership and says so in writing ("a workspace member who is NOT on the project passes the gate and gets 200"), a guest IS a workspace member, and the guest arm of `visibleTicketsWhere` is the explicit clause, so their shared ticket is on that board; the gate withheld a working affordance from exactly the person the section is for. **The page's resolver now searches `shared`** — it turns a ticket id into `/w/:ws/p/:projectId?ticket=…` by looking the id up in the view, and searching only `assigned` and `inTransit` made every shared-but-unassigned row a silent dead click (no navigation, no error), which is the normal case for this section rather than an edge. **The project line also stopped being BLANK when the name is withheld** — `WorkItem.projectName`'s contract says the item "renders id-only rather than blank" and the renderer printed nothing at all, a PRE-EXISTING defect reachable before this change (a guest ASSIGNED an invisible-project ticket) and fixed here with one rule for every section, because this is where it becomes the normal case. **The fallback's TITLE text is pinned too, not just its id (case 13.309)** — the withheld row's `title` attribute reads `You have not been told this project’s name.` exactly, asserted as the RENDERED attribute and paired with a lookup that must MISS on a straight apostrophe: the source spells the string with U+2019 and the plan that specifies this screen spells it with U+0027 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-08-hig-per-screen.md:902`), so a case transcribed from the plan would compare the wrong character, fail, and read as a missing fallback. `packages/app-core/src/views/attention-strip.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/my-work.tsx`
