The fixture that proves it is skewed by THREE DAYS on purpose, so showing either clock alone prints a different string and either mutation dies; clocks seconds apart would make the whole battery vacuous. The zone is named once from the domain's `IST_CLOCK_NOTE`, and both clocks are checked unmoved from three zones against expectations computed without `formatIstInstant`. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. The three parent facts stay three — a RESTRICTED parent renders as existence-without-title, never as `At project root`. Transitions are prompted by the workflow's own declared rules (a comment box only where `require-comment` sits, a field only where it is not already claimed, and — where `require-evidence` sits — a real upload, with the honest sentence *"Evidence upload is not available in this build yet"* surviving ONLY for a host that supplies no uploader, which both of the web's mounts now do supply; a refused or unreachable upload gets its own sentence in the server/transport split Ruling 27 established), **where a wildcard edge and an exact edge both reach one target, the EXACT edge supplies the rules and declaration order does not matter — the engine's own resolution (`find(exact) ?? find('*')`), matched here under ruling P27 after `transitionOptions` was found resolving by declaration order while `precheck.ts` resolved correctly, one package holding two answers to one question**, the instant pre-check is advisory and prints nothing when it passes, and the SERVER's refusal renders verbatim in an `alert` beside — never instead of — a disagreeing preview. **The ledger payload is the SHIPPED spelling**, `{to, comment: {text}}` per `ticketTransitionedPayloadSchema`, not the plan's `{toStateId, comment: 'string'}` — which both the ingest door and the rule engine refuse as malformed, and which the local pre-check would put in front of the user as `malformed-payload: … at to: Required`. The workflow is read at the version the TICKET PINS, not at latest. `getTicket` REFUSED against the shipped server of the day and the refusal reached the surface AS a refusal, under its own class, with the string `Loading…` nowhere (**⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this read "`getTicket` REFUSES today (`ServingGapError`)" in the present tense. Ruling P25 clause 1 — `c86c4b5c`, 2026-09-06 — served that read, and the register has been empty since 2026-09-19. The BRANCH is unchanged and still right: a served read can still FAIL, and the property is that a failure never renders as a wait.** — the plan's single `{read.error ?? 'Loading…'}` branch dressed a permanent serving gap as a wait that will end. **The History tab makes the same three-way distinction, and the ledger is where it matters most:** `useLiveQuery` leaves `data` null both while a read is in flight and after it rejects, so the tab's earlier single `history.data ?? []` drew an empty `<ol>` for pending, failed and empty alike — on the surface that IS the ledger, an empty list reads as *this ticket has no history*. Loading, failure and empty are now three renderings under three classes, with `error` tested BEFORE `data === null` because on a rejection both hold. The failure is worded and classed as an ERROR rather than a refusal: `getTicketHistory` ANSWERS — as, since 2026-09-19, does every method, `SERVING_GAPS` being empty (this read "one of the six methods", ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-20) — so it may succeed on a retry, and wearing `gn-ticket-sheet__refusal` would be a false claim in the CSS. The empty branch describes the ANSWER (*No entries came back for this ticket.*), never the ticket, because the online source returns `[]` on a 404 and a live ticket always carries at least its own `ticket.created`. **And the ledger is paged:** the shipped route defaults to 200 rows (`apps/server/src/routes/ticket-history.ts:78`), so the sheet asks with an explicit `HISTORY_PAGE_LIMIT` and says *Showing the first 200 entries — this ticket's history may go on past them* whenever the page comes back full. A full page is the only partiality signal on the wire (no cursor, no `hasMore`), so it over-reports on a ticket with exactly 200 entries — taken deliberately, because an occasional *there may be more* is a weaker claim than the truth where a silent truncation is a stronger one. It also UNDER-reports if the route's `MAX_LIMIT` is ever set below this number: the clamp returns a full-but-short page, the notice does not fire, and no client can tell — recorded at the site as the second reason the two numbers must be kept in step. No cursor paging is built. `PlainTextEditorSlot` is the honest read-only default over the domain's one `docToPlainText`: it renders and it composes nothing, because a build without Plan 7's editor cannot write a comment and says so by having no control rather than one that discards what is typed. `EditorSlotProps` is the seam Plan 7 fills — declared here with `doc: unknown` per amendment 12; **`packages/editor` declares no counterpart today**, and Plan 7's text declares `doc: DocJson | null`, which under this repo's `strictFunctionTypes` is not assignable to `EditorSlotComponent` (owner: step 12). `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/history-list.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/transition-dialog.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/editor-slot.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`

An earlier wording of this sentence said "zero call sites repo-wide", which independent review measured as an overreach, and the ONE PRODUCT CALL SITE is the claim that holds.) The footer is now the prototype's own (`desktop.dc.html:1371`), it is drawn unconditionally, and it carries **`Delete`**. **The owner ruled the `‹ Previous / Next ›` pair OUT in the same breath — *"remove the navigation"* — and that is a DECISION rather than a gap:** nothing computes a queue position, the pair rendered on no shipping screen, and the prop, its two neighbour ids, its `onNavigate`, the per-step `setTab('details')` reset and `.gn-ticket-sheet__nav` all went with it. `16.43`, which asserted the walk, is retired to 🚫. **The delete is SOFT and the product says "deleted" rather than "erased"** — the FAQ's own sentence, *"The ticket stops appearing in views. Its history is retained, because other events cite it — a comment, a block, a workflow version"*, is what the feature implements: `ticket.deleted` sets `TicketState.deleted`, the row and the whole ledger stay, and a single exported predicate, `read/visible-where.ts`'s `LIVE_TICKET_WHERE`, is AND-ed into **every view-serving SELECT**. ⚠ **The filter has NO choke point and the first reading of it was WRONG in writing:** the assembler is shared, the SELECTs are hand-written ones — independent review counted 14 statements carrying the rule and no reading of "hand-written" yields the 11 an earlier draft claimed — and the repo's own `closed_at IS NULL` exclusion lives inline at five sites with no helper), so the constant exists to make the coverage greppable. **⚠ And since 2026-09-20 it reaches the ROUTES as well, not only the SELECTs**: four doors that serve one ticket refuse a tombstone through `read/live-ticket.ts` — the same rule in the same spelling, asked at the door instead of joined into a query — so the greppable coverage is no longer view SELECTs alone. See the *a soft-deleted ticket's files go with it* row for the leak that made it necessary. **Folding it into `visibleTicketsWhere` would have COMPILED and then failed at runtime** — `visibility/visible-tickets.ts` selects `FROM proj_visibility v` with no `t` alias at all. ⛔ **And the ENGINE's by-id reads deliberately do NOT filter**: `getTicketStateFromDb` feeds `EngineContext`, and a context that cannot see a deleted ticket refuses its own undo with `unknown-ticket`, so the delete would work and could never be reversed — the exclusion belongs to the views. **The undo is `ticket.restored`**, and the two halves split where they must: the SHEET emits and closes itself, the HOST owns the toast and the Undo, because an undo offered inside a component that has just removed itself is not an undo. A refused delete keeps the sheet open with the engine's own rule in `gn-refusal--server`; a silent non-answer is a transport error and never a refusal. ⚠ **A DEPARTURE FROM THE PROTOTYPE'S COPY:** its toast says `"GN-1183 deleted · …"` — a ticket KEY — and this product has none, so the sentence carries the title. ⚠ **The control is not hidden from non-admins** — `ticket.deleted` is gated on project-admin rights in `check-permission.ts`, but the repo's rule is that the ingest door is the only party that knows, so a refused press shows the refusal rather than a button that is silently absent. Cases `16.84` — the sheet's half (`packages/app-core/test/views/ticket-sheet.test.tsx`), the call site's half (`apps/web/test/sheet-delete.test.tsx`), the control's rule and contrast pair (`packages/ui/test/components.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/test/tokens.test.ts`) — mutation-witnessed ten times on the interface half plus seven on the server half, every one killing an arm on its own assertion (a count of MUTATIONS, not arms: M8 kills two and M21 three). `packages/domain/src/{events/ticket-events,ticket-state,entity-state,vocabulary}.ts`, `packages/rule-engine/src/checks/deletion.ts`, `apps/server/migrations/1789800000000_proj-tickets-deleted.sql`, `apps/server/src/projections/tickets.ts`, `apps/server/src/read/visible-where.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`
  - ⚙️ **THE COVERAGE OF `LIVE_TICKET_WHERE` IS NOW A TYPE-ENUMERATED BEHAVIOURAL INVARIANT, in two arms, mutation-witnessed 14 times** (owner approved the shape, 2026-09-20). The sentence above ends by admitting the hole — *the filter has NO choke point … so the constant exists to make the coverage greppable* — and greppable is all it was: `#591`'s own reviewer measured that un-filtering a site left the whole suite green. **Arm 1** (`apps/server/test/integration/soft-delete-client-surface.test.ts`) walks `keyof DataSourceQuery` against the real client through the real door, and its completeness comes from the **type system**: `absenceTripwire` copies `paginationTripwire`'s device, so a sixteenth read method fails `tsc` with **TS2322** rather than being silently omitted — MEASURED, and the two implementers redden beside it. Three `Record<AbsenceDecision, …>` tables make a new member a compile error in all three at once (visit it, decide it, and state whether the marker can reach it at all), and a **positive control runs in the same file before the delete**, because a reader that returned nothing satisfies every absence claim. **`getTicketHistory` is declared RETAINED on the record, not skipped** — the FAQ's *"its history is retained"* is the rule, and asserting absence there would assert a lie. **Arm 2** (`apps/server/test/read/soft-delete-background-paths.test.ts`) covers the six sites a client query **structurally cannot reach** — the auto-close and notification sweeps, `readStateOccupancy`, `readTypePopulation`, `ticketVisibleProjectIds` — and ⚠ **nothing gives that arm completeness, which the file states**: it carries the published table of all **fourteen** sites carrying the rule, which arm constrains each, and which are constrained by neither. Every case pairs its tombstone with a LIVE twin differing in exactly one respect. ⛔ **One measured LEAK is recorded here rather than fixed, with a named owner: a soft-deleted ticket's ATTACHMENTS are still served while the ticket itself 404s** (measured in one run: detail `404`, attachments `200` carrying the file; before the delete the same pair answered `200/200`). The attachments **list**, **upload** and **download** routes guard on `loadTicketRow` + `canSee` (or `canSee` alone) and **not** on `deleted`, and `canSee` does not mean "not deleted"; the sibling detail route carries the third guard they lack. It is **not one of the fourteen sites** — no predicate to un-filter, so no mutation can reach it — which is why it is published instead of left as an absence. **Owner: whoever next picks up the attachment surface.** **Three further applications are uncovered and MEASURED as survivors, not suspected** — the CONTESTED-claim title gate at `routes/my-work.ts:364` (89 tests green with its conjunct removed; the same command run against `my-work.ts:141` reports 18 failures, so it can fail), and the OFFLINE source's two gates at `packages/app-core/src/replica/replica-data-source.ts:947`/`:980` (27 app-core replica tests and 21 `dual-client` tests green with each removed). **The replica is a SECOND implementation of the same interface and Arm 1 walks only the online one.** Cases `16.88`–`16.90`. `apps/server/test/integration/soft-delete-client-surface.test.ts`, `apps/server/test/read/soft-delete-background-paths.test.ts`
- ✅ **The ticket editor's rich-text toolbar** (owner ruling *"all 3 yes"*, 2026-09-20) — `B i code link list quote`, the prototype's own `descTools` set (`desktop.dc.html:3670-3681`, drawn at `:1167`), with its `Markdown: **bold**, *italic*, `code`, > quote, - list, [text](url)` hint line below the field (`:1173`). **It needed no door, no migration and no schema change, and that was MEASURED rather than assumed:** `buildEditorExtensions` switches off exactly three things (`document`, `link`, `underline`) so `StarterKit` already carries bold/italic/code/bulletList/blockquote, and all six commands were checked against the INSTALLED packages — `extension-bold`/`-italic`/`-code`/`-blockquote`/`-link` at 3.30.5 and `toggleBulletList` in `@tiptap/core` 3.30.5. **The prototype's buttons INSERT markdown around a `<textarea>` selection; these TOGGLE a mark in ProseMirror JSON**, which is why this is a component and not a transcription — the set of six is the prototype's and the mechanics are the editor's own chain commands. **`link` is the one control that needs input and gets it inline**, because `toggleLink` called with nothing leaves an anchor with no destination: the button reveals a URL field and an empty box is a CANCEL rather than a link to the empty string; on a selection that is already a link it removes the mark without asking. Active marks are REPORTED (`aria-pressed`, via the `ImageWrapMenu` transaction-repaint pattern) — not the prototype's doing, since its controls are inserters with no state to report, but a toggle that does not say what it toggled is a control the reader guesses at. **A read-only composer gets neither the strip nor the hint.** ⚠ **The prototype's `--fill4` strip and `--cardBg`/`--cardShadow` buttons are NOT transcribed and cannot be**: `packages/editor`'s stylesheet is structural-only by its own header (it does not depend on `packages/ui`, so no token is reachable) and every control there is drawn with a `currentColor` edge. Case `16.85` — **ten cases, and EIGHT of them click a control and read the serialized document** rather than assert that a control is on screen, because a button that rendered and did nothing would pass every "exists" assertion. (The other two read the strip itself: the control SET and ORDER, and its absence on a read-only composer. An earlier version of this sentence said "not one", which was false of those two — an independent reviewer counted them.) - ✅ **The ticket sheet's inline title editor** (owner ruling *"all 3 yes"*, 2026-09-20) — the header carries the title as a borderless `<input>` (the prototype's own shape, `desktop.dc.html:1116`: 22px/600, no border, transparent) where it was a read-only `<h2>`. **A surface over a door that already existed:** `ticket.field_set` reduces `fields[fieldId]`, and `ticket.created` has written `fields: {title}` since the reducer existed, so this adds no event, no route and no migration. **NO client-side blank rule, deliberately** — the engine already refuses a spaces-only title (`checks/fields.ts:118-123`, `value.trim().length > 0`, answering `field.title-required`/`field.type-mismatch`), and a second rule here would be a second, weaker door to one field; the value travels AS TYPED and the server's own sentence is what the reader sees, in the existing `serverRefusal` slot. **An UNCHANGED title sends nothing at all** — the ledger is append-only, so re-sending the title it already has would put a line in the ticket's history saying somebody changed it to what it already said. A refusal KEEPS the draft on screen beside the answer; Escape abandons it and commits nothing. **The dialog's `aria-label` stays the SERVER's title while a draft is on screen** — a control mid-edit is not a name — which is why `Sheet` takes a `titleSlot` AND still takes the string. ⚠ **The three answer slots (`serverRefusal`, `transportError`, `editorError`) MOVED out of the details tab** to below the tab control, because the title control is in the header, outside both tabs: an answer that is invisible depending on which tab happens to be open is the failure the three-slot split exists to prevent. Cases `16.86`. `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/components/sheet.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`

The state is still on the sheet, on its state chip, and case `16.69` asserts both halves so the removal is a decision rather than a loss. ⚠ **AN ANCESTOR CRUMB IS A LINK that opens THAT ticket's sheet** (the authority's `isLink` arm), through the same optional `onOpenTicket` the `Subtask of` badge uses — with no callback it degrades to the same plain span, because *"a control that cannot be honoured"* is not drawn. The EPIC crumb is CONDITIONAL on there being one, as the authority's `if sheet.epicId` is; a RESTRICTED ancestor is NAMED rather than dropped, because dropping it would make a restricted chain render as a shorter one (invariant 5). ⚠ **The two names still travel as OPTIONAL PROPS from the host** (which computes `useWorkspaceName`/`useProjectName` at `:174-175`); the EPIC and the KEY come from the ticket read. **The nav is drawn WHOLE or not at all** — a crumb list missing its middle level states a hierarchy the product does not have. A withheld or not-yet-loaded name draws NO crumb rather than the id. - ✅ **The job's comments, and the box that writes them** (executor 2's comment-thread dispatch, 2026-09-20) — the details tab now ends with `Comments · {count}`, a thread of who wrote what and when, and a composer under it, drawing `desktop.dc.html:1268-1330`. **This is the read `comment.added` has never had:** the event was fully wired (vocabulary, reducer, mention fan-out, sync, a `require-comment` seed rule) and its text reached only `proj_search.comments` — a CONCATENATED TEXT column built for full-text search, with no author, no time and no per-comment identity, which cannot serve a thread and is not press-ganged into one. `GET /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/tickets/:ticketId/comments` serves it over a new `proj_ticket_comments` (one row per comment, ordered by `workspace_seq`, the ledger's own stream position). **A comment's FILES come from its OWN DOCUMENT, never from the ticket's `attachment.added` events** — read `apps/server/src/read/ticket-comments.ts`, which carries the measurement that nothing in the ledger links an attachment to a comment; a chip therefore means *this comment's text names this attachment*, resolved against the `attachments` table scoped to the ticket, and an id that resolves to nothing is drawn AS ITSELF rather than dropped. **A comment's box is the product's first `attachment.added` producer** (`Features.md` §9 recorded that the web client uploaded bytes without ever posting the claim), and an upload is what makes an image part of a comment at all. The composer is `TicketSheet`'s own `EditorSlot` mount in `mode="comment"` — the same seam as the description row — and it renders **no box at all** when the app has mounted no editor, because `PlainTextEditorSlot` cannot compose. `packages/app-core/src/views/comments-thread.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `apps/server/src/projections/ticket-comments.ts`, `apps/server/src/read/ticket-comments.ts`, `apps/server/src/routes/tickets.ts`, `apps/server/migrations/1790000000000_proj-ticket-comments.sql`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`- ⚙️ **Attachment list — a held file and a Drive pointer are not the same thing** — `AttachmentList` / `AttachmentListEntry`, wired into the ticket sheet's details tab through an optional `attachments` prop. An UPLOAD links into this board (`/v1/attachments/:id`); a DRIVE REFERENCE links into Drive, wears its last-seen name and the day we looked, and says only that its contents are **unknown** — there is no "reachable" state on purpose, because a link that answered a moment ago may not answer now and one that failed may be fine, so `deleted`, `missing`, `no longer`, `available` and `verified` are all absent by test. The two fixtures share a filename deliberately: the name proves nothing, and a list keyed on it would show one row where there are two. The capture date goes through the one renderer rather than an ISO slice, which would report the UTC day and disagree with every other date on the sheet for five and a half hours every night. An absent prop renders NOTHING — not a heading with nothing under it, and no promise that there are no attachments. Plan 6 Open question 13 says `packages/editor` re-declares this union so the editor never depends on app-core; **correction (Plan 7 Task 16): it now does** — `AttachmentListEntry`, `packages/editor/src/components/AttachmentListItem.tsx:50`, landed by Task 12b — and Plan 7's `& DriveReference` spelling is the same six members this one inlines, both verified against the domain type in both directions (a dropped required member breaks the probe, dropping the optional `iconLink` does not), so the two texts describe one type rather than two. `packages/app-core/src/views/attachment-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`; the counterpart is `packages/editor/src/components/AttachmentListItem.tsx` — see its own §9 bullet below.
- ⚙️ **The reopen-option flow — live children under terminal parents (owner decision)** — `submitChildEvent` / `reopenAndProceed` / `reopenStateOptions` (`src/flows/guarded-child-event.ts`) with `NewTicketDialog`, `ReopenDialog` and the wired `CreateTicketFlow`. A `hierarchy.parent-terminal` refusal is not an error: it is the moment the offer appears, at the moment of refusal, never silently applied. On acceptance the parent's `ticket.transitioned` — carrying `cause: {kind:'reopened-for-child', childTicketId}` and the SHIPPED destination spelling `to` — is ingested in its own awaited call BEFORE the child's, because the parent must be non-terminal by the time the child claim is evaluated; the child is the SAME envelope that was refused, since an interactive refusal records nothing. **The parent workflow's own transition rules still apply to the reopen:** its refusal surfaces verbatim and the child is never sent, which is what keeps the flow from being a permission escalation wearing a convenience's name. State options are STRUCTURAL only — non-terminal targets with a declared edge, rule-bearing ones included, so the server and not this client decides them. A refusal wears the name of whoever made it: `refusalOrigin` renders the instant pre-check as `instant check — ` (and submits nothing at all on that path) and only an answer that came back through `ingest` as `Refused by the server — `. A post-reopen child refusal reports both truths and compensates nothing away — the reopen is recorded history. **Known gap:** CLOSED by ruling P25 clause 1 — the offer needs the parent's state and workflow from `getTicket`, which REFUSED against the shipped server (`ServingGapError`) and now answers, so the flow builds the offer online. The refusal PATH is still pinned by 3.80 against a stubbed refusal, because what it guards is the flow's behaviour when a read it needs does not answer, whatever the cause, so online the flow renders the refusal and says the offer could not be built rather than guessing — case 3.80. **Ruling 37 (backlog/closed spec §2, 2026-09-17, Task 9): on a CLOSED parent — not merely terminal — the accepted reopen ingests `ticket.reopened` (empty payload) and the transition together, reopen FIRST, in ONE `source.ingest` batch call, before the child ever goes out; a merely-terminal (not closed) parent's batch stays the single-element array every case before this task exercised.** `ReopenPlan` gained `parentClosed: boolean`, set by `CreateTicketFlow` off `parentRead.ticket.closed ?? null` (the domain flag is optional on 441 fixtures that predate it) rather than `!== null` alone, which would misread an unflagged legacy ticket as closed. Results are read back in the batch's own order; `parentResult` in a committed outcome is always the TRANSITION's — the last element — never the reopen's. Cases 13.593a–c (`test/flows/guarded-child-event.test.ts`), 13.593 (`test/views/reopen-flow.test.tsx`).
- ⚙️ **Online HTTP transport** — `createHttpClient(baseUrl, fetchImpl)` (`packages/app-core/src/online/http.ts`), the injected-`fetch` JSON transport behind the online `DataSource`. `credentials: 'include'` carries the better-auth session cookie; **404 answers `null` on a GET, and only on a GET** — every `/v1` READ route answers one frozen body for "no such thing" and "not yours" alike, so a client that distinguished them would be inventing a distinction the server refused to make, while on a POST or a PUT the same status is a refusal and answering `null` there let `putPreferences` report a save the server had declined (a member revoked mid-session); 204 answers `undefined` without parsing a body; every other non-2xx, and every non-2xx at all on a write, throws a status-carrying `HttpError`. `packages/app-core/src/online/http.ts`
- ⚙️ **Online `DataSource` — the HTTP + WebSocket implementation of amendment 10** — `createOnlineDataSource({baseUrl, streamBaseUrl?, fetchImpl?, webSocketFactory?, random?})`, exported from the `@gisik-nokma/app-core/online` subpath (the web shell consumes it. **The "nothing under `apps/web/app/`" clause this bullet carried after Task 14 is no longer true and is corrected rather than deleted**, because the distinction it was drawing still matters: the TRANSPORT is still constructed at one composition root — `grep -rn "app-core/online" apps/web/app apps/web/src apps/web/test apps/web/e2e --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx"` returns 8 lines, of which `src/providers.tsx` is the only one that CALLS a `create*` function; the three page hits (`app/workspaces`, `app/invite/[token]`, `app/w/[workspaceId]/members`) import error classes and types only, so a page can name a refusal without knowing a URL. Nothing under `packages/app-core/src/views/` may import this subpath at all, enforced separately by a scanner.) It ingests through `POST /v1/events` with the contract's 1..100 batch cap enforced client-side, subscribes through Task 8's `createStreamSubscriber` at the STREAM origin, and answers **all thirteen** (⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this read "twelve of thirteen", and the enumeration that followed stopped short of `search` — whose serving, on 2026-09-19, is exactly what emptied the register, the entry above. NO method refuses now. The list is kept as the order they joined.** `getTickets` and `getTicket` joined the seven when P25 clause 1 closed their eight absences; `getMyWorkspaces` with P32/P38; `getMyWork` and `getAttention` with P39/P40 — `getMyWork` fetching the workspace roster beside its own body for `stateProvenance`, and `getAttention` joining `/v1/contests?open=true` for its fourth item kind) `DataSourceQuery` methods: `getMembers`, `getTicketHistory`, `getWorkflowDef`, `getPreferences`, `putPreferences`, `getBoard` and `getTicketTypes`. `getTicketTypes` re-raises its 404 as `HttpError` rather than answering `[]`: this route's 404 is `door(..., 'members-only')`, and `[]` would say *this workspace defines no ticket types* — a claim no real workspace can make, since Plan 4's bootstrap materialises the four built-ins as real `ticket-type.defined` events at creation. `getBoard` regroups the server's flat `columns[]` by `(workflowId, workflowVersion)` — the key `views/board.tsx` renders by, so a straggler column never joins a published definition's states — and joins the workspace roster off the board body's own `workspaceId` to name assignees, a client-side join over two shipped routes rather than an invented fact. Three signatures grew a `workspaceId` (ruling P25 clause 2): `getMyWork`, `getPreferences`, `putPreferences`, whose routes read it before the door. `getPreferences` re-raises a 404 as `HttpError` for the same reason `getBoard` does and one more: the server's answer for a person who has never saved anything is a 200 carrying the EMPTY document, so folding the 404 into defaults would show a revoked member stock columns and no saved filters and give them no way to tell that from their own settings. `packages/app-core/src/online/online-data-source.ts`
- ⚙️ **The one adaptation layer, and its refusal register — now EMPTY (six until ruling P25 clause 1, four until P32/P38, one since P39 and P40, and ZERO since 2026-09-19)** — ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this headline read "its ONE remaining REFUSAL (…one since P39 and P40)" and stopped at one. The register has carried no key at all since the owner ruled, on 2026-09-19, that the search QUERY must derive `matchedIn`; the test pins the emptiness.** — `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`, the only module that knows a server spelling. It carries three error classes with three owners: `MappingError` (the response is not the shape this client can read), `ServingGapError` (the response is right and the view type still cannot be filled honestly), and `UnexpressibleFilterError` (a REQUEST the shipped query language cannot say — a two-assignee filter against a singular `filterSpecSchema`). **NO method refuses rather than returning a plausible view** — ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20, and the withdrawal is the informative half. This read: "ONE method refuses rather than returning a plausible view: `search` — and it is the only DATA gap of the six, the one no join can close, because `routes/search.ts` destroys which column matched inside its own query." That "no join can close" is exactly what the last ruling overturned: `proj_search` had stored the three columns separately, and weighted them apart, all along — so the remedy was the QUERY rather than a missing capability.** `getMyWorkspaces` ANSWERS since rulings P32/P38; **`getMyWork` and `getAttention` ANSWER since rulings P39 and P40**, and their absence lists were DELETED rather than emptied (an `Absence[]` with no entries is a refusal nobody can read). `getMyWork` refused over three facts and none was a missing capability: `WorkItem.stateName` was a read the route could always have made (`PROJ_TICKET_COLUMNS` has selected `t.workflow_id, t.workflow_version` since it was written — the claim in `read-models.ts` that a My Work card "names no workflow version" was true of the CARD and false of the ROUTE, and is corrected in place there); `ContestedClaim.title` was one join off a table that already stores `ticket_id`; and `ContestedClaim.claim.serverTime` was closed by DELETING the field, since a refused claim was never committed and never stamped. `getAttention` refused over one field, the contested kind's title, and closes on the same join. Its projects list is served by `read/visible-projects.ts`, which applies `resolveEffectiveRole`: owner sees every project (the shipped `canSeeResolved` switch exempts owner by name), every other role sees only `eff.projects`. **A partial workspace is the CORRECT answer, not a defect** — a project's NAME is itself a disclosure and is withheld for any project the viewer is not in, while its ID may already be known through the ticket surface. `WorkItem.projectName` follows the same rule one door over and is `string | null`: an item whose project is unnamed for this viewer renders id-only. **Task 16 added three OPTIONAL counts to that mapper** — `memberCount` on the workspace, `ticketCount`/`blockedCount` on each project — read through `withheldOrNumber`, which maps an ABSENT key to `null` (withheld) and refuses a key that is present and not a number. There is deliberately no `?? 0`: defaulting a withholding to zero would convert it into the assertion "this project holds no work", silently and in a shape no renderer can detect. `archived` is REQUIRED on each project, because it is served to every caller who is told the project's name at all. **`getTickets` PAGES TO EXHAUSTION since ruling P41 (Door B)** — `mapListPage` maps ONE page and carries the server's `nextCursor` out, and `getTickets` follows it until the server says there is no more, assembling the complete `TicketListView`. It used to REFUSE any non-null cursor, which was honest and left the method unusable past 50 tickets; the refusal moved rather than vanished. The view type is UNCHANGED and that is the point: `TicketListView` means *the complete row set*, `views/list-model.ts` licenses client-side grouping on exactly that promise, and `test/views/list-model.test.ts` enforces it as a TYPE guard on `keyof TicketListView` — a cursor on the view would have deleted that guard and inverted the grouping decision in one commit, while leaving nothing able to act on the cursor, since `DataSourceQuery.getTickets` takes `(projectId, filter, grouping)` and has nowhere to put one (P30 holds; `data-source.ts` is not widened). The client SENDS `limit=200` — the route's validated `MAX_LIMIT` — so a large project costs a quarter of the round trips it would at the server's default, and the bound below is the client's OWN arithmetic rather than a coupling to a non-exported constant in another package. The loop is BOUNDED at `LIST_ROW_CAP` (2000 rows = `LIST_PAGE_CAP` 10 × `LIST_PAGE_SIZE` 200, the page cap DERIVED so the two cannot drift) and the bound REFUSES with `LIST_TOO_LARGE_ABSENCE` rather than returning what it has — a truncated set served as a project is the original defect one bound further out. **The cap is a disclosed assumption, not a measurement**, and both the constant's docblock and the absence's `owner` say so. The replica source is untouched: it reads the local confirmed set directly and has always answered the whole project. **The assembled view is NOT a snapshot and the loop says so** — ten requests are ten transactions, so a ticket made visible mid-walk below the passed cursor is absent until the next reload; disclosed at the loop rather than deduced, with an owner. A mid-loop 404 (access revoked between pages) is raised as `HttpError(404)` rather than reaching the mapper as "expected an object", the same conversion `getBoard` makes. **`getTickets` and `getTicket` refused until ruling P25 clause 1 and now answer** — their eight absences (`ListRow.typeRank`, `.epicId`, `.stateName`, `.assignedProvenance`, `.hasRestrictedParent`; `TicketRead.stateProvenance`, `.ticket`, `.title`) were served, not deleted, and the register's tests pin them as SERVED so a closed gap leaves a trace rather than a shorter array. Each refusal ENUMERATES the absent facts as data (`{field, wireKey, wireRoute, because, owner}`, exported as `SERVING_GAPS`), never as "not fully implemented", and every absence names who closes it and WHICH `/v1` body the server-side cross-check measures its key against — the contested absences named `GET /v1/contests` rather than the my-work and attention routes they used to blame, and that route grew the `title` and `projectId` they were waiting on. **The attention strip's fourth kind is now that client-side join**; My Work's contested claims are NOT, because "my claims" is viewer-scoped and only the server knows the viewer — ruling P40 put them on `/v1/me/work`'s own body as the additive key its docblock had reserved. `mapRollup` refuses a nonzero `uncategorizedChildren` (unreachable in production, and `RollupToViewer` has no room to admit an incomplete count) while an unnamed assignee degrades to `{id, name: id}` (reachable today — a guest is refused the roster route whole): refuse what cannot happen, degrade what can. Every OTHER substitution is gone — `blocked`, `cards`, a transition `cause` of an unknown kind and the three preferences containers are all REQUIRED on the wire, so each now throws `MappingError` naming its path instead of rendering "not blocked", "this state is empty", "no cause" or "you saved nothing". **The preferences pair is total in both directions (ruling P24):** `mapPreferences` reports a project half the server does not send as `{origin: 'default'}` rather than filling it with a stock value, and `toServerPreferences` contributes no key for such a half — so a grouping chosen alone is stored alone, a stock value is never PUT under the user's name, and a half-set project round-trips as half-set. `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`
- ⚙️ **Amendment 10 enforced, not asserted** — a scanner over the checked-in `packages/app-core/src` proving nothing outside `src/online/` imports the online module or touches `fetch`, `WebSocket`, `XMLHttpRequest` or `EventSource`. The import check reads all four specifier spellings — `from`, a bare `import`, `await import(...)` and `require(...)` — because anchored on `from` alone it answered "clean" for the two call forms, measurably, in the same run the `fetch` control reported a hit; the anchor stays (widened, not dropped) because this scan reads raw lines, comments included, and would otherwise report prose quoting the path as an import — measured as zero such lines outside `src/online/` today, so that half is a guard against the comment nobody has written yet rather than one that exists, and it is written down at that strength. It reports the FILES it walked beside the hits, so a clean tree can be told apart from a scan pointed at the wrong directory, and it is proven against planted defects before it is trusted. `packages/app-core/test/helpers/http-scan.ts`, `packages/app-core/test/no-http-in-views.test.ts`
- ✅ **`apps/web` — the Next.js 15 App Router shell** — the running web client, and the deliverable the rest of Phase 3 was blocked on. Routes: `/` (redirect to `/workspaces`), `/sign-in`, `/workspaces`, `/w/[workspaceId]/my-work`, `/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]` (Board | List tabs, create flow beside them, ticket sheet via `?ticket=`; **the attention strip was removed from this route on 2026-09-18** — owner: *"the attention section should not be there in the project page. its already there in the my work page"* — and lives on `/w/[workspaceId]/my-work` alone, cases 13.881/13.882). Both themes ship: `themeStylesheet()` is inlined in `<head>` and a pre-paint script re-applies a stored explicit choice, so an OS-preference default never flashes past a user's own decision. The root layout imports `@gisik-nokma/ui/tokens`, NOT the package root — the root is a barrel carrying `Dialog` and `Sheet`, both of which call `useEffect`, and reaching it from a server component fails the whole build; the subpath gives a server context the colours without the components. **Style is a third runtime axis beside theme (Task 14, 2026-09-09): `StyleToggle` lets a user pick HIG, Minimal or Neu (`data-style` values `hig`/`minimal`/`neo`), persisted under `localStorage` key `gn-style` the same way `ThemeToggle` persists `gn-theme`, applied pre-paint by `layout.tsx`'s `styleInitScript`.** Unlike theme, style has no "system" state — **`neo` is the default** (cases 13.344–13.346) applied whenever nothing is stored, not the absence of a choice. **MOVED, owner request (2026-09-09): `StyleToggle` no longer rides in `AppNav` beside `ThemeToggle` — it lives on `/account` now, as a `<select>` rather than `SegmentedControl` (a settled preference belongs on the settings page, not the persistent nav on every screen). `ThemeToggle` is unaffected and stays in `AppNav`.** **Migrated to the shared `Select` component (Phase 2 Wave A, 2026-09-14): `StyleToggle` no longer renders a plain `<select>` — it renders `@gisik-nokma/ui`'s `Select` (the same component `members/page.tsx`'s Channel/Role fields use), a non-searchable single-select whose trigger exposes `role="button"` (not `role="combobox"`) and opens a portal-rendered `role="listbox"` of 3 `role="option"` entries on click; selection is conveyed by a checkmark plus the trigger's own visible label text, not `aria-selected` on sibling elements. `apps/web/e2e-stack/viewport.spec.ts`'s dated comment carries the full accessible-role history across all three identity changes (`SegmentedControl` → `<select>` → `Select`).** **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-15 — this control is back on `SegmentedControl`, a FOURTH identity** (`SegmentedControl` → `<select>` → `Select` → `SegmentedControl`), on an owner reference image for the Appearance card showing all three styles visible at once with the chosen one raised. The Wave A rollout's actual invariant — that no native `<select>` remains in scope — is untouched: this is a segmented picker, not a native select. What changed is that `StyleToggle` is no longer a `Select` CALL SITE, so the paragraph above describes history for this one control (it still describes `members/page.tsx`'s fields, which are unchanged). Its labels also changed with the reference, from `HIG`/`Minimal`/`Neu` to **`Apple HIG`/`Minimal`/`Neomorphic`**, and it gained a line beneath it describing the selected style. `ThemeToggle` became a `SegmentedControl` in the same change, replacing its icon-only cycle button — and **kept three segments (`System`/`Light`/`Dark`) where the reference image shows only two**, because `system` is the default AND the only route back to the device's own preference once a choice is made; a two-segment control could neither express the default state nor return to it. **The accessible-name ratchet is unaffected, checked rather than assumed:** `apps/web/e2e-viewport/accessible-names-baseline.json` records only signed-OUT routes (`/help`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, a 404 probe) and so contains no `/account` entry, `apps/web/e2e-stack/accessible-names-baseline.json` is `{}` (that suite has never been executed), and the assertion is `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual` (`viewport.spec.ts:663`) — a floor (**CORRECTED 2026-09-18: read that `{}` as history, not as the current tree. The suite HAS since been executed — `5aab0a90` seeded this file from run B and proved it asserts in run C, negative control included — so it now carries real per-route floors (`stack-phone-390|/p/:projectId` `ariaLabel: 66` among them), and the assertion has drifted to `viewport.spec.ts:673`. This matters beyond bookkeeping: a floor that is live can FALL, and case 13.881's removal of the attention strip from `/p/:projectId` is expected to take that 66 to 65 — recorded in `test-cases/13` with its owner rather than hand-corrected here.**) — while this change RAISES `role` and `ariaSelected` and holds `ariaLabel` LEVEL. Counted live on `/account` rather than reasoned: the two Appearance controls carry exactly two `aria-label`s (`Style`, `Theme`), replacing the two the `Select` trigger and the cycle button carried, so that attribute neither rises nor falls; the route's own total is **6** (the other four are `AppNav`'s sign-out and help controls plus the workspace row's link and avatar), `[role]` totals 8 and `[aria-selected]` 6 where both were lower before. **Not a substitute for running that suite**, which needs the full stack and could not be executed here — this is why the floor cannot fall, not a green from the spec itself.** `apps/web/app/layout.tsx`, `apps/web/src/theme-toggle.tsx`, `apps/web/src/style-toggle.tsx`, `apps/web/app/account/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `apps/web/test/style-toggle.test.tsx`, `apps/web/e2e-stack/viewport.spec.ts`
- ✅ **Passwordless sign-in — only the methods that can work, and nothing password-shaped** — magic link unconditionally; phone OTP (two steps: request, then verify) only where an SMS channel exists; Google and Apple only where configured; **passkey by an explicit button, restored by the account/help-screens plan's Task 8 now that `/account` can register one** (it was absent while no registration flow existed, because nobody could hold a credential to sign in with). **The title of this bullet used to say "all four methods", and each round since has removed one that could not work for anybody — that phrase was a promise the product did not keep, three times over.** a `?next=` parameter carried into every callback. `src/auth-client.ts` is matched to the version the SERVER pins, not to the plan text: at `better-auth@1.7.2` the client plugin module exports `phoneNumberClient` and `magicLinkClient` but NOT `passkeyClient`, which lives in `@better-auth/passkey/client` — the client half of the same package `apps/server/src/auth/auth.ts` imports `passkey` from. The suite mocks the auth client wholesale, so it cannot fail on a member name; **`typecheck` is what checks those**, and it does — dropping `passkeyClient()` answers `Property 'passkey' does not exist` at the call site while the suite stays green. **HIG surface (2026-09-08):** page header with icon tile and tagline ("The keeper of memory"), `SegmentedControl` for the method (replacing the old `.gn-view-tabs` markup, same `tablist`/`tab`/`aria-selected` roles), filled `.gn-field` inputs, fill-and-accent alternatives, helper text on the email method only ("It expires in 15 minutes."), Terms/Privacy footer. All four methods retained; no password method (forbidden, `auth.ts:84`). Cases 13.292–13.294. **Item 2 of the same round — the page offers only the methods that can WORK.** Two of the four visible options could not succeed for anybody: Google rendered unconditionally while `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` was unset, and passkey has **no registration flow anywhere in app code**, so nobody can hold one to sign in with. `GET /v1/auth/methods` answers from `auth.options.socialProviders` — the constructed better-auth instance — so a social button renders only for a provider this server will accept, and no build flag can drift from it. Google and Apple are icon-only logo marks whose `aria-label` carries the exact shipped wording (`Continue with Google` / `Continue with Apple`), so the accessible name survives the restyle; Google's four-colour mark is an asset under `apps/web/public` because the colour-literal gate scans TSX and a `fill="#…"` attribute is a string literal to it (measured with the gate's own `findColourLiterals`), while Apple's is inline with `currentColor` so it follows the theme, which a fixed-colour file could not. The dead passkey BUTTON was removed here and the email field kept `autocomplete="username webauthn"`, while the conditional-mediation REQUEST was deferred **on a measurement**: `signInPasskey` issues `GET /passkey/generate-authenticate-options` as its first statement, charged against the shared `auth:` bucket (10/60s in production), and review demonstrated ten such probes making the eleventh real magic-link POST answer 429 against a control that answered 500 — with the client key measurably collapsing to one per web container. **ACCOUNT/HELP-SCREENS PLAN, TASK 8 — STATE OF THE FEATURE, PLAINLY: the passkey entry point is BACK, as an explicit "Sign in with a passkey" BUTTON, and deliberately NOT as conditional mediation.** Registration now exists on `/account`, so the offer has a population it can serve; the measured 429 above is exactly why the call is reachable from one `onClick` and from nowhere else — no effect, no render path, and `autoFill` passed **`false` explicitly** rather than left to a library default. The rule it keeps is that **an anonymous `/sign-in` load charges the `auth:` bucket ZERO times**, which a click-triggered call satisfies unarguably. That measurement no longer lives only in a review comment: `apps/server/test/http/security-middleware.test.ts` now drives the ten probes through the assembled app with a zero-probe control on the identical path, and pins that the probe charges the general `auth:` bucket and never the tighter `signin:` one (`/passkey/*` is absent from `SIGN_IN_PREFIXES`) — mutation-checked in both directions. Case 13.326 pins the client half: not called on mount, called once with `{ autoFill: false }` on click. `docs/manual.md` and `docs/faq.md` needed **no edits** — they have described this exact button in these exact words the whole time, and `pnpm check:help` passes unchanged, so the copy became accurate again rather than being rewritten. **Not witnessed end-to-end:** `apps/web/e2e-stack/passkey-registration.spec.ts` (register over a CDP virtual authenticator, then sign in with it) is written and typechecked but was never RUN — the stack port was occupied — so its first green is owed to CI. Apple ships env-gated and INERT until credentials exist, with its client secret a minted ES256 JWT that expires. Cases 13.324–13.326; 13.292 and 3.84 amended in the same change set. **Round 2c (owner, 2026-09-08) — the Phone tab was the third dead control, and it was the one the screen OPENED ON.** `selectOtpSender()` returns a `NullOtpSender` that refuses every code with 501 `PHONE_SIGN_IN_UNAVAILABLE`, so the route now also answers `phone`, derived from **the sender the running auth instance holds** rather than any env flag: `createAuth` carries it on the instance it returns and the route reads `auth.otpSender`, which is the same object `sendOTP` awaits. Threading it through `AppDeps` instead would have been the two-call-site shape `routes/auth-methods.ts` argues against in its own docblock — nothing stops a future caller feeding `createAuth` and `buildApp` different senders, which is precisely the arrangement where the page offers phone and the server refuses. On the page `method` is **derived, not stored** (`phoneAvailable ? (chosen ?? 'phone') : 'email'`): the stored-state shape has a window in which `method` is `'phone'` with no Phone tab rendered, leaving a card with no form in it and no tab to click back, and deriving makes that unreachable for any ordering rather than merely unlikely. With phone unavailable there is **no segmented control at all** — a one-option control asks the user to make a decision already made — which drops the `tab` roles and is why the `/sign-in` accessible-names baseline falls in this change: a dead control leaving, not a ratchet going slack. The in-app manual and FAQ described the dead tab and told users to sign in "or a passkey"; both passages are rewritten and `content.generated.ts` regenerated, leaving Task 12's two other passkey passages untouched. Cases 13.334–13.336; 13.292 and 3.84 amended AGAIN, and 3.84's lost sentence — *"Phone and email need no provider and are unconditional"* — is the finding. **Owner feedback round (2026-09-08):** the sheet is centred in the viewport — `.gn-shell--centred` on the sign-in `<main>` only, never on `.gn-shell` globally, because every other screen scrolls; `min-height` is declared twice (`100vh` then `100dvh`) so mobile Safari centres against the viewport a reader actually sees (13.321). **Neo restyle (Task 15, 2026-09-09):** `.gn-sign-in`'s own literal `border-radius: 16px` — found while auditing the invite/workspace radius inversions above — tied `.gn-card`'s token-driven radius at identical specificity and won only because `layout.tsx` imports `globals.css` after `ui.css`, silently pinning this card at the pre-neo value through the whole style axis. Removed (case 13.355); the card now takes `.gn-card`'s own radius, 20px under neo. *(The per-screen plan's file list named this bullet as living under §6 — it does not; §6 is server core. Corrected in place, same pattern as the two prior Features.md pointer errors this plan series has hit.)* **Round 2d feedback (2026-09-09):** the retired accent hex reaches this screen through `textAccent` (footer `Terms`/`Privacy` links, the ghost-styled social marks) rather than the now text-unsafe `accent` — see the design-tokens bullet above. The email field gains the export's `placeholder="you@example.com"` verbatim (an addition; the shipped input had none). Social marks (`.gn-sign-in__mark`) widen to `min-width:56px` (was 44px, matching the export) and, under `[data-style="neo"]` only, take a raised neumorphic-square treatment (`background:var(--gn-surface)`, `box-shadow:var(--gn-shadow-raised)`) instead of the flat ghost fill `hig`/`minimal` keep. The footer link now underlines on hover/focus-visible and carries the branded `--gn-focus-ring` outline — a deliberate departure from the export's own colour-and-opacity-only affordance, because WCAG's "identifiable by more than colour" is not satisfied by an opacity shift on the same hue. A standalone Playwright case (13.362) drives `/v1/auth/methods` to `phone:true` and witnesses the segmented control's rendered neo shadow directly, outside the signed-out accessible-names sweep so that ratchet stays unmoved (13.363). - ✅ **A refused read is rendered as the refusal** — every read in the shell checks `error` BEFORE `data`, and shows the `ServingGapError`'s own words — the field, why the shipped route does not serve it, and who closes the gap — carried without paraphrase. This is an adaptation, not the plan's text: the plan renders each read as `data === null ? <p>Loading…</p> : …` and the attention strip as `items={attention.data ?? []}`, and against the server that ships today both are false claims — **these reads are called here** (⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this read "four of the six refusing methods are called here". The count is ZERO since the register emptied on 2026-09-19, and the mechanism survives the correction rather than dying with the count — a read that FAILS, whether by a transport error, a 404 turned back into an exception or a `MappingError`, still lands here rather than in a spinner**), so the spinner never stops and `AttentionStrip` returns `null` for an empty list, which reads as "nothing needs your attention". A 401 is deliberately NOT a refusal: it means the viewer is unknown, and it redirects to `/sign-in`. **THAT SENTENCE WAS TRUE OF ONE PAGE AND WRITTEN ABOUT THREE, and is now true of all three** — the rule lived inline in `app/workspaces/page.tsx` as `message.includes('401')`, so an expired session on `/w/<id>/my-work` or `/w/<id>/p/<id>` rendered a refusal panel carrying `HTTP 401 for /v1/...`, telling a person their work *cannot be read* when the actionable truth was *sign in again*. It is now one exported predicate all three call (`src/expired-session.tsx`), ANCHORED on `HttpError`'s own `HTTP ${status} for ${path}` format rather than a substring, with a notice on screen for the frame before the router moves so the suppressed refusal does not become a spinner. It recovers the status from a MESSAGE because `LiveQueryResult.error` is a `string` and the transport's `HttpError.status` does not survive the hook; what that cannot see is named at the site. `apps/web/src/shell-refusal.tsx`, `apps/web/src/expired-session.tsx`, `apps/web/test/page-refusals.test.tsx`
