(1) `parentCandidates` was `(list.rows ?? []).map(…)` and `getTickets` REFUSED against the shipped server of the day (⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this read "REFUSES" in the present tense — ruling P25 clause 1, `c86c4b5c`, 2026-09-06, served it and the register has been empty since 2026-09-19), so the parent picker permanently offered *None (project root)* and nothing else — measured with the branch's own `stubServices({})` as `PARENT OPTIONS: ['None (project root)']` — while the Board tab on the same page rendered the very tickets it said did not exist, because `getBoard` IS served. (2) `typeOptions` hardcoded `epic`/`story`/`task`/`sub-task` as this workspace's types, so a workspace whose types differ (the server's own fixture answers `epic`, `chore`, `task`) got a picker offering four it does not have and `ticketTypeExists` refused the CREATE — a rejected write about the actor standing in for a read this build could not perform. The types now come through `getTicketTypes`, and the built-in ladder survives ONLY as the loading fallback, labelled on screen as what it is (Plan 6 Open question 7). (3) `currentUserId` was `user?.id ?? ''`, and `useCurrentUser()` answers null both while the session resolves and when signed out, so the button was offered to a signed-out viewer and a fast click in the load window posted an event with an empty actor — refused by `checkRolePermission`'s actor-identity check, which told the person about their PERMISSIONS when the truth was that this browser had not finished asking who they were. The flow is now mounted only on `useSessionStatus`'s `signed-in`, with the wait state and the signed-out state saying which they are. **That cost is PAID OFF, and the entry is corrected rather than left standing:** it used to read that `getTickets` refuses against the shipped server so the control is not offered at all and no ticket can be created from this page — *not even at project root* — with Plan 7 owning the gap. The premise was that the refusal is temporary and route-owned. It is not for the refusal a real customer meets: `LIST_TOO_LARGE_ABSENCE` is a bound this CLIENT chose and no route change clears it. Creation no longer gates on the list read (types still gate it, and that asymmetry is the point — a ticket needs a TYPE, not a PARENT); the dialog is TOLD the parent list is unavailable rather than handed an empty one, and **withholds the parent from the DRAFT and not merely from the screen**, because hiding a control does not clear the state behind it. **KNOWN AND NOT CLOSED HERE:** `TicketSheet` still builds submitted events from the same `currentUserId`, so the empty-actor window survives inside the sheet; closing it changes that component's contract. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/project-create-gate.test.tsx`
- ✅ **New tickets are born in the backlog; Start in focus** (backlog/closed spec §1.3/§5.4, 2026-09-17, Task 9) — `NewTicketDialog` states "Starts in the backlog" under the title field, unconditionally; `NewTicketDraft` gains a REQUIRED `start: 'backlog' | 'focus'` (never optional here, even though the wire's `ticketCreatedPayload.start` is optional and an absent value means focus for events written before the field existed — that compatibility rule says nothing about what a client built after it exists may omit). A checkbox **"Start in focus"** appears ONLY when the parent actually chosen in the dialog is itself in focus (`parentCandidates` carry `inFocus: boolean` beside `id`/`title`, computed by the caller — this component derives nothing): with no parent, a withheld or narrowed-away picker, or a backlog/closed parent, there is no checkbox and the ticket is born in the backlog, always. Checked by default, unticking it flips the draft to `'backlog'` without touching the parent. `apps/web/…/page.tsx`'s `parentCandidates` map computes `inFocus: row.stateId !== BACKLOG_STATE_ID && row.closed === null` off the list row it already has. `CreateTicketFlow.create` sends `start: draft.start` on every `ticket.created` it builds — ALWAYS present, never left to the wire's absence-means-focus fallback, proven by a case that asserts `Object.keys(payload)` contains `'start'` rather than only its value, since a `toEqual`-style comparison cannot tell a dropped key from one holding `undefined`. Cases 13.591a–e (`packages/app-core/test/views/new-ticket-dialog.test.tsx`), 13.592a–b (`packages/app-core/test/views/reopen-flow.test.tsx`). `packages/app-core/src/views/new-ticket-dialog.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/create-ticket-flow.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`
- ✅ **Backlog tab with Start** (backlog/closed spec §5.4/§5.5, 2026-09-17, Task 10) — a fifth tab, **Backlog**, joins the project page's segmented rail FIRST (ahead of Board — a ticket sits in the backlog before it reaches the board, spec §1.3), while `view`'s own default stays `'board'`: the rail's order and the page's default selection are independent facts. `backlogNarrowing` cuts `listNarrowing.rows` (the shell's own exclude-mode-filtered set) to rows whose `stateId` is the universal `BACKLOG_STATE_ID`; `total` counts backlog rows in the UNFILTERED `list.rows`, never the already-filtered set, for the same reason `listNarrowing`'s own `total` does. The List tab is unchanged (spec §5.5: it keeps showing everything). Each Backlog row gets a **Start** button (`BacklogStartButton`, `apps/web/src/backlog-start-button.tsx`) submitting `ticket.transitioned { to: initialStateId }` directly through `dataSource.ingest` — **Ruling 41** struck the brief's "a gated initial edge still asks" branch: the universal Start edge (`backlog → def.initialStateId`) "carries no rules, ever" (spec §1.2; `universalTransitions`, `packages/domain/src/workflow.ts:71`, builds it with `rules: []`), so no page-level `TransitionDialog` and no `MoveArrows` extraction exist for it. Mirrors `board.tsx`'s `submitDirect` idiom exactly: a `submitting` latch reset in a `finally`, a `committed` answer calling `list.reload()` (the row leaves the tab on the next read), a real refusal rendered `Refused by the server — <rule>: <reason>`, silence or a transport failure rendered as a separate, never-"Refused" sentence. **Ruling 42** resolves the Start target: `ListRow` carries no `workflowId` of its own, so `startTargetFor(row, types, defs)` (`apps/web/src/backlog-start.ts`) joins `row.typeId` through the workspace's `TicketTypeDef[]` to a `workflowId`, then reads that `WorkflowDef`'s `initialStateId` from a `Map` the page fetches ONE `getWorkflowDef` per DISTINCT workflow among the backlog rows on screen (never one per row); `null` (unknown type, or a definition not yet arrived) disables Start rather than submitting `{to: null}`. The whole `rowAction` capability is gated on `session.kind === 'signed-in'` (mirroring the board's own `moves` gate, case 3.144), since `BacklogStartButton` needs a real `currentUserId` for `makeSubmittedFactory` — an addition beyond Ruling 42's own literal prop list, named as such. `TicketList` (`packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`) gains the two seams this uses, neither exclusive to Backlog: `rowAction?: (row: ListRow) => ReactNode` (a trailing table column, present only when a caller supplies one) and `emptyCopy?: string`, which defaults to ABSENCE — before this task `TicketList` rendered nothing at all for a zero-row set, and a component-owned default sentence was refused because `rows.length === 0` is ambiguous between "the project has none" and "a filter hid them all"; only the Backlog tab passes one ("Nothing in the backlog — new tickets land here."). A second `<Chip tone="neutral">Closed</Chip>` renders beside the existing state chip when `row.closed !== null`. `.gn-row-action` (`packages/ui/src/ui.css`) is a byte-identical alias of `.gn-board-card__close`, declared separately because this button sits in a table row, not a board card. Cases 13.594–13.597 (`apps/web/test/backlog-tab.test.tsx`, `apps/web/test/backlog-start.test.ts`, `packages/app-core/test/views/ticket-list.test.tsx`). **AN EPIC IS VISIBLE AS A NAME AND ABSENT AS A TICKET HERE (owner, 2026-09-20, answering the controller's own question about this tab: *"it shouldnt show up in the backlog either. only in the list view."* → *"Row only."*).** `backlogNarrowing` drops rank-1 rows from `rows` AND from `total` — the same two-term argument the Board's fold states — while the SECTION HEADERS still name the epic, because a section's label comes from the CHILD's `epicId` through `labels.epicTitle` (which reads `list.rows` unnarrowed), never from the epic's own row. ⚠ That header exists only under the EPIC grouping — the default, `DEFAULT_GROUPING` — and under a stored status/assignee/type/flat grouping there is none, as there was none before. With the row gone the header is the only thing naming the group, and the owner was offered a clickable header and did not take it. One consequence: the epic row was its children's parent, so `nestRows` roots them at depth 0 and they move 22px left — the honest drawing of "the parent row is not here". **AND THE TAB'S EMPTY SENTENCE SPLITS IN TWO (owner ruling, 2026-09-20):** an all-epic backlog now reads *"Nothing in the backlog — epics appear in the List."*, while a backlog holding nothing keeps the original *"Nothing in the backlog — new tickets land here."* — which was a claim about the backlog SET, and an epic is literally a member of it (`stateId === BACKLOG_STATE_ID` is the field this fold reads), so the old sentence denied the existence of something in the set it named. The choice is the PAGE's, not `TicketList`'s: the component is handed only the surviving rows and cannot tell the two empties apart. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/backlog-start.ts`, `apps/web/src/backlog-start-button.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ✅ **The project/board page's desktop-prototype-port begins — page chrome (Task 2, 2026-09-16), `/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]`** — restyles the page's existing chrome rather than rebuilding it; `role="tablist"`/`role="tab"`/`aria-selected` are unchanged throughout (Task 1 of this port was STRUCK — no new token roles — so this is the port's first landed code). **The tab rail becomes the shared segmented control**: the container's class moves from the page-local `.gn-view-tabs` to `.gn-segmented`, and each tab gains `.gn-segmented__item` plus `.gn-segmented__item--on` when selected, driven from one boolean per tab rather than two comparisons kept in agreement by hand — the same classes `/sign-in`, `/account` and `/help` already share. `.gn-view-tabs` now survives nowhere in the product (`apps/web/app/globals.css`'s own docblock had already recorded it as surviving "only on the project page"); its three now-dead rules are left in place, out of this task's file scope, and reported rather than silently removed. **The New-ticket control moves out of the tablist into a new `.gn-project__header` row** — it used to render as the tablist's fifth child, a plain `<button>` (or a `<p role="status">` while gated) inside a `role="tablist"` beside four `role="tab"` children, an ARIA defect the segmented track's 2px gaps would only have made more visible; `.gn-project__header` is the container later tasks add the search box, the workflow `<select>` and the Filters button to. `.gn-project` itself is consumed as the page's content wrapper, nested inside the existing `.gn-shell__main` rather than merged onto it, so the two files' rules do not cascade-order against each other. Case 13.430. **Task 2 fix round 1, same day (coordinator Rulings 5 and 6) — both findings this bullet originally flagged are now closed.** Ruling 5: `.gn-project` DROPS its horizontal padding entirely — the original literal `padding: 0 2rem 1.5rem` stacked on top of `.gn-shell`'s own `padding: 0 1.25rem 3rem`, landing this page's content at 52px from the viewport edge (20px + 32px) instead of the prototype's own 32px; `.gn-workspaces` (`globals.css:210`) already answers this exact shape for a sibling page — keep only what `.gn-shell` does not already provide — and `.gn-project` now follows it — **round 1 kept the rule's bottom padding, which fix round 2's Ruling 8 corrects as wrong: see below, `.gn-project` now sets no padding on either axis.** Ruling 6: **`.gn-project__breadcrumb` and `.gn-project__title` are no longer unconsumed.** `ProjectRef.name` already travels on `WorkspaceRef.projects`, served by the SAME `getMyWorkspaces()` read this page's own `<AppNav>` already makes for its nav link — no new endpoint. The new `apps/web/src/use-project-name.ts` mirrors `use-workspace-name.ts`'s shape and its "omission, never the id" contract exactly, but deliberately carries NO module-scoped name cache: `AppNav` remounts on every client-side navigation (Round 26's reason for caching there), while this hook mounts once per page load, so a cache would buy nothing here — stated as a decision in the hook's own docblock. The breadcrumb and title each render independently, omitted (not the id, not a placeholder) while their own hook holds `null`, and neither reserves layout space while absent. `.gn-project__summary` is UNCHANGED: still declared, still unconsumed, left for the task that adds the `N of M shown` count. Cases 13.431–13.432.

**Task 2 fix round 2, same day — one Critical restructuring, one amendment to round 1's own Ruling 5, one test-isolation fix.** Critical (coordinator review): round 1 had rendered the breadcrumb and title as children of `.gn-project__header` alongside `createRegion` — but `.gn-project__header` is `flex; align-items: center; flex-wrap: wrap`, a single line, so the 34px/700 title sat vertically centred beside the 13px breadcrumb and the New-ticket button, and Tasks 4/5/8's search box, workflow `<select>` and Filters button would each have landed on that same line too. The metrics table's separate entries ("Breadcrumb 13/18", "Title 34/41", "Header row top margin 24px") describe three STACKED children of `.gn-project`'s own column, not one flex line. Restructured: the breadcrumb `<p>` and the `<h1>` are now direct children of `.gn-project` itself, spaced by its own 14px `gap`; `.gn-project__header` holds only the controls (`createRegion` alone, for now) and keeps its `margin-top: 1.5rem`. The ARIA fix from Task 2 proper — `createRegion` out of the `role="tablist"` — is unchanged by this move. **Ruling 8 (amending round 1's Ruling 5):** round 1 told `.gn-project` to keep its bottom padding, reasoning `.gn-shell`'s bottom padding "does not double as this row's tighter one" — wrong, and corrected here: `.gn-shell` already supplies 3rem (48px) of bottom padding, so `.gn-project`'s own 1.5rem (24px) stacked to 72px against the prototype's 24px alone, the identical additive-stacking defect Ruling 5 existed to remove, just on the other axis. `.gn-workspaces`, round 1's own cited precedent, adds no bottom padding at all — `.gn-project` now matches it exactly: no `padding` property on either axis. The residual gap is recorded on both axes now (20px-vs-32px sides, 48px-vs-24px bottom), not fixed, for the same reason as before — closing either would move `.gn-shell` and shift every route in the product. **Test-isolation fix:** `project-page-chrome.test.tsx` never cleared `useWorkspaceName`'s module-scoped `nameCache` between its two cases, so 13.432 (run second) could paint 13.431's cached name on its own first render rather than starting from the pending/refused state it names — the same order-dependence `app-nav.test.tsx` already guards against for the identical cache, fixed the same way (`clearWorkspaceNameCache()` in `beforeEach`). Confirmed fixed by running 13.432 alone (`vitest run -t 13.432`), not merely by the full file passing in order. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/src/use-project-name.ts`, `apps/web/test/project-calendar-timeline.test.tsx`, `apps/web/test/project-page-chrome.test.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`BoardFilterSpec` and `matchesBoardCard` — a filter vocabulary the board can actually answer (Task 3, 2026-09-16)** — the plan's own draft proposed a `boardCardToFilterState` adapter synthesizing a `TicketState` from a `BoardCard` so the board could reuse `matchesFilter` directly. **Rejected before being built.** `matchesFilter` fails CLOSED on every axis a `BoardCard` cannot supply — `collaboratorIds` runs `some()` over the `[]` a card has no way to carry, `dueBefore`/`dueAfter` read `claimedDueDay`, which returns `null` for a card with no claimed date, and a `fields` clause compares `null` to the wanted value — so a synthesized state would not narrow the board, it would EMPTY it, while the list tab showed real matches on the same data. Building the adapter would also mean fabricating a `FieldClaim` with an invented `actorId`/`clientTime`/`serverTime` just to satisfy `state.fields['title']`, inventing provenance nobody claimed in a product whose whole ledger is about who claimed what and when. `BoardFilterSpec` is instead `Pick<FilterSpec, 'text' | 'assigneeIds' | 'typeIds' | 'stateIds' | 'stateCategories' | 'projectIds'>` — derived with `Pick`, never hand-copied, so it cannot drift into a second vocabulary — and the four axes a board cannot answer become a **compile error** at the call site rather than a wrong answer on screen. `matchesBoardCard(card, column, projectId, spec)` evaluates the six axes directly off `BoardCard`/`BoardColumn`, agreeing with `matchesFilter` axis for axis (asserted, not assumed: a 13-case corpus runs the identical spec through both predicates against equivalent card/ticket fixtures, plus an anti-vacuous control proving at least one case actually rejects — agreement alone would pass if both sides answered `true` to everything). The compile-time negative is witnessed, not just claimed: temporarily widening the `Pick` to bare `FilterSpec` and re-running `tsc` turns both `@ts-expect-error` directives on `collaboratorIds` and `dueBefore` into `TS2578` ("Unused '@ts-expect-error' directive") — proof the narrowing itself is what rejects those axes, not a typo the compiler happened not to catch. One signature, not two: Task 4 (the `N of M shown` count) and Task 6 (which cards render) both call `matchesBoardCard` per card from the same pass, so the count is never computed independently of the render. Cases 13.433–13.434. `packages/app-core/src/filter/board-filter.ts`, `packages/app-core/test/filter/board-filter.test.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/index.ts`
- ⚙️ **`matchesListRow` — the same six-axis vocabulary, answered by a `ListRow` instead of a `BoardCard` (exclude-mode spec §2, 2026-09-17).** Unlike `matchesBoardCard`, a `ListRow` carries the due bounds and field claims too, so `ListFilterSpec` (`filter/list-filter.ts`) is `BoardIdAxes` plus `text`/`dueBefore`/`dueAfter`/`fields` — only `collaboratorIds` stays a compile error, for the same reason `board-filter.ts` gives. `id-axes.ts` holds the shared `narrowsTo`/`excludedBy` rules both predicates call. Cases `13.538`–`13.544`. `packages/app-core/src/filter/list-filter.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/filter/id-axes.ts`
- ✅ **The board gets a search box, an honest `N of M shown` count, a removable chip, and a scope note — client-side (Task 4, 2026-09-16), the caller `matchesBoardCard` (Task 3) had none of yet.** One `FilterSpec`-shaped state (`filter`) lives above the tabs in `page.tsx` so a tab switch never resets it — a deliberate widening of the brief's own "`BoardFilterSpec`-shaped state" wording: typed literally as `BoardFilterSpec`, the four axes it omits would be UNREPRESENTABLE and the scope note below would be dead code with nothing it could ever report. Only `text` has a control today (the search box); Task 5's Filters sheet and Task 8's workflow picker set the other axes on this SAME state, and only the BOARD path narrows it down to `BoardFilterSpec` before it reaches `matchesBoardCard` — exactly the brief's own closing sentence. **One pass, per coordinator Ruling 1:** a single `useMemo` walks `board.data.groups`, calling `matchesBoardCard` once per card, and returns the narrowed groups AND the `shown`/`total` counts together; `<Board>` renders `boardNarrowing.view`, never `board.data` directly, so the grid and the `N of M shown` count cannot disagree — there is no second `.filter().length` anywhere on this page. The narrowing drops CARDS only, never a column or a group (`board.tsx:216-219`'s own rule), so a fully-filtered board renders empty columns with the count and the chip as the only disclosure. **The disclosure invariant, built as the shared mechanism Task 8 reuses rather than something private to the search box:** a `.gn-project__narrowing` strip (count + removable chip(s) + scope note, one conditional block) renders on the Board tab only, and ONLY when there is something to disclose — `activeFilterChips.length > 0 || boardUnappliedAxes.length > 0` — so a person who has touched no control never sees an empty bar, and removing the one active chip makes the whole strip (count included) disappear rather than leaving a stale "M of M" behind. **The scope note is a SEPARATE fact from the count**, watching `filter.collaboratorIds`/`dueBefore`/`dueAfter`/`fields` — the four axes `BoardCard` cannot answer at all — and reads "Not applied on the board: …" beside the count, never inside it; merging them would put a number on a filter that never ran. **The chip is page-local, not `Chip`:** `components/chip.tsx` is a dismiss-less `<span>` and `.gn-chip` is a shared, do-not-edit selector, so `.gn-project__filter-chip`/`__filter-chip-remove` are new page-scoped rules built from token roles the block above it already verified (`--gn-fill-quaternary`, `--gn-radius-control`, `--gn-shadow-sunken`, `--gn-text`, `--gn-text-muted` — no new role). **The search box's own accessible name says "the board"** (`aria-label="Search the board"`), not a bare "Search" — it narrows only the Board tab today (Ruling 1 scopes this task there; `CalendarView`/`TimelineView` already accept a `filter?: FilterSpec` prop, confirmed by reading `calendar-view.tsx`/`timeline-view.tsx`, but wiring it is explicitly out of this task, since narrowing those tabs with no count and no chip would itself violate the invariant this task exists to satisfy) — naming the scope in the control's own name is what stops it from being a control that implies a reach it does not have. **`.gn-project__summary` is DELETED**, not left declared-and-unconsumed a second task running: the count that shipped is `.gn-project__count` inside the new strip, at its own metrics, never a reuse of the old rule. **The Filters button is NOT rendered in this task** — Task 5 owns the sheet, and an inert button that opens nothing would itself be a control that lies; it ships once it has real behaviour. **`.gn-view-tabs` is deleted from `globals.css` in this task (coordinator Ruling 7)**, both its rules (`:1244`, formerly `:1391-1392`) and the docblock sentence claiming it "survives… only on the project page" — false the moment Task 2 moved this page's own tab rail onto `.gn-segmented`, and left uncorrected in the file for one extra task rather than two. **Test 2 of the brief's own Step 5 could not be run as literally written**, and the substitution is recorded because it is a real deviation backed by a primary source: `getBoard(projectId: string)` (`data-source.ts:134`) takes no filter argument at all, so the board's own `M` is the complete board total under every value of the "Only tickets in progress" toggle, and the brief's assertion that a bare `M` would falsely claim "the project holds `M` tickets when it holds more" cannot be true of it. The real, reachable hazard is `M` sourced from `list.rows.length` (which the toggle DOES narrow, server-side) instead of from `board.data` — case 13.436 reproduces exactly that (a three-card board, a TWO-row list, the toggle on) and asserts the count reads the board's three, never the list-sourced "1 of 2". Cases 13.435–13.438 (later widened to 13.441, see the fix-round paragraph below). `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`

**Task 4 fix round 1, same day — coordinator Ruling 10 (the search box's own reach was undisclosed) and concern 4 (an unexecuted pure function).** Two independent findings from the controller's review of the DONE_WITH_CONCERNS report. **First, `13.436` as originally shipped could not discriminate the bug it claimed to catch:** its list fixture was zero rows, so ANY wrong `M` — not only a `list.rows`-sourced one — would fail its one assertion equally; rewritten to a two-row list fixture (nonzero, unequal to the board's three) plus a negative assertion naming the specific wrong string ("1 of 2 shown") a `list.rows`-sourced bug would actually print, and confirmed by mutation: temporarily wiring `boardNarrowing`'s `total` to `list.rows?.length ?? 0` was verified to render that exact string and fail the case, before the production code was reverted byte-for-byte. **Second, and the one real behaviour change: the search box's reach was undisclosed on the tabs it does not narrow.** The box sits in the page header on every tab (Task 2's own reserved container), but only the BOARD path applies it — the identical asymmetry the spec's own search ruling exists to prevent ("one search box would... answer differently on two tabs"), now approached from the opposite direction: a WIRE search would have narrowed the list and left the board unnarrowed; this CLIENT search narrows the board and leaves List/Calendar/Timeline unnarrowed with nothing on screen saying so — a sighted person on those tabs types into the box and watches nothing happen. Fixed with the SAME mechanism already built rather than new UI: on every tab but Board, a `.gn-project__scope-note`-styled line reads *"Search narrows the board only — nothing on this tab is filtered by it yet"* — gated on a search term actually being present (a permanent sentence on three tabs would be noise nobody reads) and never on Board itself (where the limit would be a lie, since the search DOES apply there). Case 13.439. **The board's own scope-note logic (`describeUnappliedBoardAxes`) was also extracted** out of `page.tsx`'s inline `useMemo` into a standalone pure function (`apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`) specifically so it has a UNIT test that calls it directly (cases 13.440–13.441) rather than shipping with zero executions until Task 5's Filters sheet became its first real caller — the controller's own framing: "a function that ships having never executed is the thing that turns out to throw on its first real call." `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`, `apps/web/test/board-search-scope.test.ts`

**Task 4 code review, same day — one Important finding: the disclosure gate enumerated a DIFFERENT axis set than the narrowing did.** `page.tsx`'s strip was gated on `activeFilterChips.length > 0 || boardUnappliedAxes.length > 0` — but `activeFilterChips` covered only `text`, `boardUnappliedAxes` covered only the four board-blind axes, while the `boardFilter` object handed to `matchesBoardCard` carries **six** (`text`, `assigneeIds`, `typeIds`, `stateIds`, `stateCategories`, `projectIds`). Five of those six could narrow the rendered cards while tripping neither gate term, silently failing to render the count and chip on cards that had, in fact, been dropped — `board.tsx:216-219` exactly. Not a live bug (only `text` has a UI control today), but reachable the instant Task 5's Filters sheet or Task 8's workflow picker writes any other axis onto the same state, which the code's own docblock says they will. **Two independent remedies, not redundant — they cover different mechanisms.** (1) The chip list is now built by `buildFilterChips` (renamed 2026-09-17; was `buildBoardFilterChips`) (`apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`) reading `boardFilter` itself — one enumeration of the six axes, not two hand-kept in sync — with a human-readable label and a working `onRemove` per axis: `Assignee`/`Type`/`State` resolve an id through a name map (the roster, `typeOptions`, and the board's own column names respectively, falling back to the raw id when unresolved), `Category` capitalises the bare enum value, and `Project` — no name source exists on a page already scoped to one project — states a count. (2) The gate gained a THIRD, independent term: `boardNarrowing.shown !== boardNarrowing.total` (`shouldRenderNarrowingStrip`, renamed 2026-09-17; was `shouldRenderBoardNarrowingStrip`), because Task 8's workflow picker is specified to narrow by dropping whole GROUPS, never by setting a filter axis — no enumeration of axes, however complete, could ever notice that on its own; only a fact about the actual render (fewer cards shown than exist) can. The reviewer asked for a test that would fail without remedy 2 specifically; since that state (narrowing present, no axis set) cannot be constructed from this page's own current inputs, `13.443` tests `shouldRenderNarrowingStrip` (renamed 2026-09-17; was `shouldRenderBoardNarrowingStrip`, which `13.443`'s describe title still names) as a gate expression directly rather than writing a page-render test that would pass for the wrong reason. `boardFilter` itself was also promoted out of `boardNarrowing`'s own memo into its own `useMemo`, so both consumers read the identical value. One Minor alongside it: Ruling 10's mirror note (`13.439`) had List/Timeline coverage but not Calendar — added (`13.444`). Cases 13.442–13.444. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`, `apps/web/test/board-search-scope.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`

- ✅ **The Filters sheet — a real dialog wired to the board's existing filter state, not a second one (Task 5, 2026-09-16).** The Filters button Task 4 deliberately left unrendered ("An inert Filters button is a button that lies... I chose do not render it yet") now opens a `role="dialog"` side panel that writes straight through to the SAME page-level `filter` state the search box and the removable chips already share — every checkbox and chip commits on click, no draft copy, no "Apply". Five of `BoardFilterSpec`'s six axes get a control (`text` already had the search box): Assignee and Collaborators as "person row" checkbox lists over the workspace roster, Type and State as "category chip" multi-selects, and Category over the three fixed `stateCategories` values. `projectIds` gets none — the page is already scoped to the one project it shows, and Task 4's own report found no natural label source for it here. `dueBefore`/`dueAfter`/`fields` get no control either: the design spec's Filters-sheet metrics name exactly two control shapes ("person row", "category chip"), and this task's own brief curates those same two into its metrics line while dropping the spec's "Toggle" row entirely — the discriminating fact that no boolean/date control was meant to land here. Both remain unsettable anywhere in the product after this task, a named gap for whoever builds their control next. **`collaboratorIds` is included deliberately, even though `matchesBoardCard` cannot evaluate it at all** — Task 4's brief explicitly deferred its third Step-5 test ("names the axes the board could not evaluate... helper added in Task 5... do not stub the helper") to this commit; `openFiltersAndPickCollaborator` does the real interaction rather than reaching into `setFilter` directly, and it is `describeUnappliedBoardAxes`'s first execution through the rendered page — it ran clean. **The scope note's stale "— set on the list tab" clause is REMOVED, not re-pointed at the sheet**: the note can join up to three axis names in one sentence, and after this task only `collaborator` has a control anywhere (in the Filters sheet); naming that location for a joined sentence covering `dueBefore`/`dueAfter`/`fields` too would be right for one axis and silently wrong for the other two the moment either gains a control elsewhere. **Absence, not an invented empty list**, mirroring `visibility-editor.tsx`'s own rule: the roster not yet read, the board not yet read, and a FAILED (not merely pending) ticket-types read each render their own sentence rather than a silently empty picker or the built-in type ladder surviving a refusal it was never meant to survive. **Accessibility contract, mutation-verified in both directions**: focus moves off the Filters button and into the panel on open (deleting that effect fails the assertion that proves it), and Escape returns focus to the exact button that opened the sheet (deleting the focus-return line fails that assertion too) — the same vacuous-check trap `account-menu.tsx`'s own docblock warns about, closed by proving each half fails without its code rather than asserting once and trusting it. **Fix round 1, same session (`advisor()` review before reporting done):** Assignee/Collaborators and State had collapsed PENDING and REFUSED into one `members === null`/`!boardLoaded` check — Type already distinguished `typesError`/`typesPending` for the identical reason, and the other two sections would have named "has not loaded yet" over a genuine roster/board refusal. Fixed by threading `members.error`/`board.error` into the sheet as `membersError`/`boardError`, checked ahead of the pending branch in both. Also fixed: `.gn-filters__scrim` had no `z-index`, so it would paint below `TicketSheet`'s `.gn-sheet__scrim` (`z-index: 40`) were both ever open at once — invisible to every test here, since jsdom computes no paint order; matched the house scrims' own value rather than inventing a second scale. **Ruling 12 (coordinator remediation, same session): "Clear all" made a promise its own code did not keep.** It cleared the sheet's own five axes and left `text` alone — correct about which control owns the search box, wrong about what the button's own visible label promises. Fixed: `clearAll` now clears all six `BoardFilterSpec` axes the narrowing strip's chips can represent, `text` included, keeping the chips as the one disclosure surface. The search `<input>` needed no separate reset — checked, not assumed: it is `value={filter.text ?? ''}`, controlled, no local state — so clearing `filter.text` empties it for free. **Ruling 13 (coordinator remediation, same session): Ruling 12's own fix cleared the WRONG six.** Checked axis-by-axis rather than trusting the "clears all six" claim, the reviewer found `clearAll` included `collaboratorIds` (not a `BoardFilterSpec` axis — no chip) while omitting `projectIds` (one of the six, with a working chip) — harmless only because nothing on this page sets `projectIds` yet. Fixed by extracting the exact set into one exported constant, `FILTERS_SHEET_CLEARABLE_AXES`, and a pure `clearFilterAxes` function, so `clearAll` and its test read the SAME list instead of two hand-typed copies — the mechanism that produced the wrong six twice. Mutation-verified: temporarily removing `projectIds` from the array fails the covering test at exactly that field. **Minor, same review:** "Done" and the scrim click were wired but had no passing assertion — added. Cases 13.445–13.462. **2026-09-17: every value is tri-state** — off → include → exclude → off (`TriStateChoice`, state in the accessible name, no `aria-pressed`), the filter vocabulary gained an `exclude` half (`IdAxes`, declared once), the board honours it (`matchesBoardCard`), the strip shows "Not …" chips removable by half, and Clear all clears both halves; cases `13.538`–`13.555`. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/test/filters-sheet.test.tsx`

- ✅ **The board's columns and cards restyle to the prototype's sunken trays and cards — geometry only (Task 6, 2026-09-16).** `.gn-board__columns` renames to `.gn-board__rail` (spec Board `:354-399`'s "Column rail" row; grep-confirmed the only referents anywhere were this file and `ui.css` itself, so the rename breaks nothing). `.gn-board__column` becomes the sunken tray — `--gn-surface-sunken` fill, `--gn-shadow-sunken`, `--gn-radius-card`, 18rem lane, its own `flex-direction: column` layout with a 10px gap between header and card list (which is also why `.gn-board__column-header`'s now-redundant `margin-bottom` was removed — the two would otherwise have stacked to roughly double the spec's own gap). `.gn-board__count`'s selector is kept (`board.test.tsx:142` queries it directly) with its body updated to 13px/18px — a size INCREASE over the prior 12.8px, so the `LABEL_FLOOR_PX` (12px) ratchet in `apps/web/e2e-viewport`/`e2e-stack` cannot regress from it. **`.gn-board-card` gets the padding**, not `.gn-card`: `board.tsx:719` already composes `gn-card gn-board-card` (`card.tsx:7`), the new rule is bare — `(0,1,0)`, the identical specificity `.gn-card` has, not higher — and it wins on SOURCE ORDER (it sits later in `ui.css` than `.gn-card`'s own rule), so every other `.gn-card` consumer in the product is untouched. **One real behaviour change, wired rather than left dead:** `.gn-board-card--pending`'s dashed in-transit outline had a rule with nothing pointing at it — `board.tsx:719` never added a pending modifier — so `BoardCardView` now reads the same `card.pending` flag the "claim in transit" chip already uses; proven both directions and mutation-checked (a never-wired class fails the positive case, an always-wired one fails the negative). **Ruling 15 (coordinator remediation, same day): the spec's `cursor: grab` is REMOVED.** It shipped in this task's first pass with no drag behaviour behind it — the same "a control that lies" shape Task 4's own brief withheld the inert Filters button for, which this task's first pass had not held itself to; it belongs in Task 14's commit, beside the behaviour it promises, not here ahead of it. **The blocked-column indicator needed no work, confirmed rather than assumed:** `board.tsx:409` already renders `<Chip tone={column.blocked ? 'blocked' : column.category}>`, and `.gn-chip--blocked .gn-chip__dot` (`ui.css:155`) already carries the tone as a coloured dot against a readable label — the prototype's tinted-ground-plus-same-hue-text version measures 3.229–4.291 against the 4.5:1 floor in all six style×theme combinations and does not ship. **A second, pre-existing finding surfaced while confirming that: `.gn-board__column--blocked`'s `border-color` had been inert since before this task** — no rule declared a `border-style`, so nothing painted, and `board.test.tsx`'s "marks a blocked state visibly" case asserts only the class string, never a computed style. **Ruling 16 (coordinator remediation, same day): the empty declaration is REMOVED**, the sixth time this session hit the same shape the dead `.gn-view-tabs` rules were (Task 4). The CLASS itself is untouched — still applied conditionally in `board.tsx`, still asserted by `board.test.tsx:56-61` — only the misleading declaration that painted nothing is gone; confirmed to change no rendered pixel, since nothing else in the cascade sets `border`/`border-style` on that selector either. **Ruling 14 (coordinator, same day): the brief's own `.gn-board__column-title` metric (17px/22px, 600) is STRUCK, the same way Task 1 was.** The open item this bullet originally reported — no attachment point under the header markup Tasks 3–5 already shipped, `Chip` taking no `className`, the column name rendering as the chip's own child text — was confirmed rather than guessed past. A split into a separate `<h3>` title plus a standalone blocked chip (closer to the prototype) was considered and rejected: that chip's tone carries CATEGORY for every column, not only blocked, so separating title from chip would change what the colour means across the whole board — a semantic change outside both this geometry-only task's scope and the plan's as written. Column names render at the chip's 13px, not the prototype's 17px heading, because the name lives inside the tone chip; a future task that wants the prototype's separate heading has to settle what happens to the category tone first. **Ruling 17 (code review, same day): `.gn-board-card`'s own literal `border-radius: 0.75rem` is removed too — the exact defect case 13.355 already fixed one surface over.** It carried the same `(0,1,0)` specificity as `.gn-card`'s token-driven radius and sat later in the file, so it won on source order for every style, pinning every board card at 12px where `neo` should draw `.gn-card`'s own `--gn-radius-card` at 20px. The padding line two properties over got the "does this compete with `.gn-card`'s own token" scrutiny; the radius line, transcribed from the brief's own CSS block, did not. A `.gn-board-card`-specific radius role to preserve the prototype's flatter 12px corner was considered and rejected — the same precedent 13.355 set, a new token role to reproduce one decoration. The board card now falls through to `.gn-card`'s radius and moves with the style axis like every other card; case 13.464 asserts the rule declares no `border-radius` of its own, mirroring 13.355's own source-scan shape, so the literal cannot quietly return. Cases 13.463–13.464. `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/app-core/test/views/board.test.tsx`

- ✅ **The pinned empty-board sentence is gated on zero CARDS rather than zero groups, and each empty column gets its own empty state — Ruling R6 (Task 7, 2026-09-16).** `board.tsx:70` (pre-R6; that line is unrelated comment prose today — the gate it names was superseded by this very ruling, not merely moved) — its `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` branch used to trigger only on `board.groups.length === 0`; a project with a workflow attached and zero tickets in it (`groups.length === 1`, every column empty) took the old gate's false branch, skipped the sentence, and drew a silent rail of empty column trays — one layer under the exact "loaded, refused nothing, said nothing" failure that branch exists to prevent. R6 widens the gate to a new `totalCards` fold over every column of every group: the sentence (`emptyBanner`) renders whenever the board holds zero cards in total, and the rail keeps drawing beside it — the zero-groups case stays the ONLY one with no rail, since it alone has no columns to draw. `groups.length === 0` implies `totalCards === 0`, so this is a strict superset, not a replacement: the three pre-existing `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` assertions (3.112, 3.113, 3.114's CONTROL) pass UNMODIFIED — run and confirmed, not assumed. A mutation witness (reverting the gate to `groups.length === 0`, re-running the suite, restoring with a targeted edit rather than `git checkout --`) confirms exactly two tests are R6-dependent: the new whole-board case (13.466) and one PRE-EXISTING test whose own assertion the ruling falsifies by construction — 3.114's second case (`test-cases/03-foundation-ledger.md`), which asserted `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` absent over a single-group, all-empty-columns fixture that IS the R6 case in miniature; amended rather than left standing, its rail-survives half unchanged. **Each empty column separately renders `EMPTY_COLUMN_COPY`** ("No tickets in this column." — the "yet" was dropped later the same month, see the filter bullet below; this bullet keeps the wording as it was added, and the epic split further down is the second sentence to sit beside it, a new exported constant, deliberately not a re-spelling of `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` since both can render on the same screen at once) plus a 34px icon circle replicating `.gn-chip`'s own three-branch chipBg/chipShadow per-style treatment on a NEW selector (`.gn-board__column-empty-icon`, never editing the shared, do-not-edit `.gn-chip`) and an optional "Add a ticket" action. The action reuses the SAME unscoped `onCreateTicket` handler the whole-board button already calls — `BoardProps` gained no new prop, and no per-column target exists to pass — worded differently from the whole-board button's "Create a ticket" only so two buttons rendered on one screen (a board that is both globally and per-column empty, R6) are not identically named to assistive tech. Reused `@gisik-nokma/ui`'s `Button` component UNCHANGED for the action rather than inventing a `ctlBg`/`ctlShadow` treatment — that shadow is the SAME open decision `ui.css:22-30` already records as deliberately left to the repo owner, and this port's own plan settles it for every board control ("Board controls use `.gn-button` unchanged"). **The per-column card carries no data on WHY a column is empty** — `BoardColumn` (`view-types.ts`) has no such signal, the client-side board filter that would know runs above this component (in the shell's `boardNarrowing`) and already discloses an active filter through the `N of M shown` count and its removable chips — so no second, column-scoped disclosure was added; a filtered-to-zero column and a genuinely empty one read identically, on purpose, considered and decided rather than an oversight. **AMENDED 2026-09-20 — the epic ruling added a THIRD cause that was never part of that decision, and it is the one this reasoning does NOT cover.** A column whose only card is an epic is emptied by a rule that is not a filter, and nothing on screen discloses it (an all-epic board has no strip, no chip and no count) — so *"considered and decided"* was false of it, and `BoardColumn` now carries an OPTIONAL `epicsHidden` so that column can say *"No tickets in this column — epics appear in the List."* Only when **nothing else** was removed from that column: a column a filter ALSO emptied keeps the neutral sentence, because the epic clause would be a half-truth there. The stale quotation of the constant in this bullet is corrected with it, as promised there. Cases 13.465–13.466. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/app-core/test/views/board.test.tsx`

**Task 7 fix round, same day — Ruling 19 (found in review): R6's sentence must be gated on the board BEFORE narrowing, not after.** `page.tsx` hands `<Board>` `boardNarrowing.view`, the POST-FILTER groups (Task 4's client-side board narrowing), never `board.data` directly — Task 4's narrowing drops CARDS only, never a column or a group. Pre-R6 that was harmless, since the old gate read `board.groups.length`, which a card filter never touches; R6's `totalCards` fold reads the narrowed cards, so without a fix a search matching nothing rendered `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` — "create one" — over a project holding real tickets, one row beneath a strip reading "0 of 10 shown" with a removable chip: the page contradicting itself, and the sentence naming the wrong remedy. **Fix: `Board` gains an optional `isNarrowed?: boolean` prop** (`BoardProps`, following `onCreateTicket`'s own precedent — a mount with nothing to say passes nothing, unchanged behaviour), and `emptyBanner`'s gate becomes `totalCards === 0 && isNarrowed !== true`. `page.tsx` passes `isNarrowed={boardNarrowing.shown !== boardNarrowing.total}` — the SAME expression `shouldRenderNarrowingStrip` (renamed 2026-09-17; was `shouldRenderBoardNarrowingStrip`) already reads as one of its three terms, not a second definition of "narrowed"; since narrowing drops cards only, this is true exactly when real cards were filtered away, and `shown === total === 0` whenever the board is genuinely empty, so R6's real case is never suppressed. `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY`'s text is unchanged — this only changes WHEN it shows. **Deliberately NOT changed**: the per-column `EMPTY_COLUMN_COPY` under a filter — judged neutral and approved deliberately in this task's own review, left to the plan's final review rather than reopened mid-task. Witnessed the same way as R6 itself: reverted the `&& isNarrowed !== true` guard, confirmed only the narrowed-to-zero case reddens while R6's own case and the "not narrowed" arms of the same fixture stay green, restored with a targeted edit. **Also recorded, per the coordinator's new standing instruction that every report carry `tsc` output for every touched package**: `packages/ui`'s typecheck (red since Task 6 fix round 2, `8eb40607`, on two `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` errors) was fixed by a concurrent commit from another implementer on this branch (`ddc42c21`, "Task 6 fix round 3") while this fix round was in progress — confirmed clean by re-running `tsc` directly rather than assumed from an earlier report. Case 13.467. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/app-core/test/views/board.test.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`

- ✅ **The workflow picker — narrows the board to one workflow's own group, defaulted to `All workflows` (Task 8, Ruling R3, 2026-09-16).** A `<select>` in the project header, new `.gn-project__workflow-select` rule (`ctlBg`/`ctlR`, no new token role; `ctlShadow` omitted, following `.gn-button`'s own already-recorded open decision), gated on `board.data.groups.length > 1` — a project with zero or one workflow offers no real choice between "All workflows" and that one workflow's own name, mirroring `Board`'s identical gate for its own group heading (`board.groups.length > 1 ? <h3>`). Narrows at GROUP granularity, never card granularity: `matchesBoardCard` has no group in its signature, so this axis is structurally unrepresentable in `BoardFilterSpec`/`boardFilter`, and no chip `buildFilterChips` (renamed 2026-09-17; was `buildBoardFilterChips`) builds could ever name it. `boardNarrowing`'s single "ONE PASS" fold (Task 4, Ruling 1) widens rather than gaining a parallel count: `total` sums every card project-wide, including dropped groups' own; `shown` counts only cards in a KEPT group that also pass `matchesBoardCard`. This is precisely the case `shouldRenderNarrowingStrip`'s (renamed 2026-09-17; was `shouldRenderBoardNarrowingStrip`) independent third term (`shown !== total`) was built for in Task 4's own review, before anything could exercise it — case `13.470` is that exercise, mutation-witnessed (hardcoding the gate's `shown` argument to `total` reddens `13.470`/`13.471` and nothing else). The picker's own removable chip is built separately from `buildFilterChips` (renamed 2026-09-17; was `buildBoardFilterChips`) and merged with `activeFilterChips` only at the render site, so the disclosure invariant (`board.tsx:216-219`) is satisfied through the SAME strip the client filter uses, not a second one. Picking a workflow whose own group holds zero cards makes `isNarrowed` (Task 7, Ruling 19) true and correctly suppresses `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` — checked directly, not assumed; the per-column empty state and its own "Add a ticket" button still render. Cases `13.468`–`13.471`. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ✅ **2026-09-17: the filter reaches every tab.** `list.rows` is narrowed once through `matchesListRow` (`filter/list-filter.ts`) and List, Calendar, Timeline and Backlog all read the narrowed rows (backlog/closed spec §5.1, 2026-09-17, Task 10, added after this bullet was written); the strip renders on every tab with that tab's own count and unapplied axes (`shouldRenderNarrowingStrip`, per tab); the mirror note is gone because what it apologised for is no longer true; the create dialog's parent picker and `epicTitle` keep the unnarrowed rows. Client-side throughout — the wire has no negation term and every tab already holds the complete project (≤ 2000 rows); `toServerFilterSpec` refuses a non-empty exclude. Cases `13.556`–`13.561`. Finding 3 of the final whole-branch review's fix wave (2026-09-16), alongside that same review's mirror-note widening (since retired, above): `EMPTY_COLUMN_COPY` drops "yet" (`No tickets in this column.`) — under an active filter, "yet" reads as asserting none were ever created — and its docblock is corrected to say `Board` now HAS an `isNarrowed` signal (Ruling 19) that simply is not threaded down to `BoardGroupSection`, rather than claiming the data is structurally unavailable. See `test-cases/13-deployment-dogfooding.md`'s own "Final whole-branch review, fix wave" section for Findings 2, 4 and 5 (a 34-citation line-number sweep and two docblock hedges, no new cases). `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`
- ✅ **Closed filter; Backlog in State chips; Calendar/Timeline hide closed by default** (backlog/closed spec §5.5, Ruling 44/45, 2026-09-17, Task 11) — a `Closed` tri-state control in the Filters sheet (`closedMode`/`withCycledClosed`, `apps/web/src/filter-cycle.ts`) cycles `filter.closed` through All/Closed only/Not closed, disclosed by removable `Closed: only`/`Closed: hidden` chips (`buildFilterChips`, `apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`) on every tab but Board (unchanged, spec §5.2); the Filters sheet's State section gains the universal `Backlog` entry alongside the board's own columns (Ruling 45 — no second def fetch). (showClosedOnDates ? undefined : false)`), disclosed by their own mandated `Closed: hidden` chip (independent of the explicit control's chip) whose removal only lifts the default; the List tab is unaffected by it in either direction. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/filter-cycle.ts`, `apps/web/src/board-search-scope.ts`

**Task 8 fix round, same day — coordinator Ruling 20, two closures.** **20(a)** takes up the report's own offer: the empty-picked-workflow/`isNarrowed` interaction above was verified by reading two unchanged expressions (composition); the coordinator wanted it run, because this session already carries two instances of a correctly-reasoned path hiding something once it actually executed. Case `13.472` runs it — mutation-witnessed, `isNarrowed={false}` reddens only that case. `EMPTY_COLUMN_COPY` joins `EMPTY_BOARD_COPY` in `packages/app-core`'s public re-export so the new case can assert the same string that renders. **20(b)** fixes a real gap the report itself flagged: a picked workflow that drops out of `board.data` mid-session (reassigned or detached elsewhere, page left open) left `workflowFilter` naming an id nothing matches — recoverable, but until removed the chip's label fell back to the RAW INTERNAL ID, asserting a phantom narrowing as real. `resolvedWorkflowFilter` — a plain, unmemoized `const`, never written back into `workflowFilter` itself — falls back to `'all'` whenever the picked id matches no group in the CURRENT `board.data`; every narrowing/display consumer reads it, only the `<select>`'s own `onChange` still writes raw `workflowFilter` (always valid when written, since options are drawn from the current group list). Case `13.473` exercises this through a REAL mid-session update, not a mount-time shortcut: `rerender` with a new `Providers services` whose `getBoard` drops the picked workflow, keeping the SAME `ProjectPage` instance mounted so `workflowFilter`'s state survives — the identical effect-dependency path (`useLiveQuery`'s `source`-keyed first effect) a real workspace-stream event drives in production. Mutation-witnessed: collapsing `resolvedWorkflowFilter` to raw `workflowFilter` reddens only `13.473`. Cases `13.472`–`13.473`. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/board-narrowing.test.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/index.ts`

- ⚙️ **`istToday()` — the one place this shell turns an instant into a day** — the device supplies the INSTANT, a fact it genuinely owns; `istDayOf` turns it into the IST day, and the device's own opinion about which day that is never enters. `now` is injected. Two wrong answers, two independent guards: `toISOString().slice(0, 10)` (yesterday's cell for five and a half hours every night) dies on the IST-midnight pair, and `toLocaleDateString()` (a different day per collaborator) dies on the three-zone loop. Stated plainly because the branch has corrected three overclaims of this shape already: the zone loop CANNOT discriminate against the current implementation — `istDayOf` reads only `Date.parse` of an offset-bearing string and the UTC getters — so it is a regression guard whose teeth are the mutation, and the `TZ` control below it proves the knob moves, nothing more. `apps/web/src/ist-today.ts`
- ⚙️ **Dev topology — two rewrites, and the third one's absence is tested** — Next proxies `/v1/*` and `/api/auth/*` to `SERVER_ORIGIN`, so cookies stay first-party and the online `DataSource` is mounted with `baseUrl: ''`. The WebSocket is dialled DIRECTLY at `NEXT_PUBLIC_SERVER_URL` because Next's dev server does not proxy protocol upgrades. A third rule for `/api/workspaces/*` was dropped when ticket reads moved to `/v1`; nothing serves that prefix, and a proxy rule for a path nothing serves fails differently through Next than direct, which is the confusing half of a 404. `test/next-config.test.ts` re-derives the served prefixes from `apps/server/src` on every run — comments stripped first, so a path merely DISCUSSED in prose cannot vouch for itself — and refuses a rule that matches none. `apps/web/next.config.ts`
- ✅ **Invite links — minting, holding, accepting, withdrawing** — spec §6's onboarding path, whole, over the four routes Plan 4 Task 14 shipped (Plan 6 Task 15 calls these an unowned surface gap; that sentence is stale and the paths were re-derived from `apps/server/src/routes/invites.ts` before a client line was written). An admin mints from `/w/[workspaceId]/members`; the link is `${WEB_ORIGIN}/invite/${token}` built by the SERVER, so this shell's route segment is one half of a contract already shipped. **The token is server-owned and hashed** — the raw copy leaves in the mint response and nowhere else, so the minted link renders exactly once under copy that says so, the pending list carries `expiresAt` and no token, and no "show it again" affordance exists because nothing could serve one. `GET /v1/invites/:token` needs no session, deliberately: the token IS the credential, so a stranger is told which workspace they are being asked to join before being made to authenticate — and the page distinguishes a session still LOADING from a signed-out one, which the plan's two-branch test does not. A dead link says used, withdrawn **or** lapsed, all three, because the door answers one frozen 404 for all of them and a holder must learn nothing about which. Withdrawing is an ordinary `invite.revoked` through `DataSource.ingest`, gated by `ADMIN_ONLY_TYPES` at the door rather than by a second check here. **HIG surface (Task 4a, 2026-09-08):** the invite card sits on `surface-raised` with no border, and the dead-link paragraph is muted — the card's fill and the page's are what separate them, per R1(c). **Neo restyle (Task 15, 2026-09-09):** the card's literal `border-radius: 12px` — a deliberate BUMP over `.gn-card`'s then-8px base, which had inverted into an unintended SHRINK once Task 14 moved that base onto a `--gn-radius-card` token reaching 20px under neo — is removed (case 13.355); the card now simply takes `.gn-card`'s own radius at whatever style is active. No markup changed. The body sentence is UNCHANGED and pinned: the prototype's version drops the role, and a person accepting an invitation is being told what they will be able to do, so the copy rule rejects it. **Case 13.300** drives the real page in a real browser behind a stubbed summary and matches the role alternation against the shipped `InviteSummary['role']` union rather than a guessed list. What this route still LACKS is a one-way accessible-name floor — the signed-in sweep has no standing invite token, and a token-less visit would baseline the dead-link branch, so the gap is recorded in `docs/pre-launch-checklist.md` with a named owner instead of being closed wrongly. **AND THE LINK READ NOW CARRIES THE TOKEN'S EXPIRY (2026-09-19, the `isInvite` expiry ruling, case `13.988`).** `openableInvite` had read `expires_at` from the beginning — it is the whole point of its `expires_at > now()` predicate — and **threw it away**; it is now served as a fourth field on `GET /v1/invites/:token`. **An `ADDITIVE FIELD` on a read that already existed: no route, no migration.** **It is the TOKEN's window and not the invitation's, and that is the distinction the case pins:** `invite_tokens` has one row per PRESENTATION while `proj_invites` has no expiry column at all, so an invitation revoked and re-issued has one invitation row and several token rows, each with its own window — a read answering the invitation's lifetime would answer the WRONG question on a re-issued link. The value served is the row `openableInvite` matched on, so it cannot disagree with the predicate that admitted the call. **It does not erode the read's disclosure guard**, which protects the ADDRESS the invitation names — a fact belonging to somebody other than whoever holds a forwarded link — where `expiresAt` is a property of the credential the caller is already holding; that read's own docblock is corrected in the same change set (it said *"the three fields Plan 6 pinned and nothing else"*). **The invitee-facing page does not render it yet** — this row's scope is the DOOR, and the client's `expiresAt` still lives on the admin side only; owner named in the catalogue. `packages/app-core/src/online/invites.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/invites-admin.ts`, `apps/web/app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx`
- ✅ **The invitations addressed to YOU — and the ability to say no** (Task 17) — `GET /v1/me/invitations` answers the pending invitations for the signed-in person's **verified** email, scoped from their own `"user"` row and never from a parameter, and `/workspaces` renders them as the *"One invitation is waiting"* banner: who invited you, to what workspace, on what terms (the role as a sentence, never the raw enum), when it was sent and when it lapses, with **Accept** and **Decline**. Before this the invite surface was admin-and-token shaped — a person who lost the email had no way to discover they had been invited, and **Decline existed nowhere in the product**: the only exits from `pending` were an acceptance or an admin's revocation. `invite.declined` is the new terminal claim, attributed to the person who made it, refused from every client door for the reason `invite.accepted` is (an inviteId is readable by every member, so a client-submitted decline would let any admin destroy a pending invitation *under the invited person's name*). **Accept from the banner is a second door, not a second implementation**: the raw token is unrecoverable (sha256 at rest, one copy, handed out at mint), so a caller-scoped list has none to serve — `apps/server/src/invites/acceptance.ts` holds the acceptance sequence once and the two doors differ only in the proof they demand, link possession or a verified address, the second being strictly the narrower. All three actions spend the SAME credential row through the same conditional DELETE, so accept-by-link, accept-from-the-banner and decline are mutually exclusive by construction. An empty answer renders NOTHING — no heading, no empty state — because a person with no invitation must not be told about invitations, and a failed read is one muted line rather than the page's refusal. A name the server cannot supply becomes words rather than a hole in the sentence, blank as well as null: better-auth's `"user"."name"` is `NOT NULL` with no non-blank constraint and the dev workspace's own owner carries `''`. Named absences: phone-channel invitations do not surface here (this read is email-scoped), and a phone-only account has no verified address and is answered an empty list. **Restyled, 2026-09-15 (the workspaces-prototype-port plan's Task 8): the banner becomes a FLAT warning wash, not a card you could open.** The prior treatment (Task 17) borrowed `.gn-card`'s own raised surface (`--gn-surface-raised`/`--gn-shadow-raised`) plus an accent inline-start edge; both are gone. The fill is now `--gn-tint-warning` (the design-tokens bullet above), which holds its 4.5:1 legibility only because this banner sits at PAGE level, never inside a card — the same measurement's own precondition. A decorative, `aria-hidden` warning-circle icon sits beside the heading/list/actions, now grouped under `.gn-invitations__body` so the icon can sit as a flex sibling; no behavioural assertion changed, since the restyle is CSS and one added `<svg>` only. **The date line (`.gn-invitations__when`, "Invited … · lapses …") briefly lost its subordinate rank in this restyle** — it was merged into `.gn-invitations__offer`'s own rule and the two became typographically identical (whole-branch review, Minor 5; the prototype has only one body line, so there was nothing there to port for a second). Fixed in the same fix wave: `.gn-invitations__when` is its own rule again, smaller than `__offer`, at this branch's own established secondary-meta size (13px/18px). Cases 13.410. `apps/server/src/routes/my-invitations.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/my-invitations.ts`, `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`.
- ✅ **The workspace card, and a nav that reaches Help and Account** (Task 18) — `/workspaces` renders each workspace as a card through the style axis: a monogram from the name with a colour derived deterministically from the workspace **id** (`Avatar`'s `tint`, FNV-1a into four token pairs `CONTRAST_PAIRS` already holds to the 4.5:1 TEXT minimum — a monogram is read text, not a decorative dot); the member count; chips for active and blocked work; the caller's role as a SENTENCE (*"You're an owner here."*, never the raw enum); each project with the tickets this caller may see; archived projects marked with a chip AND hidden behind a per-workspace disclosure; and *"N project · name restricted"* from the server's new count. **Every gated affordance reads ONE role value** — the sentence and the withholding come from the same `viewerOf(role)` call, not from `role` and `memberCount === null` separately, which agree on every body the shipped server sends and disagree on the one case 13.381 seeds. **A guest card shows a single "Restricted" chip and no number anywhere**, because `0` is a claim the server declined to make by omitting the key. "active" on the chip means NOT BLOCKED — `total − blocked`, exact arithmetic over one set — and is deliberately not `StateCategory.active`, which is a different fact this route serves no field for. Zero renders nothing: no *"0 projects · name restricted"*, no disclosure toggle over an empty list. The nav gains **Help** and **Account**, both global and both unconditional on the workspace, and 11.185's closed-link-set tripwire was widened from three doors to five in the same change set. **Round 19b (2026-09-09), owner feedback — the card's head is now a DOOR to `/w/<id>`:** the monogram and the name, `aria-label` set to exactly the workspace's name, keyboard-reachable, hover/focus affordance; every other control on the card (project links, the archived toggle, "My Work") stays a sibling, never nested inside it. **Round 19c, same day, owner feedback — the WHOLE card is the door, and nothing else on it is clickable:** the per-project link, the archived-disclosure toggle and the "My Work" link are all GONE from this card (not merely moved inside the anchor, which invalid HTML forbids) — the project name is now plain text, and "N archived" is a count chip in the same row as the member/active/blocked chips, gated on `viewer.countsWithheld` the same way they are. Nothing removed is unreachable: `/w/<id>`, one click through the door, already carries "My Work", a per-project "Open board" link and admin archive/restore (Task 19, Round 19b), so this is one fewer redundant path to the same controls, not a loss of function. Case 13.390 asserts the stronger, direct shape this makes possible — exactly one link and zero buttons anywhere in the card — replacing its Round 19b version, which could only prove the weaker "every other control is still independently reachable" because Round 19b still had other controls to prove it about. **The nav is now CONTEXTUAL** rather than a fixed set: without a workspace in the route only the four always-items render (Workspaces, Inbox, Help, Account) plus a scope note; with one, the workspace's own served name, My Work, Members and Settings join. **A Board item joined when the route ALSO carried a project, until Round 23 removed it (owner: "the board on the nav bar doesn't work. lets remove it") — and it could not have worked: this product has one board per PROJECT, so its only possible target was `/w/<id>/p/<pid>`, so it could render only when the reader was already standing on that board. It was a link to the current page in every state it could reach, with `aria-current="page"` every time it appeared. `AppNav`'s `projectId` prop went with it, Board being its only consumer.** So there are now TWO nav shapes, not three. The current item carries `aria-current="page"`. Sign-out's visible label shrank to "Sign out" beside a 24px monogram; its accessible name keeps the full "Sign out {name}" via `aria-label`, so the floor this shell ratchets does not fall. 11.185 is now three closed sets — one per nav shape when there were three of them; since Round 23 collapsed the shapes to two, its third arm keeps its place proving that a project in the route adds NOTHING, with an expected array deliberately identical to the second's. **Round 19c, same day, owner feedback ("move members into settings and settings should always be visible on the nav bar"): **Settings** left the workspace-scoped group and joined the always tier — five always-items now, not four — pointed at a NEW global `/settings` (`apps/web/app/settings/page.tsx`) rather than `/w/<id>/settings/ticket-types`, carrying the workspace forward as `?workspaceId=` when one is in scope rather than as a path segment, since nothing in this shell remembers a "current workspace" outside a route or query param for an unconditional link to target. **Members did NOT move or lose its nav slot** — a first draft of this change also dropped it, which the owner's own reference screenshots (same feedback round) contradicted by keeping Members as its own top-level link beside Settings; it is unchanged, still `/w/[workspaceId]/members`. `/settings` itself is a REAL working page against those screenshots, not a stub: it shows the workspace name as a subtitle, a three-item sidebar (Ticket types, Workflows, MCP tokens), and renders Ticket types — the default tab — inline via the SAME `TicketTypeListScreen`/`useBuilderWiring` pair `/w/<id>/settings/ticket-types/page.tsx` is built from (a second thin caller, nothing reimplemented). `/settings` also gives `/mcp/tokens` its first nav presence anywhere: that page was already global (minted per person, not per workspace) but until now was reachable only by finding it inside some workspace's home first. With no workspace in the query, Ticket types and Workflows render as plain text naming that a workspace must be picked first, linking `/workspaces`; MCP tokens stays a real link regardless. **Round 19d, same day, owner feedback ("workflows and mcp tokens should not be opening up a separate route. it should live inside settings. check the settings page inside the attached prototype"): the sidebar's Workflows and MCP tokens tabs are now INLINE too, matching the owner's `.dc.html` prototype, which models Settings as one screen (`isSettingsShell`) with a sidebar swapping content by an `sc-if` — never a navigation away from it.** `WorkflowListScreen` joins `TicketTypeListScreen` as a second thin `useBuilderWiring` caller, one tab over. MCP tokens had no reusable component — its whole implementation lived inside `app/mcp/tokens/page.tsx` — so that was extracted into `src/mcp-tokens-screen.tsx` (`McpTokensScreen`, taking a `heading` level prop so `/settings`'s nested `<h2>` doesn't collide with `/mcp/tokens`'s own top-level `<h1>`); the standalone `/mcp/tokens` route still works, now as a two-line wrapper around the shared screen. A `tab` query param (`ticket-types` | `workflows` | `mcp-tokens`, default `ticket-types`) drives the swap alongside `workspaceId` — `/settings?workspaceId=<id>&tab=workflows` is still one route, so the nav's Settings link stays unconditional. What did NOT move at the time: the ticket-type EDITOR and the workflow BUILDER (their own `[typeId]`/`[workflowId]`/`new` routes) — the prototype itself draws that line differently per screen (its type editor is inside the settings shell, its workflow builder, a full-width canvas, is not), and matching that exactly would mean extracting a second, materially more complex screen for a distinction the owner did not name; only the two LIST-level tabs were called out. **THE BAR FLOATS as of 2026-09-15 (owner: *"make the top nav bar always be floating on the top"*)** — `.gn-nav` takes `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 20`, reversing a decision its own comment used to record ("the bar sits in normal document flow (not `position: sticky`/`fixed`)"), which is rewritten rather than left contradicting the rule beneath it. `sticky` and not `fixed` on purpose: it stays in flow, so it still occupies its own height and nothing below needs a compensating margin that would be a second copy of that height to keep in sync. **Two things the word "floating" does not say out loud but requires.** (1) A `background`, which this rule never had — the bar only looked solid because `body` paints the same `--gn-surface` behind it, and the instant content scrolls underneath, transparent means the text shows through; it is declared now, at the value that keeps the resting appearance byte-identical. (2) `scroll-margin-top` on every anchor target, because a fragment navigation scrolls its target to the top of the viewport, which is now *under* the bar — measured before the fix, clicking "§4 — Getting your people in" put its heading at `top: 0.04px` against a 69.6px bar, so the one heading you asked for was the one thing you could not see. `.gn-help__section`/`.gn-help__entry` carry it now. Both read `--app-nav-offset` (4.35rem — a 2.75rem control row plus 0.8rem of padding twice, matching the 69.6px the browser reports, and identical at 768px and 1512px), which is **deliberately not a `--gn-` token**: `tokens.test.ts` requires every `var(--gn-…)` in `ui.css` to name a declared role, and this is one shell's layout fact, not a design value — `apps/desktop` renders no nav and resolves the `0px` fallback, reproducing its previous behaviour exactly. `z-index: 20` sits below the tree's existing 30 (`.gn-select__panel`, `.mention-popup`) and 40 (`.gn-dialog__scrim`/`.gn-sheet__scrim`), so a dropdown opening near the top renders over the bar and a modal scrim still covers it. The sticky help sidebar's own offset moved with it. Case 13.402. **Round 21 (2026-09-16), owner: "move back the nav bar settings to only be visible once a workspace is opened... and move inbox to just before help." Settings REJOINED the workspace-scoped group**, reversing Round 19c's move to the always tier — four always-items again (Workspaces, Inbox, Help, Account). **Only the visibility rule reverted; the TARGET did not:** the link still points at `/settings?workspaceId=<id>`, not the pre-19c `/w/<id>/settings/ticket-types`, because that bare path is the sidebar-less list page whose Edit button opened the editor as a third route — reverting the href as well would re-open a defect the owner asked to have closed, in the name of a ruling that said nothing about where the link points. **This turned case 13.391's scope note back on, and with it a test that had been RED since `762b1c8d`.** That commit moved Settings to the always tier, which made the note's sentence ("pick one to reach its board, members and settings") false, so the note was commented out and the case left failing; with all three of those nouns workspace-conditional again the sentence is true and the note renders. The case went green because the claim became true, not because the assertion was relaxed. **Inbox moved to just before Help**, grouping the three person-scoped destinations (Inbox, Help, Account) at the bar's end; it stays unconditional, so this is a position change only. 11.185's three closed sets moved with both changes, and case 13.414 pins the Inbox/Help adjacency, which nothing else in the suite asserted. Cases 13.390–13.393, 13.414. **Round 24 (2026-09-16), owner: "Current Signout button: make it into a round profile pic like thing, that opens up a context menu on click. signout option will be moved to the bottom of that menu. along with the help and account item from the nav bar" and "make inbox an icon. with an indicator (green dot?) if there is an unread event. place it on the left of the round profile pic like thing." The sign-out button is replaced by a round monogram trigger opening an `AccountMenu` (`apps/web/src/account-menu.tsx`, NEW) that holds Help, Account and — last, behind a separator — Sign out.** This is the one surface on this branch with **no prototype to transcribe**: `desktop.dc.html`'s nav is a flat `navDef` list with no avatar, menu or popover anywhere, so the menu follows the prototype's CONTROL metrics while its behaviour is the product's own. **The menu names the signed-in person (name + email) at its head, and that is a restoration, not decoration:** the control it replaced was `Sign out {name}`, whose accessible name carried the signed-in identity on every page in the product; a monogram carries none, since two people with the same initial render identically. `useCurrentUser` gained `email` (`string | null`, omitted rather than rendered blank) to serve it. **Sign out keeps the exact `Sign out {name}` accessible name it always had** — the shell's accessible-name floor is ratcheted on that string and `e2e-stack/passkey-registration.spec.ts` addresses the control by it, so the control moved and the name did not (13.418; the e2e gained a menu-opening step and its selector is otherwise unchanged). **Help and Account are still bar links when SIGNED OUT**, because `AccountMenu` needs a name, an email and a sign-out target and has none of the three — folding them in unconditionally would have deleted the only route to `/help` from the signed-out shell, on a page explicitly reachable signed out. **The inbox became an icon** with a `--gn-success` dot when unread, and — owner's ruling — **the count moved into the link's accessible name** (`"Inbox, 3 unread notifications"`, bare `"Inbox"` at zero) rather than being dropped: a bare dot would have deleted the number for exactly the readers least able to go looking for it, and would leave unread state signalled by colour alone (WCAG 1.4.1). `UnreadBadge` is untouched and still renders the number on `/inbox` itself. **Case 13.414 is SUPERSEDED — its subject is gone**, since Help is no longer a link in this bar for "Inbox immediately before Help" to be true or false about; it is replaced by 13.416, which pins the same KIND of claim at the arrangement that now exists (the inbox immediately left of the avatar). 11.185's three arms shrank by two entries each, and 13.417 is what makes that safe to assert — a shorter expected list is also what deleting the links would produce, so the reachability of Help and Account through the menu is pinned separately. Both new controls inherit the 2.75rem tap floor from `.gn-nav a, .gn-nav button`. Opening the menu focuses its first item and the arrows walk it, wrapping at both ends — **which is also what makes the Escape-returns-focus assertion measurable**: before focus moved into the menu it had never left the trigger, and that case passed with the focus-return line deleted.
