Cases 13.416–13.423. **Round 25 (2026-09-16), owner: "keep the last opened workspace in local storage, and based on that, the workspace scoped navbar items should be made visible. so that just moving to a separate page (inbox, help, account, etc) does not automatically hide them." `apps/web/src/use-last-workspace.ts` (NEW) remembers it under `gn-last-workspace`**, following the `gn-style`/`gn-theme` convention; `AppNav` takes the route's workspace when there is one and the remembered one otherwise, so leaving a workspace for `/inbox`, `/help` or `/account` no longer strips the workspace-scoped links. **The bar's founding rule is unchanged — a link renders only when its target exists — and the two sources are deliberately NOT treated alike.** A ROUTED workspace renders its links immediately, because standing on `/w/<id>/members` is proof it is there; a REMEMBERED one renders them only once `useWorkspaceName` resolves a name from `getMyWorkspaces()`, because a remembered id is a guess about the past and may name a workspace you have since been removed from or that was deleted — the same hazard that got the Board item removed in Round 23, arriving from storage rather than from a route. It reuses the read the bar already makes: no second request, and no second notion of "reachable" to drift from the first. **The record is KEYED BY USER (`{userId, workspaceId}`) and a record belonging to anyone else is ignored**, because `localStorage` outlives a session: sign out, hand the laptop over, and an unkeyed memory renders the previous person's workspace NAME in the next person's chrome — the defect case 11.169 exists for, one surface over. It is not cleared on sign-out but simply not honoured, which also covers the case sign-out never runs at all (a cookie expiring in place). **Two assertions in case 13.391 changed and neither was relaxed to fit the code:** the scope note is now gated on a positive "storage read, nothing remembered" rather than on the synchronous "no workspace", since the synchronous form would flash *"No workspace selected"* at every viewer who has one on every global page; and "no workspace in the ROUTE, no Settings link" became "no workspace REACHABLE, no Settings link", the old wording being exactly what this instruction revokes. 13.424 is the counterpart that keeps that honest — without it the "no Settings" assertion would also be satisfied by a nav that had stopped remembering anything. **A cost stated rather than hidden:** when a workspace is remembered but its name never resolves, neither the scoped links nor the scope note render, because the alternative — clearing the memory on a failed name — would destroy valid state on a transient read failure to recover a hint. Cases 13.424–13.427. **Round 26 (2026-09-16), owner: "the nav bar item jitters on changing pages. the workspace scoped items briefly disappears and reappears." THE MOUNT WAS THE BUG.** `Providers` lives in the root layout and survives client-side navigation, but `AppNav` is rendered by each PAGE (`app/inbox/page.tsx:210` and fourteen siblings), so every navigation unmounts and remounts it — resetting `useLastWorkspace` to `unknown` and `useWorkspaceName` to `null`, so the workspace-scoped links vanished for the tick it took to re-read storage and re-fetch the name, then returned. Round 25 made this loud by putting four links behind that state; **the workspace-NAME half predates Round 25 entirely** and flickered even when navigating between two pages of the same workspace, where it was a single item and easier to miss. Both hooks now hold their last answer in MODULE scope — which survives the remount — and initialise `useState` from it, so the second and every later navigation paints on the first frame; the first load of a session still resolves asynchronously, because nothing is known yet and painting links from no information would be inventing state. **Two hazards the fix creates and closes:** a cache read on every remount would RESURRECT a workspace you have lost (13.426's defect displaced in time), so a successful read that does not contain the workspace EVICTS while a rejected read — transient — leaves the entry alone; and neither cache passes through the stored record's user guard (the remembered-workspace cache is checked before it, the name cache is keyed by workspace id alone), so both are dropped when the observed user id changes, since signing out and back in as someone else is a client-side navigation that does not reset module state. Cases 13.428–13.429, both mutation-witnessed. **Round 27 (2026-09-18), owner: "new nav item :right to the workspace name -> last open project name. on click, open the project page (save the last open tab as well). on hover, show the non archived projects as a dropdown, and clicking them opens those projects. if there is no last opened project, then this defaults to the first in the order that we display in the workspace page." THE ITEM IS `LastProjectNav` (`apps/web/src/last-project-nav.tsx`, NEW), mounted immediately right of the workspace name and gated on the same `!noWorkspace`/`effectiveWorkspaceId` the My Work, Members and Settings links read** — one answer to "which workspace is this bar about", not a second one. It names the project you were last in, resolved through the SAME `getMyWorkspaces` read `/workspaces` renders (`apps/web/src/use-workspace-projects.ts`, NEW: the project LIST, `null` for pending/refused and never `[]`, module-cached and evicted on a successful read that lacks the workspace, exactly `use-workspace-name.ts`'s contract one level down), so it can never name something unreachable. **With nothing remembered it falls back to the first NON-ARCHIVED project in served order** — `workspace.projects` with `archived` filtered and NO client-side sort, which is precisely what `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx` renders; deriving a second order was the thing to avoid. **With no reachable project it renders NOTHING**, the rule Rounds 22–23 established when the Board item was removed for offering a link that could not resolve: the workspace's `projects` list is empty, or every row in it is archived. **It is NOT the Board item returning, and the difference is the whole reason it can exist:** Board's only possible target was the route you were already standing on, so it was a link to the current page in every state it could reach; a project id is now something the bar actually has, so this is the first project-scoped destination that points somewhere else. **Archiving the project you were last in FALLS BACK rather than disappearing** (case 13.868) — the remembered id is matched against the NON-ARCHIVED rows only, because `archived` is served precisely so a client can tell and a match against all served projects would name a project the workspaces page no longer lists. **The dropdown opens on HOVER and on FOCUS/click** (the owner answered both directly) and is the `account-menu.tsx` menu pattern reused rather than reinvested: `role="menu"` with the accessible name `"Projects"`, `role="menuitem"` anchors that keep their `href`, Escape closing, arrow keys walking the items and wrapping at both ends, and a pointer-down outside closing. **One deliberate divergence from that pattern, and it is load-bearing:** focus is NOT pulled into the menu on open, because this item opens on FOCUS and moving focus would make tabbing through the nav swallow the reader into the menu every time they passed it — so Escape returns focus to the trigger and a `suppressNextFocusOpen` ref breaks the loop that would otherwise reopen the menu immediately (case 13.871 measures it). **TAB MEMORY IS GLOBAL, which the owner also answered directly:** `apps/web/src/use-last-project.ts` (NEW) keeps `gn-last-project` keyed by user AND WORKSPACE — a project id is unique inside its workspace and meaningless outside it, so a record keyed by user alone would resolve `p_1` inside whichever workspace the viewer happened to be standing in (case 13.853) — and `gn-project-tabs`, a `projectId → tab` map keyed by USER, separately, because which tab you left each project on must outlive any single one of them. Both follow `use-last-workspace.ts`'s paid-for conventions rather than inventing a second set: three states (`unknown`/`none`/`remembered`, so no false sentence flashes before storage is read), every access wrapped because a blocked browser throws on the ACCESSOR, records belonging to another user answered `none` rather than cleared, and a module-scoped cache keyed by user+workspace so the remount on every client-side navigation paints on the first frame. The project PAGE consumes the tab half — `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx` seeds `view` from the map in an EFFECT (a `useState` initialiser would read storage on the server as `null` and disagree with the client at hydration, the same reason `theme-toggle.tsx` reads `gn-theme` in one) and writes on every `setView`, so a project reached from a card, bookmark or pasted link lands you where you left off, not only one reached through this item. `view`'s union moved out of the page into `use-last-project.ts` as `ProjectTab` so the page and the hook cannot drift about which tabs exist; `Record<typeof view, string>` on the page's `TAB_NOUN` still fails `typecheck` for a member added in one place and not the other. **Clicking either the item or a dropdown row writes the project record BEFORE the navigation lands** (one exported writer, shared with the page's route-authority path), so a destination that fails to render cannot leave the memory pointing at the project you were on before, and seeds the destination's tab to its remembered value or `board`. Cases 13.850–13.877. **Also in this round, and not about the nav:** `apps/web/test/setup.ts` now clears `localStorage` before every case, because nine test files mount this page and it now writes on every tab click — and its docblock records why NOTHING in that file may import from `src/`: the first version did, which pulled `providers.tsx` and `auth-client.ts` into the module graph before any test file's hoisted `vi.mock` could register, silently answering `{ kind: 'loading' }` to every session read in the suite. **A REVIEW ROUND THEN FOUND FIVE THINGS, ALL CLOSED, AND THREE WERE REAL DEFECTS THIS PARAGRAPH WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE DESCRIBED AS WORKING.** The Critical: `useWorkspaceProjects`' cache was keyed by workspace id alone — copying `useWorkspaceName`'s key onto a list that is NOT the same for everyone, since the server serves a non-owner only the projects they hold a membership in (`apps/server/src/read/visible-projects.ts`); sign-out is a client-side navigation, so sign in as someone with narrower access and the nav's first paint names projects they cannot open. It is keyed by user AND workspace now (case 13.878). The Important: the nav took its project from memory alone, and because `AppNav` renders INSIDE the project page, its effect is a descendant of the page's writer effect — **React commits passive effects child-first, measured rather than read out of the docs** — so on every arrival that is not through the item itself the nav named the project you had just left, and self-healed one navigation later. It now reads the route's project id (`useParams`), so the answer no longer depends on who wrote storage first (case 13.879). The third: the tab seed re-ran when the session landed, over a tab the person had clicked during the session read, because `tabUserId` is that effect's dependency and is `null` while loading; a chosen-here ref now outranks the memory (case 13.880) — with the residual limitation recorded rather than hidden: a tab clicked in that window is still not REMEMBERED, because there is no user id to key the write by. Two more were prose and an assertion: a docblock justifying the user+workspace key by a cross-workspace id COLLISION (impossible — `proj_projects.project_id` is the PRIMARY KEY and an entity id is a ULID belonging to exactly one workspace), corrected in the file; and 13.872's first assertion, which its own fixture satisfied — the record is now removed before the click, so only the click can produce it. **The fix round also broke fourteen cases in `test/settings-page.test.tsx` and that is worth recording as a class:** adding `useParams` to the nav made four test files' partial `next/navigation` mocks throw at import — only one rendered the nav, so three were latent — and `cacheKey`'s first spelling joined the two ids with a NUL byte, which made `grep` report its own file as BINARY and refuse to search it; it is `JSON.stringify([userId, workspaceId])` now.

**The workspaces-prototype-port plan (2026-09-15, Tasks 1–9) restyles the `/workspaces` card and page shell against the owner's `desktop.dc.html` export, and changes the card's CONTENT CONTRACT, not only its geometry.** Three things this round changes about what the card SAYS, stated plainly because a reader of the Round 19b/19c prose above would not otherwise learn any of them happened: **(1)** the card head gains a new meta line, `"{n} projects"` (`projectCountLine`, `apps/web/src/workspace-viewer.ts`) — `live.length` alone, deliberately never summed with `nameRestrictedProjectCount`, and gated on `!viewer.countsWithheld`, so 13.380's own guest-card property ("no number anywhere") still holds; the members chip already states the member count, so the prototype's own `"5 members · 2 projects"` phrasing would say the member count twice on one card and is not reproduced. **(2)** the per-viewer role sentence (13.357) MOVES — from under the workspace name to its own line BELOW the chip row, matching the prototype's own layout. **(3)** the per-project `<ul>` Round 19c left as plain project names is GONE, replaced by one truncated project line (`projectLine`, same file) plus a decorative chevron: up to `PROJECT_LINE_CAP` (3) names joined by `·`, and past the cap a counted suffix (`· +N more`) that drops its number to `· and more` under `countsWithheld` — the same withholding rule the meta line keeps, applied to a second surface. **The `project-card-${projectId}` entity-keyed testid this page used to carry is GONE with the `<ul>` it keyed** — case 3.154, previously ✅, is now 🚫 (`test-cases/03-foundation-ledger.md`); its sibling `workspace-card-${workspaceId}` (3.153) is untouched. Cases 13.405–13.407.

**Geometry, over the same shared `ui.css`/`globals.css` this style axis already drives:** the workspace monogram grows to a 44px ROUNDED SQUARE (`size="workspace"` on `Avatar`, a new `.gn-avatar--workspace` rule — `border-radius: 0.6875rem`, not a circle — plus `flex: none`, added in the whole-branch-review fix wave, Important 3: without it a long unbroken workspace name pulls width from every flex sibling including this one, and a rendered proof against the real stylesheets measured the square compressing to 23.62px wide while staying 44px tall; with `flex: none` it holds 44×44 regardless of name length); the chip's fill moves off `surface-raised` (which made every chip invisible against the card it sits on under `hig`/`minimal`) onto `fillTertiary`, with `minimal` going fully transparent (`background: transparent`, zero horizontal padding, the prototype's own treatment) and `neo` instead carrying the CARD's own colour plus an inset `shadow-sunken` — a well carved into the card rather than a tinted pill, the same neumorphic idiom `.ticket-type-list__row` already uses; the grid swaps `auto-fill` for `auto-fit` so three workspaces no longer sit at their minimum width with dead space beside them; and the card's own "door" (the clickable wrapper Round 19b built) becomes one flex column at the prototype's 20px padding, replacing three ad-hoc top-margins with one `gap`. The page gained its own `.gn-workspaces` shell, a page title, and a one-line summary (`"N workspaces you belong to"`, gated on a SUCCESSFUL non-empty read — `workspaces !== null && workspaces.length > 0`, both terms independently mutation-proven, case 13.406).

**The changes below rode along on this page's work and reach OTHER pages, recorded here so a reader of this file is not the last to find out.** `.gn-page-title`'s `font-size`/`line-height`/`font-weight`/`letter-spacing` addition is GLOBAL — it is the base rule, not a `/workspaces`-scoped override, and reaches every page that carries the class: `/account`, `/inbox`, `/invite/[token]`, `/mcp/authorize`, `/mcp/tokens`, `/settings`, `/workspaces`, every `/w/*` screen that owns one (workspace home, members, my-work, ticket types, the ticket-type editor — standalone route and inlined — and workflows), all of which previously fell through to the browser's own `2em` default with no tracking at all. (The rule's `letter-spacing` was first shipped at `-0.025em`, dividing the prototype's `-0.4px` tracking by the ROOT font size instead of the element's own 34px — corrected same-day to `-0.0118em`, matching the two sibling `.gn-account .gn-page-title`/`.gn-help__title` rules independently corrected the same way the same day.) Separately, `.gn-workspace-list__role`'s resize (`0.8rem` → `0.9375rem`, for the card head's own sake) silently reached its two OTHER borrowers — `/settings`'s workspace-name subtitle and the workspace-home header's meta line — since all three call sites shared one rule; both are now pinned back to `0.8rem` via a dedicated `.gn-workspace-list__role--pinned` modifier class added at those two call sites only, leaving the card's own (namesake) rule at the new size. **The pin was incomplete until this fix wave: the shared rule's `margin` also changed, `0.15rem 0 0` → `0`, and only the modifier's `font-size`/`line-height` were pinned back — not the margin (whole-branch review, Minor 6).** Restored on the modifier for byte-parity with the pre-Task-6 rule, but MEASURED rather than assumed to matter, because the review's own "2.4px, under-recorded" figure was reasoned, not rendered: at `/settings`, the `<h1 className="gn-page-title">` and this subtitle `<p>` are adjacent block siblings inside `.gn-shell` (a plain block container, not flex), so their margins COLLAPSE to the larger of the two — `.gn-page-title`'s own `0.4rem` bottom margin, which already exceeds the subtitle's `0.15rem` top margin on either side of this fix. Rendered both ways (a static fixture against the real stylesheets): the gap measures 6.4px identically with the modifier's `margin-top` at `0` or `0.15rem`. The pre-existing Task 6/7 comment on this rule already said as much ("its 0.15rem top offset is gone, so the gap above it is now `.gn-page-title`'s own 0.4rem bottom margin alone") — the restoration is real and matches the pre-Task-6 rule, but it is NOT a visible change at `/settings` today. The workspace-home header's meta line is the opposite case, and here the restoration IS load-bearing: its parent, `.gn-workspace-home__header-titles`, is a FLEX column with its own `0.15rem` `gap`, and flex layout does not collapse a child's own margin into that gap — rendered proof: the modifier's bare `0.15rem 0 0` there would ADD to the gap (measured 4.8px) rather than restore anything, doubling the pre-Task-6 spacing instead of matching it. The modifier's margin is therefore zeroed specifically at that call site (`.gn-workspace-home__header-titles .gn-workspace-list__role--pinned`, measured 2.4px — the correct, pre-Task-6-equivalent gap once the parent's own `gap` is counted once). Case 13.409.

**`.gn-page-title` is not the largest-blast-radius change on this branch — four rules in the SHARED `packages/ui/src/ui.css` are, and this is their first record.** That file is imported by every web route AND by `apps/desktop/src/main.tsx`, so all four reach the desktop client too, with no style-axis or page scoping. `.gn-button`'s padding went `0.4rem 0.85rem` → `0 1.125rem` (vertical padding now supplied entirely by the pre-existing `min-height: 2.75rem` floor, so every button in the product is ~9px wider); `.gn-field`'s gap went `0.45rem` → `0.4375rem`; `.gn-field__label` went `font-size: 0.8rem` with no declared `line-height` to `0.8125rem`/`1.125rem`; `.gn-field__input`'s padding went `0 0.85rem` → `0 0.875rem` and its `font-size` went `1rem` → `1.0625rem` — every input in the product is now 17px instead of 16px. All four are Task 3's deliberate port of the prototype's control metrics (see the spec's *Blast radius* section for the reasoning); what was missing until now was this record. `.gn-chip`'s fill/metrics change is the same shared-file shape and IS already recorded, two paragraphs below (the `fillTertiary` move, minimal's transparency, neo's carved-in treatment).

The invitation banner's restyle (flat `--gn-tint-warning` wash, no shadow, no accent edge) is recorded on its own bullet above. The founding form's spacing and heading now match the prototype (`margin-top` 1.1rem → 2.125rem, heading 1.05rem/no-line-height → 1.25rem/1.5625rem) — recorded on the Founding-a-workspace bullet below. `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `apps/web/src/workspace-viewer.ts`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/ui/src/components/avatar.tsx`, `apps/web/app/settings/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app-nav.tsx`, `apps/web/src/use-workspace-name.ts`, `apps/web/src/mcp-tokens-screen.tsx`, `apps/web/app/mcp/tokens/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/inbox-nav-link.tsx`

**Round 19e, next session, owner feedback: "the ticket type edit is opening in a separate route. it should open directly in the same route, same as the prototype." The half of the asymmetry above that WAS an un-requested change stopped being one.** The ticket-type editor is now inlined too, the same way Workflows and MCP tokens were: `TicketTypeEditorPane`/`NewTicketTypePane`, extracted from `.../ticket-types/[typeId]|new/page.tsx` into `apps/web/src/ticket-type-editor-pane.tsx` (the same `McpTokensScreen` shape — a `heading` prop so `/settings`'s nested `<h2>` doesn't collide with the standalone routes' own `<h1>`), inlined via a THIRD query param, `typeId`, layered under `tab=ticket-types` (`/settings?workspaceId=<id>&tab=ticket-types&typeId=<id|new>`). It is deliberately not folded into the existing `tabHref` helper, whose three callers are the sidebar's own links: if the sidebar carried `typeId` forward, opening a type and clicking Workflows and back would resurrect the editor instead of the list, so `typeId` is read out of the query only inside the `tab === 'ticket-types'` branch. The standalone routes keep working, now as thin wrappers around the same pane — nothing that already linked there breaks, and 10.65–10.70 (`apps/web/test/authoring-routes.test.tsx`) pass unchanged against the refactor. **The workflow BUILDER still does not move** — that half of the asymmetry stands on its own reasoning (a full-width canvas a 248px sidebar would crowd), unaffected by this round. Cases 10.71–10.76. `apps/web/src/ticket-type-editor-pane.tsx`, `apps/web/app/settings/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/settings/ticket-types/[typeId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/settings/ticket-types/new/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/settings-page.test.tsx`
- ✅ **The workspace home — `/w/[workspaceId]`** (Task 19) — the page a workspace's own name lands on, and until this commit there was none: a workspace was reachable only through a project link, and `/w/<id>` rendered the 404 (the e2e sweep's own comment recorded that, and is corrected in the same change set). It lists the projects this caller may be told the name of, each with the tickets they may see and a blocked chip when there are any, archived ones marked with a chip AND hidden behind a per-workspace disclosure; the caller's role as a sentence; *"N projects · name restricted"*; and four doors — My Work and Members (workspace-scoped path segments) and Ticket types and Workflows (`/settings?workspaceId=<id>&tab=…`, the workspace as a QUERY PARAM, which is why the four are literal paths and not one prefix). **MCP tokens was a fifth until Round 21 moved it to `/account`; Workflows became the second query-scoped door in Round 22.** **Ticket types joined MCP tokens as a non-`/w/<id>/…` door (Round 19e, following the ticket-type-editor inlining above): owner feedback — "the ticket type edit is opening in a separate route... repoint it" — since the bare `/w/<id>/settings/ticket-types` list this link and the header's own "Settings" button pointed at has no sidebar and its Edit button opened the editor as a third, separate route, the exact defect just closed on `/settings`. Both now target `/settings?workspaceId=<id>[&tab=ticket-types]`. Workflows is unchanged on purpose — its own editor, the builder, was never inlined into `/settings` either, so repointing its list would be cosmetic rather than closing an equivalent gap.** For an owner or admin it adds **archive / restore per project** and an **admin-gated new-project form**. **The read is `GET /v1/me/workspaces` and nothing new** — every number is Task 16's, so the card on `/workspaces` and this page cannot disagree about the same workspace; the cost is a directory read that answers for workspaces this page will not render. `viewerOf` and `restrictedLine` MOVED to `apps/web/src/workspace-viewer.ts` in the same change set rather than being copied, because two places deciding "may this person be told numbers" is the defect they were written to prevent. **Founding a project is TWO events in one batch** — `project.created` then `project.member_added` with `projectAdmin: true`, the sequence `seed-dev-workspace.ts` already uses: `visibleProjects` filters everyone but an OWNER to `eff.projects`, so an admin founding with one event would watch the project vanish on the re-read, and nobody could ever create a ticket in it. **Hiding a control is not enforcement, and the two halves fail at their own sites**: the client gate is `mayAdminister(role)`, and the server refuses `project.created`/`project.archived`/`project.restored` from a non-admin out of `resolveEffectiveRole` whatever the page rendered (case 13.388 drives all three at the real door with no UI). Every mutation READS the door's per-event answer and re-reads the directory rather than editing its own copy of the list. **Named absence:** a workspace `admin` who is not a member of project X is not told X's name here (rulings P32/P38), so X cannot be opened, archived or restored through this screen even though the door would accept that admin's claim — the restricted line discloses only the part of that gap the caller already holds a ticket id in. **Round 19b (2026-09-09), owner feedback:** the page gained a HEADER — a 56px/radius-14 monogram tinted from the workspace id, and a meta line *"N members · M active projects · you're an owner"* reading only served numbers, with the role as a CLAUSE from `viewerOf` (extended with a `clause` field beside its existing `sentence`, so the header and the `/workspaces` card's sentence read one source) — plus raised Members/Settings buttons at the header's end. Project cards now show an explicit **"Open board"** button (accent fill, for archived projects too — archiving is not a permission, so "Open read-only" is not adopted) rather than the name doubling as the link. The "Projects" heading and the archived toggle now share one row, and the toggle is still absent at zero (Task 18/19's rule, unmoved). The five doors gained NOTES, verbatim from the prototype's `wsLinks`, with the Members note's number following the same withheld-not-zero rule. The "New project" form is now `.gn-project-founding` (a raised card, inline row) — the same split that turned `/workspaces`' "New workspace" form into `.gn-workspace-founding` (a bare column, max-width 420px, per the owner's *"shouldn't be that big"*) — and non-admins now see a refusal note where the form would be. Cases 13.394–13.399. **Round 20 (2026-09-16), the desktop prototype port — owner ruling on the one item that reverses a decision either way.** The project list becomes a GRID of per-project raised cards (`repeat(auto-fit, minmax(17.5rem, 1fr))`), and **each card's informational body is now the DOOR** — a single `<Link>` named by the project — which reverses Round 19b's separate **"Open board"** button. That button is REMOVED rather than left beside the new door: a card that was a link and also held an "Open board" button would be the two-doors-to-one-target failure Round 19b named, arrived at from the other side. Archive/Restore cannot live inside the anchor (`<a>` may not contain `<button>`), so it sits as the door's SIBLING in the same card — which is where the prototype puts it too, and why this card is not clickable edge-to-edge the way `/workspaces`' is: that one could be, only because Round 19c had removed every control from it. The door's accessible name carries **", archived"** for an archived project, because `aria-label` overrides the visible Archived chip and a non-admin gets no Archive/Restore button to infer the state from. The "Projects" heading and its toggle left the `<Card>` that used to wrap the section (each project is its own card now, so an outer one would be a card holding cards); the archived list MERGED into the one grid, dimmed to 0.72 and badged IN PLACE via a single `filter(p => showArchived || !p.archived)` that preserves the served order — `[...live, ...archived]` would sink every archived project to the end, moving it twice on one click; the toggle took the reference's visible label (*"1 archived · show"* / *"Hide archived"*) while KEEPING the accessible name that says which workspace; and the five door-cards moved BELOW the projects and the founding form. **Four prototype elements are deliberately not transcribed**, each for a reason already in the repo: "Open read-only" (13.396 forbids it — archiving is not a permission), "0 archived · show" (13.397 forbids it), "Ask to be named" rows (rulings P32/P38 serve a COUNT, not rows), and "Delete this workspace" (the prototype's own handler refuses it as irreversible). **Named absence — the prototype's bottom "Workspace" card is NOT ported.** Its rename field is gated on admin, but `check-permission.ts:349-358` records that `workspace.renamed` is not in `ADMIN_ONLY_TYPES`, that a workspace GUEST is admitted for it today, and that which roles may rename a workspace "is a product decision no plan in this series has taken". An admin-gated field would therefore render a rule the product has not decided — unlike every other admin affordance here, whose hiding DISPLAYS a real server gate, there is no gate behind this one to display. **Owner: whoever next takes the rename-permission decision.** Case 13.413. **Round 21 (2026-09-16), owner corrections to the port — one of which reverses Round 20 by name.** **Archived projects now sort to the END of the disclosed grid** (`[...live, ...archived]`), reversing Round 20's single-filter served order: the "moves twice on one click" cost Round 20 argued from is unchanged and was ACCEPTED, not refuted — disclosure is a mode change, and in the disclosed mode retired work groups at the end. Case 13.413 is the same check pointing the other way, and its Round 20 fixture (archived project in the MIDDLE) is what made a one-line flip possible; the shared fixture would have been vacuous in either direction. The grid becomes a FIXED three-column layout with `/workspaces`' own 60rem→2 / 40rem→1 breakpoints, replacing `auto-fit`, so the two pages no longer arrange the same cards differently at the same width. **Archive/Restore now lights under the pointer** (`brightness(0.97)` light / `1.18` dark, the card hover's own measured pair — `.gn-button` carries no `:hover` rule anywhere in the product, so this is scoped to `.gn-project-card__actions` rather than added globally), and the card body deliberately stays unlit, which needed no change: the card's `:has(> .gn-project-card__door:hover)` matches the door as a DIRECT CHILD only. **The archived dimming moved from the card onto its door**, a deliberate deviation from the prototype: the prototype's archived card holds no control, this one holds Restore, and a button at 0.72 beside full-strength ones reads as disabled when it is the one control on an archived card that is always live. The four section cards lighten with the same pair, replacing a background swap that made this the one card surface in the product answering the pointer differently. **The header's Members/Settings buttons are REMOVED** (neither destination is orphaned: Members is a section card and a nav item, Settings is the nav's own workspace-scoped item), and `.gn-workspace-home__header-actions` went with them. **MCP tokens LEFT this page for `/account`** — see that bullet. Cases 13.387 (revised to four doors, with the absence asserted), 13.413 (reversed), 13.415. **Round 22 (2026-09-16), five more owner corrections, four shipped — the fifth ("once inside a workspace, the board nav bar item should be always visible") was put back to the owner and DECLINED, so Board stayed conditional on a project being in the route. SUPERSEDED HOURS LATER by Round 23, which removed the Board item outright (owner: "the board on the nav bar doesn't work. lets remove it") — see the nav bullet in §1.** **Workflows now opens INSIDE the settings shell** (`/settings?workspaceId=<id>&tab=workflows`), joining Ticket types as the second query-scoped door — owner: "clicking workflows from the project screen opens up a separate page. it should open up the workflows tab in the settings page." Round 19e had deliberately left it alone, reasoning that repointing closed no defect the way ticket types did (the workflow BUILDER was never inlined either, so the move looked cosmetic); the owner asked for it on its own merits, and the merit Round 19e missed is that the bare page has **no sidebar**, so following this card dropped you out of the shell with no way back to the other sections. The bare route still resolves and `authoring-routes.test.tsx` still drives it. **THE WHOLE CARD IS NOW THE DOOR**, via a stretched `::after` rather than `/workspaces`' full-bleed negative margin: that trick works there only because that card holds no controls, and an anchor stretched over THIS card would sit on top of Archive/Restore and eat its clicks while leaving it drawn and keyboard-focusable. The anchor keeps its own in-flow box (what assistive tech reads) and projects a transparent overlay for the pointer; the actions strip is raised above it. Three behaviours follow from hit-testing and all three are wanted: the card body lights the card, the button lights only itself (Round 21's rule, preserved exactly, because the anchor is not the button's ancestor), and a click anywhere but the button navigates. The focus ring moved onto the stretched layer, since a ring around the top third would describe a click target that is no longer the real one. **Every card in a row is now the tallest one's height** — `align-items: start` removed (it was the transcribed reference's, and it is what let a card with a blocked chip stand taller), `height: 100%` on the card, and `margin-top: auto` on the actions so the buttons line up rather than floating at ragged heights. **The same fix landed on `/workspaces`' cards** (owner asked for both), which needed a dedicated `.gn-workspace-card` class to hang it on: `.gn-workspace-list .gn-card` is the exact selector case 13.355 asserts does not exist. **The header gained `margin-top: 1.25rem`** — `.gn-shell` declares no top padding at all, so this header sat flush against the sticky nav; fixed here rather than on `.gn-shell`, which would have moved the first element of every route for a complaint about one. **The home can also RENAME the workspace** (2026-09-19, the owner's ruling) — a control offered to an owner or an admin and to nobody else. `workspace.renamed` had been in the vocabulary since Plan 1 with **no client UI at all**, and until the ruling the server asked **nobody** who may emit it, so a workspace GUEST could rename the container they were invited into; that absence is now closed server-side and the refusal **names the act** — *"Only a workspace owner or admin can rename a workspace"* — rather than producing the generic admin-only sentence. The control's own gate is `mayAdminister`, which is `role === 'owner' || role === 'admin'`, **the same expression as the server's `isWorkspaceAdmin`**, so the two cannot drift apart without one being edited. Hiding the control is not the enforcement — the server refuses the same act, and the control exists so a person learns that before typing. Cases 13.962–13.965. **AND THE NAME ITSELF IS NOW THAT CONTROL (owner, 2026-09-19: *"have the rename be there right on the header. THE CLICK TO RENAME."*, case `13.969`):** the workspace NAME in the header is a **real `<button>` inside the `<h1>`**, so clicking the name puts it into edit mode **in place** — an input where the heading was, carrying the current name, with **Save name**/**Cancel**, **Enter commits** and **Escape cancels**. A bare button replacing the heading would have cost the page its only first-level heading, and an `h1` carrying an `onClick` would have been **reachable by pointer only** — not focusable, not in the tab order, and announced as a heading rather than as something that does anything; **the property is one the previous control already had** (focusable, and it moved focus into the input), so it is preserved rather than newly claimed. **The separate `Rename` button is GONE** — the name does the job, so no second control stands in this header, which is the strongest form of the argument that comment was already making; **the header's own comment was re-aimed in the same change set**, because its operative clause named *"the `Rename` control beside the titles"*, a thing the page stopped having the moment the name became the control. **The accessible name still carries the WORKSPACE** (`Rename Convenience Corner`) and **the authority and the door are unchanged** (owner + admin, server-enforced; `workspace.renamed` through the ordinary ingest). **The four existing cases all still hold of the new shape and none would notice it changing** — they query `button[name^="Rename "]`, which the name-control carries, and every property they assert is equally true of a separate button — so `13.969` adds the three arms that discriminate: the visible text IS the name, the control takes focus, and exactly one rename control exists. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/workspace-viewer.ts`, `packages/ui/src/components/avatar.tsx`, `apps/server/src/permissions/check-permission.ts` **THE PROJECT DESCRIPTION (door A, 2026-09-19 — cases 13.976–13.987).** Each project card now draws a **short description**, and the New-project form can author one. The prototype has had the field all along — `{ name: "Delivery", desc: "Sprint work for the rollout" }`, drawn READ-ONLY at `desktop.dc.html:264` as a muted 15px line INSIDE the card's own door, between the name row and the chips — but **nothing let a person write one**: its `addProject` takes a name only and hard-codes `desc`. **Owner's ruling, verbatim:** *"New-project form gains an optional description input: go with this."* So the description rides `project.created` — **no new event, no second door** — and `projectCreatedPayload`, which is `.strict()`, gained an OPTIONAL `description` (refusing blank and over-500, and NOT trimming, for `displayName`'s recorded reasons). **The column is `text NULL`, and the nullability is the design rather than a default:** NULL is the ABSENCE of a claim — "nobody has described this project" — where `''` would be the CLAIM "this project has no description". It is the same withheld-vs-empty distinction `ProjectRef.ticketCount` draws, which is why the projection writes `payload.description ?? null` and why the client type spells NOT DESCRIBED as an absent key rather than an empty string. **⚠ THE HALF-WITNESSED CONTRACT IS CLOSED, NOT PROMISED.** The queue's own warning is that *"every `ProjectRef` construction site must carry it or the field arrives `undefined` on some route"*; enumerated rather than spot-checked, there are **two production sites** — `apps/server/src/routes/workspaces.ts` (the wire object) and `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts` (the client type's one construction site), every other hit being a consumer, and `packages/mcp-server`'s `read-tools.ts` building its own inline `{projectId, name}[]`, a different type. Rather than trusting two sites to stay in step, **the route ALWAYS sends the key** (`string | null`, never omitted) **and the mapper REFUSES a row that omits it** — so a third site that forgot the field fails loudly at the mapper instead of rendering as a correct-looking card that says nothing. **It rides with the NAME:** `visibleProjects` filters ROWS, not columns, so a caller not on a project receives no row and therefore no description — the disclosure decision is the one already made by the filter, and no new visibility rule is introduced. `apps/server/migrations/1789600000000_proj-projects-description.sql`, `apps/server/src/read/visible-projects.ts`, `apps/server/src/routes/workspaces.ts`, `packages/domain/src/events/membership.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/view-types.ts`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`
- ✅ **`/account` — the global account screen** — a client shell composing Appearance (Style + Theme,
  moved off `AppNav`), Profile, Your workspaces, Notifications, Sign-in & devices (Sessions +
  Passkeys), and **Connected assistants** (the MCP token list, moved here in Round 21 — see its own
  bullet in §14; appended LAST rather than inserted into the card order, because that order is the
  prototype's `isAccount` screen and the prototype has no MCP section to transcribe a position from,
  and because it lands beside the two other cards about credentials that can act as you). **Profile (this task):** the caller's `name` is editable through better-auth's core
  `updateUser` (no plugin needed — `authClient.updateUser({ name })`, verified against the installed
  `better-auth@1.7.2` client's generated types rather than assumed); `email` renders READ-ONLY as
  plain text, not a disabled `.gn-field__input`, because changing it needs a verification round-trip
  this plan does not build (spec non-goal), and the established "disabled input" read-only pattern
  would put the value where `getByText`/a screen reader's read-everything pass can't see it. A save
  failure renders the door's own `error.message` verbatim in a `role="alert"` when the door sends one —
  never a paraphrase. **Named absence:** `saveName` reads only `result.error?.message`; better-auth's
  own client type also allows an error with no `message` (only `status`/`statusText`), and that shape
  collapses to `{ error: null }` — the save silently fails with no alert at all. Untested and unfixed
  in this task; the shipped `authClient.updateUser` refusal path always carries a `message` today, but
  a future caller of this same `saveName` shape should not assume that holds. Only the current
  session's user renders the section at all — no user, no Profile. **Your workspaces (Task 4):** every
  workspace the signed-in caller belongs to, from `dataSource.query.getMyWorkspaces()`, listed with two
  real fields only — `role` (served verbatim from `proj_memberships.role`, the same field `/workspaces`'
  own card renders as a sentence) and `projects.length` ("N project"/"N projects"); `memberCount` and
  `nameRestrictedProjectCount` are real but deliberately unused here, since this is a compact convenience
  list beside `/workspaces`' own fuller card, not a second copy of it. **Named absence, disclosed rather
  than silently decided:** a failed read collapses to the same empty list an honestly-empty answer would
  produce — this section is a convenience list, not `/account`'s primary content the way `getMyWorkspaces`
  is `/workspaces`' own (whose 401 redirects to `/sign-in`, case 13.298) — matching the same silent-omission
  treatment `AppNav`'s `use-workspace-name.ts` already gives this exact read on this exact page; a
  `ShellRefusal` treatment is a one-line `error`-state addition if a reviewer wants the stronger form
  instead. Renders only once the read resolves (`workspaces !== null`); no heading or list while it's in
  flight. **Notifications (Task 6):** a checkbox row per real `NotificationKind` — `assignment`,
  `mention`, `contested-claim`, `date-approaching`, `stale-ticket`, no invented "email" column and no
  "weekly digest" row, since only in-app Inbox delivery is real — reading and writing the global,
  per-kind mute list `GET`/`PUT /v1/me/notification-preferences` (Task 5) serves, through the new
  `notificationPreferences` member on `AppServices`. Checked means NOT muted: `mutedKinds` is a mute
  list, not an enable list, so "everything on" needs no second constant kept in sync with the five
  kinds as they grow. A toggle click is optimistic — the row flips immediately, then reverts to the
  freshly-reloaded true state if the `save()` rejects — the same distrust `/inbox`'s own `markRead`
  catch has for its optimistic flip. **A second named absence, alongside Your workspaces' above:** a
  failed `load()` also collapses to a value indistinguishable from an honest empty answer
  (`{ mutedKinds: [] }`, everything rendered on), for the same reason — a convenience control, not this
  page's primary content — but it is deliberately NOT `EMPTY_NOTIFICATION_PREFERENCES`: that constant's
  own docblock defines it as what a person who has *never saved a preference* reads, a claim about
  their history a failed read has no standing to make; the literal is unlabelled precisely so it makes
  no such claim. **Sign-in and devices, Sessions half (Task 7):** every active session
  `authClient.listSessions()` answers (better-auth's core session route, no plugin needed — confirmed
  against the installed `better-auth@1.7.2` client's generated types, which differ from the plan's own
  guess: `createdAt`/`updatedAt` come back as `Date` instances, not strings, because the client revives
  ISO date strings on the way in — the adapter converts explicitly rather than assuming either shape),
  each row showing an honest device label (a short substring match against `userAgent` — e.g. "Chrome
  on Mac" — never a fabricated specific, falling back to "Unknown device" for anything unmatched or
  absent) and, for every session but the current one (matched by token against
  `authClient.useSession()`'s own `session.token`), a "Sign out" control that calls
  `authClient.revokeSession({ token })` and re-lists afterward. No optimistic removal — unlike
  Notifications' toggle, there is no local edit that would be honest to show before the server confirms
  a session is actually gone. "Last active" goes through the domain's one `formatIstInstant` (via this
  package's `formatDateTime`), the same clock rule every other date on this shell follows, never a raw
  ISO slice. **Named absence, on the same silent-omission footing as Your workspaces and Notifications
  above:** a failed `list()` collapses to the same empty render an honestly-empty answer produces.
  **A second named absence:** a refused revoke's `error` is read and discarded — unlike Profile's save
  refusal, no `role="alert"` (or any other signal) tells a caller "Sign out" was refused; the session
  simply fails to leave the list. **Sign-in and devices, Passkeys half (Task 8):** the credentials
  `authClient.passkey.listUserPasskeys()` answers, each row carrying a delete control whose ACCESSIBLE
  name is its own passkey's (`Delete passkey {name}`, so three credentials are three distinguishable
  controls rather than three announcements of "Delete"), a named empty state ("You have no passkeys
  yet."), and a name field plus "Add a passkey" that runs the real WebAuthn registration ceremony
  through `authClient.passkey.addPasskey({ name })`. An empty name is allowed through deliberately —
  the plugin then labels the credential from its AAGUID, which is a better answer than a disabled
  button with no reason. Add and remove both RE-READ the server rather than editing the list locally:
  the stored label is the server's answer, so a locally-invented row could show a name the account
  does not carry. **The three client member names were settled by the COMPILER, not by documentation
  or by a grep:** only `signIn.passkey` and `passkey.addPasskey` are hand-declared by
  `@better-auth/passkey@1.7.2`'s client plugin; `listUserPasskeys` and `deletePasskey` are generated
  from the server endpoint paths (`/passkey/list-user-passkeys`, `/passkey/delete-passkey`) by the
  same `PathToObject`/`CamelCase` machinery the Sessions half documents, and a typecheck probe WITH a
  negative control (an invented member, which failed `TS2339`) is what rules out the vacuous reading
  that the surface is `any`. **Unlike every other section on this page, a refusal here is NOT silent
  and NOT collapsed:** a refused add or remove renders better-auth's own message verbatim in a
  `role="alert"` (`gn-refusal gn-refusal--server` — **corrected by the final whole-branch review**:
  this used to be the same `.gn-shell__refusal` class Profile's save refusal uses, copied forward from
  Task 3's ruling without noticing `PasskeysSection` lives in `@gisik-nokma/app-core`, not `apps/web`,
  and `.gn-shell__refusal` is defined only in `apps/web/app/globals.css` — invisible on `apps/desktop`,
  which never loads that stylesheet. `gn-refusal`/`gn-refusal--server` is the real, visually identical
  app-core-level equivalent, already used the same way by three other app-core views), and a refusal
  that carries NO message still surfaces, because `error?.message ?? null` would read a message-less
  refusal as success and a silent no-op after somebody touched a fingerprint sensor is
  indistinguishable from the passkey having been saved. Only the passive list read keeps the
  silent-omission treatment its three siblings have. **The passkey list is also the one mount-time
  read on this page that is GATED** (on `user !== null`): it lands under `/api/auth/*`, charged to the
  shared `auth:` bucket, and sits behind better-auth's session middleware — measured as a real 401 in
  `apps/server/test/auth/sign-in-flows.test.ts`, not asserted in prose — so an anonymous `/account`
  load would spend a unit of everyone's sign-in budget on a request that cannot succeed. **Named cost,
  disclosed rather than slipped in:** for a SIGNED-IN visitor this does add a third `auth:` charge per
  `/account` load (`/get-session`, `/list-sessions`, `/passkey/list-user-passkeys`) against a
  `RATE_LIMIT_AUTH_MAX` of 10; `/passkey/*` is absent from `SIGN_IN_PREFIXES`, so none of it touches
  the tighter `signin:` bucket. **And the CEREMONIES cost more than the page load, which is the half of
  this disclosure an operator actually has to act on:** counted from `@better-auth/passkey`'s own
  `dist/client.mjs`, a successful Add is **3** units (`generate-register-options`,
  `verify-registration`, then the re-list), an Add aborted at the prompt is **1**, a Delete is **2**,
  and a passkey sign-in click on `/sign-in` is **2** (`generate-authenticate-options`,
  `verify-authentication` — so a completed click costs two, not one). Arriving on `/account` and
  registering once is six units of ten, and a person who fumbles the prompt a few times and retries can
  exhaust the window on a key shared across every browser behind the web container. **This does NOT
  weaken the rate-limit-neutral constraint and the two must not be run together:** that constraint is
  specifically about an ANONYMOUS `/sign-in` PAGE LOAD, which still charges ZERO — measured in a real
  browser as one `GET /v1/auth/methods`, a `/v1/*` route the limiter never charges, and nothing under
  `/api/auth/*`. Every figure above is a deliberate action by a signed-in person; the remedy if it ever
  bites is an operator raising `RATE_LIMIT_AUTH_MAX`. **The page is now STYLED, closing the cumulative named absence
  the final whole-branch review had disclosed** (owner, 2026-09-15: "take the styling of the account
  page and fix the styling in the code"). The absence was real: every class the sections emit carried
  zero rules, the lone exception being `.gn-account ul`'s list spacing, so every section wrapper,
  heading, row and field rendered as plain text and default browser control chrome. Tasks 2–8 had each
  correctly deferred its own section's styling, and the sum of five reasonable deferrals was a page
  with none. **THE REFERENCE IS THE DESKTOP PROTOTYPE'S `isAccount` SCREEN**
  (`design-system/interactive/desktop.dc.html:1471-1529`), which is what this spec names
  (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-account-help-screens-design.md:8`) — recorded explicitly
  because the first cut of this work styled against the MOBILE canvas's screen labelled "Account",
  which is the phone's More TAB (an identity header over three groups of navigation links) and not
  this page at all; the owner's "it still doesn't match the Style" is what caught it, and every value
  below was then measured against the real reference in a rendered browser rather than eyeballed.
  `apps/web/app/globals.css` carries the documented `/account` block: a **centred 680px column**
  (`.gn-account__column`, verified 680px live) holding an `<h1>Your account</h1>` and the prototype's
  own lead sentence, then one card per section at the prototype's 20px padding / 20px column gap,
  built from `--gn-surface-raised`, `--gn-shadow-raised` and `--gn-radius-card` — the same three
  tokens `.gn-settings__sidebar` already uses. Section headings are the prototype's 20px/25px/600.
  **Rows are TRANSPARENT**, separated by the card's gap and their own 44px (48px for a workspace)
  min-height, which is the prototype's own answer and also removes a `neo` defect an intermediate cut
  carried: a `--gn-surface-sunken` row is invisible under `neo`, where `tokens.ts` collapses
  surface/raised/sunken to one hex, the same bug already recorded for `.ticket-type-list__row` —
  confirmed in a dark-`neo` screenshot, not assumed. Notifications rows now carry the prototype's
  **switch** (51x31 track, 27px knob, `order: 2` so it sits at the trailing edge) implemented by
  restyling the REAL `<input type="checkbox">` rather than building a `role="switch"` div, so the
  label association, keyboard behaviour and checked state the existing suite asserts are untouched;
  the knob is `--gn-surface-raised` with a `--gn-border-strong` ring rather than the prototype's
  `#fff`, because a literal reddens the token-discipline suite and there is deliberately no
  always-white role. Workspace rows became **doors** — a real `<a href="/w/{id}">` filling the row
  with a `ui` `Avatar` (tinted by a hash of the workspace's own id, since `WorkspaceRef` has no
  colour field to match the prototype's seed `w.bg`) and an accent "Open" at the trailing edge, the
  same `.gn-workspace-card__door` shape, with `aria-label="Open <name>"` so the link is named once
  rather than repeating the name `Avatar` gives itself. **This row navigation is the one piece of new
  BEHAVIOUR in the port** (the list was inert) and it stays inside the spec's non-goals, which forbid
  membership changes from `/account`, not navigation. Sign-in and devices gained a **hedged empty
  state** ("No signed-in devices to show.") — it was previously a bare card with a heading and
  nothing under it, measured live — worded that way because the page collapses a FAILED read to `[]`
  and so cannot distinguish "no devices" from "no answer". Both cards gained the prototype's
  footnotes, kept only where TRUE: "Signing a device out does not remove what it recorded" (history
  is append-only) and "These apply everywhere. A workspace cannot override them for you."
  (preferences are a global server-owned row). **What is deliberately NOT ported** is the
  prototype's per-row notification notes ("Email and in-app", "One email on Monday") and its Weekly
  digest row: no email delivery exists for notifications, so those are the invented-data trap the
  spec designs around. **AND ITS PER-DEVICE SIGN-OUT BUTTON, which the prototype draws TINTED — the
  one prototype detail this port refuses, on a measurement rather than a preference.** A tinted
  version was built and then removed: measured in a real browser across all six theme x style
  combinations, `danger` text on `surfaceSunken` is **3.84:1** and on `surfaceRaised` **4.29:1**
  (light/`neo`), both under the 4.5 this 0.9375rem label owes, while the shared solid `Button
  variant="danger"` pairs `dangerText on danger` at **5.0511:1** and is the only one of the three that
  `CONTRAST_PAIRS` declares and gates — `danger`-as-TEXT is declared nowhere in this product. So the
  solid button stays and no class was added. **Two PRE-EXISTING findings that measurement turned up,
  named rather than rediscovered and NOT fixed here** (both are product-wide palette decisions, not
  `/account`'s): `.ticket-type-editor__issues` (`danger` on `surfaceSunken`) measures the same 3.84,
  and `.gn-contest-banner__action--discard` (`danger` on `surfaceRaised`) measures 4.29 under
  light/`neo` — `neo` being the aggravating factor, since it collapses `surfaceRaised` onto the same
  near-white paper as `surfaceSunken`, dropping a pairing that clears 5.05 in `hig`. Zero colour
  literals (gated) and zero new `CONTRAST_PAIRS` rows — not because a row was considered and
  declined, but because the tint could not have been DECLARED at 4.5 in the first place: at 3.84 it
  would have failed the moment it was added to that array, which is exactly what the array is for. The three docblocks
  that had DISCLOSED the absence in prose (`workspaces-section.tsx`, `sessions-section.tsx`,
  `passkeys-section.tsx`) are retracted in the same change set. **Card ORDER is now the spec's, which is the
  prototype's** (owner, 2026-09-15: "change the card order"): Profile, Appearance, Notifications,
  Your workspaces, Sign-in and devices, Passkeys. The desktop reference runs Profile →
  Notifications → Your workspaces → Sign-in and devices, and the spec's own section numbering (§3-§7)
  is that same order with Appearance inserted after Profile, so the two agree and this is what they
  agree on — replacing the order the sections happened to land in as Tasks 2-8 each appended its own,
  and satisfying §2's "composed in the prototype's order", which the page had not actually done.
  Passkeys stays last and adjacent to Sessions because it is not a section of its own in the spec:
  §7 covers both as one "Sign-in & devices" concern. **Rendering those two as ONE card rather than
  two is the remaining divergence from §7** and is deliberately not done here. **THE APPEARANCE CARD WAS REBUILT TO AN OWNER REFERENCE IMAGE**
  (2026-09-15, "the appearance section also needs to be made like the attached") — it is the one card
  with NO prototype counterpart, since it is this page's own section per spec §4, so that image is
  its only design authority. Both axes are now labelled `SegmentedControl`s showing every option at
  once instead of a `Select` trigger and an icon-only cycle button: Style as `Apple HIG` / `Minimal`
  / `Neomorphic` with a line underneath describing the selected style, Theme as `System` / `Light` /
  `Dark`, and one footnote closing the card — "Appearance is per device, not per workspace — nobody
  else sees your choice", which is TRUE because both axes live in `localStorage` (`gn-style`,
  `gn-theme`) and are applied to `documentElement`, never sent to the server. The style descriptions
  were written against `tokens.ts` rather than from impression: `minimal` "has no shadow in either
  theme — its cards/fields are flat" and shares `hig`'s colour mapping outright, so shadow alone is
  the honest difference named between those two. **The Theme control keeps THREE segments where the
  reference shows two**, which is the single deliberate departure from the image: `system` is both
  the default state and the only way back to the device's own preference once a choice is made, so a
  two-segment control could neither display the state the page starts in nor return to it — the
  argument `theme-toggle.tsx`'s own docblock had already made, now pinned by a case. **One further
  known difference, named rather than discovered:** the rules live in `globals.css`, which
  `apps/desktop` never loads (`apps/desktop/src/main.tsx:6` imports only `@gisik-nokma/ui/ui.css`,
  and `grep -rn "gn-account" apps/desktop apps/mobile` is empty), so the three `app-core` sections
  render styled in web and still-unstyled in desktop — unchanged from before, not a regression;
  fixing that means promoting the block to `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, a deliberate cross-app decision
  and not part of a styling pass.
  `apps/web/app/account/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/account/profile-section.tsx`,
  `apps/web/src/account/workspaces-section.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/account/notifications-section.tsx`,
  `packages/app-core/src/account/notification-preferences-gateway.ts`,
  `packages/app-core/src/online/notification-preferences.ts`, `apps/web/src/providers.tsx`,
  `apps/web/src/account/account-clients.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/account/sessions-client.ts`,
  `packages/app-core/src/account/sessions-section.tsx`,
  `packages/app-core/src/account/passkey-client.ts`,
  `packages/app-core/src/account/passkeys-section.tsx`
- ✅ **Founding a workspace** — a name field on `/workspaces` calling `POST /v1/workspaces`, routing the founder into their own workspace on the 201. It sits OUTSIDE the list's three branches on purpose. **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-20 — this read: *"`getMyWorkspaces` refuses against the shipped server, so a form nested in the empty-state arm would be a form nobody could ever reach"*. That read is gone: rulings P32/P38 served `getMyWorkspaces` and the register emptied on 2026-09-19, so the empty-state arm IS reachable and the placement's stated reason has expired.** The placement is kept and the owner is named at the page: *whoever next touches `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`* — and the person who cannot be told which workspaces they are in is exactly the one who most needs to found one. The empty state gained the second sentence it always needed ("— or create your own."). The name is submitted AS TYPED: trimming would repair a claim the person made and would make the door's own 400 unreachable for the one input it exists to refuse. **HIG surface (Task 4a, 2026-09-08):** the form carries its own `surface-raised` panel — it sits beside the list rather than inside a card, so it needed one — and the name field adopts the shared `.gn-field`, keeping its `htmlFor`/`id` association and the label text exactly. The input keeps its border, which is the one ruled exception to R1(c): an empty field has no label inside it to do the identifying. **Neo restyle (Task 15, 2026-09-09):** the panel's literal `border-radius: 12px` is replaced by `.gn-card`'s own token roles (`var(--gn-radius-card)`, plus `box-shadow: var(--gn-shadow-raised)`, which it never had) — under neo it now reads as a raised surface like every card on the route, instead of a flatter, smaller-cornered outlier beside them (case 13.355). **Each workspace card also gains its own caller ROLE, rendered beneath the name (case 13.357, the additions table's one owner-approved invented row on this screen)** — `WorkspaceRef.role` was disclosed by `GET /v1/me/workspaces` since the route first answered but never read by the client; **the "other seven invented rows stay unbuilt" clause that stood here is superseded by the owner's 2026-09-09 `/workspaces` brief** — member counts and the ticket/blocked chips have real server data as of Task 16 (`memberCount`, `ticketCount`, `blockedCount` on `GET /v1/me/workspaces`), the invitation banner is Task 17's caller-scoped read, and avatars/Help/Account are Task 18. The RULE the clause protected is unchanged: a field is rendered only once a server read computes it, and a number the caller may not count is withheld rather than painted. The CARD that renders them is Task 18; Task 16 is server-first. **Cases 13.298–13.299 (2026-09-08):** `/workspaces` now also carries a one-way accessible-name floor in the signed-in sweep — it had none, despite the plan saying it did — and two browser cases pin the states the prototype never drew. 13.299 asserts the empty state and the founding button, in that ORDER, because the form renders outside all four branches and so proves nothing about the answer if asserted first. 13.298 pins the one thing every other case only assumed: that a 401 moves a REAL browser to `/sign-in`. Its jsdom twin awaits `expect(push).toHaveBeenCalledWith('/sign-in')`, and a double that records a call is not a browser that moved. **Spacing/heading match the prototype, 2026-09-15 (the workspaces-prototype-port plan's Task 9):** `.gn-workspace-founding`'s `margin-top` grows `1.1rem` → `2.125rem`, and `.gn-workspace-founding__heading`'s `font-size` grows `1.05rem` → `1.25rem` with a new `line-height: 1.5625rem` (previously unset). Geometry only — the form stays the bare column it always was, `.gn-project-founding`'s raised-card treatment (the workspace-home "New project" form) is untouched and still the one ruled exception, and 13.394's own assertion (not a raised card) is unaffected by either change. `packages/app-core/src/online/founding.ts`, `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`
- ✅ **The people page, gated by the server's own 403** — the roster from `GET /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/members`, and for owners and admins the mint form, the pending list and per-invite withdraw. **The role gate is the pending read's 403 (`INVITE_ADMIN_ONLY_BODY`), not a client-side role check** — an adaptation, because Task 15b gates on the caller's role from `/v1/me/workspaces` and `getMyWorkspaces` refuses whole, so no client on this branch can read it and a role-only mapper would be routing around a refusal. The door already answers 403 to a non-admin because an invitation's address is PII, so the read IS the question, asked of the thing that knows; the cost is one round trip a member makes and a 403 in the server's log. A member sees the roster, no invite machinery, and the door's own sentence rather than a silence they must interpret. The role picker offers no `owner` and the projects field is hidden on `guest` with the KEY omitted from the body, both matching refusals `inviteSentPayload` would otherwise make. **The roster itself is now the prototype's members screen (2026-09-18, cases 13.760–13.766):** `desktop.dc.html@6d7b67c0a52c:651-798` draws a five-column table and two side panels, and the port is partial BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by omission — `readMembers` selects `user_id`, `u."name"` and `role` (`apps/server/src/routes/builder-reads.ts:254`) — plus `u."email"` since 2026-09-19, and NOTHING ELSE, so the Person and Role columns are what the wire can fill and **Project access, Assigned, Last seen and the "Ticket-only access" panel with its "Add to workspace" button (`:700-718`) are all deliberately ABSENT** — named as such in the page's own file docblock and in the commit message, not left to be inferred from an empty column, and not minted as catalogue cases because they are scope decisions about surface nobody wrote rather than scenarios with an assertion. **(The `Email` line inside the Person cell, `:675`, was in that list and has LEFT it — see the fallback paragraph below. It is the one entry whose status changed, and it changed by an owner instruction rather than by a port.)** **The name fallback (2026-09-19, owner instruction, cases `13.958`–`13.960`): when a member's name is ABSENT the roster shows their EMAIL instead, and the address travels on `RosterMember.email` — never on `Actor`, which is also a ticket's `assignee` and which an offline replica synthesises as `{id, name: id}` with no addresses at all.** The population this serves is the **ORDINARY email sign-up, not an edge case**: better-auth's magic-link plugin creates the user with `name: name || ""` and this product's sign-in page sends no name (`app/sign-in/page.tsx`), so **every magic-link member has `name === ''`** and used to render an empty cell, a BLANK monogram disc, and a control announcing `"Role for "` — all three reading the same possibly-empty string, which is how they came to be broken together and why they now read ONE resolved label (`memberLabel`). **The phone population is the mirror and the two cannot overlap:** `auth.ts` sets `getTempName: (phone) => phone`, so a phone sign-up's name IS the phone and the fallback never fires for it. **`user."email"` is WITHHELD rather than blanked where it is SYNTHETIC** — a phone-only address is `<phone-digits>@phone.gisik-nokma.invalid`, i.e. a phone number wearing a non-contactable domain, so `readMembers` omits the key entirely for those rows, and the predicate deciding it lives in `apps/server/src/auth/synthetic-address.ts` beside the one spelling of the domain. Withholding costs no behaviour precisely because those rows always have a name. **The door is `no-guests`, NOT admin-only** (`routes/builder-reads.ts:664`), so the address is visible workspace-wide to every non-guest member — a breadth disclosed to the owner before the build and ruled on in terms. The `<h1>` is the prototype's `Members` (it read `People` until 2026-09-18; the nav item that reaches it already said Members). The summary line is DERIVED from the roster rather than literal — "You can see N people [in <workspace>] · O owner(s), A admin(s). Membership and roles are per workspace." — counting owners and admins rather than asserting the prototype's hardcoded "one owner", and scoped to what THIS viewer may be told (the read hides guests from a non-admin), with the `in <name>` clause omitted rather than faked when `useWorkspaceName` answers null. **`role` is carried end to end:** `RosterMember = Actor & { role: string; email?: string }` (`packages/app-core/src/view-types.ts:60`) is an `Actor` with fields ADDED, so `rosterByActorId` and the board's join are unaffected, and `role` is a plain `string` so the client is not a second copy of the role list to drift from; `mapActors` reads it with `asString` and REFUSES a member that arrives without one (`packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts:444`), because "the server did not say" and "this person is a member" are different claims. States are rendered rather than guessed: a roster-read refusal (`ShellRefusal`), the loading line, the empty roster ("No members are listed here." — `[]` is one shape for both an empty roster and the 404 a guest gets, and a workspace always has an owner, so the sentence says what is listed rather than who exists), and the member-vs-admin split via the invite door's own 403. The new CSS family (`apps/web/app/globals.css:1407-1576`) is built on the shared `--gn-*` role tokens per the mapping table at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-16-board-prototype-port.md:33-52`, with no literal colours. **The styling pass of 2026-09-18 (cases 13.886–13.888) MEASURED the screen against the prototype instead of assuming the gap, and found four of the five treatments its dispatch named to be the prototype's values already** — the card (`desktop.dc.html:658`), the tray header (`:662`), the 17px body over an 820px floor (`:659`/`:672`) and the cell padding (`:672`) are pinned as VALUES by 13.888, which asserts declarations rather than the existence of a rule. **The one treatment the family really was missing is the ZEBRA, and it lives in the prototype's DATA rather than its markup** — `memberRows` sets `style: i % 2 ? "background:var(--fill4)" : ""` (`:3077`) on the row drawn at `:671`, which a port driven by reading the JSX never sees; `.gn-members__row:nth-child(even)` now carries `--gn-fill-quaternary` (13.887), on the parity the prototype selects and NOT the odd parity the product's two other zebras use (`packages/ui/src/ui.css:1088` and `:2612`, both ported from this same construct) — that inversion is recorded at the rule with a named owner rather than harmonised in either direction. **The invite form's two native fields wear the product's `.gn-field` pattern from 2026-09-18 (13.886)**, copied from the sibling at `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/page.tsx:812-821` rather than invented, with no handler, state or conditional moved. **The `Channel` and `Role` fields wear the field pattern's LABEL half, ruled 2026-09-18 (AMENDMENT 2, owner-approved) and shipped here — case 13.889**: `.gn-field` + `.gn-field__label`, with no native-control class passed to them. The ruling's words: *"label it inside `.gn-field` … but pass no native-control class to it."* The reason first recorded for leaving them bare was FALSE and an independent review refuted it at the line — it said `.gn-field__input` is "a class for NATIVE controls" a `Select` cannot take, and the shared component puts that class on its own trigger (`packages/ui/src/select/select.tsx:254`, plus `:231` and `:432`) — which is also why 13.889 asserts the LABEL half rather than the absence of that class: it is on every `Select` in the product either way. **AND THERE IS NO VISUAL RESIDUE, which this sentence first claimed and an independent review refuted at the line**: it said these rows show a 15px trigger beside two 17px inputs, and that 15px came from the PROTOTYPE's inline style rather than from this product's cascade — `.gn-select__trigger` declares no `font-size` at all (`packages/ui/src/ui.css:1604-1612`) while the `.gn-field__input` it also wears declares `1.0625rem` (`:3866-3875`, and `packages/ui/src/select/select.tsx:254` puts both on the trigger). Equal specificity, later rule wins: **17px, the same as the inputs and the same size the prototype draws** (`desktop.dc.html:754`, `:760`). The alignment is total. **And the INVITATION HALF OF THIS SCREEN HAS NO CSS FAMILY AT ALL**: a repo-wide sweep finds `.gn-invite-mint*` and `.gn-invite-pending*` in `apps/web/app/globals.css` exactly once — `__url`'s `font-family` — and the sweep's control reaches `.gn-members` from the same run, so this is a measured null and not a failed search; the real-browser instrument says the same thing from the other side, since `apps/web/e2e-stack/non-text-contrast-roster.json` records `input` and `select` on `/members` wearing Chrome's own UA border (`rgba(118,118,118,1)`). **What is genuinely unstyled is narrower than "the whole half", and the first version of this sentence overstated it**: the mint form's own container, its `<h2>`, the pending list's own `<h2>` and `<ul>`, and the two bare label spans have no rule; the minted-link card is a `Card` (`gn-card`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css:160-165`), the submit is a `Button` and both pickers are the shared `Select` — all styled shared families, and a next task should not go and re-style a `Card`. **This is a SCOPE CALL recorded rather than fixed** — it is a family to port rather than a treatment to align, and this change's dispatch bounds the pass; owner: whoever takes the next task on this screen. **THE PENDING LIST'S HEADING NOW SHIPS (2026-09-20, case `16.46`)** — `PendingList` draws `<h2 className="gn-invite-pending__heading">` reading `` `Pending invitations · ${rows.length}` ``, the authority's own string (`desktop.dc.html:725`, defined at `:3091` as `"Pending invitations · " + s.invites.length`), which the product had rendered **no heading for at all**. Two properties are the point rather than the copy: the count is `rows.length`, **the same array the `<ul>` maps, read in the same expression**, so the heading and the rows cannot disagree — a count fed separately would be a second, weaker answer to a question the list already answers; and it renders at zero as `· 0`, because that is what the authority draws (unconditionally inside its `isInviteAdmin` block, `:722`–`:725`) and what the empty sentence below already claims. The heading is ONE text node rather than a label plus a `<span>`, deliberately — an accessible name inserts a space between sibling elements, so a composed name would not be the authority's string. It joins the unstyled family above rather than opening a second one. **The per-row role control is now DRAWN (2026-09-18, cases 13.824–13.828), and the reason it was withheld had to be CORRECTED rather than merely deleted.** The cell's own comment said the prototype's `<select>` was omitted because the product had "NO per-member role-change door at all: no route accepts one, and `role` is written only by membership creation" — both halves measured false: `POST /events` (`apps/server/src/routes/events.ts`) is the generic client batch door with no per-type constraint, and `proj-memberships.ts` upserts `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE SET role = excluded.role`. The conclusion was right for a reason that did not hold — what was missing was the GUARD, not the door: an admin could already submit a role change, and could demote the sole owner and promote themselves with it. Task 1 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-membership-role-guard` closed that in both layers (actor authority in `check-permission.ts`, world state in the rule engine), and the cell now draws the control. The change submits `membership.granted` through the ordinary ingest door — **no new route**, and the entity id is `membershipId(workspaceId, userId)`, the key the guard reads the target's current role through — and re-reads the roster on a commit rather than setting local state, so the cell shows the role the SERVER holds. A refusal renders the rule and the engine's reason under the table, because a swallowed answer would show the person their own click as a fact. **Which refusals can reach this control was corrected on 2026-09-18 after an independent review challenged the first version of that sentence, which named the two rules this plan added and was wrong about both**: `grant-owner-requires-owner` fires only on a payload granting `owner` and this control's options cannot build one, and `last-owner` needs the SERVER's row to hold `owner` while the rendered row is editable, which the row predicate suppresses — so it is reachable through a stale roster read rather than in the ordinary case. The arm is there for that race, for the ordinary `permission.role` refusal, for a malformed payload, and for transport failures. **The options are `admin / member / guest` and `owner` is deliberately absent** — the invite form's judgement, the prototype's own `Owner` option notwithstanding, because there is no ownership-transfer flow and promoting somebody is a task of its own rather than a dropdown entry. **The control is drawn only where it can STATE the current value**, which is narrower than the prototype (whose `canEditRole` is viewer-scoped, data `desktop.dc.html:3061`, and whose options include `Owner`): a `Select` whose `value` matches no option renders its placeholder (`packages/ui/src/select/select.tsx:255`), so an owner's cell would read `Select…` beside the person who owns the workspace; the owner's row keeps the chip. That narrowing was an executor CONSERVATIVE DEFAULT, filed for a ruling rather than assumed, and **the controller confirmed it on 2026-09-18**; it remains reversible in one predicate. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`, `packages/app-core/src/view-types.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`
  - ✅ **The roster's Person cell is a TWO-LINE identity** (`hig-per-screen/3b`, 2026-09-20) — the authority draws the name at weight 600 over the address at 13px/18px in `label2` (`desktop.dc.html@6d7b67c0a52c:675`), and this build had the name only, with the address kept as a *fallback*.
