  - **A registration hazard worth knowing**: `app.register(websocket)` followed by a top-level `app.get(…, {websocket:true})` SILENTLY degrades to a plain HTTP route — the plugin's `onRoute` hook does not exist yet, `websocket: true` is never read, the handler is called with `(request, reply)`, and the upgrade dies in a 500 with no warning. The route is therefore declared inside its own `app.register(...)`.
  `apps/server/src/stream/routes.ts`, `apps/server/src/app.ts`, `apps/server/test/helpers/stream.ts`, `apps/server/test/stream/stream-route.test.ts`

- ⚙️ **The integration checkpoint — one world built through the shipped doors, read back through the
  shipped read routes.** A world founded by real `signInWithPhone` → `POST /v1/workspaces` →
  `POST /v1/events` (project, roster, a parent and a child ticket, an assignment, a transition), then
  read through `GET /v1/me/workspaces`, `/v1/projects/:id/board`, `/v1/projects/:id/tickets`,
  `/v1/workspaces/:ws/tickets/:id`, `/v1/tickets/:id/history`, `/v1/search`, `/v1/me/work`,
  `/v1/me/preferences` and `/v1/workspaces/:ws/attention` — every body asserted whole with `toEqual`,
  and every route carrying a second signed-in non-member who gets that route's refusal in the same
  run. **It contains no `applyCommitted`, no `insertEvent` and no `INSERT` into any `proj_*` table**,
  which is the whole point: it closes the one gap the six absences in `test/helpers/apply.ts` do not
  cover. Recounted, five of those six are already exercised through the real door elsewhere and only
  the locks are not — *through the real door*, which is the whole claim: `lockEntity` and
  `lockWorkspace` are both covered directly by `apps/server/test/event-store.test.ts` (`:83`, the
  falsifiable hold-off; `:242`, `appendUnderBothLocks`), and what no test does is reach either one
  through `POST /v1/events`. What was missing was that every Plan 5 read-route test seeds its world with
  `applyCommitted` and none writes through `POST /v1/events`, so the absences were closed for the
  WRITE path and open for the READ path. What the round trip therefore proves and a seeded read cannot:
  the `workflowId`/`stateId`/`stateCategory` on every card were chosen by the founding sequence and
  the rule engine rather than typed by the fixture; every `seq` was minted by `nextSeq` inside the
  ingest transaction; and `rollup.uncategorizedChildren === 0` is a fact about that transaction having
  advanced `proj_visibility` and `proj_tickets` together, which `read-models.ts` names a subset-applying
  fixture as the way to break. `apps/server/test/integration/real-door-round-trip.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **The attention strip proved with `TZ` varied — the first such proof in this repository.** The
  strip is asked from `UTC`, `Asia/Kolkata` and `America/Los_Angeles` against a frozen clock thirty
  days after the ticket entered its state **and pinned to 20:00Z**, and the whole body must be
  identical in each. The pinned time of day is the load-bearing part: at 20:00Z the three zones
  disagree about which calendar page it is — 01:30 the next day in IST — which is the window
  `read-models.ts` names as "the whole Indian evening", and an instant inherited from the wall clock
  spends most of the day outside it. **Measured, not merely asserted:** making the strip's "today"
  process-timezone-dependent — `formatUtcDay` (`packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) reading
  `getDate()`/`getMonth()`/`getFullYear()` instead of their UTC forms — reddens this and nothing
  else, because the three zones then answer different `daysInFlight`. The expected count is
  **computed at run time rather than hard-coded**: pinning the frozen instant to 20:00Z makes the
  IST-correct answer 30 or 31 depending on where in the Indian evening the entry instant fell, so
  naming either as *the* answer would be a fixture that is right on some runs and wrong on others.
  Measured
  across all 48 test files on an earlier `main`: zero vary `TZ`, seven are clock-sensitive, all seven
  omit it — and both this machine and the product are `Asia/Kolkata`, so every day-boundary proof in
  the repo was consistent with the property being false. The zone switch carries its own control
  (`new Date(0).getHours()` is 0 under UTC and 5 under IST) so the three-way equality cannot pass on an
  inert knob, and the expected IST day is computed independently of `istDayOf` rather than imported
  from the code under test. `apps/server/test/integration/real-door-round-trip.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **The client-contract pins — every `DataSource` divergence asserted, so a reconciliation cannot be
  silent.** Eighteen cases pinning the shipped `/v1` bodies against Plan 6's `DataSourceQuery` and
  `view-types.ts`, each as a named negative with a positive control on the same field in the same run:
  the three **signature gaps**, **RECONCILED 2026-09-02 (Plan 6 Task 7, ruling P25 clause 2)** —
  `getMyWork`, `getPreferences` and `putPreferences` took no `workspaceId` while all three routes
  answer **400 `invalid_query`** without one, the sharpest possible pin since it is the request an
  adapter written to the client's signature would actually send; the signatures now carry it, the
  cases pin the parameter lists read out of `data-source.ts` source, and the 400/200 pair is kept
  beneath them as the reason they had to move; the **serving gap**
  `BoardGroup.workflowVersion`, **RECONCILED 2026-09-02 (Plan 6 Task 11)** — it was absent at every
  depth of the board body while `proj_tickets` and `proj_workflows` both held the number, which is
  what made it one route change rather than a product decision, and that route change has landed, so
  the case now pins the AGREEMENT (the client's `BoardGroup` field list read out of source, every
  column carrying the field, and its value equal to `proj_workflows`' published version) rather than
  the gap; and the **data gaps**
  `BoardCard.assignee` vs `assigneeId`, `SearchHit.matchedIn`, `WorkspaceRef.projects`,
  `MyWorkView`'s `{assigned,inTransit,contested}` vs the server's `{open,active,terminal}`, the board's
  flat `columns` vs the client's `groups`, `TicketRead`'s wrapper and its snake_case inner row, the
  whole-document refusal of the client's `UserPreferences`, and `FilterSpec`'s singular/plural
  disagreement. It IMPORTS nothing from `@gisik-nokma/app-core` — a by-package-name import resolves
  through `./dist` and would make these failures depend on build order — but the two divergences whose
  case turns on the client's shape (`RollupToViewer`'s three fields, `ServerStreamFrame`'s union members — `StreamItem` until closure 3 deleted the duplicate)
  READ that shape out of `packages/app-core/src/*.ts` by relative path with `readFileSync`, which is
  source text and never `./dist`, and pin it with a whole-array `toEqual`. A client half encoded only
  as a literal the test file types can change only when somebody edits that file, so it pins nothing:
  measured, before the fix, adding `scope-revoked` to the client union left the file 15/0 green. The
  remaining client expectations are quoted beside the line they came from as documentation, and what
  runs beneath each is an assertion on the real server body. Green today; RED the day the server
  moves, and red on those two the day the client moves — **which is exactly what happened to the two
  stream cases**: Plan 6 Task 8 reconciled both of the then-`StreamItem` divergences (closure 3 later removed the type entirely, closing the third), the union case went red on
  the derived five-name array, and both cases were rewritten to pin the AGREEMENT (set equality
  against `serverStreamFrameSchema`'s own option list, and the event member's declared envelope type)
  rather than deleted — a divergence that closed can silently re-open.
  `apps/server/test/integration/client-contract.test.ts`
  It also carries the SERVER half of Plan 6 Task 7's refusals: `expectStillUnserved` reads the
  absence lists out of `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts` (relative path, source text, never
  `./dist`) and asserts every `wireKey` a refusal still names is genuinely absent from the real body
  in the same run — so the day a route starts serving one, the case reddens and a stale refusal has
  to go. That is the mechanism a gap "recorded with a named owner" normally lacks, and it covers
  `getMyWorkspaces`, `search`, `getMyWork` and `getAttention` (the last
  pinned against the attention route's own source, since a missing item KIND is not a missing key on
  any body). It did exactly that for `getTicket` and `getTickets`: ruling P25 clause 1 made those
  routes serve, `expectStillUnserved` reddened, and the stale refusals had to go — the mechanism
  firing as designed rather than being edited around.
- ⚙️ **The stream union, RECONCILED, then UNIFIED — one type, one seam (closure 3, 2026-09-06).** `StreamItem` no longer exists: `data-source.ts` re-exports the domain's `ServerStreamFrame` and declares no union of its own, which closed the third and last divergence (`code: string` vs `StreamErrorCode`) as a COMPILE ERROR the moment the duplicate was deleted, and the contract test now pins that no second union may be redeclared. Before that, the client's copy had no
  `scope-revoked` member, so a revocation reached a client as an unrecognised `type` and was silently
  dropped, leaving stale authorization in the replica's cache; and its event member carried an
  `EventEnvelope`, which has no `workspaceSeq` — the field the RESUME protocol is defined in terms of.
  Plan 6 Task 8 closed both. The two cases stayed and now assert AGREEMENT: the client's discriminants,
  still read out of source text, are pinned equal as a SET to `serverStreamFrameSchema`'s own derived
  option list (so a sixth server frame forgotten on the client reddens here rather than being dropped
  at runtime), and the event member's declared envelope type is pinned to `LedgerEvent` with
  `EventEnvelope` asserted against by name — without that client half the second case would have stayed
  green through the very reconciliation its title describes. The server half is still proved through
  the real path — a `membership.granted` then `membership.revoked` appended by the owner through
  `POST /v1/events`, then the ex-member subscribing to a hub running the PRODUCTION `visibleEventsFor`
  — and both halves of the recording are asserted: `frames` is the parse, proving
  `serverStreamFrameSchema` accepts the frame, and `raw` is the bytes a client actually receives.
  `error.code` stays the one open stream divergence (client `string`, server `StreamErrorCode`); it is
  named rather than pinned, because no case here ever asserted it.
  `apps/server/test/integration/client-contract.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **Fixture witnesses — the real client stack against the real routes.** A second integration file
  beside the contract suite, for fixtures whose protocol was authored alongside their consumer. Each

  executed, never restated as a literal) against the SHIPPED route via an `inject()`-backed

  into a client-side assertion is deliberately NOT counted as a witness — that agrees with source

- ⚙️ **The mapper cluster, witnessed (7.222–7.229).** Eight further `app-core` mapper entry points now

- ⚙️ **The refusal vocabulary, witnessed (7.238–7.242).** Five further cases drive the rule
  engine's and the server's refusals through real routes and read them with the shipped client: the
  reopenable `hierarchy.parent-terminal` at BOTH of its producer call sites (7.238 `ticket.created`,
  7.239 `ticket.reparented`), `hierarchy.rank` (7.240), the migrate route's
  `migration.unknown-target-state` (7.241) and a role-gated `restrict-to` transition (7.242). **The
  standing rule this cluster established:** where a producer builds its refusal FROM an exported
  constant, asserting the wire value against that same constant is a self-comparison through one
  module and cannot fail — so every case pairs the producer's constant with a literal, and 7.238 is
  the one case comparing two INDEPENDENTLY-OWNED constants (`app-core`'s `PARENT_TERMINAL_RULE`
  against the engine's bare literal), which is the pair the whole reopen affordance hangs on and which
  nothing had ever compared. Four of the five run a client mapper (`mapIngestResults`); 7.241's typed
  consumer lives in `apps/web`, which `apps/server` cannot depend on, so it runs the shipped
  `refusalText` over the real bytes instead and the case says so rather than presenting the narrower
  witness as the wider one. **One fabrication was found and CORRECTED** — a parent-terminal reason no
  engine version emits, carried by two client files under a comment and an identifier naming the
  producer. **A second was found and RECORDED, not corrected** (7.246): a migration rule name from the
  wrong family, where the real vocabulary lives in `apps/server` and the client must not depend on it.
  Recorded with owners as not buildable from a server test: the web notifications gateway (7.243) — **BUILT 2026-09-07 and the premise was wrong**: the adapter was not web-specific, it was merely misfiled in `apps/web/src/`, and moving it to `packages/app-core/src/online/` (no new dependency edge) made the round trip ordinary — see 11.190–11.195 —,
  the contest round trip (7.244), the ergonomics of `rule-engine`'s refusal words (7.245 — the bytes
  ARE reachable through the `./fixtures` subpath; the function is not), and one `sync-client` mirror
  (7.248, reduced from five to one on review — three of the cited sites were not mirrors at all).
  `apps/server/test/integration/fixture-witnesses.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **The refusal vocabulary is exported (7.249–7.253).** `packages/rule-engine/src/index.ts` now
  re-exports `REFUSALS` and the eight refusal-sentence builders from `checks/refusals.ts`, closing the
  gap recorded as 7.245: until now no consumer could import the engine's refusal WORDS, and three
  `app-core` fixtures had retyped the parent-terminal sentence in three different wordings, each under
  a comment or identifier naming the engine as its source — **three files carrying TWO wordings, not
  three; the first two are byte-identical and only one of the three claimed a source.** Each client
  site keeps its LITERAL and pins it to the builder in a case that runs (7.249a–e), per ruling P43:
  a literal ALONGSIDE the import, never instead. **`refuse()` and `ACCEPT` are deliberately
  not exported** — they are how a check constructs a verdict, and exporting them would let any
  consumer mint a `Refusal` that looks engine-made. The finding is sharper than "a fabrication":
  **two producers say this refusal in different words** — the engine (`checks/refusals.ts:197`) and
  the client's own pre-check (`app-core/src/precheck.ts:268`) — and the fixtures carried a hybrid of
  the two that neither emits. `hierarchy.rank`'s reason was wrong the same way in two more files.
  **The export removes a class of fabrication; the PIN is what adds the check** — an import-only
  assertion moves with a reword in silence, which is why P43 required the literal kept beside it. The
  excluding mutation now reddens the witness AND the client case, each at its own sentence. A
  mis-wired export is caught by the typecheck where the arity differs, and by the builder comparisons
  at 7.238/7.240 where it does not. Two gaps remain recorded with owners: the transition-rule and pre-check sentences are inline
  templates with no symbol to import (7.252), and a web fixture truncates the server's
  `migration.unmapped-state` sentence (7.253).
  `packages/rule-engine/src/index.ts`


## 9. Editor, documents & attachments

*Plan 7's client half. §8 is reserved for Plan 6 and is deliberately absent — the coordination doc
assigns `Features.md` sections one per implementation plan, so numbering is claimed from that table
rather than taken next-free. (§6 and §7 were named here as absent too; both have since been opened,
by Plan 4 and by Plan 5 Task 2, and the reservation logic that kept them out is unchanged.)*

- ⚙️ **Document schema v1** — `DOC_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, `emptyDoc()`, `isDocJson()`, and
  `VersionedDocument`, a replacement for Tiptap's `Document` that carries `schemaVersion` on the doc
  node. The vocabulary is CLOSED: a set-equality test over node and mark types fails when an extension
  adds one, so a new vocabulary item cannot arrive unnoticed. `packages/editor/src/doc.ts`,
  `packages/editor/src/schema/versioned-document.ts`
- ⚙️ **One extension set for every client** — `buildEditorExtensions(options)` / `EditorSchemaOptions`
  (`queryUsers`, `resolveAttachmentUrl`, `placeholder`). The single origin of schema v1: web, desktop,
  the RN webview bundle and every test build their list here, so the schema cannot drift between
  clients. Everything StarterKit contributes that v1 does not name is switched off by an explicit
  `false` rather than left to a default — `document` because it is replaced, `link` because it is
  reconfigured, `underline` because it is not this product's vocabulary.
  `packages/editor/src/schema/extensions.ts`
- ⚙️ **Typed and pasted text still becomes markup, and the count is recorded rather than claimed** —
  `**due soon**` arrives bold whether typed, pasted or dropped. `autolink`, `linkOnPaste` and
  `markdownLinks` are all `false`, and they are not sufficient: **seven paste rules across five
  extensions** and **eighteen input rules across ten** remain live, counted against the built
  extension list at tiptap 3.30.5, and the two sets sit behind `enablePasteRules` /
  `enableInputRules` — `Editor` options this function returns `Extensions` for and therefore cannot
  reach. In tension with spec §2's "nothing silently becomes markup"; **left as it is, deliberately
  and unruled**, with the census pinned by a test so the number cannot drift silently.
  `packages/editor/src/schema/extensions.ts`, `packages/editor/test/rule-census.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **Ticket image node** — `TicketImage`, `IMAGE_WRAP_MODES` (`inline-left` · `inline-right` ·
  `block`), `ImageWrapMode`, `TicketImageOptions`. Wrap is validated per-attribute at the parse door
  and an unrecognised value is COERCED to `block` rather than refused, because `block` is a legal
  target and a refusal at load would empty the whole document.
  `packages/editor/src/extensions/ticket-image.ts`
- ⚙️ **Mention node** — `Mention`, `mentionAttrsOf`, `MentionAttrs`, `mentionSuggestionKey`. Both
  attributes carry their own `parseHTML` and `renderHTML`, because a node-level `parseHTML().getAttrs`
  is merged OVER by the per-attribute handlers and its returned attrs are dead code — validation
  placed there let a crafted span store a different `userId` than the one displayed, in the field
  notifications read. One trim-and-refuse rule covers both fields on insert, and a blank or
  whitespace-only id is refused at the parse door too, where a node-level `false` short-circuits
  before the merge. A present id with a missing NAME is still admitted, deliberately: refusing that
  discards the only fact the markup carried, while refusing a blank id discards nothing.
  `packages/editor/src/extensions/mention.ts`
- ⚙️ **Mention picker** — `createMentionPopup`, `mentionResultAnnouncement`, `MentionPopupProps`,
  `MentionPopupRenderer`. Arrow-key navigation with a selected item, Enter to insert, Escape to
  dismiss leaving the typed text literal, mouse selection, and a candidate with a blank userId or a
  blank name never offered. Announces its result count politely to a screen reader and hands the
  editor's ARIA role back on exit; nothing in the popup is tabbable.
  `packages/editor/src/extensions/mention-popup.ts`, `packages/editor/src/styles/editor.css`
- ⚙️ **Embeddable-image vocabulary** — `EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_MIME`, `isEmbeddableImageMime(mime)`,
  `collectImageAttachmentIds(doc)`: one definition of which attachments a document may embed, and a
  walker that survives whatever a hostile document hands it. `packages/domain/src/doc-embeds.ts`
- ⚙️ **Attachment kinds and Drive references** — `AttachmentKind` (`upload` · `drive-reference`),
  `DriveReference`, `isDriveReference()`, `DRIVE_FILE_SCOPE`, `RULE_EVIDENCE_DRIVE_REFERENCE` and its
  reason string: two statements of what a Drive reference is, pinned to each other so the second
  cannot quietly stop meaning the first. `packages/domain/src/attachment-kinds.ts`
- ⚙️ **Evidence picker refuses a Drive reference structurally, not conditionally** — `EvidencePicker`
  offers a ticket's uploads as evidence (`attachments`) and, on its own `driveReferences` prop, lists
  any Drive references the ticket carries in a separate `evidence-picker__unavailable` section naming
  each one plus the day it was last seen, under the SAME `EVIDENCE_DRIVE_REFERENCE_REASON` sentence
  the server's evidence gate refuses with (`packages/domain/src/attachment-kinds.ts`) — imported, never
  retyped, so the two cannot drift. That section maps its own array and puts no click handler
  anywhere near it: there is no code path from a `DriveReferenceSummary` to `onPick`, which is what
  makes "not offerable" a fact about the code rather than a `disabled` attribute a later change could
  quietly drop (spec §2, Drive rule 1). The separation is structural, by PROP, not by an in-component
  predicate over mime or filename — this component reads no `kind` field at all, and
  `DriveReferenceSummary` carries none; routing a reference onto `driveReferences` rather than
  `attachments` is the caller's job (`AttachmentListEntry` already discriminates by `kind` — Plan
  6/6a), and the server's evidence gate (`apps/server/src/gates/evidence-gate.ts`, Task 11a) is the
  backstop for an id that reaches `onPick` by any other path. `packages/editor/src/components/EvidencePicker.tsx`,
  `packages/editor/src/styles/editor.css`
- ⚙️ **Composer submissions are one mapping, not a call-site convention** — `composeEditorSubmission(mode, doc)` / `EditorSubmission`: comment mode becomes `comment.added {body}`, description mode becomes `ticket.field_set {fieldId:'description', value}`, the shapes read off `packages/domain/src/events/ticket-events.ts` rather than off the plan text. A description edit is not a mutation of a field — it is one more claim, so a description's history is the history of the claims that wrote it. The union is closed at two, so a third composer mode has to come here and name its event rather than fall through to a description save.
  `packages/editor/src/attachments/compose-editor-submission.ts`
- ✅ **`<EditorSlot>` implemented, mounted, and the contract written twice reconciled** — `TicketEditorSlot`,
  `EditorSlotProps`, `EditorSlotUpload`, `TicketEditorSlotProps`. The concrete component behind Plan
  6's slot boundary: a Tiptap composer plus one submit control, speaking neither HTTP nor DataSource —
  `submit` and `emitAttachmentAdded` belong to the host, so the sheet stays the one place that appends
  to the ledger. It STATES its gaps instead of pretending: mounted without `submit` (or read-only) the
  button is disabled and its title says which of the two reasons applies; mounted without an uploader a
  pasted file is refused through `onError` rather than swallowed. A refused submit never costs the user
  their words — the clear sits inside the `try` after the `await` — and only comment mode clears at all.
  Re-entrancy is guarded by a REF set synchronously before the first await, not by render state: two
  clicks in one React batch both read a pre-commit `false` and both got through, measured as two
  `comment.added` events for one comment in a ledger that can unsay neither.
  **`doc` is `unknown`, not `DocJson | null`**: Plan 6 owns the declaration, its mount site passes an
  `unknown` off a domain field claim, and under `strictFunctionTypes` props are compared
  contravariantly, so the narrower half is the one that breaks. The narrowing moved from the type to
  `isDocJson` at the mount, which keeps `null → empty composer` and extends it to every non-document.
  Both halves of the contract moved in one commit; the compile-time proof is an assignment against a
  transcription of app-core's declaration, guarded by a test that reads app-core's source and fails if
  its `doc` stops saying `unknown`. **The end-to-end mount EXISTS — added 2026-09-19.** `apps/web`
  takes the dependency on `@gisik-nokma/editor` (Plan 6's own declared arrangement, `:5667`) and
  passes `TicketEditorSlot` to `TicketSheet`'s `editorSlot` prop, so the description on a job is
  WRITTEN rather than only shown — which is why this is `✅` and no longer `⚙️`. The sheet draws the
  description row whether or not a claim exists: `ticket.created` writes `fields.title` and nothing
  else, so on a ticket nobody had described there was no row for an editor to mount into, and
  enabling the composer alone would have changed almost nothing.
  `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/layout.tsx`,
  `packages/editor/src/components/TicketEditorSlot.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/editor-slot.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`ObjectStore`, and where attachment bytes actually live** — a three-verb interface
  (`put`/`getStream`/`delete`) behind which `LocalDiskStore` (dev/test) and `S3ObjectStore`
  (S3-compatible, path-style addressing default `true` so MinIO and R2 work unconfigured) are
  interchangeable; `createObjectStoreFromEnv` selects between them and, on a `local` misconfiguration or
  an unrecognised `OBJECT_STORE` value, names every missing S3 variable in one refusal rather than one
  per restart. Object keys are traversal-proof by CONSTRUCTION, not by blocklist: `w/<ulid>/a/<ulid>`
  segments admit only letters, digits, `_` and `-` — no dot is a legal character in any segment, so `.`
  and `..` are unrepresentable rather than merely refused. `LocalDiskStore.put` writes to a temp file and
  `rename`s into place, so a torn write can never surface as a readable half-object. A fourth verb,
  **`list(prefix?)`**, was added for Plan 11's orphan sweep: `LocalDiskStore.list` walks `rootDir`
  structurally rather than by a traversal check — `readdir(..., {withFileTypes:true})` reports a symlink's
  `Dirent` as neither `isDirectory()` nor `isFile()`, so the walk recurses into real directories and reports
  real files only, never following a symlink planted inside the root — and `S3ObjectStore.list` PAGINATES
  via `ContinuationToken` rather than trusting one response, since `ListObjectsV2` truncates at 1000 keys
  regardless of bucket size. `apps/server/src/attachments/object-store.ts`, `local-disk-store.ts`,
  `s3-store.ts`, `store-config.ts`
- ⚙️ **`ops:sweep-orphans` — the bytes an upload can leave behind with no row naming them** —
  `sweepOrphanObjects` walks the attachment store's own `list()` against a UNION of the two things that can
  name an attachment id: the `attachments` table row, and a ledger `attachment.added` event
  (`payload->>'attachmentId'`, the same shape `evidence-gate.ts` already reads). The union is deliberate and
  the more conservative of the two possible readings — **the web client does not currently post the ledger
  event at all** — ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-21: this is no longer true, and this change set is what made it false.** The comment composer now posts `attachment.added` when an upload is attached to a comment (`TicketSheet.emitAttachmentAdded`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`), so the event IS produced — though only on that one path, and the union below is still the right design for the reason it gives**, so treating it as the only survival signal would flag nearly every live attachment as an
  orphan, while treating the table row as the only signal risks the opposite the day some other path
  registers an attachment through the ledger alone. Keys are only ever considered if they have the exact
  shape `w/<workspaceId>/a/<id>`; anything else — including a torn-write `.tmp-<pid>-<ms>` leftover, which
  splits into four segments shaped exactly like a real key — is recorded as `unrecognizedShape` and NEVER
  touched, at any age, because handing such a key to `store.delete` would throw on the store's own key
  validator and take the whole sweep down. An unnamed, recognized object survives for
  `ORPHAN_OBJECT_RETENTION_MS` (30 days, matching the user-preferences sweep's constant) before a live run
  deletes it; a dry run reports the identical list with nothing actually removed. **Known ceiling, the same
  class 13.34 already names for `readLedger`:** `store.list()` is called unprefixed and materialises every
  object in memory before the loop starts, so peak memory scales with object COUNT rather than anything
  bounded — a constant-memory sweep needs a paged/streaming `list()`, a change to the `ObjectStore` contract
  itself, not fixed here. **KNOWN GAP, OWNED HERE
  RATHER THAN FIXED**: `deploy/do/app.yaml`'s `backup` worker env block does not yet include `OBJECT_STORE`
  or `OBJECT_STORE_S3_BUCKET` — it carries only the five `OBJECT_STORE_S3_*` variables the SEPARATE backup
  object-sink needs, under a different bucket variable. Without those two added to that worker's block,
  `createObjectStoreFromEnv` in the deployed worker silently falls back to a LOCAL DISK store no other
  process can see, and the scheduled sweep collects nothing real in production. Editing `deploy/do/app.yaml`
  is out of scope here (another lane's file at the time this shipped); this is the handoff for whoever next
  touches it. `apps/server/src/tools/sweep-orphans.ts`
- ⚙️ **`readAtMost` — the size ceiling enforced DURING the stream, not after buffering it** — an oversize
  upload is refused while its bytes are still arriving, so it cannot exhaust memory on the way to being
  refused; the refusal names the ceiling in the units a person reads (MB), and a sub-mebibyte ceiling
  changes units in its own message rather than reporting a misleading "0 MB". `apps/server/src/attachments/read-limited.ts`
- ⚙️ **Attachment metadata: repo, hashing, filenames, mime sniffing** — the `attachments` table and its
  repo functions (insert/get/list-by-ticket), with `sha256` nullable at the column level (`NULL` reads as
  unknown, never a substituted default) and CHECK-constrained to lowercase hex only — an uppercase
  duplicate of a real hash is refused by the database itself, not merely by application code.
  `sha256Hex` hashes exactly the bytes stored, over published test vectors. `sanitizeFilename` strips
  header-injection characters, path separators and control characters (NUL/DEL included) while keeping
  ordinary dots, and falls back to `"file"` for an empty or absent name. `sniffAllowedMime` decides mime
  by MAGIC BYTES alone — a renamed executable is refused for what it is regardless of its claimed name or
  content-type, unidentifiable bytes are refused without ever falling back to the extension, and the
  allow-list is closed: a real file outside it is still refused. Every embeddable-image mime is a subset
  of the uploadable allow-list, by test. `apps/server/src/attachments/attachment-repo.ts`, `content-hash.ts`, `filename.ts`, `sniff.ts`
- ✅ **`POST /v1/attachments` and `GET /v1/attachments/:id` — built, tested, and REGISTERED on the server this repo boots.**
  The route handlers are complete: upload answers `201 {attachmentId, url, mime, filename, size, sha256}`
  built from the server's own sniffed and hashed truth (never the client's claim about its file), refuses
  a session-less request 401, a malformed one 400, an oversize one 413 (the shipped ceiling is exactly
  26,214,400 bytes — 25 MiB), a mime the sniff refuses 415, and a guest without ticket visibility a 404
  BYTE-IDENTICAL to a nonexistent ticket; download streams the exact stored bytes under `canSee`, inline
  for images and as a sanitized-filename attachment otherwise, and answers that SAME 404 for an unshared
  guest, a nonexistent id, and metadata recorded with no bytes behind it — three different truths, one
  frozen body, so nothing about which of the three happened leaks. `apps/server/src/app.ts` registers
  both through `app.register(registerAttachmentRoutes, …)`, and `AppDeps.store` — required, like every
  other field but `now`/`stream` — carries the `ObjectStore` the pair writes to, built at the one boot
  path from `createObjectStoreFromEnv(process.env)`. **Until this landed the routes were reachable from
  nothing**, and the reason it survived so long is worth keeping: both attachment suites mount a bare
  `Fastify()` and register the routes BY HAND, so both were green against an empty composition root.
  `apps/server/test/routes/attachments-wired.test.ts` is the test that can fail on it — it registers
  nothing, goes through `buildApp`, and asserts the `attachments` ROW rather than the 201.
  `apps/server/src/routes/attachments.ts`, `apps/server/src/app.ts`
- ✅ **A ticket's attachments are READABLE — the per-ticket list, and the mount that finally passes it.** Until
  2026-09-20 a ticket's files reached no surface at all: `listTicketAttachments`
  (`apps/server/src/attachments/attachment-repo.ts:76`) was written and tested by Plan 7 with **zero production
  callers** (every reference in the tree was a test), while `TicketSheetProps` has accepted an `attachments`
  prop since Plan 6 and the product's single mount never passed it. `GET
  /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/tickets/:ticketId/attachments` is registered beside the events door and rides
  **its same two VISIBILITY guards, in the same order, answering the same body** — `loadTicketRow` → 404, then
  `canSee` → 404 — so "may this caller see this ticket" is still asked in exactly one function; the case holds
  the two doors to identical bytes for an unseen ticket and a nonexistent one rather than asserting the
  sameness in prose. ⚠ **It carries a THIRD guard the events door deliberately does not** (added 2026-09-20,
  below): a soft-deleted ticket's files are not served either — see *the tombstone* row further down. Each row is `{attachmentId, filename, mime, size, sha256}` with the filename re-sanitized here as it
  is at the download door. **An empty list is a `200 {attachments: []}` and a refusal is a `404`** — the pair
  `AttachmentList`'s null-on-empty docblock depends on, since the one answer this door must never give is `[]`
  for a ticket the caller may not see. The client reaches it through a new `DataSourceQuery.getTicketAttachments`,
  which **THROWS on a 404 rather than answering `[]`**, so "no files" and "could not read them" stay two states
  all the way to the screen: the sheet draws nothing for the first (the component's own honest silence) and a
  sentence for the second, via `TicketSheetProps.attachmentsUnavailable` — the shape
  `NewTicketDialogProps.parentCandidatesUnavailable` already uses. The offline replica **refuses** this read
  with a named absence rather than deriving it from `attachment.added`, because the row and the event are two
  facts with two writers: a ledger-derived list would omit an upload whose event never arrived and include one
  whose upload failed. **NOT served, and named rather than implied: the Drive-reference arm** — the
  `attachments` table has no kind column, so a reference recorded as a ledger event is not in this list; no
  producer emits that arm today, and the owner of the day one does is whoever gives the table a kind column.
  Cases `16.47`–`16.58`. `apps/server/src/routes/tickets.ts`,
  `packages/app-core/src/online/{online-data-source,mappers}.ts`,
  `packages/app-core/src/replica/replica-data-source.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`,
  `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`
- ✅ **A soft-deleted ticket's FILES go with it — the third guard, at every door that serves one** (2026-09-20,
  owner: *"attachments leak: do as you recommend."*). `#591` gave a tombstoned ticket a third guard on its
  detail route and left the routes beside it with `loadTicketRow` + `canSee`; `canSee` is the VISIBILITY
  predicate and by its own written ruling does not mean "not deleted", so a ticket the product had removed
  from every view still served its file list, still served **the bytes**, and still **accepted uploads**.
  MEASURED through the real doors at the base: detail 404 while the list answered 200 with the row, the
  download 200 with the file, and the upload **201** — content accreting on a ticket nobody can see. All four
  doors now carry a THIRD guard (`ticketIsLive`, `apps/server/src/read/live-ticket.ts`) asked AFTER `canSee`
  and answering the same 404 body, so existence stays indistinguishable from invisibility AND from deletion.
  **It is `LIVE_TICKET_WHERE` asked at a route, never a widened `canSee`**: folding deletion into the
  visibility predicate breaks the `canSeeResolved` cross-check and makes `listVisibleTickets`, whose SELECT has
  no `proj_tickets` in its FROM, a runtime "missing FROM-clause entry". **The two HISTORY doors still serve a
  tombstone, deliberately** — *"Its history is retained, because other events cite it"* — and the asymmetry is
  asserted rather than left to be discovered. Cases `16.96`–`16.99`; the enumeration of every ticket-scoped door
  and its guard is in that suite's docblock. `apps/server/src/read/live-ticket.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/routes/{tickets,attachments}.ts`,
  `apps/server/test/integration/soft-deleted-attachments.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **The server-gate seam is wired into the one composition root** — `apps/server/src/index.ts` is the
  ONE composition root that calls `buildServerGates()` and hands the result into `createIngestService`,
  which is the narrower and true claim this bullet exists to draw: the gate seam is wired at
  `apps/server/src/index.ts`, NOT at `apps/server/src/app.ts` where every route module is registered
  — two different composition roots, and a reader who checks only the second finds no gates at all.
  (This bullet used to draw the contrast against the two attachment routes above, which were then
  registered at neither. They are now registered at `app.ts`, so that contrast is gone and the plain
  claim is what is left.) The seam is wired even though that composition root is never started in production
  (`pnpm dev` boots it; `apps/server` has no `start` script. The claim that once stood here — no
  `start` script, Dockerfile or deployment target *anywhere in the repo* — was wrong in one half from
  the day it was written, since `apps/web/package.json` has carried a `start` script since earlier than
  the sentence, and is wrong in the other half as of Plan 11: `apps/server/Dockerfile` and
  `apps/web/Dockerfile` now exist and both build. What remains true is the part that matters here —
  **nothing has been deployed to a live host** — so "wired" still does not mean "exercised in
  production"). Most
  other call sites that build an ingest service leave `gates` out entirely and get the seam's default
  `[]` and no gating at all — but three of this task's own test suites deliberately pass
  `gates: buildServerGates()` to exercise the seam directly (`test/gates/gate-seam.test.ts`,
  `test/gates/evidence-gate.test.ts`, `test/gates/image-embed-gate.test.ts`), so "every test suite" is not
  literally true; those three are the ones proving the refusals below. `comment.added`/`ticket.field_set`/
  `ticket.transitioned` all still ingest through this same composition root via the routes that ARE wired.
  The seam runs inside the ingest TRANSACTION,
  so a refusal rolls back only its own event and its writes, leaving batch neighbours committed; it runs
  AFTER the rule engine and AFTER the permission gate at the client door, so a stranger embedding a pdf on
  a ticket they cannot see is told about permission and never learns which attachment that ticket holds.
  Applies unconditionally at both ingest doors (internal and client), proven separately at each.
  `apps/server/src/gates/index.ts`, `server-gate.ts`
- ⚙️ **Image-embed gate (layer 3) and evidence gate** — `attachment.embed-not-image` and
  `attachment.embed-unknown` refuse a non-image or foreign/fabricated embed with the SAME reason shape
  regardless of which is true, so no refusal leaks whether an id exists; every embed in a document is
  judged, not just the first, and only the description field is inspected. `attachment.evidence-unknown`
  refuses fabricated or foreign evidence identically for the same reason; `attachment.evidence-drive-reference`
  (Task 11a) refuses a Drive reference named as evidence with `EVIDENCE_DRIVE_REFERENCE_REASON` verbatim
  and no id leaked into it, while a Drive reference on ANOTHER ticket still refuses as merely unknown — the
  Drive-specific refusal itself never leaks that a reference exists elsewhere. `apps/server/src/gates/image-embed-gate.ts`, `evidence-gate.ts`
- ⚙️ **Google Drive picker — attaching a reference, never a copy** — `openDrivePicker` requests exactly
  the non-sensitive `drive.file` scope with incremental consent (`prompt: ''`), loads the GSI and Picker
  API scripts, and maps a picked file to a `DriveReference`; cancelling attaches nothing, and a picked doc
  with no usable view link is dropped rather than have a URL invented for it. `composeUploadAttachmentAdded`
  and `composeDriveReferenceAdded` build the two `attachment.added` claim shapes — upload (hash included)
  and drive-reference (fresh ULID) — and both are checked against the door-facing wire schema, not merely
  the structural guard, so the composer is proven to produce something the ingest door actually accepts.
  `packages/editor/src/drive/google-picker.ts`, `attachment-claims.ts`
- ⚙️ **`AttachmentListItem` — the editor's own rendering of Drive rule 2** — `AttachmentListItem`,
  `UploadedAttachmentItem`, `DriveReferenceItem`, `describeDriveReachability`, `AttachmentListEntry`,
  `DriveLinkCheck`. An upload renders as a file this board holds (`data-attachment-kind="upload"`, a link
  into `/v1/attachments/:id`) and carries NO reachability language at all; a Drive reference renders as
  external and last-seen (its own kind marker, a link into Drive that never contains `/v1/attachments/`,
  its last-seen name and capture date on the surface) and its one reachability sentence — `not-checked` or
  `unreachable`, never a third state — is read from the exported `describeDriveReachability` rather than
  retyped, so a surface copy and a component copy cannot drift; none of `deleted`/`missing`/`no
  longer`/`available`/`verified`/`up to date` appears for either state. The capture day renders through
  one formatter (the UTC day, checked across four zones with two day-boundary fixtures in opposite
  directions) rather than an ISO slice or a local-getter reconstruction, and an unparseable `capturedAt`
  renders verbatim rather than inventing a day. This is the twin `Features.md:44`'s `AttachmentListEntry`
  bullet names — the two spellings describe one type, verified in both directions.
  `packages/editor/src/components/AttachmentListItem.tsx`
- ⚙️ **Drive rule 3, enforced three ways** — a picked Drive file never becomes an image node: picking a
  Drive PNG produces an `attachment.added` claim and inserts no node; a `DriveReference` is not assignable
  to `insertTicketImage` at the TYPE level, and refused at RUNTIME too if the type is forced past; pasting
  a Drive share link (its URL as clipboard text, no file) uploads nothing and embeds nothing; and the
  drive module CANNOT reach the insert command at all, because it never imports it — a static-import scan
  over `packages/editor/src/drive/`, proven against a planted import. The server's own image-embed gate is
  the backstop should any of the above ever be bypassed (a reference has no bytes to embed, whatever mime
  it claims). `packages/editor/src/drive/`, `apps/server/src/gates/image-embed-gate.ts`
- ⚙️ **RN webview bundle & bridge** — a single self-contained HTML bundle (`packages/editor/dist-webview/index.html`, built by `build:webview`) plus a versioned five-verb postMessage protocol
  (`loadDoc`, `change`, `submit`, `insertImage`, `requestUpload`, plus `mentionQuery`/`mentionResults`,
  `requestSubmit`, `uploadFailed`); files never cross the bridge, because the host uploads natively and
  hands back only an id. Hostile or malformed host messages parse to `null` rather than throwing —
  constrained to the top-level shape of `loadDoc.doc` (`isDocJson`'s own check), with hostile CONTENT
  nested inside an otherwise doc-shaped `loadDoc.doc` recorded as a separate, still-open gap in the
  module's own docblock, because `loadDoc` passes that object through BY REFERENCE and `isDocJson` does
  not descend. `createEditorBridge` announces `{kind:'ready'}` before any host message arrives, echoes no
  `change` for a host-pushed `loadDoc`, and `insertImage` still refuses a non-image mime through the same
  layer-1 command reached directly. Plan 10 wires the RN side against exactly this contract.
  `packages/editor/src/webview/bridge.ts`, `create-editor-bridge.ts`

## 12. Offline sync & the apps

*(**Plan 10, Tasks 4–7 and 9–17, plus closures 1–5b, plus the Lane F passes that write §12** — the
LIFTED §11 closer, the contested-claims read path, the snapshot writer, the replica bootstrap, the sync
ingress, the local replica, the outbox with its local pre-check, the sync engine with its HTTP/WS
transport, the replica `DataSource`, app-core's offline/sync UX surfaces, the dual-client harness rig,
the six scripted divergence scenarios, the `apps/desktop` Tauri shell and the `apps/mobile` Expo shell —
and the rulings and cross-lane passes applied to this bucket's surface afterwards. **Task 18 is this
banner and `test-cases/12`'s header**; it owns §12's final shape and the reconciliation the notes below
record.*

***This enumeration names CATEGORIES, not entries, and that is deliberate.** An earlier draft of it
listed Plan 10's tasks and the closures by name and was falsified within the hour by the first thing
that was neither — Lane A's fixture-mirror inventory (#187), a Lane F pass. **A banner that enumerates
by name is falsified by the next merge; one that enumerates by category with a self-naming rule is
not.** So the rule, and it is the load-bearing sentence of this paragraph: **every bullet added here
under a category rather than a Plan 10 task NAMES ITSELF and its PR in its own text** — the
fixture-mirror inventory does, and so must the socket-cutting fixture, the post-Task-18 sync fixes, and
whatever follows. A reader reconciles this section by reading its bullets, never by trusting a list up
here to have kept pace. This clause exists because the paragraph written to end incomplete enumerations
in §12 was itself incomplete three times: the "still absent" app shells, Task 3's stream cursor, and
then this.*

***Where the rest of Plan 10 is catalogued, measured rather than assumed.** Four of this plan's tasks are
NOT here and should not be moved here. **Tasks 1–2's** shared vocabulary: `formatIstInstant`,
`IST_CLOCK_NOTE`, `istDayOf`/`istDayBounds` and the clock-skew derivation live in **§3**, and the contest
*evaluators* (`contest.server-only`, `contest.unknown`, `contest.already-resolved`,
`contest.wrong-ticket`) in **§4** — the sections that own the domain and the rule engine. **Task 3's**
workspace stream cursor is in **§3** as two bullets, *Workspace stream cursor* and *Workspace stream
cursor read* (`readStreamSeq`), even though this plan's own text declared a §12 bullet for it — it went
where the ledger column it reads lives. **Task 8's** `sync-client` scaffold and storage seam sit in **§3**
beside the ledger and its persistence. Measured at this commit: those four
tokens return **0** occurrences inside §12 and **7 / 2 / 4 / 2** elsewhere in this file, with
`proj_contested` at **9** inside §12 as the positive control that the same method can find a §12 string
at all — a zero with no positive control beside it is indistinguishable from a search that cannot fire.
The same method settles Task 3: a `^- .*[Ss]tream cursor` matcher returns **0** inside §12 and **2** over
the whole file, so the matcher fires and the §12 zero is a measurement rather than a broken pattern.
The split is real and is left standing rather than tidied, exactly as the §3 notes say.*

***Still absent from `Features.md`, and genuinely so: §8 alone.** The coordination doc's reservation
table gives Plans 5–11 "§7+ in order, one bucket each", which makes §8 Plan 6's — but **that row was
superseded on 2026-09-02 (Plan 6 Task 16)**: every Plan 6 web-client feature had already landed in §3
and been catalogued in `test-cases/03` under 3.x ids, and renumbering 112+ cross-referenced ids for a
cosmetic relocation would violate the never-renumber rule. **§3 is Plan 6's permanent home; §8 and
`test-cases/08-web-client.md` are deliberately never created.** Absent by ruling, not by omission — and
`ls test-cases/` confirms no `08-` bucket. The reservation table governs a section's NUMBER, not the
order sections appear in this file, which is why §12 sits above §10 and §11 here and that is the rule
working rather than failing.*

***Nothing is on the "still absent" list any more, and that is the point of this task.** The clause used
to read "both app shells — Plan 10 Tasks 16–17" while `apps/desktop`'s and `apps/mobile`'s own bullets
sat inside this same section, and `test-cases/12`'s header simultaneously called Tasks 14–15 "absent
rather than missing" with both of their sections in that file. **Three such contradictions were live
when Task 18 measured, and two were Lane B's own drift from its own #126.** Whoever adds a task's
bullets here rewrites this enumeration in the SAME change set — this banner has twice been the drift it
warns about, once written for Task 7 and still saying "Tasks 4, 5, 6 and 7 … and nothing else" while
Task 10's two bullets sat below it. An appended "Task 9's replica is now here" clause patches the tail
and leaves the head lying, which is how a contradiction survives a whole task.)*

- ✅ **A contest cannot lie about which claim it is contesting** — `claim.contested` embeds the
  refused claim whole, and the embedded claim must name the **same workspace, the same entity kind
  and the same entity id** as the envelope carrying it. Before this, every layer validated one half
  and none compared the halves: a contest filed on a real ticket in its real workspace could embed a
  fabricated claim naming any other ticket in any other workspace, and it committed — a permanent,
  un-deletable ledger entry attributing a fabricated claim to any actor, since the ledger is
  append-only by trigger and can only ever be superseded. The check is **pure** (no database read, so
  it cannot go stale or be defeated by a missing row) and runs on **both ingest doors**, unconditionally
  — unlike the envelope-origin gate, which must distinguish doors because the server's own appends are
  legitimately server-origin. `apps/server/src/sync/contest-coherence.ts`
- ⚙️ **The server-side contest constructor** — `buildContestedClaim` reads the contest's workspace and
  entity **off the refused claim**, so it has no parameter through which they could disagree: coherence
  is structural there rather than checked. `checkContestCoherence` is the door that catches a contest
  built any other way. What neither can establish, and it is named rather than implied: that the
  embedded claim was **actually refused**, by the rule the contest names. Only the path that holds a
  real refusal can answer that, and now one does: **`POST /v1/sync` is the only production caller, and it
  passes the `Refusal` it is holding straight into `buildContestedClaim`** — so on that path the
  guarantee is structural in the PRODUCER while remaining, correctly, uncheckable at the consumer. The
  coherence check still cannot see whether a claim was refused and still says so; what changed is that
  every contest in the product is now built from a refusal the server made itself, rather than merely
  being well-formed. `apps/server/src/sync/contest-coherence.ts`, `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`
- ⚙️ **Contested-claims projection (`proj_contested`)** — one row per `claim.contested`, keyed by the
  contest event id and UNIQUE on the refused claim's id, so a sync retry finds the prior contest
  instead of filing a second one. `claimant_id` is the actor of the **refused claim**, not of the
  contest — the server files contests, so reading the contest's own actor would name the same person on
  every row and empty My Work for everyone. `original_event_id` is deliberately **not** a foreign key
  into `events`: a refused claim is never appended, so the row it would point at does not exist.
  `apps/server/src/projections/contested.ts`
- ⚙️ **Both writers of a contest row are one ticket's stream** — `claim.contested` and
  `claim.contested_resolved` are both filed against the ticket, and the engine refuses
  `contest.wrong-ticket` for a resolution aimed elsewhere, so canonical replay
  `(entity_kind, entity_id, seq)` always puts the contest before its resolution. A resolution reaching
  the handler with no contest row throws rather than silently doing nothing — the assertion of that
  premise, not defensiveness: a silent no-op would leave a resolved contest showing as open forever
  with no instrument saying so. The cost, named rather than left to be discovered: a rebuild replays the
  whole ledger in one transaction, so one inconsistent pair means `proj_contested` cannot be rebuilt at
  all until the ledger is superseded into consistency. `apps/server/src/projections/contested.ts`
- ⚙️ **The verifier runs over `proj_contested` with a real row in it** — `original_event` is the repo's
  first `jsonb` projection column, and the registry contract forbids `json` precisely because
  `verify-projections`'s `EXCEPT` diff needs an equality operator. That jsonb has one is an argument
  about the type; that a live `INSERT` and a shadow-schema rebuild agree through it byte for byte is an
  observation, and the verifier's seed now makes it. Its non-emptiness is asserted separately, because a
  clean verdict over an empty table is the check that cannot fail.
  `apps/server/test/verify-projections.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **`getContest` on the server engine context** — the engine's contest lookup, reading
  `proj_contested` through the ingest transaction's own client, which is what makes
  `contest.unknown` / `contest.wrong-ticket` / `contest.already-resolved` evaluable on this server at
  all. `null` here means "no such contest was ever recorded", which is TRUE of a projection rebuildable
  from the whole ledger — the reason the evaluator **throws** when the method is absent and **refuses**
  when it answers null. Asserted through the ENGINE — the three contest refusals `contest.unknown`,
  `contest.wrong-ticket` and `contest.already-resolved` are the whole reason the method exists, and a
  direct call would prove the SQL runs while proving nothing about the wiring that was missing.
  `apps/server/src/engine-context.ts`
- ✅ **`GET /v1/contests`** — the read surface the ticket banner and the attention strip share,
  filtered by workspace, ticket, claimant and open/resolved. Every row passes `canSee`, so a contest is
  exactly as visible as its ticket and never more; a caller who cannot see the ticket gets an empty list.
  **Each row carries the contested ticket's `title` and `projectId`**, joined from `proj_tickets` on
  `(ticket_id, workspace_id)` — LEFT, so a missing ticket row throws loudly instead of shortening the
  list, and scoped on both columns because `proj_contested` has no foreign key and nothing stops one
  ticket id appearing under two workspaces. Those two keys are what unblocked the attention strip's
  fourth item kind; serving them discloses nothing new, since the row's audience is already the
  ticket's own. **My Work reads its contested claims from `/v1/me/work` instead** (ruling P40): this
  route's `claimantId` is caller-supplied, and "my claims" is a question only the session can answer
  rather than a 403, so empty and forbidden are the same answer and the route is not an existence
  oracle. It never builds an `EffectiveRole` by hand and never reads `proj_memberships` directly — a
  project-scope row survives `membership.revoked` by design. `apps/server/src/sync/contests-route.ts`
- ⚙️ **Snapshot writer — the fold is the DOMAIN reducer, never `proj_tickets`** — Plan 1 shipped the
  `snapshots` table with no writer; this is the writer. `runSnapshotPass` snapshots every ticket whose
  head seq has run `SNAPSHOT_MIN_EVENTS` past its latest snapshot, and the stored `state` is
  `applyTicketEvent` folded over the ledger — the same reducer a replica runs. "Snapshot + tail is
  exactly full replay on every client" follows, but only JOINTLY with the serving side, and it was false
  until the fix round that added this clause: this writer skips every type outside `TICKET_EVENT_TYPES`
  while still advancing `seq` past it, so such an event reaches a replica only because
  `BOOTSTRAP_BACKFILL_TYPES` names it — and `visibility.set` was not named. Reading `proj_tickets`
  instead would look right indefinitely:
  that table is maintained by the same reducer today, so the two agree until the day they don't, and
  then a replica's bootstrap state differs from its own replay with nothing pointing at why. The
  equivalence is held by a test whose fixture leaves all three projections EMPTY and asserts it —
  under a fixture seeded through the full ingest path both implementations produce identical bytes and
  the assertion would distinguish nothing. `apps/server/src/sync/snapshot-writer.ts`
- ⚙️ **The snapshot's seq counts EVENTS, not folds** — `visibility.set`, `claim.contested` and
  `claim.contested_resolved` are all three filed against `kind: 'ticket'` and are deliberately absent
  from `TICKET_EVENT_TYPES`, so they
  move the cursor without moving the state. A writer that advanced the seq only on folded events would
  store a snapshot sitting behind events it had already passed, and a replica resuming from that cursor
  would be re-delivered them on every bootstrap. The cost of that design is paid on the serving side:
  those three exist in no snapshot, so bootstrap has to name every one of them or the ones it misses
  vanish below the cut. `TICKET_EVENT_TYPES` is TEN of the registry's thirteen `entityKind: 'ticket'`
  types, not all of them, and its own docblock used to say "the ten registered against
  `entityKind: 'ticket'`" — which is the most plausible reason `visibility.set` was missed.
  `apps/server/src/sync/snapshot-writer.ts`, `packages/domain/src/ticket-state.ts`
- ⚙️ **Reducer deps folded from the ledger's own definition events** — `buildLedgerDeps` builds
  `TicketStateDeps` from `workflow.defined` / `ticket-type.defined` rows, ordered by `workspace_seq`
  and NOT by `(entity_kind, entity_id, seq)`: a ticket type's key lives in its PAYLOAD and successive
  definitions of one key arrive on different entity streams (each event gets a fresh ULID entity id),
  so per-entity seq cannot order them against each other at all. Greatest `workspace_seq` wins for
  types and greatest `version` wins for workflows — the same two rules `proj_ticket_types`' guarded
  `DO UPDATE` and `proj_workflows`' `DO NOTHING` + `ORDER BY version DESC` already enforce, so the
  ledger fold and the projections cannot disagree about which definition is current — and neither can a
  replica, because `GET /v1/sync/bootstrap` serves `definitions` in this same `workspace_seq` order
  (12.117). It did not, for one task: the route shipped the plan text's `(entity_kind, entity_id, seq)`,
  so a workspace that had ever redefined a ticket-type key handed the replica the definitions in the one
  order this loader's docblock says cannot resolve them. Both rules are
  pinned by a fixture that defines one key TWICE, with the winning definition deliberately given the
  smaller ULID entity id so the plan text's ordering picks the loser, and the maps never cross a
  workspace. *That workspace filter is pinned by ONE case — dropping it reddens 12.93 and nothing else,
  974 of 975 green across all 88 server test files, re-measured; the "red in seven cases" this file's
  test-case entry used to claim was never run.* `apps/server/src/sync/ledger-deps.ts`
- ⚙️ **The writer's absences, named** — no locking (safe because the row is keyed by seq and the fold is
  pure below it, so a late arrival is the next pass's work, not a corruption), tickets only (there is no
  other reducer to fold), no visibility or tenancy gate (a background sweep has no caller identity — the
  route that SERVES a snapshot owes that), and no retention: the table grows one row per snapshot per
  entity forever. *Retention is unowned.* Retention is NOT how a stale snapshot is invalidated, and the
  distinction is worth keeping straight: `readLatestSnapshot` is `ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT 1`, so a
  higher-seq row supersedes for every reader — a `DELETE` would buy nothing but a longer replay. The
  writer's deps also resolve the NEWEST definitions, which is safe only because the publish door below
  re-snapshots at head and so removes any tail that spans a version boundary; a ticket that door SKIPS
  is exactly one this pass can still throw on, and the interval wrapper logs and swallows that.
  `apps/server/src/sync/snapshot-writer.ts`
- ⚙️ **Refresh on publish, SNAPSHOT door — the repair is a snapshot ABOVE the offending event, not a
  deletion** — a `workflow.defined` re-snapshots every ticket standing on an older version of that
  workflow, at the ticket's own stream head, holding the state walked forward by the domain's
  `foldTicketToWorkflowVersion`. It runs inside the publish's transaction, before the projection loop,
  and it closes a gap that had no owner: until it, publishing invalidated no snapshot, so bootstrap
  could serve a snapshot plus a tail carrying a `ticket.transitioned` into a state the new version had
  dropped, and both the replica's `loadBootstrap` and the server's own `runSnapshotPass` threw
  `applyTicketEvent: workflow "…" has no state "…"` on it. Deleting or refusing to serve the stale
  snapshot makes it WORSE — both serve the ticket's full history instead, which holds the same event
  earlier — and no fold at the serving end can reach it, which is why the fix is on the writing side.
  Before the projection loop is not incidental: `proj_tickets`'s own refresh-on-publish sets exactly
  the rows this selects to the new version, so running second would match nothing. Not a projection
  either: `snapshots` has no shadow copy, so an unqualified write from inside one escapes
  `verify-projections.ts`'s `search_path` fence and writes LIVE during a verify. Each ticket folds
  inside a `SAVEPOINT`, so a fold that cannot be walked forward leaves that ticket's stale snapshot
  standing and the publish still commits — *a skip is silent on the live path; ingest has no logger to
  put the returned list in.* **"Every ticket" includes tickets that have never been snapshotted**, and
  the candidate query carries no snapshot-existence filter for that reason: below `SNAPSHOT_MIN_EVENTS`
  the background pass has written no row, bootstrap serves such a ticket its FULL history, and that
  history holds the offending event too — so it needs a snapshot above that event more than a
  snapshotted ticket does, not less. The publish therefore folds such a ticket from event 1 rather than
  from a tail, and writes a row below the threshold that the background pass would not; both are the
  price of the repair, and both are a FIRST-publish cost per ticket — the row this door leaves at the
  head is what its next publish resumes from. Narrowing the query to tickets that already hold a
  snapshot reopens the throw for everything under twenty events, permanently, since under that filter
  neither door ever writes them a first row (measured — 12.159 reds, 12.156–12.158 stay green).
  `apps/server/src/sync/snapshot-refresh.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/ingest/ingest-service.ts`
- ⚙️ **`SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_MS` (300000) and `SNAPSHOT_MIN_EVENTS` (20)** — additive config, both
  `.positive()` integers refused at the door. Not tidiness on either: `setInterval(fn, 0)` runs the
  pass on every tick of the event loop, and a zero `MIN_EVENTS` makes every ticket due on every pass,
  which recomputes a snapshot at a seq that already has a row and collides on the snapshots primary
  key. Neither is a correctness knob — a server that never writes a snapshot answers with exactly the
  same state, only slower. `apps/server/src/config.ts`, `.env.example`
- ⚙️ **Started at the one boot path, stopped by Fastify's `onClose` — and the shutdown that fires it
  now exists** — `startSnapshotWriter`'s stop function is hung on `app.addHook('onClose', …)`,
  registered BEFORE `app.listen` because fastify 5 throws `FST_ERR_INSTANCE_ALREADY_LISTENING` on an
  `addHook` after start. It is not registered inside `buildApp` with the routes: `buildApp` is called
  by around sixty test files, and a five-minute timer in each of them could fire mid-test and write
  snapshots into a fixture nothing expects them in. **This bullet used to end "today nothing calls
  `app.close()` … it stops by itself on the day a real shutdown lands", and asserted that
  `apps/server/src/index.ts` had no SIGTERM handler, no `pool.end()` and no `app.close()`. All of that
  is now false**: the graceful-shutdown bullet in §13 is that day — `installShutdownHandlers` awaits
  `app.close()` on SIGTERM/SIGINT, which runs this hook, and only then ends the pool. The deferral cost
  nothing: not one line of this registration changed when the shutdown landed. What is unchanged is the
  `pnpm dev` case — a process nobody signals still runs the writer for its whole life, which costs
  nothing because the interval is `unref`'d. `apps/server/src/index.ts`
- ⚙️ **One assembled test server** — `buildTestApp(db)` builds the real app, real auth, real ingest and
  the real projection registry, returning the app, its own OTP sender and the ungated internal ingest
  door. It replaces four hand-copied assemblies that had to be extended one by one every time
  `buildApp` gained a route. `apps/server/test/helpers/app.ts`
- ✅ **`GET /v1/sync/bootstrap`** — first-run replica build without replaying full history: for every
  ticket `listVisibleTickets` returns for the caller, the latest reducer-folded snapshot (if one exists) plus a per-entity tail
  — everything after the snapshot's seq, PLUS every `comment.added` / `attachment.added` /
  `visibility.set` / `claim.contested` / `claim.contested_resolved` regardless of position. That list is
  not a taste judgement and it is not hand-kept: it is **every one of the thirteen types the registry
  binds to `entityKind: 'ticket'` that the reducer does not fold**, because the snapshot writer skips
  exactly those while still advancing `seq` past them, so an unlisted one is in neither the snapshot nor
  the tail. `visibility.set` was missing from the list and was therefore dropped — but only when it sat
  at or below a ticket's snapshot seq, so what a replica received depended on when a background
  maintenance pass last ran (12.115). Every `workflow.defined` and `ticket-type.defined` event goes to
  everyone including guests (a guest cannot render a status without the workflow definition, Plan 4
  §5), **in ascending `workspace_seq` order** — the same order `buildLedgerDeps` folds them in, so a
  replica that applies them in the order it received them lands where the server did. That order is part
  of the contract: the route shipped `(entity_kind, entity_id, seq)`, which is the ordering 12.92 exists
  to prove picks the LOSER when one ticket-type key has been redefined (12.117). The whole read runs
  inside one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction with the workspace's stream cursor
  read FIRST, so the response is a consistent cut and subscribing at the returned `streamSeq` can only
  re-deliver an event the client already has, never skip one. Both halves of that claim are pinned by
  the tests rather than only by this sentence — **and for a while only one of them was**: `streamSeq` is
  checked against the real cursor (`readStreamSeq`), not merely a positive number, and the transaction's
  literal `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ` is asserted directly, but that second check is a
  MEMBERSHIP check and membership cannot see position, so the ORDERING half went unpinned until 12.118
  asserted the cursor query is issued before the first per-ticket tail read. The un-snapshotted branch (a
  ticket too new to have a snapshot
  yet: `snapshot: null`, `events` is its full history) and the `definitions` query's per-workspace scoping
  (a sibling workspace's own `workflow.defined`/`ticket-type.defined` rows must not appear) are likewise
  exercised now, not merely implied by the shape of the response type. 403 `{error:'not_a_member'}` when the
  caller resolves no workspace role; 401 with no session. `apps/server/src/sync/sync-routes.ts`
- ⚙️ **`EffectiveRole.projects` is a `Map`, and the wire shape is not** — `me.projectIds` is
  `[...eff.projects.keys()]`, not `[...eff.projects]`: the map itself spreads as `[key, value]` TUPLES,
  which the plan's own snippet got wrong. `apps/server/src/sync/sync-routes.ts`
- ✅ **`POST /v1/sync`** — the sync ingress (amendment 16): a batch of offline-originated claims, 1-100,
  answered 200 with one index-aligned `SyncEventResult` each. Per event, in order: an envelope error is
  `invalid` (a malformed claim is not a claim, so there is nothing coherent to contest); a claim whose
  `actorId` is not the session is `invalid` `actor-mismatch`; a client-supplied `claim.contested` is
  `invalid` `reserved-type`; an id already in the ledger is answered `committed` from the STORED row;
  an id already contested is answered with the ONE recorded contest; otherwise the claim goes to
  ingest, and a refusal on a **ticket** becomes a `claim.contested` ledger event (`contested`, carrying
  the contest's own event id) while a refusal on any other entity is `refused`, since there is no ticket
  to attach a contest to. **`refused` therefore does NOT mean "the entity was not a ticket"** — the
  plan's snippet glossed it that way and a replica written to that gloss would be wrong; it means *a
  refusal no coherent contest can record*, and `entity-workspace-mismatch` on a ticket is the second
  shape that reaches it (next bullet). `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/sync/sync-routes.ts`
- ✅ **A refusal whose contest the server's own door refuses is answered, not 500'd** — when
  `serverAppend` comes back `refused` for the `claim.contested`, the claimant is told the ORIGINAL
  refusal's `rule` and `reason` inside `results`, character for character the answer `POST /v1/events`
  gives for the identical claim. One shape reaches this today and it is live rather than theoretical: a
  claim stamping workspace A on a ticket bound to workspace B is refused `entity-workspace-mismatch`,
  and `buildContestedClaim` copies that `workspaceId` and `entity` onto the contest verbatim, so the
  contest is the SAME mis-filing and the same `commitOne` guard refuses it. Throwing there — which is
  what the plan's step 7b prescribed and what shipped first — made `POST /v1/sync` answer **500** from a
  route whose whole contract is that per-event answers ride inside `results`: measured on
  `[legal1, bad, legal2]`, `legal1` committed but its answer lost, `legal2` never processed at all, and
  the retry and the solo claim both 500 again — **an outbox wedged with no path out**. Degrading rather
  than enumerating the uncontestable rules is what closes it as a class. *The throw is KEPT for a
  contest-append that comes back `invalid` or is absent: a server-built envelope the server's own door
  calls malformed has no honest per-event answer, only a bug to surface.*
  `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`
- ✅ **The client's offline claim goes through the GATED door, and the deps type is what says so** —
  `POST /v1/sync` submits the claim with `ingestClientBatch`, so being made offline buys an event
  nothing: every membership, role, envelope-origin and visibility question is asked exactly as it is on
  `POST /v1/events`. The plan's own snippet called `ingestBatch` — the internal, ungated door — for
  that very event, which would have skipped `checkPermission` for **every offline-originated event in
  the product**, the case `ingest-service.ts`'s docblock names by name. This route needs BOTH doors (a
  contest is envelope-origin `'server'`, which the client door refuses by design), and the shape that
  keeps that from re-opening the hole is the one `registerInviteRoutes` already uses: the narrowed
  `ClientIngest` plus `ServerAppend` as a **separately named capability**, never the full
  `IngestService`. A reflex `deps.ingest.ingestBatch(...)` here does not compile — *Property
  'ingestBatch' does not exist on type 'ClientIngest'* — and two route tests fail on two different gate
  checks if the call is swapped for the internal door. `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`
- ⚙️ **A contest is built by the constructor, not by hand** — the append goes through
  `buildContestedClaim`, which reads the contest's `workspaceId` and `entity` off the refused claim and
  therefore has no parameter through which they could disagree; the plan's snippet hand-rolled the
  envelope with `newSubmittedEvent`, which is exactly the shape `checkContestCoherence` exists to catch.
  The contest is attributed to the **claimant**, and that is a deviation from three of this repo's own
  docblocks, recorded rather than quietly taken: they describe `env.actorId` on a contest as "the SERVER
  identity", and **no such identity exists** — `actorId` is a ULID, every ULID in the actor position is
  a real person, and `bootstrapWorkspace` (the other server-authored sequence) likewise attributes its
  appends to the human who caused them, with envelope-origin `'server'` carrying the "the server wrote
  this" fact. Minting a system user is a product decision no plan has taken. *Consequence, stated
  because it is invisible otherwise: on this path `proj_contested.claimant_id` equals the contest's own
  `actorId`, so the fan-out mis-routing that module guards against would be undetectable against
  production data — it stays detectable only because `notifications/fanout.ts`'s fixture uses two
  different users. Owner for minting a system identity, if the product ever wants one: Plan 11.*
  `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`
- ⚙️ **The `proj_contested` UNIQUE index finally has the lookup it was built for** — the migration
  comment and this section both said the index makes a sync retry *find* the prior contest; nothing
  performed that lookup, and `isUniqueViolation` matches only `events_pkey`, so a second contest
  embedding the same refused claim raised 23505 out of `commitOne` and **rejected the whole batch call**
  — the answers already decided for that batch's committed neighbours never reached the client, and a
  retry could not recover because a re-minted contest carries a fresh event id. Measured before the fix:
  `expected 500 to be 200`. `POST /v1/sync` now reads `proj_contested` by `original_event_id` before
  minting a contest, and catches the constraint by name for the concurrent-request residual two
  requests can still race into. *The defect stays live for every other `ingestBatch` caller — this
  closes the sync door, not the class.* **The first round then scoped the whole residual to this one
  mechanism, and that was wrong**: a second batch-killing throw sat in `contest()`, reachable with no
  unique violation anywhere on the path — see the "refusal whose contest the server's own door refuses"
  bullet above, which closes that one. The two are different mechanisms and only the `events_pkey` half
  is still open. `apps/server/src/sync/sync-service.ts`
- ⚙️ **The visibility pre-check the plan asked for is deliberately absent** — Plan 10's step 6 (an
  explicit `getResolvedVisibility` + `canSee` pass before ingest) duplicates
  `checkVisibilityPermission`, which already refuses every ticket-entity event whose ticket the caller
  cannot see, INSIDE the ingest transaction against the client the workspace lock has settled. Two

  observable answer is identical, and dropping the local copy is what makes the invisible-ticket case a

- ⚙️ **`duplicate-event-id` is an idempotent ACCEPT on this path, twice over** — retries are expected
  here, so the ledger lookup answers one before ingest is ever attempted, and a duplicate coming back
  OUT of ingest (the race between that read and the insert) is re-read and accepted rather than passed
  through as `invalid`, which the plan's own switch does and which would contradict amendment 16 for
  exactly the case retries produce. The order matters beyond tidiness: placed after ingest, a retry of
  an already-committed claim is re-judged by the gate, so a caller who has since lost the ticket is told
  their committed claim was rejected and the ledger grows a contest for an event sitting in it.
