- ⚙️ **The replica (Task 9) — a read model FOLDED locally, never a downloaded projection** — `createReplica({ storage })` over the Task 8 `SyncStorage` seam. Confirmed ticket state is always `applyTicketEvent` replayed from a snapshot baseline plus the local events past it, against definitions folded out of the `workflow.defined` / `ticket-type.defined` events the replica itself received. There is no code path that could assemble a ticket from a server projection, because the replica only ever receives envelopes and snapshots — which is amendment 17's point, not an implementation preference: the offline copy is a copy only while it derives what the server derives, by the same reducer. `loadBootstrap`, `applyServerItem`, `purgeEntity`, `revokeScope`, `getTicket`, `listTickets`, `listContests`, `listEntityEvents`, `searchLocal`, `getCursor`/`setCursor`, `getMe`, `buildDeps`, `getSnapshotProvenance` (the block a bootstrap delivered, ruling P36 — the only way to name who put a ticket in its state on a device whose events are below the snapshot line), `onChange`. `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`
- ⚙️ **Filter before folding — ten of thirteen, and it is the common path** — thirteen event types bind to `entityKind: 'ticket'`; `applyTicketEvent` folds TEN and throws on the rest, and `GET /v1/sync/bootstrap` backfills five ticket-entity types of which three (`visibility.set`, `claim.contested`, `claim.contested_resolved`) are among the three it does not fold. `claim.contested` is backfilled unconditionally, so an unfiltered replay throws on an ordinary first sync rather than on an edge case. The replica tests membership in `TICKET_EVENT_TYPES` before every fold, on both the stream door and the bootstrap door — and the guard is now proved on `claim.contested` itself (case 12.137) and not only on `visibility.set`: until that case landed, every sentence here rested the "common path" claim on the type no test put in front of the guard. `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`
- ⚙️ **Refresh on publish, locally — one version walk, ON BOTH DOORS, and the snapshot is the load-bearing half** — a `workflow.defined` folds every local ticket on that workflow forward through the DOMAIN's `foldTicketToWorkflowVersion`, the same function `proj_tickets` calls, **whether it arrives over the stream or inside a bootstrap's `definitions`**; re-categorised states get the new category, dropped states move where `stateMapping` sends them, and the transition log is carried across **unchanged** so each entry keeps the workflow and version whose vocabulary its `toStateId` is written in — the only reason `require-different-user` can walk it forward afterwards. The fold then persists the folded state as the ticket's **replay baseline**: without that, the next event for the ticket sends the reducer back over pre-fold history and replays a `toStateId` the new version may have dropped, undoing the fold or throwing. On the stream door the baseline only ever moves forward (`MAX` against the standing one), because bootstrap can leave backfilled events below a snapshot's `seq`; on the bootstrap door the snapshot's own `seq` is carried across unchanged, because the tail above it has not been applied yet and raising the baseline would skip it. A version the replica cannot supply is refused as a **hole in the chain** and not as an unmapped drop — two faults with different owners — and the whole arrival rolls back.

  **The bootstrap half was MISSING and this sentence used to claim it anyway.** `foldTicketsOntoVersion` had one call site — `applyServerItem`'s definition branch — so `loadBootstrap` stored the same `workflow.defined` events through `applyDefinition` and never folded, and the two doors answered differently on identical input: stream `{v:2, cat:'open'}`, bootstrap `{v:1, cat:'active'}` (measured, case 12.136). A device that bootstrapped after a publish served a stale `stateCategory` until a state-changing event happened to arrive, and the backfilled types the reducer folds are all no-ops, so nothing healed it. **Recorded, not fixed — TWO hazards, different inputs and different owners, now each pinned by a case in `packages/sync-client/test/recorded-hazards.test.ts`.** (a) **CLOSED on the server, and this sentence used to say it was open.** A bootstrap TAIL can carry a `ticket.transitioned` into a state the new version dropped, which throws on replay whether or not the snapshot was folded first; the repair is a server-written snapshot above that event, which is what `apps/server/src/sync/snapshot-refresh.ts` now writes on every version-raising publish (test cases 12.156–12.158). The client side is unchanged and stays: `foldSnapshotForward` is still the only fold on the LIVE stream door, so after the server fix the bootstrap-door fold is a no-op in the common case rather than dead code. What the server fix does NOT reach is a tail that already spanned an older state-dropping publish before it shipped — that ticket is skipped and keeps its stale snapshot. *No longer owned by Plan 10 Task 6; the residue is enumerated in `test-cases/12-offline-sync-apps.md`.* (b) The bootstrap door's `writeSnapshot` is an unconditional `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE SET seq = excluded.seq` with no `Math.max` against the standing baseline, so a re-bootstrap against the server's still-stale snapshot LOWERS a baseline the stream door had raised and replays pre-fold history against new vocabulary — the whole response then rolls back and the device can never re-bootstrap again (case 12.154, with `events: []`, which is what separates it from (a)). This used to be excused as "unreachable today, because nothing calls `loadBootstrap` a second time", and that was false in the commit that wrote it: 12.149 re-bootstraps an already-bootstrapped replica. The obvious `Math.max` guard is measurably WORSE — it removes the throw and silently drops the higher event from the transition log — so the repair is a decision about what a re-bootstrap means. *Owner: Plan 10 Task 11.* `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`
- ⚙️ **Two lookups over one table, and unifying them is the silent failure** — `buildLocalDeps` answers the REDUCER's question (what workflow W means now: latest version wins, a pure by-id lookup that must serve ANY id, because `ticket.workflow_rebound` names one the ticket is not on yet), while `workflowVersionsOf` answers the FOLD's question (what W meant at version N, for every version strictly between). Keeping every published version is therefore a hard requirement of the local schema, not an optimisation. Collapsed into one helper, a multi-hop fold silently picks a destination through a definition the ticket never sat in — no throw, no sync error, just a different board on every client. `packages/sync-client/src/replica/local-deps.ts`
- ⚙️ **Contests sit beside ticket state, never inside it** — `claim.contested` folds into a local `contests` table (upserted, so a stub written from a sync response is corrected by the server's envelope and replicas converge exactly); `claimantId` is the actor of the **refused claim**, never of the contest the server filed. `claim.contested_resolved` flips `resolved`/`resolution`. A contested claim does not move the ticket, because a refused claim did not happen — and a contest that arrives BELOW a snapshot's `seq`, which is what unconditional backfill produces, still reaches the contests table while staying out of the reducer's replay. `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`
- ⚙️ **Purge and scope-revocation — every readable trace, and one deliberate exception** — `purgeEntity` deletes the entity's ledger rows, ticket row, snapshot, **its snapshot provenance block** and the contests OTHERS raised; **the local user's own contested-claim stubs survive**, because their `originalEvent` is what this user authored on this device and silently vanishing it would break honesty invariant 5. Outbox rows for a purged ticket stay queued so a claim in transit still goes up, contests and surfaces, rather than disappearing. `scope-revoked` is the same rules applied by scope in one instruction rather than one purge per id — per-id purges would tell the recipient how many tickets existed in a scope they were never shown — and it is idempotent because every statement is a delete. A workspace-wide revocation also drops the definitions and the resume cursor. **Recorded, not fixed: `workflows` and `ticket_types` carry no `workspace_id` column, so those two deletes cannot be scoped and the local store is implicitly single-workspace** while every other statement is workspace-scoped. *Owner: whoever widens `packages/sync-client/src/storage/schema.ts` past one workspace.* `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`
- ⚙️ **`SyncItem` is a THREE-way union and the replica discriminates all three** — `LedgerEvent | PurgeInstruction | ScopeRevokedInstruction`. Plan 4's Task 11 header and Plan 10 Task 9's own interface list both name only two, and the server file says what that costs: a consumer written to the header treats a `scope-revoked` as neither a purge nor an envelope and silently drops the one instruction that tells a removed device to empty itself. The instruction switch here carries **no `default` and no trailing return**, so a fourth member is a compile error (TS2366) rather than an instruction the replica ignores — measured by adding one and reading the error, not asserted. **The union is MIRRORED, not imported**: this package cannot depend on `apps/server`, and there is no shared home for these types today. *Owner for moving them into `@gisik-nokma/domain`, where both sides already depend: Plan 10 Task 11 (the sync engine), which consumes the same union and would otherwise inherit a third copy.* `packages/sync-client/src/replica/sync-item.ts`
- ⚙️ **Instants are held, not re-rendered** — every clock the replica hands back is the server's own Z-normalised ISO string, stored and returned with no parse, no re-format and no device-offset step. That is what makes `ORDER BY contested_at` chronological rather than merely alphabetical, and it is what the later view-models' `istDayOf` reads: a device-offset render here would move a claim across IST midnight on every machine whose clock is not Asia/Kolkata, with every individual number still looking reasonable. **Task 9 derives no days itself** — its surface has no day-shaped method and it calls no ist-day symbol; the day contract binds Tasks 12 and 13, and what this task owes them is exactly the unparsed instant. Pinned under `TZ=America/Los_Angeles` as well as `TZ=Asia/Kolkata`, each run opening with a positive control that the timezone switch actually took effect — **the first TZ-varying test written in sync-client and the fifteenth of the repo's sixteen** (`test/outbox.test.ts` is the sixteenth) (12.152 in `test/outbox.test.ts` is sync-client's second, landing later in the same PR — which is what made the "fifteenth" this sentence used to claim stale by one), following an established convention rather than introducing one: 15 other test files assign `process.env.TZ`, several opening with the same `CONTROL — setting process.env.TZ really does change what this process thinks local time is` case, and recording the limit this one inherits — `process.env.TZ` is live for V8's `Date` getters under vitest's `forks` pool and inert under `threads`. The control is asserted rather than assumed for exactly that reason: in a codebase where every machine is already in IST, a day-sensitive case with a dead zone knob is evidence about the machine. The ceiling is stated too, because the lane's own `TZ` correction names it: varying `TZ` is evidence only where the value under test moves with it, and here the device-offset mutation reddens under both zones — what the non-IST run establishes is the negative, that nothing on this path moves with the machine's zone. `packages/sync-client/test/replica.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **Honest local search** — `searchLocal` matches ticket titles (read off the `fields.title` CLAIM the reducer writes, not a top-level property) and comment text through `docToPlainText`, LIKE-matched over the local ledger. It never pretends to be the server index, and a comment whose ticket the replica no longer holds is skipped rather than surfacing a title it cannot show. `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`

- ✅ **Instant, named refusals offline (Task 10) — the SAME engine, so the answer cannot disagree with the server's** — `enqueue` runs `evaluateEvent` before anything is queued, against the replica with this device's queued claims overlaid, and an illegal claim is refused with the rule id and the plain-language reason the server would have given. A refusal queues NOTHING. The context is `packages/rule-engine`'s own `buildMemoryContext` — the reducer-backed in-memory context whose docblock already named this caller — seeded from the replica's tables rather than hand-rolled: a fourth `EngineContext` would be a pre-check that CAN disagree with the server, which is worse than no pre-check because it teaches a person to trust a verdict the server will overturn. `packages/sync-client/src/precheck/local-context.ts`
- ⚙️ **The pending overlay — one fold, two consumers, and every map seeded from the replica's TABLES** — confirmed ticket state with the queued claims folded on top by `applyTicketEvent`, so the ordered pair (queue the parent's reopen, then create a child under it) passes locally exactly as it will on the server. Definitions come from `workflows` / `ticket_types`, never from a replay of the `events` log: `revokeScope` empties those tables and deliberately leaves the definition EVENTS standing, so a log-replaying context would resurrect the definitions a removed device was told to forget. The pre-check and the outbox's `pendingFor` share this one fold, because a pre-check judging against a different pending world than the board renders is the same lie told twice. A claim the reducer cannot fold (its definitions went with a revocation while its outbox row stayed) is named `unfoldable` rather than thrown: the claim stays queued and flushable, and its pending READING is `null` rather than a partial fold. **The stream door does the OPPOSITE with the same reducer and the same deps, and that is now stated on both sides rather than only on this one**: `refoldTicket` has no catch, so the same revocation race takes the whole arrival down — envelope insert included — because `applyServerItem` is machine-driven and its contract is per-arrival rollback, while `enqueue` is a user's offline action that must not brick the device. *Recorded, not fixed; pinned by case 12.155. Owner: Plan 10 Task 11 (the sync engine), the first caller of `applyServerItem` with a cursor to advance.* `packages/sync-client/src/precheck/local-context.ts`
- ✅ **The outbox — ordered, persisted claims in transit, and pending is NEVER settled fact** — `createOutbox({storage, replica})`: `enqueue` (pre-check first), `nextBatch` (position order, only rows whose backoff is due), `applyResult`, `recordFailure`, `queuedCount`, `failedItems`, `pendingFor`, `pendingCreatedTickets`. Nothing here writes to `tickets`, `snapshots` or `events`, so every settled surface keeps answering from confirmed state alone and a queued claim moves only the two pending readings. `packages/sync-client/src/outbox/outbox.ts`
- ⚙️ **What each server answer does to a claim** — `committed` folds it into the local ledger through `applyServerItem` (the same door every server item takes, so the later stream echo is ignored as the duplicate it is) and clears the row — and clears it for ITSELF, because `applyServerItem` returns early on a duplicate id and never reaches its own delete. The path that produces that is a RE-BOOTSTRAP, not the stream echo: an echo is the first arrival of its id and clears the row itself, while `loadBootstrap` inserts the device's own already-committed claim into `events` and touches the outbox not at all. `contested` records the contest locally from the response and clears the row: if visibility was lost with the refusal the real `claim.contested` envelope never arrives, and that stub is the claimant's only record. `refused` and `invalid` are TERMINAL — the row stays, visibly failed, carrying the server's own words. `packages/sync-client/src/outbox/outbox.ts`
- ⚙️ **Two failure words that are not synonyms** — `recordFailure` is TRANSIENT (the flush never reached a verdict): it bumps `attempts`, pushes `next_attempt_at` out by `backoffMs`, and leaves the row `queued` so it is offered again when due. `status = 'failed'` is TERMINAL (the server answered no). Collapsing the two strands every retryable claim on the device forever, silently. `backoffMs(attempts) = min(1000·2^attempts, 60_000)` is the FLUSH curve and is **not** `app-core`'s `backoffDelayMs` (500 ms initial, 30 s cap, ±20% jitter), which is the websocket RECONNECT curve — two surfaces, two independently chosen constants, no jitter here because one device's queue is not a thundering herd. **The two "initial" delays are not comparable and the docblock used to compare them**: `stream.ts` increments its attempt counter AFTER calling, so 500 ms really is its first reconnect delay, while `recordFailure` increments BEFORE calling, so the first flush retry waits `backoffMs(1)` = 2 s and `backoffMs(0)` = 1 s is unreachable from the only production caller. 12.148 pins the increment-before behaviour through the function (its first retry is due at `backoffMs(1)`, its second at `backoffMs(2)`) and 12.152 asserts the literal `…00:00:02.000Z`; it was only the prose that said one second. `packages/sync-client/src/outbox/outbox.ts`

- ⚙️ **The sync engine (Task 11) — one loop, and the cursor is the only thing it is really guarding** — `createSyncEngine({replica, outbox, transport, reconnectDelayMs?})` drives the honest loop: bootstrap when there is nothing to lose, subscribe at the persisted cursor, apply every frame in strict arrival order, persist the cursor only at `caught-up`, and flush the outbox whenever the stream says it is live. Status is a four-value machine (`offline` | `connecting` | `catching-up` | `live`) with `onStatus` subscribers. Frames apply on one serialised promise chain, so `caught-up` can never write a cursor over items that have not applied yet. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ⚙️ **A frame that will not fold STALLS the stream instead of wedging it** — `applyServerItem` throws per-arrival by design (an arrival the replica cannot fold is refused whole, envelope insert rolled back with it), and the two obvious things to do with that throw are both quiet failures: swallow it and let `caught-up` advance, and the item is skipped permanently with no local trace; swallow it and pin the cursor, and the same item is redelivered and re-thrown on every reconnect — a hot loop that never converges and never says anything. The engine closes the stream, schedules no reconnect, freezes the cursor, and keeps the reducer's own words in `lastApplyFailure()`. One visible terminal state instead of an invisible infinite one. **THREE CALLERS IN THIS FILE REACH THAT THROW, and the guard is on all three — enumerated, because the version of this sentence that said "both doors of the pair" was written while the third was open.** `onItem`'s chain (`applyOne` → `applyServerItem`); `onCaughtUp`'s chain, where the plan's own snippet swallowed too and fixing only the first leaves the skip intact; and **`flush`'s own `applyResult` loop**, where a `committed` verdict folds the claim through the same `applyServerItem`. The third had no catch at all: a fold failure there rejected `flush()`, left `status()` at `catching-up` and `lastApplyFailure()` at `null`, and — with no `recordFailure` — re-sent the identical batch on the next flush, which is the hot loop this whole design exists to prevent. It now records a failure for every unjudged row (so backoff applies) and stalls. Case 12.170. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ⚙️ **`lastError()` and `lastApplyFailure()` are two accessors because they are two facts** — the first is amendment 19's server `error` frame kept verbatim (the server named a reason; a shell shows it, and an errored stream has NOT caught up, so the cursor does not move). The second is a LOCAL fold failure, where the server said nothing wrong and this device could not apply what it was correctly sent. One slot for both would have made the accessor mean two things while its docblock claimed one. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ⚙️ **When the engine may re-bootstrap, which is the question the plan's guard was not asking** — the bootstrap door writes a snapshot baseline unconditionally, so re-bootstrapping a replica that has folded past the server's snapshot LOWERS the baseline and the replay then throws (recorded hazard, case 12.154, whose obvious `Math.max` repair was measured and rejected for trading a loud throw for a silent divergence). `needsBootstrap` asks whether there is a local baseline to lose — cursor at 0, and no local tickets — rather than the plan's `me === null`, which is written once and never cleared and therefore also refuses the bootstrap a workspace-wide revocation NEEDS (`revokeScope` deletes the tickets, the definitions and the cursor row, but not `me`). Both directions are pinned by mutation, cases 12.165 and 12.166. **The hazard itself is not fixed** — this closes the only production route to it. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ⚙️ **The flush loop — order, and a result that does not match its claim** — one drain at a time, batches in `position` order, `applyResult` per event. A transport rejection is TRANSIENT: `recordFailure` on the whole batch, rows stay `queued`, retried when `backoffMs` says they are due, never lost. `POST /v1/sync` answers index-aligned with the request, and every result but `invalid` also carries its own `eventId` — so the loop checks the two against each other **over the whole batch before anything is applied**, and one disagreement condemns the array: the batch is recorded as a transient failure and every row stays queued. Applying as it went would file a stranger's verdict against the rows whose result carries no id, before reaching the entry that proves the shift. **The residual is stated rather than claimed away**: an array in which every member is `invalid` with `eventId: null` carries no id information at all, so index alignment is the only contract there is and this check cannot see a shift in it. A batch whose head does not move between passes is failed rather than re-flushed forever. Case 12.169. **And the verdict itself can throw**: `committed` folds the claim through `applyServerItem`, so this loop is the third caller of a function that refuses per arrival — it records a failure for the row that threw and every row behind it, then stalls, rather than rejecting `flush()` and re-sending. See the stall bullet above; case 12.170. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`

The sweep behind that claim grepped for a frame literal and could not see server-GENERATED frames — true answer, wrong question.* Case **12.365** drives the frame off the socket and asserts the `{id, headSeq}` list by value, together with the two clauses that are the reason the arm reads fields at all — two junk elements dropped, one per predicate clause, while the good id beside them travels, and a notice for another workspace refused. **What it buys over the existing coverage is identification, measured:** the `headSeq`-dropping mutation reddens `socket-cut.test.ts` too, but as two 120-second timeouts and a null assertion (3 of 3, 261.90 s) that name neither field nor branch, where this case reddens alone in 8 ms naming `headSeq`. `app-core`'s `parseFrame` was measured and found ALREADY witnessed (its `SCHEMA_FIXTURES` loop asserts deep equality against the frame), so the carried item's "`parseFrame`/transport" was half right and the half is named rather than quietly closed. `packages/sync-client/src/net/http-transport.ts`
- ⚙️ **The post-Task-18 sync-surface fixes** *(this bullet names itself and its PR, per the category rule in this section's banner)* — **PR `exec/43-sync-surface-fixes`**, four findings the Task 18 catalogue round raised and could not fix inside a docs-only change set. **A TEXT `ping`/`pong` protocol nothing implements was deleted from four production sites** in `app-core/src/online/stream.ts` and `sync-client/src/net/http-transport.ts` — the server's heartbeat is `socket.ping()`, a WebSocket PROTOCOL control frame the platform socket answers below JavaScript, and the server's message door answers `bad-message` to any text frame that is not a `subscribe`, so both arms were unreachable and both agreeing tests agreed with nothing (case 12.328). `parseFrame`'s exported return type narrows accordingly. - ⚙️ **The HTTP/WS transport — one wire, and no second frame union** — `createHttpTransport({baseUrl, token, fetchImpl?, webSocketFactory?})`: `GET /v1/sync/bootstrap`, `POST /v1/sync`, and amendment 13's stream at `streamUrl(baseUrl)`. The socket seam is a hand-injectable `WebSocketLike` following `packages/app-core/src/online/stream.ts`'s shipped pattern rather than the DOM `WebSocket` type, so Tauri and Expo can supply their own. Parsing is deliberately more permissive than `serverStreamFrameSchema` for the reason that file argues at length — a client that hard-drops a frame it could have delivered fails silently — with TWO exceptions held to the server's own rule, enumerated because this sentence said "one" while the second was unguarded: `envelope.workspaceSeq` on an `event` frame (an integer > 0, because a frame without it cannot be resumed from) and `seq` on a `caught-up` frame (an integer >= 0 — the value this client PERSISTS through `setCursor`, and the looser bound is the empty workspace's legitimate position). `1e999` is legal JSON, parses to `Infinity` with `typeof 'number'`, and before the second guard it persisted as the string `"Infinity"`: `needsBootstrap` false forever, every later subscribe serialising as `{"afterSeq":null}` — a device that could neither resume nor re-bootstrap, silently, across restarts. Case 12.171. Owner: Plan 10 Task 12, which owns the transport seam next.* **A TEXT `ping` is answered with NOTHING** — the arm replying `{type:'pong'}` was deleted with its app-core twin (case 12.328), because no server sends that frame; 12.167 asserts the sent list is exactly `['subscribe']` and keeps it deleted. *This sentence said `ping` is answered with `pong` for one commit after the deletion: the change set corrected its app-core sibling 1,351 lines above and left this one standing, found by review.
