- ⚙️ **The transport handles every frame the server can send, and the compiler says so** — the plan's snippet declared its own five-member frame union inline and `scope-revoked` was not one of them, so a revoked device would have been told nothing and kept showing work it was removed from. This transport declares no frame union at all and carries a compile-time assertion that every member of `@gisik-nokma/domain`'s `ServerStreamFrame` has a branch — verified by deletion, not by assertion: dropping one makes `typecheck` fail with `TS2344: Type 'false' does not satisfy the constraint 'true'`. It pins the frame type strings, not that each branch reads the right fields; case 12.167 is what asserts that. `packages/sync-client/src/net/http-transport.ts`
- ⚙️ **A workspace-wide `scope-revoked` ends the subscription; a project-scoped one does not** — the instruction carries no cursor and `stream-frames.ts` states it is terminal: reconnecting into a workspace you were just removed from earns `not-a-member` forever, and the correct next move is a fresh bootstrap rather than a resume. The replica is emptied FIRST — that is the only thing the instruction is for — and the socket is dropped afterwards. A project-scoped instruction leaves membership standing and is not terminal; the wire cannot carry one today (`projectId` is `?: never` server-side and the hub refuses it), but `ScopeRevokedInstruction` admits one and this is the door that would see it. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ⚙️ **`settled()` — because "the frame arrived" and "the frame applied" are different moments** — `onItem` and `onCaughtUp` are synchronous callbacks over an asynchronous replica, so nothing else on the interface can tell those apart, and a caller asserting that something did NOT happen (a cursor that did not advance) has nothing to wait for. `settled()` resolves once the apply chain has stopped moving and any in-flight flush has finished. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`

- ⚙️ **The replica `DataSource` (Task 12) — one interface, two implementations, and the offline one answers what the online one refuses** — `createReplicaDataSource({workspaceId, replica, outbox, engine, storage})` returns a structural SUPERSET of the shipped `DataSource` (`ReplicaDataSource = DataSource & { query: DataSourceQuery & ReplicaOnlyQuery }`), so an app-core view's READ path runs unchanged against the offline copy — which is the whole of amendment 10 and the whole of ruling P30. **The WRITE door is not unchanged and the qualification is enumerated rather than characterised**: `grep -rn '\.ingest(' packages/app-core/src apps/web/src apps/web/app` matches TWENTY-FOUR lines — twenty-two call sites and two docblock mentions (`flows/guarded-child-event.ts:171`, `authoring/data-source-gateway.ts:29`) — re-measured 2026-09-20 at `14965a51`. ⚠ **This read TWENTY-TWO (twenty) until this re-measure, and the two extra are NOT a recount: two call sites LANDED between the two readings, both on the same day** — the sheet's title control and `app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`'s RESTORE, the delete's Undo path (`restoreDeletedTicket`, asserting `ticket.restored`). *A delta is a fact about a commit; an absolute is a fact about a moment.* **Re-run the command before quoting the number: the command is the count's whole defence.** ⚠ **That read "returns six call sites", and FOUR of the six citations it carried had drifted.** The `ticket-sheet` and `members/page` citations had each come to rest on a comment about something else (they carried `:276` and `:137`), `visibility-editor` on the `makeSubmittedFactory` call ABOVE its ingest (`:176`), and `authoring/data-source-gateway` on a docblock about a 422 (`:296`). **Obsolete numbers are written BARE here, and file names without their extensions, deliberately: a `file:line` string that still RESOLVES is what misleads a mechanical sweep, and labelling it stale does not stop it resolving — the same treatment `test-cases/12-offline-sync-apps.md`'s copy of this enumeration got in the same change set.** Only `flows/guarded-child-event.ts:54` and `cache-feed.ts:17` were still the sites they named, so those two keep their citations. All four are re-pointed by CONTENT — what the `file:line` actually resolves to — and one was wrong about KIND as well as line: `members/page.tsx` does not `await` at all.** The claim the enumeration carries is that callers read this result with no handler for the throw, and these are the sites that carry it — **BY HANDLER, NOT BY LINE, and that change of form is itself a finding: every citation below had rotted again since the pass that re-pointed them by content, because nine commits have touched `ticket-sheet.tsx` since that re-pointing and moved all five of its citations. The FILE and the enclosing handler are the durable half; the line number is not** — `views/ticket-sheet.tsx`'s `submitTransition`, `submitAssignee`, `submitDescription`, `submitTitle` and `addSubtask`, `views/visibility-editor.tsx`'s `apply` and `flows/guarded-child-event.ts:54` (`ingestOne`) all `await` with no `catch` — that file's `submitTicketEvent` is its ONE that does (`try`/`catch`), while `submitTitle` and `addSubtask` are bare `try`/`finally` and **a `finally` handles no rejection**, which is why they stand with the five rather than beside the one; **this clause read "its two that DO catch" until 2026-09-20, and the second of those two never caught: it was `addSubtask`, a `try`/`finally` — already so when the sentence was written** — while `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx:249` and `:342` are neither awaited nor caught: they `void` the promise and hang a `.then` off it, so a rejection there has no handler at all. `cache-feed.ts:17` and `authoring/data-source-gateway.ts:327` pass it through. Against the replica each of those sees an `OfflineAcceptError` where it saw an array — which is the honest signal reaching the caller, and it is the caller that has to learn to read it. OWNER: **Plan 10 Task 13**. The evidence is a COMPILED LINE and not a sentence: `const assignable: DataSource = ds` in `test/replica/replica-data-source.test.ts` is inside `tsconfig.typecheck.json`'s program, and rewriting the type to the plan's own `subscribe(..., cb: () => void)` makes exactly that line red (measured). Six of the thirteen query methods answer — `getBoard`, `getTickets`, `getTicket`, `getTicketHistory`, `getWorkflowDef`, `getTicketTypes` — and the list rows carry all FIVE fields `online/mappers.ts` refused `getTickets` over (tense corrected 2026-09-20 — this read "refuses", falsified for the online mapper by ruling P25 clause 1, `c86c4b5c`, 2026-09-06; the register has been empty since 2026-09-19. This bullet is about the REPLICA source filling them locally): `typeRank` and `stateName` from the local type and workflow rosters, `epicId` from the workspace's own rank-1 types, `assignedProvenance` from the local events table. `packages/app-core/src/replica/replica-data-source.ts`

- ⚙️ **`ingest` can never answer `committed`, and the fourth word travels on a throw rather than in the return type** — a local pre-check refusal IS a real `IngestResult` (the same rule engine, arriving early, queueing nothing); a claim accepted for now is queued and UNANSWERED, and no arm of `IngestResult` is true of it. So `ingest` throws `OfflineAcceptError`, carrying the index-aligned `LocalIngestResult[]` with the refusals' rules intact and the queued ids named. `'pending'` may NOT ride on the return type: annotating `ingest` as `Promise<LocalIngestResult[]>` is a compile error at the factory's own return (`TS2322`, measured), which is return-type covariance refusing the fourth member — the exact mechanism ruling P30 names. Accepted-for-now surfaces through `query.syncStatus` and the per-ticket `pending` flag instead.

- ⚙️ **TEN methods refuse, and they are the REPLICA's own** — ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this headline read "and they are NOT the online source's seven", which was wrong when written and is further from true now — the online source never refused seven (peak six) and refuses NONE since its register emptied on 2026-09-19.** The nine below are real and are this source's — **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-20: this read "seven" and listed seven, and `getWorkspaceSettings` had been in the registry since second wave Task 6 without ever being added here. The count was the symptom and the MEMBERSHIP was the defect; `getTicketAttachments` joined with the attachments read in the same change set.** **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-21: this read "NINE" and listed nine, and `getTicketComments` joined with the comment thread in the same change set** — the same drift this entry has now recorded twice, which is why the count and the membership move together here. — `getMyWorkspaces`, `getMembers`, `getTicketAttachments`, `getTicketComments`, `search`, `getMyWork`, `getAttention`, `getWorkspaceSettings`, `getPreferences` and `putPreferences` throw `ReplicaGapError` with the facts this DEVICE does not hold enumerated (no workspaces or projects table, no replicated roster, `SearchHit.matchedIn` destroyed inside `searchLocal`, `WorkItem.projectName` absent, amendment-14 preference rows not in the event stream). `ReplicaAbsence` is deliberately a different type from `online/mappers.ts`'s `Absence` and `REPLICA_GAPS` is deliberately not `SERVING_GAPS`: those carry `wireKey`/`wireRoute` because `client-contract.test.ts` measures each against a real `/v1` body, and a replica absence has no route to measure — feeding one in would pass vacuously through a `wireRoute: null`. Pinned structurally (case 12.185 asserts the absence objects have exactly `field`/`because`/`owner`).

- ✅ **`ListRow.pending` — the half of the pair that was missing, and it RENDERS now** *(promoted from `⚙️` by closure 2b, ruling P34. It read `⚙️` "deliberately: the field exists and nothing renders it until Task 13, and a legend mark is a claim about what a user can see" — Task 13 shipped no ticket list, which is why P34 moved the obligation here. The list now badges a queued claim in the SAME words the board uses, case 3.152, with the exclusion measured in one render: a settled row beside a pending one, exactly one badge, scoped to its own row.)* — `BoardCard.pending` has shipped since Plan 6 and `ListRow` had no such field, so the board could badge a claim in transit and the list could not, while the offline source's own honesty pin is "how every board/list badge renders". Ruling P30 permits this one widening: it mirrors a shipped field, `TicketListView` (which IS tripwired) is untouched, and the online answer was already written and already correct — an online-only shell has no outbox, so nothing is ever in transit for it. EXACTLY THREE `ListRow` literals existed in the repo and the compiler enumerated them, not a grep: `app-core/test/helpers/seed.ts`, `apps/web/test/project-calendar-timeline.test.tsx` and `apps/web/test/project-create-gate.test.tsx` — the third names no `ListRow` type anywhere and a name-grep misses it.

- ⚙️ **`SyncEngine.onItem` — because a bare change signal would freeze every offline view while looking wired up** — `DataSource.subscribe` must hand its callback the real 5-kind `ServerStreamFrame` and `views/use-live-query.ts` re-queries ONLY on `event` and `purge`, so a replica source emitting a synthesized `caught-up` (or nothing) leaves every app-core view static against the offline source with no type error anywhere. The engine is the only component that holds a frame-shaped value, so it now exposes the one it already has — `SyncItem`, no new union, which is what the then-deferred `StreamItem` → `ServerStreamFrame` ruling forbade inventing (closure 3 ended that deferral and the replacement is done) — it forbids inventing. Fired AFTER the fold, so a subscriber never re-queries a store that lacks the item, and never for a stalling one. The OTHER door into `applyServerItem` (`flush` → `outbox.applyResult`) deliberately does not fire it: a flush only runs while live, so the same envelope arrives on the stream and is announced there — announcing at both would fire twice for one claim. `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`

- ⚙️ **A ticket born offline reaches THREE doors, and they now agree** — `listTicketSummaries` carries it (badged, with its queued event ids); `getTickets` and `getBoard` do not, because `ListRow` and `BoardCard` both require a `stateProvenance` whose `serverTime` no server has assigned. `buildPendingOverlay` folds such a ticket and fills `serverTime: submitted.clientTime`, a substitution it documents where it makes it; repeating it in a view row would launder a device clock into a field every consumer reads as the server's answer — the same shape as the `committed` this source refuses to say about the same claim. The FIRST DRAFT had all three doors behaving differently: `getTickets` included it and THREW on the ordinary offline path, `getBoard` omitted it silently, `listTicketSummaries` carried it. `OFFLINE_BORN_ABSENCE` names the gap and its owner — **Lane A's closure 2b since ruling P34, and BUILT: `listProjectWithOfflineBorn` unions the two doors** (it read "Task 13's offline list", and Task 13 shipped no ticket list), and case 12.187 pins all three together.

- ⚙️ **`hasRestrictedParent` is gated on being caught up, because lag is not restriction** — locally, "the server never sent you this parent" and "the server has not sent it yet" are the same observation, and the field names only the first. The true arm therefore requires `engine.status() === 'live'`; a device still catching up answers `false`, following `hierarchy/epic-ancestor.ts`'s own rule that narrower than the truth shows less while wrong about the truth shows the wrong work. `rollup` is `null` for the same family of reason: `restrictedCount` counts children this viewer may not see, and a replica cannot count what it was never sent.
- ✅ **The contested-claim banner shows the claim verbatim, and the "when" is India time** — every open
  contest on a ticket, rendered through the domain's one `renderContestedClaimText`: who claimed it, at
  what instant *printed in IST and labelled IST*, what the claim was, and which rule now refuses it,
  with the three resolutions beside it. A collaborator in another timezone reads their own action in
  IST — the same instant, correctly labelled, and the label is what makes it honest rather than
  confusing. The banner is dumb by construction: it holds no state and builds no envelope.
  `packages/app-core/src/contests/contest-banner.tsx`
- ✅ **Three resolutions, each of them recorded events rather than a mutation** — discard (itself an
  attributed `claim.contested_resolved`, carrying an optional note), convert-to-comment (a
  `comment.added` quoting the claim, then a resolution linking that comment's id), and redo (the fresh
  replacement first, then a resolution linking it). One ordered `ingest` call each; a refusal comes back
  named — the door's `rule`/`reason`, or an invalid answer's `code`/`message` — instead of being
  swallowed. `packages/app-core/src/contests/contest-view-model.ts`
- ⚙️ **A redo may not reuse the contested envelope, and saying so is a throw** — the original id is
  already contested, so re-sending it would idempotently re-answer `contested`. `resolveContest` refuses
  before it calls the door, which is why the banner's "Redo legally" button calls `onRedo` and never
  `onResolve`: the host seeds a NEW envelope from the contested one and only then resolves.
- ✅ **The three contest resolutions, and the pair the prototype invented that the product does not
  have** — `ContestBanner` renders exactly three controls, **`Redo legally` / `Convert to comment` /
  `Discard`**, and an exactly-three guard (case 13.306) pins them **by exact accessible name** and
  asserts the prototype's `Keep mine` / `Keep theirs` are ABSENT. The exactness is load-bearing rather
  than tidy: the pre-existing arm finds the third control with `/discard/i`, which the prototype's own
  third label **`Discard mine`** also satisfies, so a rename to the prototype's wording reddened
  nothing until this guard existed. The audit files the pair as *"I — SHOWN BUT DOES NOT EXIST. Do not
  build."* (`docs/hig-reconciliation-audit.md:306`) while the prototype still draws it, which is why a
  guard exists at all. **`ContestBanner` is mounted nowhere in the product**
  (`docs/hig-reconciliation-audit.md:577`), so this is a **component-level** guard and not a statement
  about any screen: the WEB ticket sheet renders no contest UI at all, while the MOBILE ticket screen
  renders its OWN three buttons from its own markup (`apps/mobile/src/screens/ticket-screen.tsx:119-121`)
  — neither is this component. `packages/app-core/test/contests.test.tsx`
- ⚙️ **A queued resolution is never laundered into an accepted one** — against the replica source
  `ingest` throws `OfflineAcceptError` for a claim it has only queued, and `resolveContest` deliberately
  does not catch it. Catching it and answering `ok: true` would put "the server accepted your discard"
  on screen for a discard the server has never seen — the same laundering the replica refuses one layer
  down, for the same reason.
- ✅ **The offline reopen pair, in the only order that can pass** — accepting "Parent X is done. Reopen
  it and proceed?" builds the parent's `ticket.transitioned` carrying
  `cause: { kind: 'reopened-for-child', childTicketId }` FIRST, then the child claim untouched, for the
  caller to enqueue adjacently. The wire field is `to`, translated from the view's `toStateId` at the
  envelope boundary — the same translation `reopenAndProceed` and `TicketSheet.submitTransition` make,
  at the same seam. `packages/app-core/src/offline/reopen-pair.ts`
- ⚙️ **`nonTerminalStates` is published API and is NOT the reopen picker** — it filters a definition's
  states by category and cannot filter by reachability, because it never receives the parent's current
  state. "Which states may this parent be reopened into" already has one answer,
  `reopenStateOptions(def, currentStateId)`, which the online dialog is fed from and which the offline
  dialog must be fed from too. The divergence is pinned by a test rather than left to be rediscovered
  at a picker.
- ✅ **Gross clock skew renders as a flag attributed to the DEVICE** — derived from the two kept
  timestamps (neither is edited), shown only at or beyond the domain's 10-minute threshold, and phrased
  as "device clock ran N min ahead of / behind the server". An unparseable client claim is flagged
  *unreadable* rather than silently producing no badge — the gate runs on `hasGrossClockSkew` first
  precisely so the unreadable case stays visible. `packages/app-core/src/history/clock-skew-badge.tsx`
- ✅ **The sync-truth strip both apps mount** — connection status, how many claims are waiting to send
  (singular-aware), and a "needs attention" count that renders only when non-zero, so a healthy client
  shows no empty chrome. `role="status"`, not `alert`: a standing readout, not an interruption.
  `packages/app-core/src/sync/sync-status-bar.tsx`
- ⚙️ **These three surfaces' BEM classes and the rules that style them are tied by a test** — `ui.css`
  had no `gn-contest-banner`, `gn-clock-skew-badge` or `gn-sync-status-bar` selector at all when the
  components were written, so their class names bound to nothing and the token discipline their own
  contract names was not actually being consumed. The rules were added and a set-equality binding test
  now catches a rename on either side, plus a rule for a class nothing renders.
  `packages/app-core/test/sync-ux-css-binding.test.tsx`
- ✅ **The stream's own error, said out loud without claiming a policy that does not exist** *(Ruling
  P34)* — the sync strip takes `engine.lastError()` and prints the code verbatim with the server's own
  message. It says this client is **still retrying**, because that is what `createSyncEngine` does for
  every one of the three `STREAM_ERROR_CODES`; only `scope-revoked`, a different frame type, is
  terminal. A label reading "this device will not reconnect" would be the surface asserting a decision
  on the engine's behalf. Whether `not-a-member` *should* end the retry loop is still open, still lives
  in the engine, and is named there rather than implied here.
  `packages/app-core/src/sync/sync-status-bar.tsx`
- ⚙️ **THREE obligations pointed at Task 13 from code Task 13's own plan text does not reach, and
  Ruling P34 re-assigned all three** — `OFFLINE_BORN_ABSENCE`'s offline union list and a renderer for
  `ListRow.pending` are app-core view/DataSource concerns and went to **Lane A as closure 2b** — **both
  are now BUILT**: `listProjectWithOfflineBorn` unions the settled list with the offline-born tickets and
  tags each entry with its kind (cases 12.240–12.242), and the list badges a queued claim in the board's
  own words (case 3.152). The union keeps the two kinds APART rather than flattening them, because an
  offline-born ticket has no `serverTime` any server assigned — **the opposite answer to ruling P36 on the
  same surface, and the distinction is why both are right**: P36 copies an instant a ledger row really
  recorded, this one refuses to invent an instant nothing recorded. The
  per-`StreamErrorCode` deferral split, its SURFACE half landing here (above) and its POLICY half
  staying in `createSyncEngine`, unassigned. Task 13's Files block contains no ticket list, no `ListRow`
  renderer and no error surface — those annotations named the next task touching app-core UX by NUMBER,
  not by content, which is the failure mode P34 exists to stop repeating.
- ⚙️ **The dual-client harness rig** — two sync-client replicas on in-memory SQLite driven against the
  REAL ingest service, the REAL sync service, `visibleEventsFor` and real Postgres, with no sockets and
  no HTTP: the spec's named testing discipline (§7) gets its instrument. It proves itself before any
  scenario trusts it, and the property it exists to give Task 15 is asserted first — full `TicketState`
  equality between a replica's reducer fold and the server's own `rowToTicketState`, so server and
  clients can never quietly disagree. `apps/server/test/dual-client/rig.ts`
- ⚙️ **The harness's online stand-in uses the GATED ingest door** — `ingestClientBatch(events, userId)`,
  the same one `POST /v1/events` calls, never the internal `ingestBatch` that passes `door = null` and
  skips `checkPermission` entirely. A harness whose "online user" skipped the gate would let a scenario
  assert a permission outcome the product does not have; the internal door remains correct for the SEED
  and is labelled as such where it is used. Pinned by a forged envelope-origin the internal door would
  have committed.
- ⚙️ **Its bootstrap is the server's, not a copy of it** — the backfill types are imported from
  `BOOTSTRAP_BACKFILL_TYPES` rather than restated, because a hand-copied list was missing
  `visibility.set` and would have had the harness proving replicas against a bootstrap the server never
  sends: the replica would hold a different AUDIENCE for a ticket than the server does.
- ⚙️ **`goOnline()` fails loudly rather than quietly** — a claim still queued after the flush throws,
  carrying the outbox's own `last_error`. The engine answers a transport throw by backing off and
  returning normally, which in a harness with no network is never transient; without this a scenario
  would assert an outcome that never happened and blame the product.

- ⚙️ **`apps/desktop` (Task 16) — a Tauri 2 + Vite React shell over the replica `DataSource`, not a verified running app** — `wrapTauriDatabase`/`createTauriSqlStorage` implement `SyncStorage` over `@tauri-apps/plugin-sql`, run through the SAME behavioural contract every adapter answers to: `storageConformanceTests` from `@gisik-nokma/sync-client/testing`, backed here by a real in-memory `better-sqlite3` database standing in for the plugin's own connection rather than another `vi.fn()` mock. `app.tsx` wires `createReplica`/`createOutbox`/`createSyncEngine`/`createHttpTransport` (`@gisik-nokma/sync-client`) and `createReplicaDataSource` (`@gisik-nokma/app-core`, not `@gisik-nokma/sync-client` as the plan's own snippet has it — Ruling P30's package) to the OS's `online`/`offline` events and renders sync status plus a `listTicketSummaries` list; `SyncStatusBar`/`ContestBanner` do not exist in `@gisik-nokma/app-core` yet (grepped, not assumed), so the strip is inline markup rather than the named component the plan's own text flags as an assumption. **The gate for this task is `cargo check` + `tsc` + `vitest` on the storage adapter — no simulator, no app launch, no `tauri build` claim** — `tauri::generate_context!()` additionally required an `icons/icon.png` the plan's file list omits (verified against `tauri-codegen`'s own source); a placeholder ships, real artwork does not. All three checks were re-run independently of the implementer's own report. `apps/desktop/src/app.tsx`, `apps/desktop/src/storage/tauri-sql-storage.ts`

- ⚙️ **Measured, not fixed: the adapter's queue serialises callers, not connections, and this is a live failure mode, not only a race** — `tauri-plugin-sql` 2.4.1's public `Builder` exposes only `add_migrations`, no pool-size or connection-pinning option; `DbPool::connect` opens the database through sqlx's `Pool::connect` (default `max_connections` 10, no `busy_timeout` set anywhere in the plugin); and every `execute`/`select` Tauri command independently acquires and releases a pooled connection for one `sqlx::query(sql)` call — all verified by reading the crate's own source in the local cargo registry cache. **A standalone probe against sqlx 0.8.6 (the plugin's pinned version), replicating its exact connect/execute calls with ZERO concurrency** — one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` then one `INSERT` as two sequential calls, this adapter's own pattern — reproducibly hits `error returned from database: (code: 5) database is locked` on the `INSERT`, with the pool opening a second physical connection rather than reusing the idle first one. **A single `execute()` call carrying a whole transaction as one multi-statement string was also measured and DOES work** — sqlx runs all statements on the one connection that call acquires, and the pool is left clean afterwards — but it does not generalise here: every real production caller of `SyncStorage.transaction` (`packages/sync-client/src/storage/schema.ts` and all four sites in `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`) reads with `tx.all()` INSIDE the transaction and branches on what it reads, which cannot be expressed as one SQL string decided before the transaction opens. Neither adapter test can see any of this: both back the wrapper with one `better-sqlite3` handle, which has no pool to race against and no separate command boundary to violate. Closing it for real needs a Rust-side fork of the plugin (to pin the pool or expose a held connection to JS) or a different plugin/architecture, both outside a Tauri-shell scaffold task. *Owner: whoever next hardens this adapter for real multi-device use, a decision no task owns yet.* `apps/desktop/src/storage/tauri-sql-storage.ts`

- ⚙️ **Mobile app shell (Task 17) — Expo/React Native over the same replica `DataSource`, gated but never run on a device** *(`⚙️` deliberately, not `✅`: `tsc` and `vitest` pass on the three declared test files, but no simulator or physical device has ever shown this UI to a person, and a legend mark is a claim about what a user can see)* — `apps/mobile` wires `createOpSqliteStorage` (op-sqlite behind the shared `SyncStorage` interface, `packages/sync-client/src/storage/storage.ts`) into the same `createReplica`/`createOutbox`/`createSyncEngine`/`createReplicaDataSource` stack Tasks 9–12 shipped, with native screens (`BoardScreen`, `TicketScreen`) reading it exactly like a web view-model would. `apps/mobile/src/app.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`op-sqlite-storage.ts` — the fourth `SyncStorage` engine, proven against the shared conformance suite, not a canned fake** — `wrapOpSqliteDatabase` maps `run`/`all` onto op-sqlite's `execute(sql, params) → {rows}` shape and serialises transactions on a promise queue, same discipline as `better-sqlite3.ts`. Its test runs `storageConformanceTests` (`@gisik-nokma/sync-client/testing`) against a fake `execute()` facade backed by a REAL in-memory `better-sqlite3` database — op-sqlite is a native module unavailable under vitest — so the suite exercises real DDL/DML/transaction behaviour rather than a `rows: sql.startsWith('SELECT') ? […] : []` responder that would pass regardless of correctness. `apps/mobile/src/storage/op-sqlite-storage.ts`
- ⚙️ **The editor rides Plan 7's REAL webview bridge, not the plan text's assumed one** — `packages/editor/src/webview/bridge.ts` ships a versioned `{bridge, kind}` protocol and a single-file HTML bundle (`build:webview` → `dist-webview/index.html`, vite-plugin-singlefile) that predates this task's own planning text, which had assumed an unbuilt `getEditorWebviewHtml()` export and a bare `{type: 'editor-ready'|'set-doc'|'doc-changed'}` shape. `apps/mobile` speaks the real protocol; `editor-web-view.tsx` uses `WebView.postMessage`, which `entry.tsx` already listens for on both `window` and `document`, rather than hand-built `injectJavaScript` scripts. **The host-side validator for editor→host messages does not exist anywhere in the repo** (`packages/editor` exports only the host→editor direction, `parseHostMessage`) — `editor-bridge.ts`'s `parseEditorMessage` is the first one, scoped to this app rather than added to the shared package, because at write time Task 16 (Tauri) was landing in parallel and might have needed the identical function — two lanes editing the same shared file concurrently was judged the larger risk. **Task 16 landed without an editor webview integration at all** (checked: no `editor`/`bridge` file anywhere under `apps/desktop`), so the duplication did not happen this round; the concern stands for whenever Tauri's shell does add one. The bundled HTML itself is baked into a checked-in generated constant (`scripts/generate-editor-html.mjs` → `src/editor/editor-html.generated.ts`) since Metro has no runtime filesystem access to read it from `packages/editor` directly; **it goes stale the moment `packages/editor`'s webview content changes, and nothing in the gate checks its freshness** — regeneration is a manual, documented step. `apps/mobile/src/editor/`
- ✅ **Contest resolution and sync status use the real app-core helpers, wired after Task 13 landed** — `board-screen.tsx` renders `syncStatusLabel` (the same wording the web `SyncStatusBar` shows, minus its `<div>` markup, which RN cannot mount); `ticket-screen.tsx`'s contest banner calls `resolveContest` for all three resolutions (redo, convert-to-comment, discard), rendered through `renderContestedClaimText` exactly as the web `ContestBanner` does. `resolveContest` deliberately does not catch `OfflineAcceptError` (answering `ok: true` for a queued claim would show "the server accepted your discard" for a discard the server has never seen) — the screen awaits/catches it explicitly, the same discipline as its comment-post path. "Redo legally" resubmits the identical claim under a fresh envelope rather than offering inline editing first — `ContestBanner`'s own docblock pushes building the replacement envelope to the host precisely because what "redo" means depends on the claim's type, and a full per-event-type redo editor is out of this task's scope. `apps/mobile/src/screens/`
- ⚙️ **What Task 17 does NOT offer, named rather than silently absent** — no push transport on the server (`/v1/push/registrations` does not exist yet — `registerPushToken` exercises its real 404 path, not a hypothetical one). No attachment upload flow (`onRequestUpload` is wired but nothing answers it). No inline editing before a contest redo (see above). No device or simulator run of any kind. `apps/mobile/src/screens/`
- ✅ **Six scripted divergences, asserted exactly** — two replicas diverge offline, sync, and the
  outcome including every contested claim is checked against the final ledger, the SERVER's own
  projection, both replicas and `proj_contested`: a concurrent field race (later wins, earlier survives
  in history, nothing contested), a stale `restrict-to`, a workflow republished out from under a queued
  transition, a narrowed visibility, the offline reopen pair, and a retried batch. This is the spec's
  §7 discipline executed rather than described. `apps/server/test/dual-client/scenarios.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **Client and server refuse in the SAME WORDS** — the reopen scenario asserts that the sentence the
  local pre-check produced for a terminal parent is `===` the sentence the server's contest carries. A
  device that explained a refusal differently from the server would be two products.
- ⚙️ **Task 15's S4 predates Ruling P23 and its premise was false** — the plan expected a `purge` on
  `visibility.set`, and the per-event purge walk was DELETED rather than narrowed. The scenario is
  written against the SHIPPED rule: the claim contests, and nothing is delivered to the narrowed-out
  replica. The stale copy that remains is the accepted under-purge residual of case 6.104 — **recorded,
  and deliberately not pinned by a test**, because 6.104's owner is Plan 10 and an assertion would be a
  test that closing the gap must break.
- ✅ **A re-widened ticket comes back even if the client read something else meanwhile — a bounded
  NOTICE on newly granted visibility, and the client pulls what it lacks** *(this bullet was ⚙️ and
  described the DEFECT; closure 5 closed it by PUSHING the history, and closure 5b re-shaped it under
  ruling P37.1 because pushing wedged)* — when an event widens a ticket's audience, everyone in the NEW
  audience is sent a `reach-granted` frame naming that ticket and every inheriting descendant, and each
  client fetches the ones it does not already hold through `GET /v1/sync/bootstrap/ticket`. The trigger
  is the visibility event itself and never a reconstructed prior audience — there is no before-image to
  reconstruct and re-deriving one is banned — and what makes that coarse trigger safe is an asymmetry
  rather than a check: a narrowing is invisible to the person it narrowed away, so it never becomes their
  trigger. A widening self-selects; a narrowing self-excludes.
  *Why it is a notice and not the history:* the pushed form was an atomic burst that advanced no cursor,
  so a slow-consumer close mid-burst left the client reconnecting into the identical burst for ever. A
  list of ids is a list of ids however long the histories behind it are. It also bounds an amplifier the
  permission check does not: a member may re-issue a visibility change that changes nothing, and a client
  that already holds the subtree now does no work for it at all. *And it is not a purge walk arriving
  from the other direction* — a purge names an entity to a device that may never have held it, which is
  news; a notice names ids the recipient may at that moment read in full.
  *The defect it replaces, kept because it is what the tests measure:* the sync cursor is the highest
  sequence DELIVERED, so one unrelated commit between a narrowing and a re-widening left the client
  holding two events where the server held four, permanently, with no purge, no backfill and no resync
  path. Cases 12.274-12.283. `apps/server/src/visibility/grant-backfill.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/sync/sync-routes.ts`, `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`,
  `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ✅ **`GET /v1/sync/bootstrap/ticket` — one ticket's replica payload, so a client can fetch what a
  notice named** *(closure 5b(d))* — the snapshot, the tail, and the workspace's definitions, which
  travel WHOLE rather than scoped to the ticket's own workflows because a scoped list missing one
  intermediate version fails silently at the fold and throws a step later. A ticket the caller may not
  see and a ticket that never existed get the identical 404: splitting them would make
  presence-versus-absence an oracle, the disclosure the reconcile door already refuses. The response
  carries no stream cursor, and the omission is load-bearing — a per-ticket pull that moved the workspace
  cursor would skip every intervening event for every other ticket and look exactly like convergence.
  Cases 12.299-12.303. `apps/server/src/sync/sync-routes.ts`
- ✅ **A pull that fails is remembered, not fatal and not forgotten** *(closure 5b(d))* — the ids a
  notice named are recorded in durable client storage, so a pull that fails or times out leaves the
  cursor free to advance while the id stays owed, and the next catch-up retries it. Both alternatives are
  wrong: throwing reaches the engine's stall, which is a one-way latch that only a process restart
  clears, so a brief network wobble would brick the device; swallowing and forgetting advances the cursor
  with the ids lost and no path back, because a fresh bootstrap is refused over an existing local
  baseline. Each pull is bounded by a timeout enforced in the ENGINE rather than the transport, because
  the transport has other implementors and a wait that never ends would stop the serial apply chain in
  total silence. Cases 12.292-12.298, 12.305-12.308.
  - ✅ **…and an id the server will never serve is dropped — keyed to the NOTICE, not to the status code**
    *(ruling P37.3)* — remembering without a bound was a quiet permanent leak: a revoked project left one
    HTTP request per catch-up, for ever. The drop cannot key on the 404, because the route answers "you may
    not see it" and "it never existed" identically **by design**, so the device cannot tell a phantom from a
    re-widening that raced the pull. Each pending entry instead carries the `workspaceSeq` of the notice that
    named it, and the 404 drops the entry only if that seq is still the one the pull was issued for — a later
    notice has already replaced it, so the entry survives and the next catch-up fetches it. **A 404 is never
    the last word; the next notice is.** Cases 12.317-12.318.
  `packages/sync-client/src/replica/replica.ts`, `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ✅ **`POST /v1/sync/reconcile` — the client names what it holds and the server says what is no longer
  theirs** *(closure 5's other half; it closes case 6.104, the stale copy nobody takes away)* — a member
  un-shared by a plain visibility change used to keep the copy until a full resync, because a
  server-initiated purge would name an entity to a device that may never have held it. This inverts the
  direction instead: the device presents its ids, the server answers only about those, and existence is
  never consulted — a fabricated id comes back exactly as a genuine loss does, so the reply's SHAPE
  carries no oracle either. The engine deletes only what it itself presented, because a purge is
  destructive and local and this device does not need to trust the server to be safe from it.
  **The schedule is catch-up completion — which is once per RECONNECT, not once per catch-up**
  *(closure 5b, case 12.288)*, because the hub sends that frame once per subscription. The moment a
  device's cursor is current is the moment its holdings may be stale, because a narrowing sends the
  losing device nothing at all. Gating instead on "the catch-up delivered something" would skip the
  zero-frame catch-up, which is precisely the case reconcile exists for, so the bound is a minimum
  interval rather than activity: reconnects are on a fixed delay with no growth, and a flapping socket
  would otherwise reconcile every two seconds for ever. **Because that schedule is per-reconnect, a
  grant notice ALSO reconciles the ids it names, before deciding what to pull** *(closure 5c's minimal
  form)* — otherwise a device on a stable socket that was narrowed and re-widened would keep a stale
  partial copy indefinitely, the holdings filter being unable to tell it from a complete one. A failure there is swallowed — reconcile is idempotent and the
  next catch-up retries — and concurrent callers share one in-flight run. **A guest gets the same
  answers as a member**, which closes case 6.102: the door asks only `canSee`, and `canSee` resolves a
  guest through the explicit share list exactly as it resolves a member through a project, so nothing
  about it was ever role-aware — what was missing was the measurement.
  Cases 12.284-12.289, 12.304. `apps/server/src/sync/reconcile-route.ts`, `packages/sync-client/src/engine/sync-engine.ts`
- ✅ **The backfill was an ATOMIC burst that could wedge a subscriber — the MECHANISM is gone, and the
  convergence it asked for is now measured END TO END against a real socket** *(ruling P37.1; disclosed
  and capped by closure 5, re-shaped by closure 5b, flip condition discharged 2026-09-06)* — the grant
  side streams no history at all now, so the non-progressing burst does not exist, and the 200-ticket cap
  survives bounding the WALK and the client's pull fan-out rather than the wire. **What kept it 🟡 until
  now, and what changed:** the ruling's flip condition was a slow-consumer close MID-PULL followed by
  convergence on reconnect, and nothing in this repo could cut a socket — the dual-client rig has none by
  construction and the engine's tests use a fake transport with no buffer bound — so the re-delivery
  chain was argued from the code and measured only link by link. `apps/server/test/dual-client/socket-cut.test.ts`
  now cuts one for real: a real `buildApp` on a real `listen()` (a real HTTP upgrade, which `app.inject`
  cannot make), real Postgres, real bearer-token auth on the handshake, the real unmodified sync-client
  engine over a real `ws` socket, and **`stream/hub.ts` and `stream/routes.ts` unmodified and driven only
  from outside**. Shadowing `bufferedAmount` on one live socket instance — an own property over `ws`'s
  prototype accessor, so nothing else is affected — makes the next ordinary commit trip the shipped 1 MB
  bound, and the client records a real `1013 / 'slow-consumer'`. Convergence is asserted against server
  truth after the reconnect. The chain has **two halves and one cut cannot see both**: while the notice is
  regenerated (its `backfilled` set is per-drain and a notice moves no cursor) the reconnect repairs the
  pull by itself, so a second cut taken with the cursor past the trigger is what reaches `onCaughtUp`'s
  own retry. Cases 12.290 and 12.340–12.343. What was already measured and still is: a failed or
  timed-out pull leaves its id in durable client storage and the next catch-up retries it, so convergence
  after an interruption does not depend on the notice arriving twice. *The defect it replaces, kept
  because it constrained the fix:* every backfilled envelope sat at or below the client's cursor, so none of
  them advanced it, and the only one that did arrived last. A slow-consumer close before that point
  leaves the client reconnecting into the identical burst for ever, because the cursor is persisted only
  on catch-up and a fresh bootstrap is refused over an existing local baseline. An interim cap bounds the
  WALK at 200 tickets — breadth-first, so the trigger ticket and the shallowest descendants survive — and
  it is explicitly not a bound on a single ticket's history. **The one-line repair of sending the trigger
  first is unsafe and the code says so**: `ticket.reparented` is folded, so a client that never held the
  ticket would replay a transition with no creation event and stall its engine. 12.291 pins the cap;
  12.290 no longer owns a residual, and the residual it DID own is the one 12.340–12.343 discharged.
  `apps/server/src/visibility/grant-backfill.ts`, `apps/server/test/dual-client/socket-cut.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **The heartbeat is a PROTOCOL ping, and a peer that answers one as if it were an application frame
  is told so** *(case 12.344)* — `stream/routes.ts` pings with a WebSocket control frame, which `ws`
  answers automatically and routes to `socket.on('pong')`, never to `message`; the abstraction the sync
  client declares for its transport (`WebSocketLike`) has no ping event at all, so a client built against
  it cannot see one. That reasoning is what 12.328's deletion of a text `ping`/`pong` protocol rests on,
  and `webSocketFactory` being an injected seam is what keeps it a property of what gets INJECTED rather
  than of either module. The socket-cut fixture holds a real `ws` socket on **both** ends of a real
  upgrade, so it is the only place the hazard is expressible: wiring the client's own `on('ping')` to a
  text `{"type":"pong"}` draws `{type:'error', workspaceId:null, code:'bad-message', …}` from the
  unmodified `badMessage` path — a complaint, not a disconnect. **Measured as an excluding pair on one
  connection**, the same server-side `.ping()` twice with only that listener added in between, so the
  complaint is attributable to the added frame and not to the connection or the heartbeat; the control
  half is production's own behaviour, every thirty seconds, on every socket. The residual 12.361 records
  is untouched by this: the owner rule at both `webSocketFactory` defaults is still a docblock, and a
  case showing what breaking it costs is not a check that it is kept.
  `apps/server/src/stream/routes.ts`, `apps/server/test/dual-client/socket-cut.test.ts`
- ✅ **Visibility that widens because the READER's reach grew, not the ticket's** *(closure 5b, case
  12.283)* — `membership.granted` and `project.member_added` reveal tickets whose history sits below the
  client's cursor, the identical gap. It could not be another entry in the ticket-event trigger list, for
  a structural reason rather than a scoping choice: these are roster events filed against the roster
  entity, so there is no ticket to start a subtree walk from, and the question inverts with the widening
  — not who can now see this ticket, but which tickets can this viewer now see. It answers with what the
  reader can see NOW rather than what they gained, because there is no before-image to subtract, using
  the same visibility predicate every read endpoint uses rather than a second definition of it. It
  therefore over-names, which is affordable only because the client drops ids it already holds without
  asking the server anything. `apps/server/src/visibility/grant-backfill.ts`,
  `apps/server/src/visibility/visible-events-for.ts`
- ⚙️ **A contest filed against a ticket that never came to exist reaches nobody** — surfaced by the
  reopen scenario and asserted as product behaviour: `canSee` is false for a ticket with no
  `proj_visibility` row, so the child's `claim.contested` is streamed to no one. The claimant's only
  record of it is the outbox stub. *What is permanent is the non-delivery, not any cursor lag — an
  earlier version of this bullet said the cursors stay one behind the head for good, and the next
  visible event moves them past.*
- ⚙️ **The fixture-mirror inventory.** 157 fixtures across `app-core`, `sync-client`, `apps/web`,
  `apps/server` and the periphery, each classified against the producer it models: 80 MIRROR (a
  protocol owned elsewhere, with no round trip pinning it), 24 WITNESSED, 53 N.A. (the fixture
  implements an interface its own package owns). The discriminator is whether some OTHER process
  authoritatively defines the shape the fixture encodes. Recorded in `docs/lanes/executor-state.md`
  with owners; the buildable subset is witnessed by the file above, and the rest carry ❌ cases
  (12.326–12.328) naming what remains unwitnessed and why.

## 10. Workflow builder & ticket types

The authoring half of spec §4: a Jira-style visual builder plus a template gallery — no AI drafting.

**A dictated sentence this section transcribed and measurement refutes.** The plan's own §10 preamble reads *"All views live in `packages/app-core` (shared with desktop); `apps/web` contributes pages and the DataSource adapter"* (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-20-08-workflow-builder.md:7086`), and the first version of this section shipped it almost verbatim. It is false on this branch, and it is the fourth dictated sentence §10 has had to correct against the code. The *screens* do live in `packages/app-core` and are shared with desktop — canvas, inspectors, panels, both list screens, the gallery, the type editor — and every route below mounts one of them for the authoring itself rather than keeping a second copy. But `apps/web` authors view code of its own, with its own user-facing copy: six route pages, two of which render four distinct states each, plus `WiringNotReady`'s three non-`ready` arms. The constraint that is actually *enforced* here is a different one, and it is the one worth stating: nothing in `packages/app-core/src` outside `src/online/` speaks HTTP (amendment 10), and that is held by a scanner (`packages/app-core/test/no-http-in-views.test.ts`), not by where a file happens to sit.

- ✅ **Workflow canvas** — React Flow: states as nodes (name, category picker, blocked checkbox, start marker), transitions drawn as edges, a distinguished from-any source node, select-to-edit inspectors, header Add state. `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/` **AND THE CANVAS IS NOW A MEASURED BOX RATHER THAN A PRESENT ONE (2026-09-19, case 13.911, owner-prioritised).** It rendered its whole chrome around an **empty canvas card**: four `.react-flow__node` elements were in the DOM the entire time, laid out inside a container computing to **zero height**. `.gn-workflow-canvas` was `height: 100%; min-height: 480px` and React Flow's own stylesheet gives `.react-flow` `height: 100%` — **a percentage height cannot resolve against a parent sized only by `min-height`**, so the child's `100%` computed to `auto` → 0. The rule was present and the canvas was still invisible, which is why the witness asserts a **measured** height and not a rule or a class name, and why it cannot be a jsdom test (jsdom has no layout engine, so `getBoundingClientRect()` returns zeros for everything and the assertion would pass on the broken tree). The fix is one declaration, **`display: grid`**, on `.gn-workflow-canvas`; `min-height: 480px` is untouched and load-bearing for the narrow-screen layout — the pair is the fix. Measured both states in a browser: `.react-flow` **0px → 480px**, the first node's box lying inside the canvas **0px² → 6923px²**. **What does NOT discriminate, and is therefore not asserted:** the container's own height (480 in both), the FIRST node's height and width (75.27 / 91.98 in both — the nodes are not all the same height, but every one of them is non-zero, which is all the argument needs) and the node count (4 in both) — React Flow sizes each node intrinsically inside a container that draws none of it, so DOM-shape assertions pass on the broken tree. The case carries a planted control that reproduces the pre-fix cascade in-page and must read 0, through the same helper as the measurement. A sweep of `ui.css` + `globals.css` for the same shape found **no second invisible canvas** — the three other `height: 100%` sites either sit under a definite (`inset: 0`) parent or have intrinsic content height; owner: whoever next adds a percentage-height child to a `min-height` container. `apps/web/e2e-stack/builder-canvas.spec.ts`, `apps/web/e2e-stack/helpers/canvas-box.ts`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ✅ **The builder screen's chrome, fitted to the backend rather than to the drawing** (2026-09-18) — the four BEM families that carry the screen (`workflow-builder-screen*`, `state-inspector*`, `inspector-panel*`, `transition-inspector*`) shipped with **ZERO CSS** since Plan 8 and rendered with user-agent defaults; the refreshed prototype's design now sits on them, built from existing `--gn-*` roles only (`surface-raised`/`shadow-raised`/`radius-card` are `.gn-card`'s three, `fill-tertiary`/`text-accent` are `.gn-button`'s, `surface-sunken`/`shadow-sunken`/`fill-quaternary`/`border` are `.gn-field__input`'s). The `Add state` action and both inspectors' actions moved off bare `<button>`s onto the shared `Button` (`variant="danger"` on the destructive two, the precedent `fields-editor.tsx:151` set), and the inspector's name field onto the shared `.gn-field`/`.gn-field__input`. **FOUR THINGS THE PROTOTYPE DRAWS ARE NOT BUILT, and each is a stated refusal rather than an omission.** (1) Its two-column layout, which would delete `SystemRulesPanel` and the whole rule-authoring subsystem — `docs/hig-reconciliation-audit.md:691-718` rules those *"EXISTS AND THE PROTOTYPE OMITS IT. Keep and restyle; never delete"*, closing at `:718` with *"Both must be treated as requirements to preserve, not redesign away"*; three rails are preserved instead. (2) Its `wfStatus` line ("Draft · last saved 11:58") and its `Cancel` button — `wfStatus`/`lastSaved`/`last saved` are 0 hits repo-wide, so there is no state to render. (3) Its consolidated inspector card (Category select, "Blocks the ticket" toggle, "Initial state" switch) — Category and Blocked live on the STATE NODE, and `hig-reconciliation-audit.md:703-708` already ruled that card *"SHOWN BUT DOES NOT EXIST. Do not build"*; moving them would be a product change to a tested layout, not a design fit. (4) Its `--tintDanger`/`--tintSuccess` washes, which this product has no tint role for (`tokens.ts:201-202`, `:342-344` — `tintWarning` is the only one that exists). **The workflow name is this screen's title and stays an editable `<input>`** — the prototype draws an `h1`, but the product's name is editable and already on screen inside the field, so a heading would print the same words twice in one header; the prototype's `34px/41px/700/-0.4px` is therefore applied to the field via `.gn-page-title`'s own four declarations (read from `apps/web/app/globals.css:127`, never re-derived, because a second reading of the same four numbers is how two titles drift apart), and the field KEEPS its boundary — a control that looks exactly like static text is not editable-looking. **The heading gap this leaves is real and PRE-EXISTING** — this route renders no `<h1>` and the base page had none either — and is recorded rather than fixed inside a design port. *(The reason first recorded here was that a duplicate heading would make `getByText` ambiguous; the reviewer refuted that with a probe, since `getByText` matches text content and an input's value is not text content. Corrected rather than deleted.)* The route also gained the breadcrumb back to the workflow list: **the element `apps/web/app/settings/page.tsx:94-96` already named as the reason the builder is the only settings sub-screen NOT inlined into the `/settings` shell** — *"it carries its own breadcrumb back ('Settings › Workflows') instead"* — which was true of the prototype and false of the product until now. It renders above the load states, not inside them, and in `apps/web` rather than app-core because routing is the one thing app-core cannot know. **TWO GAPS RECORDED WITH OWNERS RATHER THAN FIXED.** (1) **Four more families are still at zero CSS inside the rail this change styled** — `add-rule-menu*`, `restrict-to-editor*`, `require-field-editor*`, `require-different-user-editor*`, proven in the DOM, plus `template-gallery*` on `/workflows/new`. The prototype draws no rule-authoring UI at all, so there is no drawn design to fit them to; **owner: whoever next touches the rule editors**, with the audit's "keep and restyle" (`:694`) as the warrant. (2) **`.advisories-panel__title` sets `color: var(--gn-warning)` on `--gn-surface-sunken`, measuring 3.711 under `hig`/`minimal` light** — below the 4.5 text owes, and worse than the 4.401 `danger`-as-text this change declined to add a sixth instance of (`packages/ui/src/ui.css:1303`, pre-existing). Cases 13.767–13.775. `packages/ui/src/ui.css`, `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/workflow-builder-screen.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/state-inspector.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/transition-inspector.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/settings/workflows/[workflowId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`
- ✅ **Rule editors** — the closed five-kind vocabulary behind pickers: member/role pickers for restrict-to, a picker-only field chooser (built-ins + bound types' fields), a named-move picker for require-different-user with the start-state honesty note; plain-language summaries on every edge. **The member and role pickers can say “nobody is in this workspace” honestly, and today they can also say it dishonestly — disclosed here rather than only in a review.** `WorkflowBuilderScreen` loads the roster these pickers read as `props.gateway.listMembers(props.workspaceId).catch(() => [] as WorkspaceMember[])` (`packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/workflow-builder-screen.tsx:70`) — a blanket catch on the promise, written for the one refusal it names in its own comment (a guest may not call `GET /v1/workspaces/:id/members`), but unable to tell that 404 from a 500, a dropped connection, or a roster body it could not parse. All four become an empty roster, and an empty roster is rendered as fact on the surface whose entire subject is who may do what. It became reachable only when Task 13 shipped an HTTP adapter that can reject with something other than a 404; before that the only implementation was `fake-gateway.ts`, whose `listMembers` (`async () => seed.members ?? []`) cannot reject at all — the fake's occupancy and population reads *can*, on their seeded error flags, so the absolute belongs to that one method and not to the fake. Closing it is not a catch clause but a `gateway.ts` contract change touching four files (the adapter that throws, the wiring that narrows, the screen that should, and the fake), and it is recorded in the coordination doc as **unowned** — measured, not assumed: Plan 8's two remaining tasks are the Playwright smoke and this catalogue, and Plans 9, 10 and 11 mention `WorkflowBuilderScreen` and `workflow-builder-screen` zero times each (Plan 8 itself: 18 hits, as the control). `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/rule-editors/`
- ✅ **Live validation** — `validateWorkflowDef` on every change; issues panel in the validator's own words, anchored per code to the offending node/edge, click-to-select; publish disabled while any of the eleven issue codes stands. `packages/app-core/src/workflow-builder/ui/issues-panel.tsx`
- ✅ **Advisories — warnings that do not block** — a second, visually distinct section below the issues, fed by `adviseWorkflowDef` and anchored the same way, headed `Worth a look — none of these block publishing`. It carries two codes: `rdu-bypassable` (a four-eyes rule whose guarded move can be reached without ever entering the watched state) and `rule-bypassable` (any of the five rule kinds guards one edge into a state also reachable by an edge carrying no rule of that kind).
